(applause) thank you for the applause for standard and poor’s. so they are right that the crisis is far from over and that we still need a sustainable solution. let us be honest about this. the solution is not on the table for the moment. what we need are stability bonds, common bonds – choose whatever name you want. the german proposal for a redemption fund is also a very good idea. but if you want this crisis to be over, you have to act now. that is not just my opinion, or our opinion here; it is also the message from italy’s prime minister, mario monti, in today’s financial times. he says that he is not complaining about the downgrading of the ratings: the problem is the persistent policy weakness at european level. he also says that he does not just need a pat on the back from mrs merkel, but a commitment regarding eurobonds and the eurobond market. he is right. there is no sustainable solution for italy with today’s interest rates of six or seven percent. the only way that he can come out of this crisis is to continue with his economies and savings, but at the same time we must lower those six or seven percent interest rates on the eurobond market. otherwise we are asking an impossible task of mr monti. it is not fair to say that it is very good to continue with your austerity programmes and at the same time to continue having interest rates of six or seven percent. the second point, prime minister, is that you have another crisis to tackle: the crisis concerning our values. not just in hungary: look also at what is happening in romania today. i know that the commission has started a number of infringement procedures. i hope that we will find a majority here in the parliament to launch the procedure based on article 7 of the treaty. by the way, mr president, congratulations. you will give me more time now, will you not? will you, prime minister, put it on the agenda of the next european council? i think it would be a mistake if you do not do so. the core of europe is not only about money and about the euro, but also about our liberties and our values.

on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, what we are doing is a very dangerous exercise. we are working on this treaty and my fear is that it could be used in future by the council and the member states as a way of tackling a number of problems outside the acquis communautaire, outside the secondary legislation and outside the normal treaty provisions. we agreed with the negotiators – and this is also reflected in today’s resolution – to limit everything as much as possible to the so-called ‘golden rule’. if we go beyond that then we are in fact destroying the community method. we have three objectives. first of all there must be no double standards; we cannot accept that in the future in europe. the stability pact is based on the treaty and the secondary legislation, but the stability and growth policy is based on this treaty. this is unacceptable. there has to be one standard in the european union and the eurozone, not two. the same applies to the excessive deficit procedure. in future, if we are not able to amend this treaty, there will be two types of excessive deficit procedure: one on the deficit criterion and another with other rules on the debt criterion. again double standards, that is my big fear. secondly, we have to have a binding provision in the treaty that this new treaty must comply with the provisions of existing treaties within at least five years, if possible within three. this must be a binding provision for the contracting parties. finally, we have to secure the role of the european parliament so that parliament and the president of the parliament are able to participate in euro summits. these are informal summits and, as such, parliament must be represented at them. having said that, i am not sure that if we improve the treaty we can back the treaty. that is another discussion and another decision which we will have to take in the coming weeks.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of this very critical resolution on the draft treaty on stability, coordination and governance in the economic and monetary union which is currently being negotiated by the member states. the resolution adopted reflects a common position (once is not always...) of the european parliament. it was important to recall that in this pact all the measures to be implemented must respect community rules and procedures. and let that be written in black and white, including the right of co-decision of parliament! the resolution also stresses respect for the revised stability pact and cooperation with national parliaments. these are fundamental elements.

president. - ladies and gentlemen, william painter was the inventor of the crown cork and he said to his friend and colleague camp gillette - i suspect he is the one who made the razor blades - at the turn of the 19th century: if you really want to get rich, you have to make products that people throw away. i think the two gentlemen didn't quite realize at the time that this was the beginning of what we all know as the throwaway society, long a symbol of our progress, but today a really huge, huge problem. when the directive first came out in 2003, we rightly focused on the problems of this throwaway society for our environment, air quality, soil quality and the social consequences of poorly dismantling and recycling this electronic material in third world countries. i would like to stress that these are still very important arguments. but, as the commissioner said, there is of course an additional argument, namely the argument of the precious metals, the important raw materials in our mobile phones and our other electronic equipment. that is why we have had a great deal of support from a certain section of industry for this revision. the support, ladies and gentlemen - the rapporteur has already referred to it - which we have also received from the commission, but unfortunately not from the council which for some reason is in a mood that it still thinks that the rules are not good. well, in this case, rules are not only good, but they're very necessary to achieve our goals. high recycling targets, very strong export regulations and ensuring that even and especially the small material, our mobile phones which contain a lot of important materials, meet higher targets. finally, i would like to thank the rapporteur very sincerely - and i do so with great conviction - because it is under his expert leadership and with his experience that we have been able to push the strong position of parliament as far as possible with, as i said just now, a council which is actually on a totally wrong wavelength. that is why i am absolutely convinced, as is my group, that i will vote in favour of this result tomorrow.

the agenda calls for the joint debate on:- the report by josé bové, on behalf of the committee on agriculture and rural development, on the distribution chain of agricultural inputs: structure and implications (a7-0421/2011),- the oral question to the commission on imbalances in the food chain, by maria do céu patrão neves, luis manuel capoulas and santos james nicholson, on behalf of the group of the european people's party and the group of the progressive alliance of socialists and democrats in the european parliament (o-000239/2011 - b7-0675/2011), and- the oral question to the commission on transparency and fairness in the food supply chain by bair de chabre, on behalf of the confederal group of the european united left/nordic green left (b7-0230/2011 - b7-067000/2011).
president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, back in 2010 we already approved the report by josé bové, which focused on the market forces to which farmers sell their produce: the manufacturing industry, the distribution sector. now we are looking upwards, with ever-rising commodity prices on the one hand and increasing dependence of farmers on a highly concentrated sub-contracting sector on the other. mr. lyon was just talking about that. this new bové report gives a helping hand to the active farmers and sets out a strategy for a more social agricultural policy which will provide a decent income for farmers. a policy that is also greener. a policy that takes even greater care of sustainable agricultural practices. it is also a policy that makes a concrete contribution to the fight against climate change. it is also a policy aimed at the ruthless monopolies of the seed and food giants. this report creates a close alliance between this parliament and active farmers, and it defends the autonomy of farmers and consumers and it deserves our full support. congratulations to josé bové!
the question is "blue cardboard". madam president, mr fajmon, i have just heard you say that it was absolutely necessary to abolish quotas to allow the agricultural sector to develop. do you realise that in the milk sector, for example, where quotas had kept the system running without major crises for 25 years, their abolition was barely announced in 2015, but their abolition was in fact planned well before 2015, and the sector experienced its most serious crisis in the autumn of 2009, when the price plunged from 45 cents to 19 cents? is this what you want to impose on all other types of production, supposedly to protect the agricultural sector?
madam president, commissioner, i should first like to congratulate mr josé bové on his excellent report, which clearly shows the importance of protecting the producers at the bottom, because although the three players involved are well known, namely the producers, the processing industry and the small processing and distribution companies, only the producers are suffering today. the other two major sectors are concentrated or very concentrated. that's the case with distribution. the producers are poor and widely dispersed. so we must try to organise them as we have just done with the milk report with mr nicholson. we tried to give them more strength. i don't think that's going to be enough. there will certainly be, in my view, two important aspects. firstly, they will have to be organized, but under the control of the public authorities because - and this was said earlier by a colleague - there must be an observatory of prices and margins, because only the public authorities can guarantee a balance between the various actors. secondly, i think we must stop giving competition the sacrosanct value we give it. what matters first and foremost is the well-being of each of the actors in the food chain in the service of the well-being of all. this is above the rules of competition.
in writing. this is a technical correction to the provisions on airport transit visas (atv). this correction will not change the practice of the member states. the amendment to the regulation provides that third-country nationals holding a valid visa or residence permit issued by a member state which does not (or does not yet) apply the full schengen provisions (united kingdom, ireland, cyprus, bulgaria, romania) are exempted from the requirement to have an airport transit visa. under this new provision, a person of iraqi nationality, for example, can travel without an airport transit visa if he holds a visa issued by bulgaria. the amendment seems logical: there is no need to check again a person who already has a visa or residence permit (who has therefore already been checked before and who, as such, has already been recognised as not posing a risk of illegal immigration). in addition, the proposal should also reduce the administrative burden on member states. i fully support this proposal.
in writing. the resolution on pedestrian safety and light emitting diodes will promote the harmonization of standards in various countries. it will therefore open up new markets and enable many firms to trade. competitiveness within the union will thus be boosted. in addition to this advantage for commercial operations, this resolution offers safety for pedestrians and is in line with environmental protection as leds have a long life.
in writing. this new agreement between the european union and indonesia on certain aspects of air services will boost economic growth, which is a vital issue today. this policy encourages the exploitation of space systems, including financial and legislative mechanisms. it will help to instill a spirit of initiative and innovation, which will boost european competitiveness. this policy will also make it possible to achieve coherence within europe by harmonising and strengthening national space programmes. finally, by replacing certain provisions of the bilateral agreements, discrimination between union carriers will be eliminated.
in writing. the report i voted for is a classic agreement, requiring a simple european parliament approval procedure, and which is already provisionally applied. the aim of the agreement is to strengthen cooperation between the union and icao, in particular through the exchange of experts and the establishment of programmes to harmonise upwards aviation safety and security standards.
in writing. the consumer society in which we live forces us to rethink the recycling of electrical and electronic waste. the updating of the directive on this issue was necessary from an economic, environmental and social point of view. it is just as unacceptable to waste raw materials such as gold, silver or palladium as it is to dump toxic products such as arsenic, cyanide, mercury, etc. or to export them to developing countries, thus contributing to the creation of uncontrolled landfills. in doing so we have become accomplices in the export of toxic waste in disguise, leaving the men, women and children of the developing countries exposed to the toxic fumes of heavy metals and dioxins emitted by the incineration of waste. it is our duty to ensure the harmonious, balanced and respectful development of our planet by re-framing our modern lifestyles so as not to leave future generations with a plundered and devastated planet.
in writing. belgium is the european champion in the collection of waste electrical and electronic equipment. this is not to be taken lightly, with nearly 10 kilos of household appliances collected per capita thanks to the effectiveness of the recupel system that has been in place since 2001. this is an encouraging figure for environmental protection and well above the minimum collection target of 4 kg per capita per year set since 2003 by the european directive on waste electrical and electronic equipment. of course, belgium and europe can still do better and draw inspiration from the very ambitious new collection targets set out in the european parliament-council agreement finalised this afternoon with the adoption of the florenz report: a collection target of 65% for 2019 based on the volume of electronic equipment in existence over the last three years, a new scope extended to solar panels in six years, and the incentive to separate waste for reuse addressed to member states. to make the picture complete, the union must also set an example beyond its borders by combating the illegal export of hazardous electronic waste to third countries, a real health and environmental scourge, particularly on the african continent.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report because it provides for stricter controls in the eu not only on pesticides (such as insecticides, disinfectants and repellents) but also on all products treated with biocides. it also provides for simplification of the marketing authorisation process and prohibition of the most problematic substances, such as carcinogens. once this agreement is formally adopted, the updating of the 1998 legislation (directive 98/8 ec) will ensure that treated products such as furniture treated with fungicides will be included in the scope of the new regulation. this vote was therefore of paramount importance in ensuring a high level of consumer health protection, which is my first priority! it should be noted that pesticides for agricultural use will continue to be covered by other european legislation, and more work will have to be done on this!
in writing. space activities and applications (including the galileo and gmes flagship projects) are essential to the growth and development of our society. the european commission's communication recognises the crucial role of space and marks a first step towards an integrated space policy, which will be based on the new legal basis provided by the lisbon treaty. the report voted today supports the commission's position that it intends to strengthen europe's space infrastructure and calls for more research in the space sector. europe is already playing a leading role in the strategic sector of satellite communication technologies. however, in order to maintain this lead and extend it to other space-related subsectors, industrial and research policy will need to be intensified.
in writing. consumer waste is estimated at 90 million tonnes per year, while about 16 million people in the eu depend on food aid. more than one billion people still suffer from hunger in the world. in view of these considerations, it was high time that the european parliament called on the commission and the eu member states to take concrete measures at national level to halve food waste by 2025. this is not only an economic, environmental and social imperative but also an ethical requirement for the poorest. urgent and strategic measures are needed, by raising awareness of waste in general. awareness campaigns, food education courses, improved packaging, optimised packaging and date-of-sale labelling are ways for everyone to achieve decent and tangible results.
in writing. i voted in favour of the caronna report on a subject which is important for european households as it directly affects their budget and the value of saving money by avoiding food waste. this is a growing practice, as according to a study by the commission, food waste in the 27 member states of the union amounts to 89 million tonnes (or 179 kg per person) and is expected to increase by 40% by 2020. but too often this waste is food that is perfectly edible, as opposed to food waste, but which is removed from the food chain for aesthetic reasons, or often because of the proximity of the expiry date. that is why i support the idea of encouraging the primary sector and short-term supply chains in the future common agricultural policy. another measure which would certainly bring results is the multiplication of channels for the free or reduced-price redistribution of unused food. supermarkets specialising in these products are being set up. they are a real demand of the citizens in these times of crisis and "lean cows" and the public authorities must encourage them.
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, the summit on monday was about jobs and growth, but i must say that, in my opinion – and my assessment is the same as that of my colleague joseph daul – it has mainly produced words. seven pages of words and seven pages of good intentions. i think what we need now in the middle of this crisis is not seven pages of words, but acts – acts of the european union and acts of the european institutions. let me give you just three examples. the first is the european patent. it is a major instrument for growth in europe, but, for more than a year now, its introduction – which is so needed – has been blocked. why is it blocked? because, for the moment, the big countries cannot agree on the seat: that is the reality in europe for the moment. on the seat! this is completely ridiculous and i should say even tragic, because, in the mean time, our small and medium enterprises are paying eight times more for the protection of their innovations than their american counterparts do. so i ask the president of the council and the president of the commission: if germany, britain and france really cannot agree, why can the eu leadership not put the seat temporarily in brussels? why are they not doing that – as we did, mr cohn-bendit, with the european food agency in 2001? i think it is a shame to have a council summit about growth but not to be capable of definitely launching the european patent in europe. let me give a second example of this, namely the project bonds. how many times have we already discussed the project bonds in this parliament? we have been discussing them for years. my question is: why do we not start tomorrow with project bonds in the european union? why is it necessary to wait for two years of discussion inside the council and inside other institutions? let me give a third example of this, namely the enormous amount of unused money in the structural and other european funds. we could change the regulation immediately. you know what the problem is – if you put in one euro from the structural funds, you also need one euro from the country in question, and these countries, for example greece and portugal, cannot do this at the moment. so why is it not possible immediately to change the regulation and immediately to use this unused, unspent money for countries in recession, such as portugal and greece? that is what we need today – acts and not words, not the seven pages we have seen up to now. finally, i think another problem we have today is the existential problem that we have in this euro crisis. we have now secured a new treaty. we now have fiscal discipline – let us be honest, a six-pack, a two-pack, a new treaty – but how will we now deal with the high interest rates we still have, even after the intervention of the european central bank, in countries such as spain and italy? everybody here knows that an interest rate of around 6% is not sustainable for italy. whatever decision mr monti may take, it is not sustainable and italy cannot recover with a 6% interest rate. we have already put more than eur 1 000 billion into the fight against this crisis – eur 1 trillion of taxpayers’ money – and the end is not in sight. the reason for this is that we always take half measures. we need a structural solution to this crisis. in my opinion a structural solution means a mutualisation of a part of the debt in a disciplined way. that is the proposal of the five wise german economists: to create a redemption fund. let us be clear. my conclusion is a message to the german taxpayers. if the solution we opt for is a redemption fund, it will be the bond holders who receive lower interest rates: they are the solution to the crisis, and not the german taxpayers. so my message today to all of you is: let us take up that proposal of a redemption fund and let us in our legislative work bring it about as fast as possible.
mr president, i would firstly like to welcome the fiscal compact. in fact it comes 10 years too late. a monetary union needs budgetary underpinning, without which it will crumble. but the long-term objective does not solve short-term problems. greece will not recover in the eurozone, portugal is a cliff-hanger, and italy, spain and france lost their competitiveness years ago, partly because of the euro. transfers will not cure the loss of competitiveness. nor will increased taxation, as mr sarkozy thinks. every time he appears on television he proposes a new tax. i wonder why french socialists still need a presidential candidate because mr sarkozy is turning socialist by the day. now we also have a brussels-based budget tsar. mr rehn is from a country that once lived under tsarist rule. he knows all about it, but in the end moscow never succeeded in imposing policies on neighbouring states like the one of budget tsar mr rehn. mr barroso, this is an important historic lesson to keep in mind – but mr barroso has gone.
in writing. this intergovernmental convention is not only unnecessary, it is also undesirable. and there are two important reasons for this. first of all, it is undemocratic. the european council creates a legal divide by putting mutual agreements between member states above european legislation. in the first case, the role of parliaments is limited to a simple 'yes' or 'no'. the european legislative procedure, on the other hand, must take account of the european parliament's position as a full-fledged co-legislator. secondly, the european leaders are putting a remedy to the wrong disease into this treaty. the fact that conservatives and liberals have been preaching for three years now that only blind austerity can bring europe out of the crisis is no longer news. but the fact that this ideological agenda is now also being constitutionally enshrined is beyond imagination. the gap with citizens' concerns is only made more striking by the comparison with the growth strategy that the same leaders put forward just two years ago, the europe 2020 strategy. the commission has already taken a number of measures to improve the quality of life of the people of the community.

first of all, i would like to congratulate mr santiago fisas on his excellent report and the broad support he has won in this parliament. sport has such obvious benefits from a social point of view, social cohesion, health, that we no longer have to dwell on them. but it cannot be stressed enough and i think it is important that we therefore create space for measures which guarantee the integrity of sport to the maximum and step up the fight against money laundering, against illegal practices. in this connection, mr president, and madam commissioner, i have a message for some of our british eurosceptic friends who have already left the chamber. look at what happened today in egypt, where dozens of football fans were killed in violence. look at what happened today in vienna, where dozens of fans were jailed and convicted in court for violence in football stadiums. that is why, ladies and gentlemen, we need a european sport policy. i shall quote the word for word from mr fisas' report: "to deny entry to stadiums to supporters who have shown violent or discriminatory behaviour", and "to ensure that stadium bans are also enforced at international matches". that is what this report says, and it is very timely, and that is why we need a european sport policy, and that is why we will support you, commissioner, 100%. a database to exchange information on hooligans who are guilty of violence and racism, i think that can be done much more efficiently at european level, as, as the honourable members have said, the fight against doping, against gambling fraud, etc. so let us concentrate on that, ladies and gentlemen, instead of sitting here all night whining about a european flag that can be displayed on a t-shirt, a proposal which i support 100% because, mr kelly, it is indeed on a voluntary basis, with 100% respect for subsidiarity.
first of all, i would like to thank mr schmidt for his excellent report and for the good cooperation. he referred to the eurocrisis, the debt crisis, which is forcing us all to think about how we can better integrate our economy. it goes without saying that we must also come together on taxation, within the eurozone in particular, but within the european union in general. it implies fairer taxation for my group. the elimination of tax evasion and avoidance within the european union, but also, of course, a common strategy of the european union towards third countries. in this context, we are advocating a common european approach, agreements between the european union on the one hand and switzerland and other third countries on the other, rather than agreements between individual eu member states on the one hand and switzerland on the other hand, to solve this. of course, measures are also needed such as a shift of taxation from labour to the environment and wealth, common taxation instruments such as the financial transaction tax and finally a harmonisation of tax scales, particularly for corporate taxation. i think that's absolutely important.
in writing. we live in the age of globalisation. the challenges are no longer continental but global. immense progress in ethics, intelligence and organisation is essential at all levels, whether in the field of the environment, development cooperation, etc. the global universalism of tomorrow will be based on a developed dialogue between all civilizations. we must be open to the changes in the world. the challenges are many: population growth is accelerating, while the ecology and climate are deteriorating. there is an urgent need to build a stronger, more united global governance. it is urgent to build a 'world state', as jacques attali says. we need to learn from china, brazil, south africa, india, turkey... we must give them their place, as well as others, in the renewal of international institutions. addressing the major global challenges will require multipolar and global cooperation. europe will only be able to convince the rest of the world if it has a project supported by the entire political body, from left to right. europe can set the tone if it abandons its sovereignists, if it speaks with one voice.
in writing. the development of a harmonised approach to collective redress is essential in the context of the single market in order to ensure the rights of 500 million european consumers. the integration of european markets and the increase in crossborder activities make the implementation of this approach essential. the national procedures do not provide real solutions at the cross-border level. the class action system, currently used at national level in 14 eu member states, does not provide optimal compensation in the areas of class action damages, such as flight cancellations, defective products, etc. an extension of this to all eu member states would allow for an effective and respectful approach to european consumer protection. this would increase judicial certainty for the parties and the judicial system.
in writing. - i welcome the envi motion for a resolution on the list of permitted nutrition claims, as it will prevent consumers from being confused by the nutrition claim 'now contains x% less sugar (or other nutrients) ' when buying food. i support the efforts of the food industry to make the contents of food 'healthier', but this new advertising message will lead to much more opacity for the consumer than it will encourage the industry to invest in 'improving' their products. the food industry still has the option of advertising products with the words 'reduced sugar' or 'reduced salt'. however, these advertising claims are only allowed if the nutrient content has been reduced by at least 30% compared to other products of the same food category. the advertising claim 'now contains x% less x (nutrients) ' which is to be re-authorised, however, does not have such a restriction and may therefore confuse the consumer. in addition, consumers expect a corresponding reduction in energy content when they hear the phrase '15% less sugar'. however, the commission proposal allows the use of the advertising message even if the energy content remains the same due to other additives. this leads to the impression being given to consumers that this product contains fewer calories due to the reduction in sugar, although the energy content remains the same. in particular, it is of the utmost importance that foodstuffs be provided with clear, easy to understand and transparent information, so that the consumer can make a quick and easy choice among the many products on offer.
in writing. − this report is very much to be welcomed as it promotes a sustainable and efficient waste management strategy. there has been a high number of petitions on waste management plants, so it is useful to summarise the main problems in a report in order to be able to deal with them in practice. these main problems include the lack of environmental impact assessments and existing deficiencies in waste management systems. i welcome the fact that the acceleration of the implementation of the waste directive 2008/98/ec by the member states is explicitly called for. the waste framework directive contains important rules which provide for the protection of both the environment and human health. deficiencies in waste management systems cause environmental damage, such as groundwater pollution. this does not stop at the border, but has a transnational effect. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented in the member states.
in writing. violence against women in wartime is unparalleled. i am particularly committed to these deadly practices. as co-president of the acp-eu joint parliamentary assembly, i have already put this issue on the agenda of the acp twice: firstly at the plenary session held in the drc in november 2010, and secondly at the regional session held in cameroon in april 2011. i also, as president of the jean rey circle, awarded the jean rey international prize to dr denis mukwege for his work on behalf of these women in the drc. these women are not just victims, they want to be actors of change, actors of a better future for their children, especially for their daughters. their commitment to a more just society, one which respects human rights, should also be welcomed. the european union stands by them and regularly takes action against any form of discrimination against them. the creation of a post of european union special representative for women, and in particular the appointment of this representative, will provide greater efficiency and coherence in all the policies it pursues.
in writing. the 2010 report on competition policy by the european commission clearly states that recent consolidations in the banking sector have in fact allowed several financial institutions to increase their market shares. i voted in favour of this report because it very clearly and urgently asks the commission to continue to monitor developments in the european banking sector closely. as regards fines and settlement procedures for antitrust and anti-competitive conduct, i also believe that the policy on fines remains an important deterrent and enforcement tool.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report, the main purpose of which is to entrust the ohim with certain tasks relating to the management of the european observatory on counterfeiting and piracy in order to better combat infringements of intellectual property rights. the aim is to raise public and private sector awareness of the impact of counterfeiting and piracy and to inform consumers of the dangers of buying counterfeit goods and that the practice remains strictly illegal.
in writing. the european union's economic prosperity is the result of its creativity, cultural diversity and capacity for innovation. the trade in counterfeit and pirated goods does not enhance the innovation of european companies and leads to an increase in job losses. in addition, counterfeit products, such as medicines, children's toys, and household appliances, can be dangerous, even deadly, to the consumer. this is a matter of concern to the committee on consumer protection. therefore, the protection of intellectual property rights benefits not only innovators but also businesses and consumers. finally, the merger of the ohim and the observatory will make efficient use of financial and human resources by providing the observatory with the necessary funding and infrastructure to carry out its work.
in writing. taking advantage of the opportunities offered by the single market and globalisation, many companies are operating across national borders. the demand for access to company information in a cross-border context (either for commercial purposes or for better access to justice) is therefore increasing. but this cross-border access to company information requires cross-border cooperation between trade registers. this cooperation already existed to some extent, but was still limited to certain types of information and did not concern all member states. i therefore welcome the adoption of this directive, which establishes a purely technical interconnection of existing national registers. a platform has also been created to improve and accelerate communication and information for citizens and businesses.
in writing. on the basis of the mandate given by the council, the european commission negotiated with the republic of guinea-bissau the renewal of the protocol to the fisheries partnership agreement between the european community and the republic of guinea-bissau dated 17 march 2008. the new protocol, which has just been approved by the european parliament, covers a period of one year from june 2011. it restates the terms of the previous protocol and contains - which i welcome - a clause allowing its suspension in the event of violations of human rights and democratic principles in guinea.
in writing. twelve years after the signing of the association agreement between the european union and morocco, and the granting in 2008 of advanced status to that country (for the record, advanced status reflects the ambition to promote and maintain a structured and open political dialogue, of which human rights, democratisation and governance are essential elements), i welcome the adoption of the euro-mediterranean protocol voted today as it covers the general principles of morocco's participation in current and future eu programmes open to that country, one year after the arab spring, in line with the objectives of the european neighbourhood policy.
in writing. morocco has long been a natural ally of the eu. the adoption of this protocol, which recognises morocco as having a legal basis for participating in community programmes in seven fields such as energy, customs, entrepreneurship, consumer policy, research, health, etc., is a new political signal underlining our desire to make the euro-mediterranean partnership a reality and our support for the democratization efforts undertaken by the moroccan government. this positive vote is in line with the advanced status that the eu granted it in 2008. in view of the profound changes that have been taking place in the southern mediterranean since the arab spring, it was essential to strengthen our relations.
in writing. payment systems that offer efficiency, reliability and security for users are essential to ensure the proper functioning of the internal market. this is why the introduction of euro payment services is essential. it would bring considerable economic benefits while encouraging competition and innovation. however, the slow pace of implementation calls for a deadline to facilitate the integration of the payments market. these measures are therefore essential to ensure consistency in terms of efficiency and safety at european level.

answer is "blue cardboard". i think the question is quite valid. i would like to join in what the representative of the presidency said. i think the best way to ensure sustainable jobs in europe is to embark on the green transformation - sorry to use that adjective, but it is still the case - the green transformation of our economy. this is really where it's going to go. if europe can become a world champion in energy efficiency, resource efficiency, that is, in raw materials, i think that is the best way for us to ensure sustainable jobs for today and for future generations. that is the direction in which we are going to work.
in writing. the fact that we as a parliament can only give our input on the policy lines in the context of the european semester through an own-initiative report, indicates the problem at the outset. neither the european parliament nor the national parliaments have a say in or control over the final policy guidelines. not a disaster in itself, as long as these policies were used as they were intended: as guidelines. however, there is a gradual shift, whereby these directives suddenly become much more binding. an example of this is the commitment of the heads of state or government at the european summit in december to implement these directives fully. to deny the parliament (whether european or national) the right to co-decide in such cases is to undermine the democratic process. the consequences are already being felt: the social dimension of the policies is treated as a stepmotherly issue and is completely overshadowed by macroeconomic and budgetary objectives. however, one cannot exist without the other, and i hope that the european council will also recognize this and give the social dimension of the guidelines the role it deserves.
in writing. today's vote is to endorse the participation of the republic of iceland, the principality of liechtenstein, the kingdom of norway and the swiss confederation, which assist the commission in the exercise of its executive powers in the field of the implementation, application and development of the schengen acquis. parliament gave its agreement to the representatives of these associated states to submit comments on the proposals for measures developing the schengen acquis, or to indicate any problems linked to the application of these measures. in addition, each of the associated states will decide on the transposition of all measures adopted under the procedures of the schengen comitology committees concerning the application and development of the schengen acquis, thus confirming the rules laid down in the basic association agreements. a good thing!
in writing. − i strongly support the eu food aid scheme for the needy, as it allows member states to withdraw products from public intervention stocks for food aid purposes. charitable organizations and especially the needy whom they help depend on this ongoing program. it is of the utmost importance to ensure that this assistance continues beyond 2013 and thus to allow the continuation of the work of charitable organisations.
in writing. in this time of economic, social and financial crisis, the eu must show solidarity with the poorest, who number more than 18 million citizens in europe. that is why i voted for the extension of this aid programme which is over 25 years old. this decision is right, because the eu is much more than an economic project, it is a project of living together, it is a community of values. without solidarity, europe has lost all legitimacy. a growth strategy only makes sense if it is accompanied by a social strategy. we need to think beyond 2013. a european strategy is needed to help the 3 million homeless in europe. the european model is social. as an example, the europe 2020 strategy makes the fight against poverty and social exclusion one of its five priorities. we must not forget our commitments when the new financial framework is being established, which will be in place from 2014. a lasting solution must be found to leave no one behind.
in writing. since the digital age, frequency bands have become free and can meet the growing demand for new technologies and services. due to the scarcity of spectrum and its economic, social and cultural value, priorities for the allocation and use of radio spectrum should be set at european level. this programme provides for general regulatory principles and policy measures and common conditions to enhance efficiency and flexibility, preserve and encourage competition, and calls for an inventory and monitoring of current and emerging spectrum needs. the report also stresses the need for efficient use of spectrum, greater flexibility to promote innovation and investment and ensure a level playing field in the union, and the need to consolidate the proposal for an inventory of current spectrum uses and emerging needs. the ep has succeeded in imposing a large-scale inventory of current spectrum use between 400 mhz and 6 ghz, in order to put in place a flexible and coordinated spectrum policy in europe, allowing for the correction of inefficiencies in spectrum use and to cope with the exponential increase in wireless data exchanges through future frequency reallocations.
in writing. the collapse in milk prices between 2007 and 2009 and the distress of producers are still well known. following the difficulties faced by the dairy sector, a high level expert group (hlg) was set up to examine the long and medium term provisions applicable to the sector. in its report, supported by the agri committee, the ghn points out, among other recommendations, that action should be taken to improve the concentration of supply, which remains low and leads to an imbalance in terms of bargaining power between farmers and dairies. in order to ensure the future stability of the sector, the ghn advocates strengthening the bargaining power of producers, which should lead to a more equitable distribution of value added throughout the chain. i also welcome the proposal to create a european legal framework allowing the setting up of inter-branch organisations in the milk sector.
in writing. - i welcome the report on contractual relations in the milk sector, as it strengthens the bargaining power of milk producers. the aim is to enable dairy farmers to negotiate common contractual terms, including prices, with dairies. although i believe that formal written contracts are useful, inter alia, to raise awareness among stakeholders, i welcome the fact that it is up to the member states to decide whether or not they want to make these contracts binding. i also welcome the strengthening of trade associations, as they can facilitate dialogue between the players in the dairy sector and also promote best practice and create market transparency.
in writing. - mr cornelissen's report contains many sensible recommendations which were rightly supported by a large majority in this parliament. there are also some politically correct passages, as is bad practice, and a lot of euro-jargon is used, but we would like to cover that with the cloak of love. but i cannot understand why in a report like this not a word is said about the unbearable burden of non-european immigration on our jobs, on our social security and on our societies in general. this taboo on a problem which may well become the problem of the 21st century should be unbearable to responsible people. we are not going beyond the classic narrative of the fight against discrimination of all kinds in this report, and that is that while the immigration problem is growing to a scarcely manageable size, it is being exacerbated by the advance of a particularly hateful islamist fanaticism which is finding a breeding ground there. that is not the only reason, but the main reason why i did not approve this report in the end.

message), this instrument would create not only solidarity but also real discipline in our monetary system. it would in any case be a better and more structural solution than doing what we are doing now. we have already put 1 trillion euros of taxpayers' money into resolving the crisis; we have three member states under assistance programmes; and we now have the downgrading of the ratings of the main economies in the world - france, britain and perhaps tomorrow germany. this is a crisis that is causing thousands of europeans to live in hardship. so i think now is the time to have a structural solution and to let not only the taxpayers but also the financial institutions and the bondholders pay by means of a structural reform of the bond market in europe. my final remark is that italy is roving minister for the whole of europe where there are difficulties in the euro area. thank you mario.

are you going to give us money? we're not asking you to intervene for us, but are you going to help us? we want to fight ourselves. are you capable of that? and i said, it's not even in the resolution, because i'm told that the syrian opposition is not united enough, we don't have the right channels, etc. but if we don't answer this fundamental question, what credibility are we going to have? so i'm asking you to construct these answers. i'm not asking you to have them here, i'm not asking you to have them, but i'm telling you that's what's expected. the country expects us to do something concrete, too. the third thing i would like to say is about bashar al-assad. perhaps not many of us have believed for a long time that we could change the syrian regime. we met, some of us at length and sometimes personally, for an hour, an hour and a half, with the syrian president - i remember being in bashar al-assad's apartment, and he was explaining to me how his country had to change: the emergency law, multiparty system, etc. and we were fooled. today, when i hear promises of a new constitution, i say to myself: "all these promises, we believed in them". i'm not asking for bashar al-assad's head on a stake. i'm still sick of seeing saddam hussein strangled or seeing gaddafi's head on a spike. but i say he will be held accountable for his actions. we won't let that go. the international criminal court, it exists for crimes against humanity. and with that, i said to the syrian people, "wait, let's do something". but i really ask the council and the commission to work on concrete measures here, and i ask parliament to support this position.

blue-card answer. – madam president, for the moment there is no agreement in the security council. if, however, we have an agreement in the contact group, that may change china’s and russia’s position. if china and russia see that the contact group, which will be established on 24 february, wants to move in any way to protect the people in syria, then i am sure that this will exert more pressure than any of the discussions in the security council that have taken place in recent months. but we have to stick to that. we have to show that we are really defending them and that we want to go in if it is necessary.
mr. chairman, we are all frustrated. since russia and china used their veto power in the security council last week, the brutal violence has been escalated even further. the situation seems hopeless and is likely to continue for a long time, with undoubtedly some very dramatic events to come. the gradual tightening of sanctions does not seem to have much of an impact on the assad regime for the time being. but the pressure is mounting. firstly, there is the remarkable ability of the arab league to act and also the clear commitment of turkey to this matter. secondly, there is no doubt that the economic sanctions weaken a number of the regime's pillars and thus also increase the isolation of the regime. so we must continue to increase pressure, including on countries like russia and china, which are still standing in the way of clear international condemnation. for example, i am in favour of very clearly drawing up a blacklist or something of companies that are still trading arms with syria or otherwise supporting the regime. given the complexity of the country, its place in the region and the many minorities, it is necessary that we offer a clear and credible perspective, a blueprint also for a new syria, in which the minorities who are still hesitant to leave the regime feel at home. in the short term, it is important that we as the european union take on this humanitarian task, provide food aid, medicine, also develop the idea of a corridor and finally join hands with the arab league and turkey to increase pressure further.
the resolution on the table today is fairly balanced and i can easily identify with most of the recommendations. i do regret that this parliament is avoiding the fundamental and structural problem of the un human rights council. that structural and fundamental problem is the fact that some of the world's greatest human rights abusers - for example china, cuba, saudi arabia, to name a few - can all be undisturbed members of that human rights council. that is unacceptable. only states which have a clean record of their own can and should teach other states how to do things. the fact that the aforementioned dictatorships are justified is turning the whole thing into a theatre of the absurd. that is a structural evil that must be addressed, otherwise the compliance procedures will never work adequately. i therefore hope that the parliament will adopt the amendments i have tabled to that effect tomorrow.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject of the directive on family reunification. the problem is not that some member states do not apply the directive or do not apply it sufficiently. the problem is that the whole philosophy of the directive is hopelessly out of date. family reunification and family formation are among the main sources of mass immigration in europe. very often these are people who cannot speak the language of the host country and who have no prospects of integration or employment. the little integration that has already been achieved is repeatedly destroyed by the arrival of relatives who have to start the whole process from scratch. it's moping with the faucet open. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. we must get rid of the unilateralism of a directive which only allows member states to adopt a more lax policy, but not a stricter one. we must also urgently get rid of the delusion that the right to family life, which is enshrined in the charter of fundamental rights, automatically means that family reunification can only take place in one of the european member states and not in the country of origin. it is time that the rights of immigrants are taken into account, as well as the rights of the member states and the rights of citizens and taxpayers in the member states. the speaker is prepared to answer a 'blue card' question (rule 149 (8))

in writing. freedom, democracy, equality, non-discrimination and respect for fundamental rights are the core values of the european union. some seem to take what is happening in hungary for granted. but it is not insignificant. what is happening in hungary is tragic for the very idea of the european project which embodies humanism, openness, solidarity, progress, justice and equality - in a word, universality. the european project is the rejection of intolerance, nationalism and withdrawal from society. what i find offensive about the measures taken is the grave sin of partiality. we are trying to set up a partial state, a state in the service of a political force. these measures, which are translated into legislative and/or constitutional acts, are turning a european country from a democratic regime into an authoritarian regime. now, the fear of saying, of denouncing, of expressing an independent opinion exists. this change from a democratic regime to an authoritarian regime in a european country is very serious for the very idea of the european project. we've crossed the line. we cannot accept what i call "the tyranny of the majority". we cannot join the european union and violate its founding principles.
in writing. − it is clear that countries belonging to the european union must respect the basic rules of democracy; i am not opposed to objective observers looking into whether or not there are problems. that said, some may well be putting their hands in their own bosom here. a few years ago, laws were voted in flanders and special courts were set up to ban the country's largest, most popular party. and in most european countries there are actual muzzle-loading laws to muzzle free speech on the enormous immigration and crime problems in the name of so-called anti-racism. just recently, france voted a law to impose a new official and inviolable historical truth, this time about the armenian genocide. anyone who questions the official truth is severely punished. these are all matters of particular concern. it is time that this parliament paid attention to this.
in writing. this agreement, which is important both economically but also politically and geostrategically, further strengthens the ties between morocco and the eu. on the economic front, it is a great opportunity for the development of the euro-mediterranean area, stimulating the economic reforms needed to establish a wider, safer and more balanced free trade area in the mediterranean, developing employment and limiting migration. while it offers morocco access to a market of more than 500 million consumers, european agriculture will not be left behind, particularly as regards milk and cereals. politically and geostrategically, europe must support countries like morocco in their efforts to implement democratic reforms. we must help to consolidate democracy peacefully.

will be enough to put an end to the crisis, that the fiscal compact will put an end to the mess, and that it is sufficient to merge the two rescue funds with eur 750 billion? do you really think that will end the crisis? i think that what is happening for the moment is completely different. what is happening for the moment is that we are seeing a calming down of the bond markets because mr draghi has bought us time. he has printed eur 1 trillion worth of new money. that is what mr draghi has done and that is what we are feeling today: the effect of printing new money eur 1 trillion. i do not think that is a real structural solution to this crisis. it could work for a few months or a few weeks, but it will not be a structural solution to this crisis. in fact we all know what the structural solution for this crisis is. we all know that we need a partial mutualisation of the debt. i was at the us treasury in washington and they said that we should do this create our redemption fund, a structural solution for it. the same thing at the international monetary fund. i think that everywhere they can see the light. it is only in europe that we do not see the light. we know what it is. it is to mutualise debt above 60% so that everybody pays lower interest rates, including germany. this is in compliance with the constitutional law in germany, it is compliant with the international and european treaties and it is a fund of eur 2.3 trillion. that is the real firewall. you talk all the time about a firewall, but there's no firewall even if you merge the two rescue funds. that is a fire extinguisher, not a firewall. it is there if you need it when there is a problem a crisis in greece, in portugal, in ireland but not a real firewall around the big economies like spain and italy. mr president, people lose confidence in europe because its leaders are misdiagnosing the euro crisis. in 2010 mr barroso said the euro is a protection shield against the crisis, but the euro fuelled the crisis – cheap money generated debt and bubbles. last year mr van rompuy said the fundamentals of the euro are sound, yet the euro caused a huge competitiveness gap in its own zone. last year he said that stress tests show that european banks were doing well; recently the ecb injected eur 1 trillion to prevent their collapse. last weekend he said the worst of the crisis is behind us; on the same day, german minister of finance mr schäuble, said greece may need a third bail-out. the day before, the president of the ecb, mr draghi, predicted that there was more economic contraction to come, and spain announced it would break the fiscal compact shortly after signing it. even the dutch government, the headmaster of fiscal discipline, is struggling. mr van rompuy and mr barroso, are you amazed that ordinary citizens do not believe you any more?
the european council conclusions of 1 and 2 march, at the very end, in the section 'other items', in paragraph 45, to be precise, dedicate two lines to the fact that herman van rompuy was reappointed president of the european council. my sincere congratulations, mr van rompuy, but it may be an indication that there is something wrong with the democratic legitimacy of the whole way the european union works. you have spoken of a turning point and said that the banks are prepared to forgive a large part of the greek debt. i am afraid that you are giving the matter a little too much of a rosy picture, because, of course, nothing has changed in the main. greece will soon receive fresh billions and then it will just be waiting again until money is needed again. we are all going to continue to struggle with no prospect of improvement. it is in the interest of the eurozone, but also in the interest of the greeks themselves, that greece should be supported in its exit from the eurozone. only in this way can greece be helped in the long term, because it is now trapped in a monetary union for which it is simply not competitive enough. another rescue operation won't change that. instead of keeping the greek people hostage to this whole story, the european establishment would do better to admit that it was a mistake to admit greece into monetary union and that this mistake needs to be corrected.
on behalf of the s&d group. mr president, this debate on the adjustment fund and the extension of the 'crisis' criterion is of particular interest to me, because it seems to me that, apart from the non-extension of the 'crisis' criterion, the fund itself is being targeted, particularly by certain member states who no longer want to hear about union social action. however, in the context of the union's action on restructuring, the egf tool is fundamental. this fundamental character is exacerbated by the crisis and the current situation where the european citizen is losing confidence in the union. the increase in the number and volume of applications every year since 2006 is proof of this. the fund is also recognised as a perfect tool, but an effective one, because it is highly individualised and adapted to the needs of dismissed workers in each specific case, whether in terms of training adapted to the needs of the region or aid for job search. moreover, this anti-action position of the union on employment and social affairs is in total contradiction with the council conclusions on growth and employment. to move forward on this path, we must invest in instruments such as the egf and not rely solely on the completion of the internal market. at the time of the white paper on pensions, keeping older people in employment and raising the effective retirement age were quite generally stated as necessities. the fund, particularly in the context of the crisis, can and must contribute to this. finally, the commission's proposal to include farmers is another indication of this desire to 'dethrottle'. the union's social instruments are too few to be used as hybrid instruments and without coherence as in this proposal. mr president, it is essential to ensure the future of a fund which meets real needs, and therefore the crisis. is the question really one of whether or not the criterion of 'crisis' applies, or of the desire of some to deny the social role of the union?
this report on equality between men and women in the european union, which - if i am not mistaken - was voted on in committee by one man and 27 women, is full of politically correct platitudes and of the enormous european meddling in everything and everything. i therefore did not approve it, although i myself am of course - as many people are fortunate - fully convinced of the need for equal rights for men and women. it is striking that this report has a sharp focus on everything except the great taboo of today, the advance in europe of an extremist and by definition discriminatory islam. the arab spring is mentioned for a moment, but there is a great deal of silence about the problem of islam in our own european countries, from the symbol of subjugation of the headscarf to the horror of circumcision. my conclusion on that is: shame on all of us.
i voted against the report on equality between men and women in the european union because a number of legitimate concerns, such as equal pay for equal work, are drowned in a dredge of political correctness that is alien to the world. for example, there is talk of multiple discrimination, of which immigrant women are victims. why is it so hard to tell the difference between a man and a horse? why is it so hard to say that this is about the subordinate position of women in islam? it's a little easy to pretend that all cultures are equal and equal, and then come to complain that women have to live in a subordinate position within islam. it is quite crazy to say that there must be perfect parity between the vice-presidents of the european parliament. let's just have free elections, where everyone can participate, and judge people on their abilities and their ideas, rather than their gender.
equal rights for men and women, which are the achievements of men and women, are a principle which i believe no sensible person can doubt. that is one of the main reasons why i think that islam, for example, is totally incompatible with the principles of the democratic rule of law as we are fortunate to know them in europe. but, having said that, i remain absolutely opposed to the quota system to artificially increase the representation of women in political decision-making. it is the task of the electorate, of which 50% are women, to elect their representatives. there are probably more men than women who are actively interested in politics at the moment. this is not just a result of discrimination. if we as adults could agree to that, that would be a step forward. but no, that is clearly not possible, and so we are left with this report in the realm of the squeaky-voiced statements on the western wall, and that is unfortunate.
we have just voted on a report on equality between men and women in the eu. the pietikäinen report advocates the opposite, as it openly advocates preferential treatment for women. and that turns the clock back 50 years and just replaces sexism against women with a politically correct kind of sexism, sexism against men. this report wants to impose everything on the member states, even on the political parties in the member states. they would also be obliged to take account of gender differences in their governing bodies. now, these kinds of cases are an insult, an affront to all capable and active women. so let's stop this nonsense and start judging people on what they can do, instead of their gender.
in writing. this agreement extends to norway and iceland the rules on mutual legal assistance already in force and widely applied in the member states. it will undoubtedly help to combat crossborder crime and strengthen cooperation with two countries already in the schengen area and, as regards iceland, in the process of negotiations with a view to its accession to the union. i particularly welcome the possibility, expressly provided for in article 3 of that agreement, of involving eurojust in cases where a request is refused by a member state or where problems arise in the execution of a request.
in writing. the european parliament has voted in favour of legislation to clarify the legal status of crossborder successions in europe. at 450,000 a year, crossborder inheritances would represent 10% of the total inheritances in the european union, amounting to €123 billion. the resolution we adopted today by a large majority provides that the law applicable shall be, by default, that of the country of the last habitual residence of the deceased. the law of the country in which you reside may also apply to your property. in addition, the creation of a european certificate of succession will make it possible to settle crossborder successions quickly and easily by giving heirs the opportunity to prove their status and rights in another member state. with this new regulation, we will avoid legal conflicts and lengthy succession procedures in the future when the laws of several eu countries apply. the council and the commission have already informally given their agreement, so that the text should be adopted by the council without difficulty when it is examined.
in writing. by adopting the sophia in 't veld report, we have opened the way to quotas for women in senior positions in companies. the report clearly calls on the commission to legislate to increase the number of women on company boards to 30% by 2015 and to 40% by 2020. if viviane reding, the european commissioner for fundamental rights, decides, after the results of the consultation, to introduce such quotas, she now knows that she will have the support of parliament, so that is done! let us not forget that belgium was one of the pioneering member states in this respect. i'm glad you did!
in writing. 101 years after the first international women's day, one conclusion is clear: inequalities persist, including in the european union. the vote this afternoon on the report by in 't veld on gender equality in the union in 2011 is important in this respect. there is no shortage of goodwill, but attitudes change little and the glass ceiling remains as strong as ever. i strongly support two of the priorities. the first is the one aimed at eliminating violence against women. the trivialization of them in all their forms (physical, sexual, psychological or economic) is worrying. in belgium, domestic violence is the leading cause of death for women aged 15 to 44. the other priority concerns the objective of equal pay for men and women in equal jobs and degrees. there is no reason why women should earn on average 17.5% less than their male partners. i should also like to say a word about the quota policy promoted in the report. i do not think that compensating for inequality of fact by inequality of law is the right solution and influences a real change in mentality. because that is the goal to be achieved!
in writing. by adopting mrs pietikäinen's report today (508 votes in favour, 124 against and 49 abstentions), we called on the member states and the commission to use legislative measures to establish gender equality and asked the european institutions to extend it to all levels. the proportion of women in political decision-making has not changed in recent years and in some decision-making bodies has even declined. the report therefore calls on national governments to propose, after the 2014 european elections, both a woman and a man as candidates for the posts of european commissioners. we also felt that the role of the european institute for gender equality should be strengthened. the result of this vote shows that parliament has the political will to take active measures to increase parity, and i welcome that!
in writing. the presence of women in politics is a real priority for achieving genuine equality between men and women. it is therefore important to take measures to enable women to reconcile their working and family lives and to remove the barriers to their political involvement. the media can also play a major role in promoting women's access to politics. they must end stereotypes and support the introduction of women in leadership positions. finally, it is important to encourage women from ethnic minorities. the latter are often ousted on grounds of gender and ethnic criteria. balanced representation between men and women is essential for a stable and transparent democracy.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of this report. the primary objective of the bologna process is that a student enrolled in a european university has the right to obtain a degree and to have it recognised throughout the union. the creation of an open higher education area in europe is a great success that must be consolidated!
in writing. − in times when stability and growth are at the centre of much discussion, unrestricted student mobility would be an initiative that does not require financial investment but would be one of the most sustainable ways of promoting the 'european knowledge base'. the logical consequence and a milestone in european integration policy would be that students in all countries could thus obtain a degree in any eu country.
in writing. the lisbon document points to a worrying situation: european universities provide quality education but fail to promote economic growth or employment. this is particularly worrying in the light of the current economic situation. furthermore, the european union's objective is 'to become the most competitive and dynamic knowledge-based economy in the world, capable of sustainable economic growth accompanied by a quantitative and qualitative improvement in employment and greater social cohesion'. in view of this objective, it is essential to consolidate the bologna process and to achieve the goals set by the europe 2020 strategy. this resolution is essential to deal with the economic and financial crisis.
in writing. 20% of graduates will have completed part of their studies or traineeship abroad by 2020. this is the objective of student mobility announced by the european commission. this is part of the europe 2020 strategy. this is an ambitious, if not unattainable, goal in the light of the reality of the current figures. student and teacher mobility in europe is in its infancy: less than 4 students out of 100 have benefited so far. the report by mr berlinguer, which was voted on this afternoon, is timely. it points out that there are too many barriers to overcome in the area of recognition of diplomas and study facilities abroad, and suggests some interesting possibilities. parliament called on the member states to redouble their efforts to strengthen the coherence of the bologna process. this includes cooperation between universities, mutual recognition of ects credits and university degrees, and increased financial support. access to education is a universal value. it must be guaranteed throughout the european union. a mobility rate of 3.5% is unworthy and may disappoint the erasmus generation.
in writing. accurate and high-quality data are essential for sustainable and balanced regional development. indeed, in the demographic, economic and environmental sectors, statistics can be valuable for making decisions on regional development. reliable and detailed information can therefore enable the union to respond more effectively to economic or territorial realities. it is therefore necessary to develop a common methodology and to find effective solutions which will help to improve confidence in statistical agencies. finally, support should be given to eurostat, which wants to establish a legal framework for 'commitments on statistical trust'.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament. we made that clear right away. it is actually unacceptable and it is a shame that such a thing is still possible today in the 21st century. but of course, this initiative does not just fall from the sky. this initiative is not accidental. the real reason for this initiative by mr wilders' party is the sudden loss of 7 seats for his party in the opinion polls at the beginning of january. it was the first time this had happened to wilders' party. and the reason was simple. mr wilders was constantly criticizing. he had spat at queen beatrix when she wore a headscarf on a state visit to oman. he began to attack her and wilders' criticism of their queen hit the wrong throat with the dutch. so what did mr wilders do? immediately found another topic to throw it over another bow. and this time it wasn't the royal family that was the victim, this time it's the eastern european. he opened a checkpoint against the presence in the netherlands of poles, slovaks, bulgarians and other eastern europeans. that's the real reason for this checkpoint. that said, i fully agree with mr daul that the dutch government's silence is not acceptable. and whether this is a party issue or not, as the dutch government claims, is not really relevant. i think we all have a right to know what the dutch authorities really think of an initiative by one of their illustrious citizens somewhere on their territory. i can tell you, mr daul, that i have at least spoken to prime minister rutte personally about it. i hope that you will do the same with mr verhagen because, as you know, this is not only a position of mr rutte, it is a position of the government of the netherlands, and therefore of the two parties in power. it would be interesting if you too would intervene so that the dutch government can take a position. in the end, we also know that what is happening in holland is not a unique fact at the moment. it is something more serious in europe. when i see, for example, your candidate for the french presidency saying that half of the emigrants must be put abroad, stigmatising the muslim community, threatening european partners and endangering the schengen acquis, i must tell you that this is also language and behaviour that must not be accepted today. you already did this this morning and asked me who was really the candidate of the extreme right, him or mme le pen? in any case, what we must not do, and have not done with the resolution, is to mask the things that are here. when there are things that happen to hollande, we must denounce them. when there are things that happen in france, we must denounce them. and when there are things happening in hungary, we must denounce them as a whole!
