in writing. the adoption of this report was a genuine attempt to draw the commission's attention to the many remaining areas of concern regarding air passenger rights. the report has the merit of giving an overview of everything that absolutely needs to be improved for air travellers, and the list of measures to be taken is very long. because air passengers are still too often deceived when they book their plane tickets. for example, with regard to online bookings, i regret that some airlines raise the advertised price for a whole range of reasons. the commission has already proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work. another example relating to families is that children under the age of two should be allowed to sit in a seat, and not be on their parents' lap for the entire duration of a flight. people with reduced mobility should be able to travel with the equipment they need at no extra cost. it would be good if the commission took this as an example when it revised the regulation on air passenger rights (no 261/2004) in the near future.
in writing. − the rights of passengers in respect of compensation and assistance (in case of cancellation or delay) and the rights of air passengers with reduced mobility are strengthened by this report. the report also contains important demands for price transparency, and i therefore expressly support it. i particularly welcome the fact that the need for greater legal certainty is emphasised. it can't be that the passengers need a lawyer to determine their passenger rights. the regulations must contain clear definitions and be subject to uniform interpretation so that passengers receive information that is complete, easy to understand and transparent. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the rules on the free movement of goods are respected.
in writing. there is a need to improve the standardisation of the application of regulations across the european union, and it is essential to ensure that consumers are well informed about them. in order to defend their rights, citizens must be able to know about them. passenger rights must be communicated to passengers in a simple and clear manner. independent and transparent bodies should be set up to ensure the application of these regulations. disputes between air carriers and passengers and passenger complaints can be resolved. finally, it is essential that passengers have adequate safety conditions, including secure seats.
in writing. the new european programme for the resettlement of refugees (erf) from third countries, adopted today, will give more resources to member states (ms) wishing to take in people who have fled war, famine or persecution. this programme, implemented by the ms on a voluntary basis, aims to resettle people who have been granted refugee status in third countries (such as libyan refugees in tunisia in 2011) to the eu. for 2013, the list of people whose resettlement will be funded by the erf has been extended to include vulnerable people (children and women at risk, unaccompanied minors, etc.). the programme will also set a series of annual geographical priorities. for example, priority will be given to iraqi refugees in syria, afghan refugees in turkey, pakistan and iran, somali refugees in ethiopia and so on. the eu's financial support for resettling refugees will also be increased. ms will be able to receive €6000 per person in the first year, €5000 in the second year and €4000 in subsequent years (compared to a total of €4000 per person currently resettled).
in writing. the european parliament today adopted the interinstitutional political agreement on the proposal for a regulation on financial derivatives. it is one of the cornerstones of european financial regulation post-2008 financial crisis. according to the bank for international settlements, the derivatives market exceeded usd 700 trillion in 2011, with the main market share held by currency exchange rate derivatives, interest rate derivatives or hedging contracts against the risk of default of an issuer. the future regulation, which will apply at the end of 2012, aims to shed light on this market, whose products have so far been traded mainly on an over-the-counter basis and therefore in total opacity. it requires the clearing in central clearing houses of a large majority of standardised derivatives. all derivatives transactions, whether on regulated platforms or otc, will have to be reported to national registers to which national and european supervisors will have access. these data will be published. the ep hopes that with these rules many of these products will be more expensive for the companies that use them and less profitable for the banks.
in writing. the protection of workers against electromagnetic fields will remain the missing link in european safety standards at the workplace for a few years to come. eight years of procedure will not have been enough to finalise an agreement between the european parliament and the council on this issue and to complete the legal arsenal. workers are already protected by three european directives: noise, optical radiation and mechanical vibration. i had already expressed my doubts in 2008 when the employment committee (anderson report) followed the european commission's strategy of putting this directive in the closet, where i thought it would be wiser to amend it in the margin and provide for a derogation for the medical magnetic resonance imaging (mri) sector. the commission has provided for such a derogation in its new proposal, but this has not been sufficient and the legal and institutional impasse persists. it is obviously regrettable that european workers are still not protected by common standards of safety against electromagnetic waves. on the other hand, we can still welcome the "repeat" granted to the european mri imaging sector, so important for a whole category of patients, with more than 30 million medical examinations by mri performed each year in europe.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of this report and in particular the vote on paragraph 2 of the report: 'parliament's workplaces should be limited to a single seat for members and officials; calls on the council to take into account the requests already expressed on several occasions by parliament and the citizens of the union regarding the need to establish a single seat for members and officials, as recalled in paragraph 7 of the european parliament's resolution of 16 february 2012 on the guidelines for the 2013 budget.' the resolution adopted by 429 votes to 184 with 37 abstentions shows the will of the members who are clearly in favour of a single seat for the european parliament!
in writing. i welcome the adoption of this report. i would also like to point out that i voted in favour of the amendment which proposes extending the scope of the fund to selfemployed persons, in line with the commission's proposal for a revision of the regulation on the european globalisation adjustment fund for the period 2014-2020.
in writing. since the entry into force of the treaty of lisbon, the european parliament has become a co-legislator of the european union, and now has the same rights as the council in terms of the ordinary legislative procedure and the budgetary procedure. the regulation should therefore be adapted to take account of the evolution of the legislative role of parliament. it was essential to abolish question time in the council in its present form. this measure reflects the institutional changes that have been introduced in recent years. however, the possibility of questioning the council should be retained, so that parliament can consult the council on matters which do not fall within the legislative and budgetary functions.
in writing. the idea of a european democracy fund is to support all those who campaign for democracy around the world through non-bureaucratic means. although the entity is not directly linked to the commission, the initiatives taken by the european fund for democracy (efd) will have to be part of the policies developed by the european union. democracy is never bought. it must be preserved, and no one is immune to its deterioration, north or south. it's not about development or economic wealth, it's about values, public freedoms and respect for others. we all need to be vigilant, as politicians and as citizens.
in writing. i welcome the council decision of 1 march 2012 to grant serbia candidate status and hope that the union will open accession negotiations with serbia as soon as possible. in the resolution we voted on the process of european integration in serbia, we stressed the considerable progress serbia has made in meeting the copenhagen criteria. however, it is important to continue the dialogue between serbia and kosovo and to implement the agreements reached in good faith. parliament also called on the commission to take the necessary steps to ensure that the eu's financial instruments are used effectively and to ensure that the eu's financial instruments are used effectively. parliament also called for the establishment of a european union-wide network of national and regional authorities responsible for the implementation of the acquis. the resolution also calls on the eu to take action to ensure that the rights of minorities are respected. respect for minorities is indeed one of the fundamental elements of the accession criteria.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of a resolution on kosovo which calls on the five eu member states which have not yet done so to recognise kosovo's independence. the eu must play a more decisive role in the conflict between kosovo and serbia, which is essential for maintaining stability and security in the balkan region. i do regret that serbia is exerting diplomatic pressure to dissuade certain countries from recognising kosovo. as regards the dialogue between pristina and belgrade, i am pleased that nine rounds of negotiations have already been held and that they have resulted in several preliminary agreements, including one on the integrated management of border crossing points in the north of the country. it is worrying, however, that previous agreements have not been fully implemented by the serbian side. i would nevertheless stress the need for kosovo to implement constitutional and electoral reforms, in the area of public administration and the professionalism of judges and prosecutors, in the promotion of pluralistic and independent media, and on the important issue of the rule of law. it is also essential to combat corruption, organised crime and discrimination.
in writing. in this resolution, meps called on turkey to improve its relations with its neighbours. the peaceful relations between turkey and the neighbouring member states are a key factor in the reshaping of the negotiations and dialogue. we support the negotiations on the reunification of cyprus and deplore ankara's threats to freeze relations with the cypriot eu presidency in the second half of 2012 if the conflict is not resolved. as a member of parliament, however, i note the intensification of relations between turkey and athens to improve their relations. it is now important that turkey normalize its relations with armenia. in this resolution, i join the other members in calling on the member states and the international community to support turkey in its efforts to manage the humanitarian dimension of the dramatic situation facing the syrian people. it was also important to recall in this resolution that freedom of expression and media pluralism are at the heart of european values and democratic societies, and to reaffirm our concerns about criminal prosecutions against human rights defenders, activists and journalists.
in writing. the eu-turkey accession negotiations are in full swing. the turkish authorities are backsliding in their respect for the universal values on which the european union is founded, not because of the crisis. turkey is a large country, and it plays a strategically important role for europe. it is enough to stress its unfailing contribution to nato and the fact that it is the union's energy corridor for the oil and gas resources of the caucasus and the caspian. this geostrategic dimension, important as it is, must not make us forget the regular failure to respect the rights of women and minorities. these are now being supplemented by unacceptable anti-semitic provocations. the latest example is the recent tv commercial on turkish television in which hitler praises the merits of shampoo "for real men". how can we not finally have the common sense to dare as a credible alternative the option of the privileged partnership? this would at least have the merit of clarity and respond to the concerns expressed by a majority of european citizens who are concerned about possible future unprepared accessions, and the absorption capacity of the eu after the great waves of accessions in 2004 and 2007.
in writing. - this is the latest progress report on the negotiations with turkey and again the essential points are missing: turkey is not a european country. it is not even a european country geographically, nor is it a european country culturally, historically, economically or religiously. this is even more important than the secondary arguments, some of which are fortunately included in this report. what is totally lacking is a clear and unambiguous position on the gigantic sums of european financial aid that go to turkey every year and whose efficient use is questionable to say the least. in return, will we perhaps get this large-scale illegal immigration from the turkic-speaking asian countries, who are allowed to enter turkey without a visa and then of course cross the border into greece? for all these reasons and many others, i could not approve this report.
in writing. in the 2011 monitoring report on montenegro, it was important to call on the authorities to continue their efforts to implement reforms, in particular on the rule of law and fundamental rights. the most important effort must be directed towards the fight against corruption, which is widespread in many areas, and against organised crime. it is necessary to continue the progressive implementation of their obligations under the stabilisation and association agreement. the resolution also calls for progress in the transparency of the legislative process with greater involvement of civil society in the development of policies and legislation. i welcome the adoption of this report which calls on the authorities to thoroughly investigate cases of physical violence and intimidation against journalists and discrimination against the roma, ashkali and egyptian minorities. it was also important to acknowledge the country's commitment and constructive role in regional stability and the strengthening of good neighbourly relations. although on the right track, it is essential that montenegro continue its efforts to move closer to the start of the negotiation procedure for its eu accession.
in writing. i have always supported any initiative/report aimed at introducing quotas. in this case, today i voted in favour of amendment no 3, which calls on the commission to present as soon as possible complete and up-to-date data on the representation of women in all types of enterprise in the eu, and on the measures, whether binding or not, taken by enterprises and by member states to increase this representation, and which calls on the european executive, at the end of this year, to propose legislation on quotas by 2012.
in writing. it seemed important to complete the adoption of this report by voting on amendment no 3, which welcomes the eib's particular attention to climate change, in particular renewable energy, and calls on the eib to put universal access to energy at the heart of its energy sector engagement by supporting small-scale, decentralised off-grid projects, particularly in rural areas.
in writing. citizens still face too many obstacles when exercising their rights across borders, especially with regard to the free movement of persons. many citizens have difficulty obtaining residence permits or social security benefits. the european citizen must be helped to live in the member state of his choice. the number of petitions shows the lack of satisfaction of citizens with the implementation of this freedom. i supported the report because it stresses the need for more transparency and the need to strengthen communication policy so that citizens can fully enjoy their rights.
that concludes the explanations of vote.
- i declare the session of the european parliament adjourned. (the sitting was closed at 1.50 p.m.)
it is a new work to go into all the elements of this left-wing inspired human rights report. i would therefore like to mention one element not mentioned in the report, namely the situation in south africa and more particularly the murders of white farmers. south africa is facing a huge crime problem. all ethnic groups are affected by this insecurity, but the systematic nature of attacks on white farming families is very clear and therefore deserves our special attention. in the last 15 years, some three thousand farmers have been murdered in south africa, often in horrific circumstances, and even children have been left untreated. this is a very vulnerable population, not only because it is a very small minority, but also because of the geographical dispersion of these people and the remoteness of many farms. the american ngo genocide watch warns that several elements indicate that a genocide is in the making. so it is not just a part of existing crime, but there is a targeted campaign against the farmers and it is to be expected that the planned land reform could lead to a frenzy that will escalate the violence further. we are not going to be able to do that, because we are not going to be able to do that. it is therefore high time that the silence around this situation was broken and i call on you, mrs ashton, to condemn the violence against farmers in the strongest terms and to suspend aid to south africa if no effective action is taken.
first of all, this report reads like a catalogue of mostly good but also often very naive intentions, in which people really tend to assume that putting more money into all kinds of european bureaucracy will improve the human rights situation in the world; i very seriously doubt that. much worse is the political correctness of this report. for example, the precarious situation of religious minorities or women in muslim countries is very rightly pointed out, and the paradox of the so-called arab spring is just as rightly pointed out, but the word islam itself is fearfully avoided as a problem. however, i think that is the heart of the problem. finally, the paragraph calling on the eu to organise massive rescue operations at sea for the benefit of illegal immigrants, i could accept if it also called for the return of these illegal immigrants to their country of origin. but that is not politically correct enough for this parliament.
the question is "blue cardboard". madam president, my green card is not a bad thing, mr provera, but when you talk about freedom of religion, i, who am a complete agnostic, always expect to be told about freedom of belief. but when you call for a report on freedom of religion and religious rights, i feel i must ask: what about the rights of belief? it always seems to me that this house is being cut off, if not by half, at least by part of its convictions. i would be prepared to say, "but yes, mr provera, let's make this report", if you would at least let us, the secularists, say what our philosophy of life is, our spirituality, which probably does not match yours. but if we do this, then i agree with you. if we do not, i feel a little bit violated and i also feel very upset by the attack on islam that has been made by one of your colleagues.
i think i'll have a cup of coffee with mr. provera to continue this discussion. more seriously, i would like to say a few words about richard howitt's report which i think is absolutely excellent. it could do without almost all the amendments. i would like to make two points. the first point is the defense of gays and lesbians. this point has been omitted in the new version and i find that regrettable. i think that mrs ashton, the european external action service, everyone has worked to ensure that in our partnerships at least the rights of gays and lesbians are absolutely respected. there is still a tremendous amount of work to be done and we urge you, mrs ashton, to get back to work. the second point i wanted to come back to is the new neighbourhood policy and the 'more for more' principle. we have full confidence in the council, in the commission and in your services, but we are nevertheless afraid that this new neighbourhood policy may sometimes remain as blind as the old one to the realities of countries with which we have interests, with which we are allies. that's why we want to make it operational. richard howitt said it very well. we want a method, we want criteria, we want to measure improvements in democracy and human rights and we really want the real 'more for more' that can be weighed and measured. i think that is absolutely essential and that is a genuine desire for change that parliament will affirm when it votes on this report.
mr president, i think we can almost all agree that the situation in mali is tragic. i would simply like to return to a feeling of unease about this problem insofar as, as has been said, it was seen to come. it was really a chronicle of a foretold catastrophe. we - i am thinking of mr gahler, myself or mr goerens - who sit in the acp have been hearing for months, since the libyan intervention, the panicked cries of our malian colleagues who tell us: 'something is going to happen in mali'. we heard them at the acp meeting in togo, and the day before the coup in brussels, our colleague mr assari expressed a real sense of anger and despair when he said: 'but we must be helped!' and the coup took place the next day. so you want to say, "why couldn't we do anything?" we had the strategy for the sahel, we knew the facts, we knew that something was going to happen and today we hear people say: 'we are going to support ecowas, we are going to try to promote regional consultation, we are going to try, as soon as there is a transitional government, to take development measures for some of these regions which are absolutely poor.' but could we not have spared the day, knowing that it is a powder keg? it is pointless to look back and we must look to the future, but i think we must take all these problems into account, as mrs vergiat has very well said: this was a problem that was in the making and we must therefore tackle it together.
on behalf of the s&d group. mr president, we went with mrs gomes to the syrian-lebanese border at the very beginning of april, not only to meet with some of the syrian opposition who managed to reach beirut, but also to understand a little better the risks of a possible implosion in lebanon due to the tensions between sunnis and shiites generated by the syrian conflict. what does all this tell us? i don't know if we're seeing any more clearly than we were at the beginning. anyway, all the syrians we heard expressed a very great fear of what i would call a proxy war, that is, a war led by an interlocutor, with geopolitical interests playing out in syria, between sunnis, shiites, etc. with possible interference from saudi arabia, qatar, etc. these people told us, "they're going to steal our revolution". it really seems that the annan plan is the only possible outcome in syria, other than civil war. it's not very satisfying but that's the feeling that's expressed there. the annan plan, but an improved annan plan, an operational annan plan, an annan plan which would eventually see assad out, and in a short time. but it is probably here that efforts are still needed, and we have had many discussions with syrian opponents about the implementation of the annan plan. but the fear of civil war and the fear of qatari influence and so on is immense. i would like to make a second point very quickly. refugees are a priority. it's a priority that we seem to think is humanitarian, but it's a political priority. inside lebanon, this is a political priority. they're syrian oppositionists and they're fighting hezbollah. we must therefore make it our political priority. i would like to conclude by saying to you, mrs ashton, that you have rightly maintained an eu embassy in damascus, do as you did in benghazi, put up the european flag...
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, lady ashton has given a very diplomatic speech, but i have to tell her that, although i am not in the habit of agreeing with mr sarkozy, this time i must admit that he is right. i do not think that we still have an annan plan or that the plan is working. let us face the facts. she did not talk about these. more than 100 breaches of the annan plan have been identified in the last two days. more than 50 people were killed yesterday. i do not know how many casualties there have been today in syria. so how can you talk about an annan plan – an annan plan that is not being applied in practice by bashar al-assad? does the international community need several hundred more killings before everybody recognises that in fact it is not working because bashar al-assad is not willing to apply it? i think all this is leading to an increased number of killings and deaths in syria. the latest idea is for us to put observers there, and the international community and the european union are saying that we shall give every support. what does this support mean? to put in place 215 observers you need to spend several thousand euros on military equipment to protect them. otherwise it is impossible. mr vimont is a specialist, maybe he can tell us. in a normal operation in syria, with 215 observers on the ground, how many soldiers would you need? why should we do it? to protect our observers, not to protect the syrian people who are still under attack by bashar al-assad. i really do not think this is the right way forward. what we have to observe – what in fact we know already – is that the plan is not working. bashar al-assad is killing more people. we know that bashar al-assad is ignoring his promises and is simply continuing with crimes against humanity. is that what we need to know? we already know it. i do not think there is any time left now. what we need to do now is not to observe and to protect the observers but to act. we know what this means. it means three things: safe zones, humanitarian corridors and support for the syrian opposition. if we can do this with the security council, let us do it with the security council. if it is not possible with the security council because of the position taken by russia and china, we will do it without the security council. but the international community has an obligation. finally, i have a last question. can you give us some information on the recent incident with a german ship that is trying to deliver arms to syria and that is directly breaching the arms embargo? mr president – and i think i also speak for mrs de keyser – problems as serious as the crisis in syria are more important than you always putting your hammer down.
vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − mr president, the passion and frustration that members of this house feel is very obvious from all their contributions, and it is mirrored in every conversation i have with anyone who takes an interest in the plight of the syrian people and the horror of what is happening to them. i want to make three particular points in closing this debate. the first is that it is very attractive, emotionally and intuitively, to think about safe havens, corridors and so on, but we have to recognise that the violence in syria is taking place across 180 000 km2. it is not in isolated places, it is everywhere. we also have to recognise that the humanitarian organisations are very nervous about anything which would involve the military coming in in support of their work, and we have to respect and listen to the experience that they have. in addition to that, remember that putting boots on the ground is a huge thing, and to do so without the un security council is extraordinarily challenging to say the least. so it is very important that we recognise, when we talk about these ideas, what it is we are actually describing. it does not mean i am asking the honourable members to reject them, i am simply saying we need to understand what it is we are talking about. secondly, the united nations does not have an arms embargo because they could not get agreement in the security council. we have an arms embargo, the united states has an arms embargo and we are asking for details. i am working with the governments of cyprus and germany, who are looking into this right now, and i will respond with any information that i might have. thirdly, we are doing everything possible and working as closely as possible with everybody else to try and get the opposition groups to unite. in my last conversation with kofi annan, this was very much on his mind: how to bring them together through either an umbrella or collaboration around the table, in order that they could put aside their differences and come to a common view. i know that in istanbul this was one of the big issues. so we are continuing to engage with them and with our colleagues across the world to try and support that unity of opposition. but it is my view that at the moment the kofi annan six-point plan is where we have to concentrate our energy. it is where we finally have a security council resolution we can all get behind and with which we can hold everybody to account. it is clear that there are already significant problems in syria with the plan and with the ceasefire. it is also clear that the observers are ready to start their work. we have to back them and to work as hard as we can to get all the six points implemented and to do what kofi annan has asked us for, which is to offer him our full support. he has mine.
vice-president of the council/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − mr president, after decades of internal repression, we see dramatic and hopeful changes taking place in burma. here is a democratic transition unfolding in what looks like a peaceful, collaborative fashion, acclaimed by the domestic electorate and the international community. this is a rare case indeed. what we are seeing points to a government which is serious about change and wants to end its country’s isolation. president u thein sein shows courage and leadership. it would be a surprise, though, if he did not face resistance from some who have profited from the old system. most importantly, daw aung san suu kyi and the president have established a relationship of trust. i believe that we can measure the recent achievements against the three main items we have looked at in our policy review. first: national reconciliation, specifically a willingness to recognise the special position of daw aung san suu kyi and to see the national league for democracy take its place in the political life of the nation. here our expectations have been more than met by the growing relationship between daw aung san suu kyi and the government, and by the change in the electoral law that enabled the nld to register as a party and take part in the recent by-election. this made it all the more important that the by-elections on 1 april 2012 should be well conducted. it was remarkable and welcome that the government invited the european union to send experts to witness the poll. i was delighted that we were able to associate the european parliament with this historic event and that mr ivo belet could join the eu team at the elections. we did not observe the campaign, but it seems to have presented a mixed picture. in the poll itself, there was no sign of fraud and there is no reason to doubt the results. the nld won 42 out of the 43 seats they contested. compared to the 2010 elections, which were marred by massive fraud, this represents huge progress. these bare facts hardly do justice to the importance of the occasion and the meaning it had for a people who had waited fifty years for democracy and endured so many disappointments with so much patience. second: the release of political prisoners. a large majority have already been released, among them prominent figures from the so-called ‘88 generation’ and the national league for democracy. it is difficult to be sure exactly how many remain, as there is a grey zone of uncertainty, but the number is significant, and we shall continue to work for their release. again, there is real progress and a commitment to resolving the remaining cases. third: we wanted to see a real effort to achieve peace in the ethnic areas. all peace talks – with one exception – have brought ceasefire agreements. these are important steps, and we do not doubt the president’s commitment. the longest civil war in the world – the conflict with the karen – may soon be over, even if the kachin conflict remains a cause of great concern. there is also progress on less visible fronts: legislation for trade unions, easing of media censorship – including unrestricted access to the internet – and progress on the economic front, including liberalisation of the currency. there is so much to do that the process of reform will inevitably take time. there are problems with human rights, with macroeconomic management, and with health, education and energy. it is difficult to know where to start, but i believe that president u thein sein has made the right choice in beginning with the political problems. securing ethnic peace is perhaps the most important and the most immediate challenge. but here, too, an effort that brings together all political forces can make a real difference. we have followed these historic changes with respect and appreciation. in january, we suspended the visa bans on the government. at the end of this month we shall do more. decisions will be taken by in the foreign affairs council in a few days. but we need to go further and build a partnership. i will travel to burma next week. i have invited the foreign minister to brussels. i know that the european parliament made a successful visit in february. i hope that what we are now seeing is an opportunity for this country to move forward. we need to enter into active collaboration to assist the reform process and to contribute to economic, political and social development. we have secured more funding – eur 150 million. in addition to ongoing programmes in health, education and agriculture, i have launched a programme to help the national human rights commission. we will strengthen capacity in the public administration at central and regional level. i also plan to offer cooperation with the election commission to build on the success of the by-elections, for which it deserves much credit. in particular, i want to work to secure peace and stability for ethnic regions and to open a long-term perspective for their development. i hope that the honourable members will support me in working with the authorities to turn ceasefires into durable peace. in all these areas we will work with the opposition as well as the government. indeed, we very much hope they will be working together. removing sanctions and increasing aid is not enough: we all recognise too the vital contribution that the private sector can make. we want to encourage european companies to look for opportunities in burma and to bring the highest standards of corporate social responsibility with them. in a longer perspective, the european union hopes to reinstate the generalised system of preferences following the international labour organisation’s assessment. i believe i speak for all of us when i say that we look forward to collaborating constructively with the government, the opposition and indeed the people to reinforce their unity and prosperity. finally, let me express the hope that daw aung san suu kyi will soon be able to come to this house to receive the sakharov prize she was awarded 21 years ago. today is the first day of the burmese new year. i wish all the people a very happy new year. thanks to the determination of president u thein sein and the fortitude of daw aung san suu kyi, there is a chance that for once this wish might come true.
on behalf of the ppe group. – mr president, burma/myanmar is really at a turning point today: i think we agree on that. we are, of course, also aware of the fact that everything is still very fragile in burma. is the reform process irreversible? nobody knows. but we do know that the transparency and the openness of the by-elections of 1 april are not isolated factors. lady ashton, you referred to the majority of the political prisoners that have been liberated. the presence of the international media and the international observers is a big step forward. important steps still have to be made by the burmese authorities. we clearly insist in our motion for a resolution, which will be put to the vote on friday, that they must liberate all the political prisoners and that the real test of democracy will take place over three years, within which the general elections will be held. but the fact is that today we are faced with a unique window of opportunity, and we have to grab it. it is crucial that the authors of the reform process in burma/myanmar receive a clear, strong signal of support from the european union. therefore, high representative, we ask you (and this is important) – indeed, we ask the council – to start reviewing the restrictive measures substantially in order to support the reforms. a strict evaluation of the impact of such a review within a year could be part of that decision. i think that the european union and you, high representative, hold the key in this process. you referred to the renewed potential of the relationship between the eu and burma/myanmar. we can and will benefit from an open economic partnership. it will be a win-win situation for both sides, and of course we also rely on you to push for balanced development with full respect for the local population and the ecological richness of this country. we count on you, lady ashton, to take the lead in this process on a global scale and to start a new era for burma/myanmar.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, after having paid attention to the vote on 1 april, we will also pay close attention on 23 april when the new members who have been elected in this by-election take office. indeed, if we recall some facts, the nld had boycotted the november 2010 elections and today it participated in this by-election which allowed it to obtain 43 of the 44 seats at stake, which is a great success. lady asthon has very rightly highlighted the resolution of ethnic conflicts with the karen minority, ethnic conflicts which have lasted for decades, we must not forget. since march 2011, there have been real changes in myanmar. president thein sein has paved the way for reforms that have put his country back on the international stage and many changes that look to the future for burma. madam high representative, you who are going to burma next week will certainly have a message of encouragement for the reforms being implemented in burma, but perhaps you will also be sending the message that we need to be more vigilant and continue to move in the right direction.
in writing. euroscepticism is growing among employees in europe, and we know that the risk of withdrawal is ever present. the exploitation of legal loopholes by some employers to limit their wage costs to the maximum is at the expense of employees who feel subject to unfair competition, with obvious consequences for their social benefits. some low-cost airlines are experts in this practice, and the text which will be adopted today, with a clear reference to the basis of assignment, covers one of the legal gaps in which they have been falling. we need more positive signals of this kind from the union to european workers, and in particular through this parliament. the first priority must be to communicate their demands, not only on this issue but on all the issues which concern them and for which they fear they will be pitted against each other. the union cannot survive and the recovery can only take place through social dumping.
commissioner, six months ago, right here, we had a debate on the wave of redundancies in europe as a result of the economic crisis, particularly at arcelor, a multinational company operating in nine european member states, which is restructuring its production facilities in europe in the face of sluggish demand for steel. since the closure of two blast furnaces in belgium and luxembourg and the shutdown of one blast furnace in france, it is estimated that by 2012 almost 6 000 direct jobs would be threatened. beyond unacceptable practices such as stop and go - a practice which should be better regulated - the more general questions of the european steel industry and adaptation to change are those which require extremely urgent answers. six months ago the commission announced to us the need to carry out, in the light of the economic crisis, a new consultation exercise on the responsible management of restructuring with the social partners and the public authorities. my question is simple: six months later, where are we?
member of the commission. − in january the commission launched a green paper on restructuring and i believe this is a very important debate. this discussion has a certain history, with strong involvement by the social partners – the trade unions and, on the other side, the employers having strong views – especially because in the crisis period there have been heavy implications as a result of corporate relocations inside europe, but also from europe to other continents. the example that was mentioned – arcelor mittal – has indeed experienced great job losses in the recent period, including in belgium, as we discussed some months ago in this chamber. i think such examples provide lessons for how to develop this discussion. in this phase the commission is summarising the answers. we received more than 100 answers in this two-month consultation period. there was a reference in the forthcoming employment package to the continuing work on restructuring and we will soon – after evaluating these summaries – decide on what the next follow-up should be. we will, of course, coordinate with the social partners about this decision, but let me just finally mention that there is also an important financial implication because in such situations the governments can request the globalisation adjustment fund if the conditions apply. i believe that has to be taken into account when the parliament discusses the future of the globalisation adjustment fund.
i would stress the urgency of this debate, commissioner, because more than 250 000 workers are directly employed in the metal industry, 370 000 in the steel industry and i also think that a vision of the future is needed for a steel project which not only supports economic investment but also workers and their right to job security.
in writing. − i very much welcome this report as it takes account of recent developments in crossborder mobility which have an impact on the coordination of social security systems. it concerns the updating of existing regulations due to changes in national legislation and changing mobility patterns. it shall, inter alia, clarify that posted workers cannot be replaced by other posted persons after the first worker has been posted. in addition, selfemployed persons working across borders are better protected in the event of unemployment. if you were insured in the member state of your last activity and return to your member state of residence where there is no unemployment insurance, you can still apply for benefits in the member state where you last worked. i welcome this strengthening of the rights of selfemployed frontier workers. it will benefit eu citizens, especially those in border areas.
in writing. i broadly agree with the howitt report's outline and emphasis on the potential of the internet to spread the truth and win freedoms. this is one of the great achievements of the arab revolutions. on the other hand, the protection of religious minorities in the world should have been given more attention. defending religious freedom is a modern, secular struggle, which primarily affects christian minorities in all corners of the planet: copts in egypt, minorities in syria, northern mali, nigeria and kenya, laos and vietnam... the commission has also proposed a number of amendments to the directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens. it is "a mistake" to denounce the "security vigilance" of colombia, israel or morocco. these three countries have in common that they have never compromised the security of their citizens and have achieved considerable results in the fight against terrorism. these three states have synthesised freedom and security and share the same values as the european union. it is by standing up for its ideals of tolerance and freedom, but also by choosing its allies well, that the european union will make itself heard more on the international stage.
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, so, mr barroso, it is just one month ago that we had our last debate on the euro crisis here. it was on 13 march, and you remember what happened. statements were made to the effect that the crisis was over, that we had reached a turning point, that the worst was behind us – statements not made by you, i have to recognise and underline that; the commission did not do that, but statements by mr van rompuy, mr draghi, mr jünker, and also by mr sarkozy (but for quite obvious other reasons). what i deplore is that none of all these people is here today, when we invited them to come to this parliament. mr draghi is not here, and mr jünker had no time; the council is also not here – there are a number of civil servants, but the council is not here. in the middle of a euro crisis, when they were stating a month ago that everything was done and the worst was behind us, they do not have the courage to be here. it is a lack of courage that i want to deplore today. secondly, the real thing is that we all know that the crisis is not over. we said it here one month ago, on 13 march. we introduced austerity; we have established emergency funds; we have injected one trillion euros into the banking system; we have bought more than eur 100 billion of sovereign debt from countries like portugal, ireland and italy; we have decided on the biggest haircut in the history of the world (what happened with the greek debt is the biggest haircut ever carried out) – and what is the result? the result is that interest rates in spain and italy today are back where they were six months ago, at around 6%; the spreads are again around 400 basic points for countries like italy and spain, and these spreads are also going up for other countries in the eurozone – mainly for france, belgium and other countries. so one thing is clear: we need another approach. we need a structural approach, not the half measures we have seen for two years now. in my opinion this means two things: first of all, we must launch a new growth strategy (a real one), using the eib as you have indicated, and spend money from the eu budget – project bonds, not only a pilot project, but project bonds on a full scale. my proposal is that you put forward to the council and parliament a global plan on this – on a new growth strategy – as fast as possible. the second thing, as we know, is partial mutualisation of debt. you have talked, as everybody here does, about strengthening solidarity without losing discipline. there is only one good proposal: the redemption fund, a eur 2.3 trillion fund which will help all member states. everybody knows that this is the only way forward. what is important to underline is that, in the case of a redemption fund, it is the bondholders who are paying the bill by receiving lower interest rates, not the taxpayers of germany or the other countries. perhaps we should launch a campaign in the german press about this. now they are paying the bill with the so-called fire extinguisher – the rescue fund – while, with a structural solution of a redemption fund, it would be the bondholders paying. finally to greece, and i am very pleased with the announcement today. however, last week i sent you a letter about greece, which was also about the financing of the political parties. the two main political parties in greece – both irresponsibly in my opinion, given the current situation – paid themselves eur 29 million of taxpayers’ money to start the campaign on 6 may. the commission spokesman responded in a press conference by saying that it was none of the commission’s business. i do not agree with you. you interfere in pensions and in wages in greece – this is not foreseen in the treaty, but you do it, and i think it is necessary to do so. in the second memorandum of understanding it is clearly stated that there must be a reduction – and i quote (i have here the mou no 2) – ‘of the electoral spending of eur 70 million in greece’. i think that, based on that memorandum, you can interfere. i find it a scandal – and this is my final word – that greek citizens have to make sacrifices and that the european partners have to continue to pay while most greek politicians simply continue the bad practices of the past.
with the euro crisis threatening to flare up again, it is once again becoming clear that the conservative majority's approach to the crisis in europe is not working. the european social democrats made that analysis almost two years ago. a policy that focuses only on austerity, without balancing it with investment in growth and jobs, is strangling the european economy. it is commendable in itself that the commission is finally coming up with an employment package, but unfortunately this is already too little, too late. instead of placing employment and growth at the heart of the european semester, the communication simply lists a bunch of measures already in place without establishing a strong legal basis and without allocating the necessary resources. there can only be one conclusion: this "employment package" is a sham. there is a growing consensus on our criticism that the narrow view of austerity and fiscal consolidation is leading the eu straight to the abyss. it is therefore high time that the commission came up with a real growth plan and put growth and job creation at the heart of the european semester. the time of loose ends is over, we need tough legislation.
- i'm the reporter. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we are hereby introducing a new community tax framework which contains a complete set of rules for calculating the individual results of each company or branch, wherever it is established in the european union. the commission notes that the tax base of the group is the same as that of the group of companies, and that the group of companies is the same as that of the group of companies. the member states can then apply their own tax rate to the part they are entitled to, and this is done through a system whereby the entire administrative handling is done by a single main tax authority. with this ccctb we are making it easier and cheaper to set up a company across borders, which is certainly opening up new prospects for smes. we are also making taxation more transparent. we avoid overtaxation and double taxation, but also manipulation for the purpose of tax evasion. and we are certainly making europe more attractive to foreign investors. the commission's proposal is a good one, but it is not the only one. that fits perfectly into the double battle we are fighting today and which we have also been talking about all afternoon. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. and if we want to solve the debt crisis, then we must of course also ensure that the member states can collect the taxes they owe, and this proposal is mutually beneficial. so let no one invoke the crisis to put everything on the long or soft track, no, the crisis should strengthen our ambition and our sense of urgency. i would like to draw your attention to three points: first, the optional nature of the system. the ccctb may well be optional, but we do think that if the system is good enough for optional use, it is also good enough to make it compulsory. and the benefits increase with the number of people who use it. and that is why we are proposing a road map which will make the system gradually mandatory for all companies except smes over a period of five years. we want to give them an assessment of its practical usefulness and a tool to guide them in their use. secondly, there is the scope of harmonization. this proposal does not concern the tariffs, but only the taxable base, the basis on which the tax is levied. the member states retain full sovereignty in the matter of tariffs. we do however expressly ask that this issue be included in the revision clause, so that it can be examined later whether and to what extent the tax competition which continues to exist between the member states is beneficial or harmful to the europe we want to build together and which is aimed at a social market economy. thirdly and finally, mr president, we believe that we must move forward. if the council fails to reach a consensus with 27 member states - and it is a pity that the council is not here again - we ask the commission to start the procedure without delay to implement the proposal in close cooperation.
for the greens/efa group mr president, i believe that this is a very ambitious report from a political point of view. i think, marianne, that you have gotten as much as we could get if we want a large majority in this chamber. if we really want to put an end to tax optimisation, this is a first step in corporate taxation. it is obviously unthinkable for us to introduce a voluntary twenty-eighth system of corporation tax alongside the twenty-seven systems of the member states. because, of course, who will use it if it is on a voluntary basis, if not exclusively those companies for whom this would mean a lower effective tax rate than if they were to refer to their national scheme? i believe that the idea of making this system compulsory for large companies is a must and i thank the rapporteur for including it in her report. as for the council, which is once again conspicuous by its absence in tax matters, i simply want to say one thing: by playing the game of tax competition with each other, we are all the losers, all the public treasuries are the losers. this is not a zero-sum game, it's a negative-sum game. i therefore hope that the message which this house will send tomorrow will be heard by the council.
the differences in corporate taxation between eu member states are considerable, whether in terms of rates or income. that is why the commission is opting for back door harmonization. a uniform corporate tax base has advantages. companies pay taxes in a cheaper and simpler way. who gets the tax revenue in the end is none of their business. that is a matter for the member states and that is where the problem lies. when member states levy corporation tax on companies operating in more than one eu country, they must share the revenue between them. the commission's allocation key is designed to disadvantage competitive and exporting countries. they are taxed and lose corporate tax revenue. the report by mrs thyssen already explicitly mentions the solution which will soon be put to these countries, namely minimum rates of corporation tax, whereas we have not thoroughly studied the fiscal effects of the distribution key proposed by the commission. that's why i say: i do not agree with that and i think a large part of the council agrees with me.

the european parliament will deliver its final opinion on the reform of the existing tax on energy products tomorrow. i support the commission's wish to split the existing energy tax into two components, the energy content on the one hand and the co2 component on the other. this dual taxation is essential to eliminate the distortions of competition which exist today in europe. together, these two components will also determine the rate at which the different energy products will be treated. energy efficiency also plays a very important role here, as do the environmentally friendly in writing. the fight against terrorism and transnational crime are major concerns that must remain a priority in our action. the pnr agreement with the united states only very imperfectly meets the fundamental rights of citizens. the main objections are that the scope of the directive is vague, that the data are stored for a very long period without being erased, that there is no administrative or judicial remedy in the event of an error, or that the data are transmitted in a different way. while acknowledging the efforts made by the commission to achieve a more balanced agreement, it is clear that essential principles such as respect for individual rights and eu law and the protection of personal data of eu citizens are not respected. the concessions made to us partners may set a dangerous precedent in the negotiation of pnr agreements with future partners. because how can we deny other countries the advantages that we have granted to the united states? the agreement is not balanced enough and does not guarantee limited use in the fight against terrorism. this data could be used for other purposes.
in writing. i supported, along with 409 colleagues, the pnr agreement on the transfer of passenger data to the us department of homeland security. it is a compromise and the text is imperfect. i guess so. but its rejection would have had the sole effect of keeping the previous agreement in force, which was less good in terms of privacy protection. such a rejection would not have opened new negotiations with the united states. some people say this is a matter of privacy. for others, the issues are simply the fight against terrorism. our job was to place the cursor between these two requirements. and i feel like i did. the right to security is fundamental, even cardinal. without security of who we are and what we have, what good is it to be able to go out, think, say, publish or protest? and then frankly, the privacy-obsessed, who happily throw the baby with the bath water here, wouldn't they better take a look at the policing we are subjected to on social networks, an otherwise more intrusive "big brother" for the job! and, everyone will agree, less legitimate!
in writing. − the new eu rules on taxation of energy products must make a real contribution to the clean energy transition and the reduction of co2. an increase in taxes which would lead to a rise in the price of diesel fuel is not a sufficient contribution. at a time of increased demand for energy products, and given that the eu remains highly energy dependent and thus subject to price controls from abroad, there is a huge pressure on industry and thus on eu citizens. to improve this situation, renewable energy sources should be promoted, as they are essential for economic development. the real question is not how to tax traditional energy but how to create incentives for the use of renewable energy. energy products must compete so that better energy can make the breakthrough tomorrow. diesel and petrol have their advantages and disadvantages, and no fuel can be considered 'better'. the aim is therefore to reduce the tax gap between diesel and petrol, not necessarily by raising the diesel tax, but more importantly to give sustainable energy a better place in this 'energy race', which is what this proposal continues to miss.
in writing. increase excise duties on diesel? i voted against this resolution this afternoon, with a majority of my colleagues. not that the measure, which is actually aimed at putting petrol and diesel on an equal tax footing, is fundamentally wrong. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the rules on the free movement of goods are applied in the member states. i just think that right here and now, it's not appropriate. we are experiencing an unprecedented economic crisis. citizens face incredible difficulties in meeting their household energy bills. i cannot believe that the signal sent today is to penalize them even more. and may i remind you in passing that our assembly is only consulted here. it is the governments who decide, unanimously, on these tax matters, which is quite a bit of the unpleasant press commentary in recent days. it is therefore up to them to develop this debate, by forcing the commission to take a more positive approach to environmental matters and to ensure that the desired harmonization does not involve an automatic increase in taxes. there are other tools, other avenues, too few of which are being used at the moment.
in writing. the lulling report, which aims to revise the 2003 energy taxation directive, justifies the proposal for taxation of energy products on the basis of the need to achieve the objectives of european energy policy as well as those of industrial policy. however, the direct effects of the measures provided for in the report lead to an increase in fuel prices, which would have direct fiscal repercussions on final consumers. i am opposed to a report which places disproportionate burdens on the citizens. i have opposed a sudden change which penalizes fuel consumers without giving them the time or the means to adapt. although it is essential to reduce pollution and co2 levels, i consider the lulling report to be a penalising one when, by increasing the taxation of energy products, it only aggravates the economic difficulties of the citizens most affected by the crisis we are going through. the fight against climate change must be implemented without the most vulnerable paying the highest cost.
in writing. - i voted with conviction in favour of the report by mr lulling on major changes in the taxation of energy products and electricity. the current energy tax system does not provide sufficient incentives for energy consumers to reduce their energy consumption and to opt for cleaner energy. in some cases, taxation even encourages the use of more polluting forms of energy, such as coal. this doesn't make any sense. everyone benefits from the transition to cleaner energy and more efficient energy use and reduction of co2 emissions. the amendments to the energy taxation regulations will make a major contribution to this. in the epp group we support the principle that taxes on motor fuels, fuel oil and electricity should henceforth be based on both energy content and co2 emissions, and that a product should be taxed more heavily the more polluting it is. however, we could not support the principle of proportionality because it would have led to a sharp increase in the price of diesel, with all the negative consequences for businesses and private consumers. it is now up to the council to act wisely in the light of this balanced opinion of parliament.
in writing. - i voted without hesitation in favour of the resolution on combating tax fraud and tax evasion. in the same voting round we also voted on the ccctb, the common consolidated corporate tax base, a system which will also help to prevent tax evasion. these are the points to which we must pay particular attention in times of crisis, when we are asking our citizens to make heavy sacrifices. i also think this resolution is important for another reason. it is in fact suggesting that we should also further adapt the european savings directive, which has already proved its usefulness, in the light of the experience gained and conclude tax agreements with more third countries than is the case at present.

the s&d group believes that the european union should be able to take action to combat the spread of the disease, and that the european union should be able to take action to combat it. for this reason, i am convinced that the setting of standards for locally produced goods should take account of the distances between the place of production and the point of sale and the point of consumption. i am convinced that the setting of standards must encourage the cost of these externalities to be taken into account. a new criterion is therefore needed to define what could be labelled as 'locally produced'. this should be a criterion that takes into account the socially sustainable production process aimed at the choice of local products evaluated according to the producer's behaviour and the method of work. the impact on public health and consumer safety, all of which have a direct effect on our economy and sustainable development, should also be considered. it is important to include all these criteria in our business semantics. we will ensure the development of the regional and european economy and trade. i have decided at this stage to bring a higher level of consistency to public procurement in the report for which i am the rapporteur in the committee on the internal market and consumer protection by establishing preference for local products. in conclusion, i would like to say that as far as food is concerned, we must distinguish between the origin of the food and its provenance, allow for a clearer labelling of foodstuffs - this is already the case and is in the process of being done - and clearly indicate, in the case of animals, the place of rearing, slaughter and consumption, or sale. this is very important because origin and provenance are two completely different things, and you don't want to mislead the consumer.

mr president, our rapporteur, jo leinen, has stressed how important it is for our parliament and for the union to keep the environment at the top of its agenda. this is not easy in these times of crisis and austerity where these concerns are often relegated to the background in national politics and in the media. i think the presidential campaign in france is a good example of that. i believe that there are two priorities, two lines of action, for doing more with less money: first, the need to move towards greater coherence. it is mentioned in paragraph 3 of the resolution. this is what i shall call, in summary, the tobacco case law: the european union must abolish all subsidies for products which have adverse effects on the environment and on biodiversity. secondly, there is another aspect which has not been mentioned much so far, namely the need to take better account of environmental health. we have been recalling this since 2003 and since my report on the environment/health action plan. we must reduce the exposure of our populations to the cocktails of chemicals that have serious health effects. that's why it's critical to add this environmental component to plans to fight cancer, alzheimer's, obesity, all of the chronic diseases that explode in unhealthy environments.

in writing. - president, commissioner, a modern and effective higher education is crucial for the future of europe. by 2020, at least one in three jobs in the eu will require higher education. today, on average, only one in four young people graduate from college or university. so we need to put a big catch-up on the road. at the same time, we need to invest much more in cross-border studies: our new ambition is that by 2020, 20% of all students will have completed part of their studies or training abroad. a doubling from today. so there's work to be done. and finally, this: despite the criticism of the new model of ranking for higher education, i still think we should at least give this u-multirank system a chance. only because it is based on the needs of the student, and not the prestige of the educational institution. it's a fresh approach, at least. education is about much more than just available budgets for r&d and the number of publications in reputable journals. we support the commission in this project and look forward to the final result by the summer.

in writing. young graduates in europe are among the first to be affected by unemployment and a particularly sclerotic labour market. this is why higher education is important, since it is better adapted to the changing world of work and the needs of employers. this is also the message delivered this afternoon by the european parliament, which, by adopting the tőkés report by a very large majority, underlined the need for member states to modernise their higher education systems. only 200 of the 4000 european higher education institutions are among the top 500 in the world. creating the right conditions to attract the talents of tomorrow and to stem the brain drain is therefore more imperative than ever if the european union is to remain globally competitive and to successfully face the reality that by 2020, 35% of all jobs in europe will require highly skilled workers. other ways of helping young europeans deserve to be put into practice with the ultimate objective of investing 2% of the gdp of each member state in education. i am thinking in particular of real access to higher education for all social groups, the facilitation of student financial loan schemes and better recognition of qualifications acquired abroad.
on behalf of the alde group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, let me paraphrase the newly elected president of france, as did mr swoboda. he won the election saying, "change is now!" i would rather say that europe is about change. we need europe now, on 9 may. mr president, in view of the seriousness of the crisis we are in today - and we must admit this, because it is good to say so many positive things about the past, but we must also acknowledge the seriousness of the present situation - i would say that europe is being marginalized in the world. europe is facing a trend of slowing growth and innovation, the exact opposite of the incredible catch-up movement of emerging countries. europe is currently experiencing an ageing population in a world that is becoming considerably younger, a world that will soon have ten billion inhabitants. europe, on the other hand, remains fragmented, divided by its nation states in a world increasingly dominated and unified by great economic and political empires. ladies and gentlemen, contrary to what is being said, the crisis we are experiencing in europe today is not a crisis of the world economy, it is not even a crisis of world finance, what we are experiencing today is in fact a european crisis and, more particularly, a crisis of the european states. for some years now, europe - and we must admit this on 9 may - has been navigating without a horizon, without ambition, without hope, apparently unable, for the time being, to find a new direction and sustainable solutions. i believe that on 9 may, instead of congratulating each other, we must realize our inability to respond to this crisis in any other way than by the 'policy of small steps'. we must realize the need for a great leap forward, a great federal leap forward in europe. i believe, ladies and gentlemen, that europe will be federal or it will not be. the choice is as simple as that. we must stop being afraid of words and of using the word 'federal' when we talk about the future of europe. we need a federal solution, a solution which will give us the real economic government we need, the real democratic control we do not have at the moment, a real european treasury and a real federal budget, and why not, later on, a solution which will one day lead us to a real european army. we have no choice. european federalism is the only way to integrate into globalisation, the only way to negotiate on an equal footing and the only way to maintain our economic and political role. it is also the only way to protect our social model in europe. i also believe, mr president, that today, on 9 may, is the time to turn a page, a page of illusion. the illusion of some who believe that only austerity will get us out of the crisis, but also the illusion of others who think that growth can return without putting the public finances of the states in order. it is a great mistake to have opposed the one to the other in recent years. we must not choose between discipline and solidarity, between rigor and growth, we need both: growth and rigor, discipline and solidarity. and more specifically, the fiscal compact. the fiscal compact must not be changed but growth must be added to the fiscal compact, and the capital of the european investment bank must be increased. all the european funds available must be deployed. and finally, project bonds how long have we talked about them here in this house must see the light of day. second, pooling of the debt redemption fund is needed. let us pay less interest to the bond holders so that the new money becomes available for investment and for growth in europe. my conclusion of 9 may is clear: if we as the european union want to survive, we need federalism, european federalism. federalism is not something to be afraid of: federalism is the future of our continent and its future starts now on 9 may. for the greens/efa group mr president, i will continue along the same lines as my previous colleagues. i'm feeling a little mixed up today. on the one hand, it is true, i recognize mrs kroes as a liberal, as i like them, a liberal who wants real competition in the market and who is against all abuse of a dominant position. it is not for nothing that you were commissioner of competition before. i would like to thank mrs niebler for her considerable efforts to improve the commission's proposal and also to tackle these monopolies. so we can be somewhat pleased that today we see prices for data roaming on the market that can be up to 10 000 times the cost. can you imagine that? 10,000 times the cost! these are obviously extreme examples but they are part of reality and we will bring this down, in the long run, to a ratio that will be roughly 50 times the cost price. so we can say: "praise jah, you people! we're going from a ratio of 10,000 to a ratio of 50, which is a good thing!" that said, tell me in what truly competitive market do we see profit margins of this order? how is it possible that in an economy which is, in principle, a large market economy - "social market economy", yes, but still a market economy - we can still find margins of this order? in french, we call it racket! the word is not french, but you understand what we're talking about. the regulation we put in place actually keeps this racket going because, as we have seen in the past, all real prices converge towards the ceiling we set. this means that there is no real competition. i know that the commission has made a great deal of effort to ensure that there are structural measures which bring about genuine competition, but i agree with mr goebbels: how is it possible that these four large groups which are de facto present throughout the internal market - we call it an internal market - behave as if we were in twenty-seven hermetically separated national markets. this is obviously unacceptable. that is the source of my bitterness about the text we are voting on tomorrow. we voted against it in committee to encourage angelika to go further. however, it must be admitted that, while the text which will be adopted represents significant progress, it is still far, far from being a success in terms of consumer protection. i hope, therefore, that, as jens rohde said, we will not have to re-establish new standards in terms of roaming price ceilings in four years' time. it's not normal. what i want to see is real competition. we saw what happened with free in france. it's a national market effort. we need a lot more competition. the racket of the telecommunications and mobile telephone operators is a racket which must be stopped, as all rackets in a self-respecting economy.
president, commissioner, yesterday one of the main points in the belgian press was that young people in belgium send an average of 71 text messages a day. that's a lot, even for a frequent text-messenger like myself, but it does indicate what the world of young people is like. there's an insane amount of texting, just like you see a lot of people on the train downloading data and reading newspapers. it's just become our world. we don't just make phone calls, we text and we use a lot of data traffic. in this respect, it is particularly noteworthy that we have not yet found a solution to the high prices, the high national prices, especially in my country. secondly, from a european perspective it is totally unacceptable that we have not yet found a solution to these roaming charges. this is bizarre - and mr goebbels has shown this very well - because there is no good reason why telephone services from antwerp to paris should be so much more expensive than from marseille to paris. we all know that has to do with some of the big corporations that make insane profits off of it. so we are taking a step forward and, as a modern social democrat, i do not really care whether the cat is gray or black, as long as it catches mice, as long as the problem is solved. we want competition. we want an open market. if that doesn't work, we'll put a price cap on it. we are doing that today and that will at least ensure that prices are reduced by the summer and that they are put into a somewhat more normal perspective. in the long term, this parliament, and certainly this part of it, wants those roaming charges to be abolished in full and in time. we 've agreed to 2016 and you and your services especially - because it goes to the next legislature - will be addressed . national tariffs must also be drastically reduced. because picking up data traffic, calling and texting is a right and our consumers should not pay too much for it.
