the resolution before us, mr president, is a very technical debate, but we must think it through and not rush into it. i myself have a background in biotechnology and i understand that there is a necessary balance to be struck between the protection of intellectual property rights and the possibility for breeders to develop new varieties and also to protect them with breeder rights. however, this resolution focuses on some of these discussions. the 1998 patents directive excludes plant and animal varieties and essential biological processes from patenting. however, there is considerable disagreement on the interpretation of this principle and the highest court of the european patent office has also concluded in previous decisions that it was impossible to give a clear interpretation. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area. the fact that the commission has indicated that it wants to come up with a new and transparent proposal is an important signal. the resolution excludes not only the classical and conventional breeding methods and their products from patentability, but also new techniques as such, such as assisted selection and other innovative technologies, which are not patentable. this is a potential brake on innovation in the european union. another problem is the broad exception for plant breeders' rights proposed in the resolution. this breeder's exemption already exists today in the breeder's legislation, but is limited by the concept of essentially derived varieties. in the case of patent law, too, this exception exists only in a few countries and then only in a very limited way and only for the development of new plant varieties, not for the commercialisation of these varieties. the resolution does not take these facts into account at all. the amendments concerning stem cell research are also unacceptable to us and are separate from the rest of this resolution. the resolution before us contains too many factual errors and takes a different direction in the debate from ours. we need to strike a balance between protecting intellectual property, safeguarding and encouraging innovation and the possibility for growers to develop new varieties. we therefore want to amend the resolution on important points and cannot support it in its present form. europe has in the past driven a large part of the plant biotechnology industry out of europe through wrong and ideologically inspired regulation. let us not make the same mistake with this resolution.

on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, my group requests postponement of the vote on this resolution. it is a resolution which is not only very complicated but which was added at the end – indeed, at the very last minute – and i think that not only my group but also other groups had insufficient time to prepare on this very complex and technical issue. my request is simply to put this vote on the agenda for the may ii part session. i am not asking that it be scheduled ad calendas graecas. may ii means only a two-week difference, but it will give us an opportunity for serious discussion in the groups.
in writing. regional airports and air services are not only essential as transport hubs, helping to facilitate the smooth functioning of the european single market by transporting citizens and goods between regions, but they also have a crucial function as drivers of economic growth. in this respect, i welcome the vote on this report, which clearly calls for a balance with international airports and a strengthening of the role of regional airports. in this connection, i would like to highlight some of the strong demands in the text: improving connectivity with other modes of transport; banning the 'single baggage rule', which has a negative influence on the commercial activities of airports; improving the networking of regional airports, which must offer an alternative in the event of congestion in the connecting platforms. efficiency, accessibility and cost-effectiveness must be the main priorities of regional airports, and they must not become the plaything of local politics.
in writing. - i am in favour of the intention to draw up a resolution on regional airports, but it would have to be coherent and contain solutions for regional airports. unfortunately, this is not the case in the motion for a resolution which is now before us, which is why i did not vote in favour of this resolution. regional airports are of great importance for territorial cohesion and economic and social development in the regions. they are important as transport hubs and contribute to economic growth. the measures must be specifically tailored to them. while a definition of regional airports should be drawn up, it should not exclude airports whose main catchment area is above a capital city, as called for in this resolution, as this would mean excluding many existing regional airports from the scope. in addition, air freight is particularly important for regional airports. this has been overlooked in this resolution, which, with a few exceptions, is more concerned with passenger numbers and passenger concerns. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's airports are not subject to the same rules as those applied to airports in the united kingdom. the economic burden must be balanced against long-term profitability, as regional airports are often not profitable at the outset but can be financially self-sustaining in the long term and thus represent a significant added value for the whole region.
in writing. i am in favour of a single seat and that it should eventually be in brussels. i abstained on amendment no 6! if it is just a poll, no problem, i am 'for' everything being centralised in brussels. but is a vote just a poll and should this very important question be raised by way of an amendment? i cannot vote in favour of this without being able to vote on the consequences of such a decision. that would be like buying a cat in a bag, "buying a pig in a poke". what will happen to the distribution of the institutions following the transfer of strasbourg to brussels or the domino game which could prove financially painful if bad decisions were taken? i also don't want it to be a disaster for belgium or anyone else. we must think about the consequences for direct and indirect employment upstream, not downstream when it is too late! we are in the same situation here. when all the consequences for employment, the consequences for brussels and the real financial consequences are planned in the same vote, then we can have a real discussion and above all a real vote.

in writing. the european parliament has decided not to grant discharge to the european food safety authority (efsa), whose costs are considered excessive and for which conflicts of interest have been established. as a member of the parliament's committee on the environment and public health, i have seen firsthand how rightly efsa has been in the news with the resignation of its chairwoman, diana banati, who has taken over the leadership of a major food industry lobby. this has been the subject of much criticism, especially since ms banati's links with the industry have been known for a long time. asked on thursday about the matter, the european commission said it was "regrettin' that mrs banati is going straight from" the food safety agency to the lobby. although this passage is "not illegal as such", it is still contrary to the agency's spirit of independence! today's vote in parliament is therefore a very important signal that conflicts of interest with industry are no longer acceptable!
in writing. - we have today approved the new roaming ceilings. that's good news for those of you who are going abroad this summer. from 1 july, not only will rates for texting and calling continue to fall, but anyone who wants to surf abroad with their smartphone or laptop will no longer have to worry about having to pay an astronomical amount for their mobile phone after the holidays. in a europe without frontiers there is no place for tariffs which differ so much at home and abroad. i hope that the structural measures we are deciding today will lead to customer-friendly packages so that we will soon no longer have to impose price ceilings.
in writing. until now, mobile phone charges abroad have been significantly higher than domestic charges. sometimes we were witness to real scams! the new eu regulation adopted today puts an end to this totally unfair pricing. it introduces new limits for voice calls, sms and, for the first time, data downloads. the reduction of the ceilings is planned in three stages: for example, an sms will cost 9 cents on 1 july 2012, 7 cents in 2013 and 5 cents in 2014. so this is very good news for users. the new ceilings will come into effect on 1 july 2012 and will save consumers from finding very annoying mobile phone bills when they return from their summer holidays!
in writing. - i welcome this report for two reasons. firstly, this further reduction of the tariff ceilings is absolutely necessary because the prices of suppliers have still not been reduced sufficiently and competition is still being impeded. the elimination of monopolies in the roaming market and the facilitation of access to networks for smaller providers will allow fair prices for eu citizens and further promote the single market. secondly, it finally addresses the problem of involuntary roaming, which occurs mainly in border areas when the mobile signal of the domestic operator is too weak and therefore the foreign signal is used. border residents are surprised to receive their bill and to be charged for roaming, even though they have not left their home country - this must be stopped as soon as possible, and this report is a first step in that direction.
in writing. the aim of the roaming regulation (3) is to continue to cap roaming prices, to facilitate access to the wholesale market for new alternative operators, to allow consumers to choose freely another operator for roaming services independently of their national operator from 2014. with the advancement of new technologies, communications from our mobile phone are everywhere. in a europe without frontiers, we must avoid any surprise of excessive charges to the citizen. the latter must be able to use his mobile phone, with the operator of his choice, in a member state other than that of his residence. the objective of this structural process is to achieve naturally low roaming prices through effective competition between operators.
in writing. vote on "phone roaming" this afternoon, and great news for consumers' wallets, who will see their mobile phone rates abroad fall again. since roaming charges are not the main factor in consumers' choice of operator, operators are happy to accept them and charge often prohibitive rates, particularly in belgium. this is why the public sector is fully justified in placing a ceiling on these amounts within the limits of what is reasonable for voice calls and sms, but also for data roaming. for example, the maximum rates for sms abroad will be divided by 5 in five years, from eur 0.29 before 2009 to eur 0.11 in 2009 and gradually reach the ceiling of eur 0.06 in 2014, which is less than the cost of an sms for a user in belgium! the data transfer will be capped at €0.70 in july 2012, €0.45 in july 2013 and €0.20 in july 2014! the opportunity to recall that mobile phone rates in belgium are the highest in europe and to recall the need for the government to quickly remedy this anomaly...
in writing. in the age of crossborder communications, these additional costs imposed on european consumers became totally unjustified. citizens who go on holiday to europe will no longer have to break their bank to pay their telephone bill on their return. all bonuses equally for all border citizens. and for maximum convenience, the consumer will be able to use this service without having to change numbers. finally, to avoid unpleasant surprises for consumers, operators will be obliged to send a message to the user with all the costs of the country where he arrives, and wherever he is in the world. today, the european citizen is paying too much for his communications. my ultimate goal is for the consumer to finally pay a fair price. by 2016, a study will be carried out to analyse the behaviour of operators as a result of this new regulation. if we find that the price grid is still exaggerated or that the difference between the national and european levels is too great, we will re-introduce a new amendment procedure. for too long, the real competition, which is profitable for consumers, has been for absent subscribers! the european parliament comes to the rescue.
in writing. through new technologies, defamation crosses borders all too easily. so which law should we apply? what country? it is important to establish clear rules within the union in order to strike a balance between freedom of expression and the right to privacy. the creation of a single body will make it possible to decide which country has jurisdiction in the event of international disputes. in a hyper-connected world, in a world where the impact of information is multiplied tenfold, it was necessary for the eu to have such an authority. respect for the right to privacy and the right to the image of individuals within the european union must be guaranteed. the exercise of fundamental freedoms and rights must respect democratic values.
in writing. i welcome the vote on the resolution on the eu's trade strategy with the countries of the southern mediterranean. the text is very balanced and provides a good in-depth analysis of the current situation following the arab spring. i think it offers good prospects in the medium and long term. it is not just a trade strategy, but a question of developing our trade with our neighbours and of directing it in such a way that the eu can support the democratic transition of these countries. it is essential that we play a role in this area so that the people can experience a significant improvement in their rights and living conditions. that is why i thought it important to draw the attention of the inta committee to certain aspects, such as the greater involvement of civil society in the forthcoming partnerships. it would also be appropriate for the ecb to support more strongly the development of smes in the southern mediterranean countries. these should be at the heart of their growth. i would like to conclude with a point which i consider to be fundamental: the mobility and exchange of students from both sides of the atlantic. we should adopt major programmes to promote them, because the future of our countries depends on a greater understanding of each other.
in writing. i voted with 191 meps against the resolution on the patenting of essentially biological processes. indeed, behind this legal jargon lies one of the most important battles of the moment: what place is given to therapeutic freedom in an open and modern society? it is also right to question the motives of the signatories of the resolution, who brought it to the plenary "almost clandestinely", when the only legitimate respect for the variety of species, in this case broccoli and tomato, is at stake. these sympathetic claims are a thinly veiled challenge to a whole other dimension: the future of scientific research in europe. the anti-natural coalition of the christian democrats, socialists and greens has also come out with the adoption of amendments which glorify the very worrying decision of the european judges of 18 october 2011 excluding any possibility of patentability on the human embryo, even when these cells are used to treat neurological diseases. this resurgence of scientific obscurantism is cause for concern. liberals, progressives and humanists must now unite to ensure that the ethics of hope, which is demanded by the majority of patients and researchers, is triumphant in the 8th research programme.
in writing. the situation in somalia, both in terms of security and food, makes it one of the countries with the most difficult living conditions for the population in the world. piracy, partly due to this situation, has increased in recent years in this important global shipping route. the european operation atalanta has recently been extended by the council, which has also widened its scope. i think there can be no military solution to the crisis in somalia and piracy in the waters of the horn of africa/somalia. i therefore join the european parliament in calling on the council and the commission, through this resolution, to review their political strategy on somalia and to end the eu navfor atalanta operation, which cannot be considered a success given the increasing number of attacks. i therefore call on the commission and the council to support the efforts of the united nations to promote the process of reconciliation between the parties to the civil war in somalia. it is also essential to ensure access to humanitarian aid in the affected areas, the most important of which is to improve the humanitarian situation of the population.

the agenda calls for the report by ms maría irigoyen pérez on the strategy to strengthen the rights of vulnerable consumers [2011/2272(ini) ] (a7-0155/2012).
the point is closed. the vote will take place on tuesday 22 may 2012. the commission shall be assisted by the committee of the regions.
mr president, it is clear that in the present crisis the role of smes is crucial in boosting employment and combating unemployment, particularly youth unemployment. i therefore believe, as do many colleagues, that their access to the structural funds should be simplified and extended, but also encouraged through training and support in the procedures for access to these funds. i also recognize that the legal framework in which they operate must be adapted to the means they have to meet their obligations, and this is where the definition of an sme is important, because it will determine the law applicable to the company or not. the current definition would cover more than 67% of workers in the union. it is therefore an important part of our economy and of the labour market. as is often the case in our legislative decisions, a balance must be struck. the legal framework for smes must combine the objective of efficiency for their development with the objective of adequate protection of workers' rights and the general interest. but an unbalanced reduction in the administrative burden on smes may involve a risk, for example, in terms of health and safety at work. workers cannot be subject to different risks, in particular concerning measures to improve workers' health and safety at their workplace. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the health and safety at work is not seen as an administrative burden, or to reduce the accounting and financial obligations of certain smes, which, in my view, is a matter of concern for the workers' representatives. how can workers be involved in the decision-making processes of companies if they do not have the information on the basis of which decisions on restructuring, for example, are taken? a minimum of transparency towards workers and third parties is a necessity. so in the hunt for excessive administrative burdens for smes, let us not lose sight of the objective of these burdens and let us not discriminate against the workers of these enterprises.
i have abstained on this report, although i naturally agree with most of the recommendations calling for equal rights and equal opportunities for women in turkey. then why didn't i vote? firstly, because it is strange to me that this report does not address the heart of the problem, namely the rise in turkey of a very radical islam, a kind of islam that has actually remained stuck in the middle ages and where women are by definition considered and treated as inferior beings. it is equally unacceptable to me that in this report, as in many others, we do not have the courage to impose strict conditions on the flow of billions of european subsidies that have been going to turkey for years, for example on human rights and freedom of expression. finally, we should have the courage to state clearly and unequivocally that turkey is not a european country, has never been a european country, has no european culture and that, therefore, potential accession to the european union must be rejected.
i have abstained on the bozkurt report because i think that there are some positive and some negative elements in it. it is important that we as the european parliament stand up for the rights of women, both in europe and outside europe, including in an asian and islamic country like turkey, where so-called 'murders' are taking place, where forced marriages are commonplace. it is good that the report mentions this, even if it does so in a rather hesitant way. why is there not a clear discussion of the increasing islamization of turkish society? we are talking about a deep cultural undercurrent that cannot be tackled or countered with regulatory initiatives. it is clear that such a country does not belong in the european union, and therefore it is rather extravagant and strange that the report advocates the so-called 'european perspective for turkey'.
in writing. i welcome the vote on this report which emphasises respect for fundamental rights in the implementation of the european internal security strategy (iss). this european strategy, which was presented by the commission in november 2010, includes a series of initiatives on terrorism, cybercrime and border management. each aspect of these initiatives should fully respect european and international human rights standards. today we have outlined the priorities which should be pursued by the european strategy. the fight against terrorism and organised crime, including mafias, white collar crime, tax fraud and corruption, should therefore remain the 'key priority', with particular attention to freezing the assets of terrorism suspects. member states should also, in parallel, introduce effective legislation to assist victims of terrorism and are also required to strengthen the prevention of terrorist acts, such as the early detection of radicalisation.
in writing. the fight against terrorism and organised crime is, and must remain, a key priority of the internal security strategy (iss). however, the fight against corruption is also essential. the union must not only know how to react, but also how to prevent and interpret. a common threat assessment system should therefore be developed to enable early detection of signs of violent radicalisation or threats. a comprehensive approach is essential to achieve an effective security policy within the european union. the judicial systems of the member states should be able to cooperate optimally, including with the assistance of europol and eurojust. there is a need for synergies between the internal and external aspects of security. particular attention should be paid to security and border management. full respect for the rule of law and fundamental rights is an integral part of and must remain inseparable from this internal security strategy of the union. a clear division of tasks between the union and national levels is essential. parliament must also be involved in the process of formulating the union's security policies.
in writing. the borsellino report contains many worthy recommendations for strengthening internal security in our european union. but i think it is a bit of a wash with the faucet open as long as we stick to the ideological grind of 'open borders'. why should the reintroduction of border controls be rejected by definition when there are so many problems of illegality and crime? and how is it possible that in may 2012, after the elections in greece and france, which were largely about the border problem and the schengen problems, we approve such a report without talking about it? all of this seems to me to be a denial of very real problems. we are now experiencing firsthand what gentle healers do in these stinking wounds.
in writing. in voting for this report, we defended strong criteria for the implementation of the new european criminal legislation, between respect for the principles of proportionality and subsidiarity and absolute respect for the principle of the presumption of innocence. our aim was to send a message about the action the commission intends to take in this criminal area. any initiative by the european executive will now have to ensure that the rights of suspects/convicted persons are respected, with the commission considering proposals concerning the protection of the functioning of financial markets, the protection of the eu's financial interests, the protection of the euro against counterfeiting or serious breaches of data protection rules.
in writing. the lisbon treaty provides the basis for new european provisions in criminal matters. however, this is still a very sensitive area. therefore, clear and transparent rules on how and when to use european criminal law should be established. those rules must fully respect the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality. it is crucial that any european criminal law legislation can guarantee the rights protected by the charter of fundamental rights and the european convention on human rights, while respecting the different legal systems and traditions of each member state. it was necessary to set up an interinstitutional working group in which parliament would participate in defining the scope and application of appropriate criminal sanctions at european level. in this context, the information service will be a useful tool for the work of members, thus ensuring the quality of parliament's work as a co-legislator.
in writing. in voting on this report, we called on the european commission and encouraged the 27 to 'carefully and continuously analyse social and consumer situations and behaviours which may lead to the vulnerability of certain groups or individuals, for example by analysing consumer complaints'. we also called on them to tackle vulnerability through specific measures, which, if necessary, will protect all consumers 'regardless of their abilities and at any stage of their lives'. we stressed the need to increase consumer awareness of product safety, particularly targeting children and pregnant women, and asked the commission to carry out a detailed analysis of the impact of misleading and aggressive advertising, particularly on children and adolescents. the eu and its member states are urged to invest more in consumer information and awareness campaigns, as many consumers' vulnerability stems from their lack of assurance, their insufficient understanding of the information they receive or the choices available to them, and their poor knowledge of the claims and compensation systems available to them!
in writing. − many of the eu parliament's situations concerned consumer protection. all consumers are particularly vulnerable, although many of the differences mentioned are not comprehensible. of course, all the proposals made special reference to minors and people with special needs. the aim must be to increase transparency and better define responsibility for the distribution, sale or supply of products or services, in the interests of all consumers and thus also of those most in need of protection.
in writing. while the single market must ensure a high level of protection for all consumers, it must pay particular attention to vulnerable consumers in order to take account of their specific needs. all consumers are likely to become vulnerable consumers at some point in their lives. the diversity of vulnerability situations, however, makes it difficult to adopt a uniform approach and general measures. this vulnerability requires special protection and a special strategy. the capacity of vulnerable people to make optimal decisions in an autonomous manner needs to be strengthened, information and education needs to be improved and they need to have access to the same goods and services as all consumers. particular attention should be paid to persons with disabilities and to children and adolescents who need to be made aware of the risks of aggressive or misleading advertising. they are also particularly vulnerable to the use of new communication technologies such as smartphones, online games and social networks. the elderly and the vulnerable who suffer from the digital divide are also included. in all these situations, flexible legislation is needed to adapt to the different cases of vulnerability.
in writing. the year 2012 marks the 20th anniversary of the single market. it is an opportunity to recall that citizens and businesses are the first beneficiaries of a fully operational and properly functioning single market. this own-initiative report aims to promote a coordinated and cooperative approach to improving the functioning of the single market through better transposition and application of the single market rules. the report focuses mainly on the development of a stronger regulatory framework for the internal market and on bringing the internal market closer to businesses and citizens through a strengthening of existing tools.
in writing. on 9 may, the commission registered its first european citizens' initiative: a petition entitled 'fraternity 2020', which i myself signed in order to give 'a boost' to this youth-oriented initiative. what could be more relevant than to focus on future generations and to strengthen the european exchange programmes which have proved their worth, such as leonardo, erasmus and the european voluntary service. the gurmai report voted on this afternoon does not deal with the issues that the citizens want to take back, but more specifically with the procedure and the role that the european parliament will play in promoting this tool of direct democracy. it wisely proposes to hold public hearings on the most promising citizen initiatives. in view of the six petitions already registered in the race for the million signatures, i simply hope that the european parliament will make it a point to support only those petitions of general interest, those which unite and not divide. the 'uno di noi' initiative, whose objecti­ve is to ban all forms of research on embryonic stem cells, is therefore out. it is obvious that liberal and humanist vigilance is required on this kind of subject!
in writing. - the so-called 'european citizens' initiative' is little more than a layer of pseudo-democratic veneer and has little to do with real democratic participation as it exists in switzerland, for example. how is it possible that we allow a body of politically appointed high officials like the commission to decide which cases are to be dealt with and which are not? how can we call for a 'call for tenders' when essential issues - such as turkey's potential accession to the european union - cannot even be put on the agenda? the fact that the european parliament does not condemn this farce, this well-understood farmer's fraud, is yet another missed opportunity.
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, the summit tomorrow is the 18th special summit which has been organised since the start of the crisis in december 2009 – the 18th, and every time those organising these summits say ‘yes, this is a final summit, it is an important summit, it is a summit where we shall tackle the real origins of this crisis’. only two months ago, you remember president, in this room, there were a number of eu leaders who said that the crisis was behind us – that it was over – and they were wrong. they were dramatically wrong because this euro crisis started with greece, a country that has an economy representing only 2% of european gdp, but today we are facing another crisis with spain, which is the fourth economy of the eurozone, and with italy, the third economy of the eurozone. together they add up to something as big as the german economy, and these two economies, these two countries, are paying interest rates of more than 6% and we have spreads today of more than 500 basic points. that is today! so let us be clear: not only is this crisis not over, but it is not about greece. it is not a crisis about portugal; it is not a crisis about ireland; it is not about italy or about spain. it is, in my opinion, not even an economic crisis or, indeed, a financial crisis. it is simply a political crisis that we face today in europe, because our leaders today are unable to take the right decision at the right time. they are unable to create what we need, which is an economic and fiscal union alongside the monetary union, and the fact that the current situation is so dramatic is their responsibility. they have, in fact, transformed what was a small financial crisis in december 2009, involving just 2% of european gdp, into a crisis of unforeseen magnitude today. that is the main topic for a nato summit. the euro crisis is a nato summit topic today, and the other leaders of the world are pointing the finger at europe. why? because three things are necessary but our leaders are not capable of deciding on them. first of all we need a real economic and fiscal union. secondly we need to mutualise the debt with a redemption fund as quickly as possible if we want interest rates to go down for countries like spain and italy. thirdly, we need a growth pact to relaunch the european economy. my plea today is not to the council or to the member states, whose interests the council chiefly defends. my plea is to the commission: it has the right of initiative. the ball is in the court of the commission. mr obama is waiting for it; we are waiting for it; even mr cameron is waiting for it. what are you waiting for? that is my question today. my proposal is double. firstly, i ask that the commission should put on the council table a proposal for a redemption fund because that will have a positive effect on the markets, and it will be impossible for even ms merkel to deny it or refuse it. secondly, i am asking for a growth package to be put on the table. we know the various elements of that: increase the capital of the european investment bank; use the unspent money of the european funds; and launch project bonds – and that brings me to my final point. let us be blunt: they told us that tomorrow they would announce project bonds as one of the instruments for economic growth – a proposal worth eur 230 million. that is the proposal which is on the table today. but we need billions, commissioner, not millions, and we need not a temporary solution, as they are discussing, but a permanent solution for growth in europe. so my plea to the commission is this: use the right of initiative that only you have, and put on the table tomorrow a real proposal for project bonds, a real proposal for a redemption fund and a real proposal for a real growth package for europe. (applause)
blue-card question. – mr callanan, i have a hard time taking lectures on competitiveness from the uk, and especially from british conservatives. if i look at the pile of debt that exists in europe, i see that british debts – public and private debt combined – amount to 500% of gdp. right? this is the result of deregulation and basically of the recipes that you are lecturing all of us to adopt. the result is that the british taxpayer was taxed to the tune of several hundreds of billions of pounds to rescue a financial sector gone adrift through your deregulation. so, i am sorry but i have had enough of your lectures.
mr president, the invitation from herman van rompuy to the informal meeting tomorrow, a letter of three pages, has a very succinct sentence at the very bottom, and i quote: "in addition, i propose that at the end of our dinner we discuss recent developments in the eurozone". end of quote. it would almost be funny if the situation wasn't so serious. there's an elephant in the dining room, but if we all pretend together that the elephant isn't there, then maybe it will go away, or at least we'll think it's gone. how can we talk about investment, economic growth and employment without at the same time looking at these issues in the light of the euro crisis, which hangs like a sword of damocles over the prosperity of the euro countries? we have heard some very strange statements in recent weeks, for example from a european commissioner who said that a greek exit from the euro zone would be, i quote, "the end of civilization in that country", nothing more and nothing less. and it is precisely this kind of self-delusion that makes it impossible to come up with real solutions. these solutions, which are not a matter of blind flight, such as direct european taxes, common eurobonds, a so-called european redemption fund, will place even more burdens on the net payers and will only encourage irresponsible behaviour by some net recipient countries. the result will be that the economic problems will only increase in this way.
mr president, when nigel farage says something here, it can make people smile, it is generally well said, but it does not have much of a consequence. but when it is a colleague of mr rehn, namely karel de gucht, european commissioner, or a governor of the european central bank, like mr coene, president of the national bank of belgium, who begins to ramble in public about, seemingly out of nowhere, that greece could leave the euro and that this poses no more problems than that, i believe it is more serious because these people bear an overwhelming responsibility in what happened last week in greece, that is to say the beginning of a banking panic. i believe that when you are a european policy maker, you do not behave casually, as if you were at a coffee shop. (applause) i would like to thank the second thing that concerns me, as it does many of us today, is spain. let's get this straight. of course, this summit meeting tomorrow in brussels can be interesting, but if it does not provide a credible response to what we are putting in place to prevent a financial collapse in spain or italy, we are missing the urgency of the moment. we have few options: either ask the ecb to buy gold, which it cannot do because of the european treaties, or go back to the solution advocated in its day by none other than mr sarkozy, which would be to transform the european stability mechanism into a bank and allow it to lend to the member states or recapitalize the banking sector. i think that's a sensible solution, and it's time we looked at it again. finally, on the question of growth, i would rather talk about investment. if we are to do what we did yesterday in spain, by building 40 airports, or today in greece, by laying down new motorways, i think we are going the wrong way. if we do not put in place an investment programme that makes europe a world champion in energy efficiency, resource efficiency, we are missing the only real opportunity for european economic leadership in the 21st century. so that's what, and that's what only, should motivate us. it's not just investments in concrete and brick. it is investments in education and research, investments in health, which are all factors of competitiveness, to use a term you like to use. so i think that's what this is about. answer is "blue cardboard". it is obvious that investment cannot be financed perpetually by increasing debt. and we are among those who say that we must reduce our corporate debt. that's not up for discussion. so a form of debt brake, absolutely, i agree completely, but we cannot pretend that the only debt that was on future generations was financial debt. we have accumulated a considerable social and environmental debt and so, if we are all prepared to say that we are in favour of a debt brake mechanism on these three levels, then you will have us on board without any restrictions.
mr president, what i like most about the french president is his name. mr hollande turns the french expression le beurre et l’argent du beurre into government policy, and that is why there is no growth. mr hollande reduces the pension age to 60 while people in holland see their pension age increase to 67. hollande demands total solidarity from holland. growth is like love. everybody is in favour. even mr schulz would be. ‘all you need is love’, and all we need is growth, but how to get it? new bridges and motorways do not generate growth alone. no country built more than spain; its unemployment is at 25%. mr obama spend usd 800 billion on a stimulus package, the only thing he stimulated was the national debt. growth is generated by competitiveness, by entrepreneurship, by risk capital and by a low tax burden. precisely the things mr hollande dislikes. therefore he will not have his growth; he will bring about la portugalisation de l’europe. let me finally congratulate the german social democrats, mr bullmann and his friends, with their fellow party member mr thilo sarrazin and his book on the euro. i am going to read it, i hope so will you.
mr president, listening to all these speeches, here and elsewhere, on growth, one might wonder whether there is not, at last, an awareness that the obsession with brutal budgetary austerity will not bring europe out of the crisis. the informal council tomorrow must be an opportunity not to be missed, an opportunity to reorient european policy in the direction long desired by certain heads of government, now joined by the new french president. we need fiscal austerity, debt control, but with a pace of adjustment that does not throw our economies and our people into chaos. the capacity of the eib and the ecb to act needs to be strengthened. we need a stimulus through targeted investment spending, through national budgets, but also from the strengthened european budget, through household spending, by giving more resources to those who spend, a guarantee of employment and training for young people, and higher social minimums. it is through greater solidarity that we will emerge from the crisis.
in writing. a greek exit from the eurozone is not an option. the dangers of a total socioeconomic escalation are far too great. grexit could lead to unprecedented chaos in greece, with labile or extremist parties taking power. this could destabilise the eu's south-eastern flank and eventually lead to armed conflict. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. greece accounts for barely 3% of eu gdp. so the greek question is by definition manageable. the eu must take over the management of greek debt at a faster pace, as we propose with the debt redemption fund. this gives the people affected (greeks, but also spaniards, portuguese, etc.) a new perspective and a guarantee that the eu will not abandon them. there's no other way. the leaders of europe, the eu commissioners in particular, must stop trumpeting about a 'grexit'. they'll have to go another way. a path that does bring back hope and perspective.
madam president, as the commissioner has said, belgium is appalled by the murder of a young man who was kidnapped and beaten to death on 22 april because of his sexual orientation. this murder is a tragic example of what homophobia can lead to. this is a dramatic example that shows that while homophobia is still largely underestimated, it is very real. and the sad reality of homophobic violence is not unique to belgium. homosexuals are still subject to discrimination and harassment, and even physical attacks, in most member states. in belgium, the government has expressed its willingness to implement a national action plan against homophobia, in order to systematically combat violence against lgbt people. the union, which is proud to stand for individual freedom and respect for the principles of tolerance and non-discrimination, must, like belgium, fight for a european action plan to combat homophobia. a first step in this direction would be the adoption in the council of the horizontal anti-discrimination directive, as advocated in my report on social inclusion.
rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on employment and social affairs. - president. - europe and china are of course very important trading partners. china has an increasing impact on the global economy today, and so it also has a huge responsibility to uphold social standards. respect for human and social rights is also essential in the context of cooperation between europe and china. the committee on employment welcomes the efforts made in this area by the chinese central and also local governments. but we point out that sustainable financing of a stable social security and health system, which benefits all provinces of the country equally, is one of the greatest challenges facing the chinese government in the near future. the commission has also been asked to provide information on the implementation of the new rules on the protection of workers from harmful exposure to asbestos. it is not up to us to lecture china all the time, but we can help by sharing experience and working methods, setting up new systems. we also point out the responsibility of western companies operating in china. finally, we ask the commission to implement an integrated european policy in the south-east asian region and to launch the investment strategy with china as soon as possible.
mr president, i think that this report is very timely, firstly because, as has been said, commissioner de gucht is going to meet the chinese next week, but also because we in the european parliament have organised a high-level forum with the chinese with all the parties and so this report can be discussed. and i think that's a good report. i cannot say that i would sign every word of it, but i think it is a good report which has emphasized the essential points: so, as everyone has said, the question of reciprocity is extremely important, with fair competition and clear rules; there is also all that has been said about intellectual property rights, counterfeiting and piracy and here i sign with both hands , including the firm request to the chinese to introduce international rules into their legislation and to check their implementation; finally, there is another important element, namely to invite european companies which have succeeded in entering the chinese market to set an example in social and environmental standards - all this is important - and then to ask the commission to coordinate bilateral policies towards china better. what i like about this report - and i say this because we are going to meet the chinese - is that it is a respectful report. it is a report that is frank, that is direct, that is respectful, and i think that is a language that our chinese friends can understand, and even ask for. i would like to remind you that this is a great country in every sense of the word, with almost one and a half billion inhabitants, which has managed to lift a third of its population out of poverty in a very short time and which today must make efforts - and i insist, and i say this with my colleagues - but with great respect both in terms of social standards and human rights. china knows that. it has included some of these aspects in its five-year plan, and we will be here to discuss all these topics without any taboos.
mr president, madam minister, commissioner, there is a coincidence in the timing: we are discussing this tax today, at the very time when an informal summit on all the issues relating to the recovery of europe is to take place. i really believe that this is no coincidence and that the financial transaction tax is not only a fair contribution from a sector which has been largely responsible for the crisis we are experiencing today, but also a resource of its own for the european union, a resource, a recipe for financing this famous recovery that we all hope for. i think, therefore, that this own resource and the battle for own resources are fundamental. we must not separate the issue of the financial transaction tax from that of own resources. we need this resource, and perhaps others, to finance the recovery projects. we know how austerity, and austerity alone, leads to exasperation and sometimes even to votes and political situations that are truly dead ends. so i think today we have to move forward. the commission's proposal is a good one. it is not without its own objections, including the one just mentioned on the question of the place of the transaction. i hope, therefore, that we can take it forward, including in the council, which is a heavy burden for you.
mr president, as has already been stressed in this debate, the introduction of a financial transaction tax only makes sense if it has a european dimension, although it should in fact have a global dimension. it is therefore logical that the commission, with the support of this parliament, should want to make it a european tax. the next logical step would therefore be to make this tax part of the european revenue, thus giving the budget a more solid base than that of a normal budget. it has been repeatedly stressed here that the tax should contribute to growth. the introduction of the financial transaction tax as the union's own revenue - which, in order to be its own revenue, meets all the criteria set out in the lamassoure report and by the commission - would support the european budget. and the european budget is a growth budget. it would thus be an instrument for growth and at the same time would lighten the burden on national budgets, since it would lead to a reduction in national contributions to the european budget. so we have here a typical instrument which can support the dual objective of fiscal consolidation and growth and employment at the same time.
in writing. the podimata report on the common system for taxing financial transactions is a real success for the european socialists and democrats, for whom taxation is a key instrument in the response to the crisis. the resolution adopted finally gives a concrete and fair answer to finance, which until now has not been affected by any regulation, despite being directly responsible for it. the 0.1% tax rate for shares and bonds and the 0.01% for derivatives, which is favoured by the majority of european citizens, provide europe with an effective control instrument and a tool to combat tax evasion. the tax will also be applied, according to the principles of the place of issue and residence, to financial institutions and non-european securities. i am critical of the exemption for pension funds, because they are the true tigers of the financial markets and, as they carry out high-risk speculative operations, should be subject to the same controls.
mr president, can you stop for 30 seconds so we can discuss this? we have no common voting list at all, which is a small problem. just thirty seconds...
in writing. the purpose of this report is to conclude a protocol to the fisheries partnership agreement with mozambique. it comprises: a financial contribution the overall financial contribution of the protocol is set at eur 2,94 million for the entire period (3 years), and the annual financial contribution to be allocated from the union budget is therefore eur 980 000/year; fisheries: the fishing opportunities available to the european tuna fleet will be made available to 43 tuna seiners and 32 longliners, for a total of 75 vessels. this protocol will cover a period of 3 years from the adoption of the council decision on the signing and provisional application of the protocol and after the expiry of the protocol in force on 31 december 2011 (i.e. in principle from 1 january 2012 to 31 december 2014).
in writing. i welcome today's vote. the risky and sometimes unconscious behaviour of the financial sector has greatly damaged the global economy. the taxpayers, because of the bailout of the banks, had to bear considerable costs to save the financial sector. today, when the member states are in the process of cleaning up their public finances, it was not only high time, but also quite legitimate, that the financial sector should take its share of the blame! the introduction of a financial transaction tax (ftt) will ensure that the financial sector finally shares equally in the cost of the current crisis, and we have been waiting for this vote for a long time! i also hope that today's vote will help to increase the pressure on ecofin, which will take the final decision next month. i would like to take this opportunity to invite the finance ministers of the 27 member states to decide in favour of the establishment of a ftt. this would be a strong signal in favour of the introduction of such a tax at world level - and to say that james tobin's proposal dates back almost 40 years!
in writing. − the transaction tax is an important signal to the banking sector and the citizens of europe. the financial crisis was largely caused by opaque and excessive financial transactions. the banking sector must not only be regulated and controlled more strictly, but it must also contribute to the refinancing of public budgets, because the latter, and thus the taxpayers' money, have been used as never before to help the banks out of their precarious situation.
in writing. we voted in favour of the podimata report, which proposes the introduction of a financial transaction tax. this is the fourth time in this parliament that we, on behalf of the reform movement, have supported the introduction of such a tax instrument. a decision that has matured since 2008, which has a dual objective: to correct the abuses of financial markets, and to collect financial resources for common european objectives (general budget of the european union and more specific purpose, such as development aid). we do not underestimate the difficulties involved in implementing this tax at the european union level alone. it is also essential that the political decision to introduce it be preceded by precise rules on the tax bases, rates and scope. it is obvious that small consumers and pensioners should not be penalized by any additional tax. more generally, the introduction of a ftt is an opportunity for member states to reduce their national contribution to the union budget by up to 50% and to rethink an outdated financing system. it is not enough for a "vanguard" of member states to take responsibility and lead by example.
in writing. - today, the house has delivered a positive opinion on the proposal for a financial transaction tax, which i very much welcome. this is the kind of tax my party has long advocated. of course we must realize that we have only an advisory role and that it is the council which decides by unanimity. and unfortunately we are hearing that the discussions in the council are not going as smoothly as i would like. this is yet another example of the fact that the unanimity rule makes decision-making in the eu difficult and that it is therefore a difficult task to find fiscal instruments that will help the single market and the eu as a whole to move forward or improve the quality of transactions on financial markets. i hope, however, that the council will take its responsibility, both in this ftt case and in the ccctb - the proposal for a common consolidated corporate tax base for which i had the honour of being rapporteur. preferably 27, because only then can both tax instruments have their full effect. if necessary, through enhanced cooperation if there is really no other way.
in writing. i welcome the vote of the european parliament resolution calling on the commission to take action to counter 'unfair competition' by chinese companies, whether in china or in the eu. in this resolution, i and my colleagues stress the difficulties facing european companies in accessing the chinese market and the invasion of the eu by goods produced in conditions which do not comply with european rules. the eu must restore fair trade relations between our continent and the rest of the world if it is to remain competitive on the world stage. the same rules should apply to all. the resolution also calls on the commission to develop a european instrument, if possible as early as 2012, to ensure reciprocity in the area of the opening up of public procurement.
in writing. trade between the eu member states and china is an essential instrument of development for both the eu and china. however, there remains a clear trade imbalance in favour of china. it is therefore imperative to achieve a new partnership based on reciprocity of rules, trade balance and transparency of trade relations. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament and the council adopt a common position on the proposal for a directive on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to the labelling, presentation and advertising of foodstuffs for sale to the ultimate consumer. consumer protection and human rights, as well as respect for wto rules and intellectual property, are the conditions for fair competition with the union. we must not fight against chinese exports but fight for a balanced partnership with china which will allow a return to growth in europe and will contribute to the advancement of democratic freedoms in china.
on behalf of the s&d group. mr president, the european union has made a great deal of effort for sudan and south sudan. it has heavily funded south sudan's path to independence, supporting the 2005 comprehensive peace agreement. it was either that or it was war, we knew that very well. but at that time the problems were not yet solved. no problem was resolved: not citizenship, not currency, not the question of debt, and, above all, the question of the distribution of oil between the two countries, sudan and south sudan. and it explodes in our faces! but what is really striking us, and what worries me even more, is that over the last year, china has been the world's biggest oil producer, because internationally, it controls the entire oil supply chain, from the production to the refining, and it buys the oil. it seems that the european union, which has done so much, has suddenly disappeared from the scene. it's a little awkward, though. so i welcome resolution 2046, which is a huge step forward and we will be able to follow it, but i have three wishes. the first is that the european parliament, finally, is following up on election missions in a country like this, which we knew was going to be fragile, sending missions, following up and receiving south sudanese here. the second wish is that the european union, which is doing so much, has done so much and is still doing so much, thanks in particular to our special representative, should enjoy excellent visibility in the eyes of all. secondly, i would like the european union's position, which is a balanced position between north and south, to remain, despite the icc and bashir. finally, i would like to see priority given to the humanitarian emergency, particularly in relation to the blue nile and south kordofan. what is happening there is unimaginable, and we have not been able, until now, to gain access to these areas that experience daily tragedies.
in writing. we can no longer ignore it: in addition to an economic, a financial and a climate crisis, we are headed for a resource crisis. the raw materials that we used to have in abundance and cheaply and on which we have organized our consumer society are rapidly becoming scarcer and more expensive. i therefore welcome the report by mr gerbrandy, which sets out the problems very clearly and suggests ways of tackling this crisis. in particular, i fully support the proposals calling for a better functioning internal market to encourage reuse and recycling, for the elimination of environmentally harmful subsidies, for the use of price instruments to make the use of recycled materials more cost-effective in the production of new products, for the establishment of firm agreements and the establishment of measurable parameters for resource efficiency and for an ambitious revision of the ecodesign directive. this is the only way to make europe more efficient in its use of raw materials. that's good for the environment, but it's also vital for the survival of our economy. an economy that urgently needs a lasting adjustment.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, many of us are saying this again tonight, but it is necessary: the situation of young europeans is disastrous. the young are paying the unfair price of the crisis. young people are turning their backs more and more on the european project. we cannot accept that we are talking about a generation sacrificed, a generation that has been left unemployed or in precarious contracts. a europe that invests in its young people is a europe that revives growth, that gives hope to a generation - hope in the european model, in the values of solidarity. it is a europe that fights against social exclusion and poverty. so let us work together to make this 'youth guarantee' system viable so that it becomes a reality throughout europe. commissioner, several avenues for financing such a guarantee have been suggested, with the esf, through the social programme for change and innovation, with or through other initiatives which have been mentioned by my colleagues. in conclusion, let us take concrete action, and quickly, and mobilize the necessary funds.
madam president, first of all i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on her report. in 1966, in herstal, the town where i am mayor, a strike broke out that was to become historic: that of 3,000 women workers at the national arms factory - a strike that lasted eleven weeks. the workers were simply demanding the application of the treaty of rome, which provided for equal pay for men and women for equal work. this is the famous principle of equal pay for equal work. the formula was then used by civil society for the first time. forty-six years later, the debate is still alive. at present, women continue to earn 16 to 17% less than men in the european union, a figure that was recently confirmed by the oecd. this situation is all the more unacceptable since there is a legal framework which expressly incorporates the principle of equal pay, given the increased risks of poverty which this situation creates. strengthening pay transparency, expanding career prospects for women, promoting their participation in decision-making at all levels in the economic sector, and also encouraging companies that take measures to promote equal pay are some of the ways in which the pay gap, which is also found at the time of retirement, could be tackled. these are the themes which i have emphasized in my amendments and which i am pleased to see in the report. i hope that with this report and the initiatives that the commission will take to strengthen the legislative framework - particularly as regards the importance of collective bargaining and the system of sanctions - it will no longer be necessary to resort to social confrontations, as in 1966 in herstal, to advance this cause in europe.

in writing. − i am very pleased that today, with a large majority, we are calling on all policy-makers who are going to the european football championship in ukraine to denounce the political situation on the spot. we ask them to publicly express their concern about the political situation in ukraine and to take the opportunity to visit political prisoners where possible. the trial of yulia tymoshenko has been postponed again until the end of june, after the conclusion of the european football championship in ukraine and poland. the ukrainian government wants to keep the trial out of the international press spotlight. but it's accomplishing the exact opposite of what it's intended to. i realize you're pretty good at keeping sports and politics separate, but in this case, we can't just look the other way. on june 13th, the dutch play the germans in kharkiv. the stadium in kharkiv is two kilometers from the prison where yulia tymoshenko is being held. then you can't just pretend to have a bloody nose as a politician and even as a player.
in writing. i welcome the vote on the resolution on the political situation in ukraine. ukraine is of paramount importance to the eu. it is essential that the european parliament encourages the eu's immediate neighbours to integrate the eu acquis in the field of human rights, freedom of the press and democracy. it is therefore the duty of parliament to express concern and condemn the abuses that are taking place in ukraine at the moment, particularly with regard to the situation of yulia tymoshenko. i therefore call for a necessary reform of the legal and judicial systems of ukraine, so that they can operate independently of any political influence. it is essential that anyone detained for political reasons be released immediately. in addition, they must receive adequate medical assistance to ensure their health. with regard to the forthcoming european football championship, i hereby invite all officials wishing to attend a match in ukraine to do so in private and to denounce, as far as possible, the political situation in the country.
in writing. i warmly welcome the resolution adopted by the european parliament, which strongly condemns all discrimination based on sexual orientation or gender identity and strongly deplores the fact that the fundamental rights of lgbt people are not always fully recognised in the european union. i therefore call on the member states and the commission to condemn strongly homophobic hate speech or incitement to hatred and violence. i call on the commission, through this resolution, to ensure that the annual report on the application of the charter of fundamental rights includes a strategy to strengthen the protection of fundamental rights in the union, including comprehensive and comprehensive information on the impact of homophobia in the member states and proposals for solutions and measures to eliminate it.
in writing. the right to be different is a fundamental expression of human rights. the sexual difference between consenting adults, the love between two men or two women or between a man and a woman, is definitely a part of the reserved intimacy to which every human being has a sacred right. european countries, whether or not they are members of the european union, must ensure that lesbian, gay, bisexual and transgender people are protected from hate speech and homophobic violence, and guarantee same-sex partners the same respect as the rest of society. i condemn any discrimination based on sexual orientation or gender identity and i strongly deplore that the fundamental rights of lgbt people are not always fully recognised in the european union. the member states of the european union should be exemplary in the application and protection of fundamental rights in europe. i am also very concerned about certain laws or decisions that are used to arrest or fine heterosexual citizens, who express support, tolerance or acceptance of lgbt people, and that these laws legitimize homophobia and sometimes violence.
in writing. on 22 april, ihsane jarfi, a 32-year-old man, was beaten to death in a car by four men as he left a gay bar in the city of liège. a tragic sign of a society where violence and intolerance are commonplace, ishane jarfi is the victim of the first homophobic murder in belgium. that is the significance of our vote this afternoon by a very large majority. it is clear that homophobia, an intolerable discrimination which is on the increase, must be condemned. it should be remembered that homosexuality is above all a right to live like "everyone else", that it is not a simple orientation of sexual life choice, that it is a sexual identity in its own right. some religious leaders need to be reminded that the priority is to protect the right of each person to be who they are, not to return homosexuality to the underground. we need to rethink our education systems, including education in respect, tolerance and knowledge of others. it is crucial to introduce lessons on "common values, rights and duties" in school curricula, and this from an early age, if the fight against homophobia is to be successful in the future.
in writing. - it is remarkable that this resolution does not mention the problem of violence against homosexuals which is emerging in many european countries these days. that violence is mostly the work of muslim youths, and so it's obviously not politically correct to denounce and accuse that? that is one reason for abstaining on this resolution, but there are others. we are going to go very far in this respect in abandoning the principle of subsidiarity. i think it is best for peoples and member states to decide for themselves whether or not they want to introduce same-sex marriage and whether or not they want to recognise such marriages in other member states. it is not a question of discrimination. so for the love of god, stop with this european meddling and this pollution of concepts.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of this report. the initiative 'a resource-efficient europe' is one of the seven flagship initiatives of the europe 2020 strategy, which aims to foster smart, sustainable and inclusive growth. in addition, this flagship initiative aims to establish a policy framework to support the transition to a resource-efficient and low-carbon economy, which will help: boost economic performance while using fewer resources; seek and create new opportunities for economic growth; to step up innovation and enhance the union's competitiveness; to ensure security of supply of essential resources; to combat climate change and to limit the impact of resource use on the environment.
in writing. − the idea that we not only need to be resource-conscious, but also that we need to promote renewable energy in particular, seems to be supported by almost all political parties. however, the implementation in the various countries is far from this 'wish'. the different majorities in european governments are all facing the same challenge: to promote growth, to protect the environment and not to weaken their own country by the measures they take; environmental dumping is becoming the new challenge, which is even more formidable at the global level than at the eu level. this is reason enough to stop dealing with these issues with declarations of intent in europe and to take clear decisions.
in writing. the economic crisis currently affecting the european union shows that a change in our growth model is necessary. our industries are increasingly confronted with rising commodity prices. a transition to a resource efficient economy is becoming inevitable and necessary. to improve the use of their resources, member states must remove any obstacles to the proper functioning of the european waste recycling and reuse market. promoting research and technological innovation in areas such as waste collection and sorting could indeed be a way of maximising our resources. gradually phasing out certain inefficient subsidies and replacing them with smart environmental taxation will help boost the eu's competitiveness. resource efficiency action should be applied mainly in three key areas: food, housing and mobility. however, the parliament's ambition is to integrate the concept of resource efficiency into all other eu policies.
in writing. by adopting the gerbrandy report on better use of natural resources this afternoon, the european parliament has sent a clear message: the scarcity of resources is a reality, not a fatality! the causes of this are as follows: the intensive use of resources in agricultural policy, fisheries policy and industrial policy, as well as speculation in commodity markets and the rapid growth in world consumption. a figure sums up the situation well: the real price of basic commodities has increased by 147% since the beginning of the century. the fight against waste must therefore be one of the union's objectives in the years ahead, particularly in the key areas of food, housing and mobility. as rapporteur in 2005 on legislation on the eco-design of energy-using products, i know how much action upstream is the best way to protect our resources. another key to success is innovation. how can we justify, for example, that the standstill of technological products contributes to 1% of global energy expenditure? to encourage and create the conditions for industry to participate more and better in the conservation of resources.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of this resolution which calls on member states to introduce national plans for young people and urges them to take practical and concrete measures at national level to ensure that young people have decent employment or are in education or training again within four months of leaving school. i believe that there is an urgent need for economic and labour market strategies and policies to create more and better jobs for young people, thus avoiding the trap of precariousness for young people in a succession of temporary jobs and unpaid traineeships which lead to no increase in wages, no improvement in working conditions, and no more stable forms of employment.
in writing. in the european union, nearly 5.5 million young people are unemployed. more than one in five people under 25 are unable to find a job. the unemployment rate among young people is 21%, and the situation is not improving. concrete action is therefore needed to tackle the problem of youth unemployment. the member states must act on the drop-out rate of young people and promote the development of their skills in the light of the needs of our enterprises. it is indeed becoming urgent to make the labour market more accessible to young people so that they can access their first job quickly. investing in a young people guarantee is one way to do this. but first, we must use the funds already committed wisely and put them to good use. of the total of €347 billion for the period 2007-2013, €82 billion from the european structural funds had not yet been allocated in january this year. it is imperative that these funds be used to train young people so that the european union can be a beacon of hope and a future for them.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of this report! the latest studies show that, across the european union, women receive on average 16.4% less than men and that the pay gap between women and men varies from 4.4 to 27.6% depending on the member state. and it is clear that despite the huge arsenal of legislation in place for almost 40 years and the actions and resources being used to try to reduce the gap, progress remains extremely slow (the disparity at union level was 17.7% in 2006, 17.6% in 2007, 17.4% in 2008, 16.9% in 2009 and 16.4% in 2010). in the bauer report voted on today, parliament calls on the member states to implement and apply directive 2006/54/ec in a consistent manner and to encourage the private and public sectors to play a more active role in closing the gender pay gap and to allow partners to negotiate gender equality plans at company, national and european level.
in writing. equal pay for men and women for equal work is still to be achieved. this is a key objective of gender equality in the general sense, but it can only be achieved if all member states make every effort to achieve it. it is essential that the commission can strengthen existing legislation with appropriate sanctions against those who fail to do so. women in the union currently earn 16.4% less than men. this gap is unacceptable and cannot continue to widen.
in writing. - first of all, the principle: we are naturally in favour of equal pay for equal work for men and women. and that women, given their actual role in child rearing, deserve extra social protection is equally self-evident to me. that's it. i would like to stress that the principle of subsidiarity must also be respected in the field of labour law and social security, and we must not shift more and more powers to the european union. this report is again full of proposals which do not add any substance but cost a handful of taxpayers' money. the european parliament has been asked to give its opinion on the proposal for a regulation on the european parliament's role in the european union. so i abstained from the vote.
