answer is "blue cardboard". i do not want to "unveil the crown", as we say, but i can tell you, mr president, that the human rights issue has not been ignored in the groups. there was, of course, realpolitik, with the energy context, our crisis, what azerbaijan could represent in terms of pipelines that would allow us to bypass russia, etc. you know the problem as well as i do. then there is the argument that if we only made agreements with countries that were genuinely democratic, we might be a little bit out of business. but i can tell you that the issue of human rights has not only been raised, but it remains a real obsession for us. and i believe that one day we will have to combine - and that is really our ambition here in the european parliament - the question of trade with that of democracy. and it's a balancing act every time.
president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, good evening, a free trade agreement - we all agree - can be a boon to the economy and also to the well-being of the people on both sides of the agreement. but there can also be serious shadow aspects, and there can be sectors, also in europe, commissioner, which suffer heavily from such agreements. that was the case and is the case today with the free trade agreement we concluded with south korea. the agreement is having a serious impact on the european automobile industry. the fact that european car manufacturers cannot sell their cars in south korea is not due to a lack of innovation or a lack of competitiveness. on the contrary, it has everything to do with the persistence of non-tariff barriers to trade in south korea, with a manifest lack of a level playing field. the deterrent through the so-called safe guard clauses does not seem to be working in this case either. we cannot afford a repetition of this, a repetition of a similar scenario in the case of japan. mr lange has made it very clear - and i agree with him 100% - that the japanese, like the south koreans, are extremely adept at creating, maintaining and perpetuating such non-tariff rules. in particular, technical standardization, where they are on a very different wavelength and much less voluntarist than we in the european union, is crucial. there are also other non-tariff barriers, for example of a fiscal nature, of an environmental nature, and so on. we must make that very clear in the context of the scope before we start negotiations. i hope you're willing to admit that. i think we can say that the european car industry must not again be the victim of a global deal - in this case with tokyo - a deal which is of course a give and take. we are faced today in europe with a real overcapacity in the automobile industry, with possible additional restructuring and additional problems which will arise as a result of this unbalanced deal with japan and which we can of course miss today. in short, commissioner, a future fta with japan can only be concluded if we have tough safeguards, tough safeguard clauses, and if there is a level playing field in the industrial sectors, particularly in the automotive sector. this is also stated in the cars 21 report, which the commission agreed with the car manufacturers very recently last week. i suggest we implement this agreement meticulously in that respect.

the agenda calls for debate on the commission's statement on public consultations and their availability in all the union's languages.
it is only natural and obvious, mr president, that the documents for public consultation should be available in all the official languages of the european union. it should not be necessary to have the commission make a statement on this and to have a debate on it here in parliament, but commissioner de gucht now says that the commission does not have sufficient resources to translate all the relevant documents into all the official languages. i am afraid that this is a euphemism, because the necessary documents are usually only available in one language. this is not an acceptable situation, all the more so because the commission should use the instrument of public consultation to fine-tune the regulatory process and at the same time to narrow the gap between citizens and the official europe. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european parliament is able to express its views on the subject. the rule should therefore be that all public documents of the european union should be available in all the official languages, and budgetary arguments are not acceptable in my view. if we look at the budget of the european union, we see that there are more than enough items to save and cut. and the cost of relevant key documents, such as those for public consultations, represents only a fraction of what could be saved on other budget lines.

on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, next thursday – in two days’ time – we will celebrate schengen’s 27th birthday. so let me thank the minister very much for the gift. it is a shame that we are having this debate today. in fact it is a disgrace. certainly it is a disgrace, mr minister when, a few minutes before starting this debate, you publish a press statement in danish – and we have some danish members who will be able to read that – saying that this agreement is good for europe and that it has been the outcome of a very deep dialogue with the eu commission and with the eu parliament. that is what it says in the statement released by your services this morning, just a few minutes ago. well, mr minister, we are no longer living in the middle ages. in the middle ages there was a dialogue between the king and the parliament, but today we live in the 21st century. it is not based on dialogue but on codecision in a modern society and in a modern parliament. so we do not need your dialogue. what we need is for you to recognise the powers of the european parliament. in fact, colleagues, this dispute is not about the power of the european parliament. it is, in fact, a question of an attempt by the council to renationalise schengen by doing two things: first of all changing the legal basis so that this parliament lies outside the decision-making process and secondly – and more importantly – by clearly stating that implementation of the new acquis will be delegated to the council and not to the commission. so this is not an attack against parliament but, more importantly, but an attack against the commission, against the communitarian method, and against the fact that schengen is a full part of european union policies. what is an even bigger scandal is that the united kingdom, which is not in schengen and has an opt-out, will be treated better than parliament. under article 70, the uk will continue to be present at the negotiations –without having a say, apparently, but it will be present – but this parliament will not. so we have to take clear action today. firstly, i think we need to go to court. secondly, why is it necessary to pay for all this? if they want to renationalise, let them renationalise, for example, and then pay for the schengen acquis themselves. instead it is the european union which has to do it. thirdly, minister, why are we continuing with you on justice and home affairs? why continue with the dublin ii report on asylum? we have a trialogue on 19 june. why is it necessary to go on with it? why continue with the díaz de mera report on the visa safeguard clause? we have two trialogues in the next two weeks. why are we continuing with this? why are we continuing with the weber report on the reintroduction of border controls if we have no say on the coelho report? why should we continue in any way with you, the danish presidency? why are we not suspending all talks on justice and home affairs until the new presidency is in place? (applause)
in writing. the council's decision to exclude the european parliament from the reform of the schengen agreement opens the way to a violation of the fundamental rights of european citizens. the fact that parliament is not consulted on the schengen evaluation mechanisms means that it is not given democratic control over decisions which may even lead to the temporary reintroduction of passport controls. and this is unacceptable. the council should review its decision because it infringes the principles and fundamental freedoms of the european union, in particular the free movement of persons. i therefore fully support the proposals which have been put forward to give parliament a strong response to this. the commission has already taken the necessary steps to ensure that the council's position is not compromised. in the difficult times the eu is now going through, what is needed is more solidarity and more trust, a european approach and european solutions. the european union must not be allowed to undermine the european institutions, to put national interests first and to give priority to the wishes of the large member states.
president-in-office of the council. − mr president, i am grateful for this opportunity to participate in this debate on the two proposals so fluently expressed by the rapporteurs, which are intended to strengthen economic governance of the euro area. the purpose of the discussion this morning and, of course, of the vote tomorrow, is to establish parliament’s position in view of upcoming trialogues. i would like to thank in particular ms ferreira and mr gauzès for their dedicated work; we have talked a number of times about their work on these issues and their contribution to establishing parliament’s positions on the text put forward by the commission. the council is keenly awaiting the outcome of your deliberations and looks forward to a constructive trialogue. you will be aware that the danish presidency steered discussions in the council to a rapid conclusion, so that already by 21 february we were able to adopt a general approach in council on both these proposals. the general approach of the council is to close the original proposals of the commission which we believe to be an important contribution to ensuring sound and sustainable public finance in the euro area and thereby also to growth and employment, with those two things being two sides of the same coin. the proposed regulation on the strengthening of economic and budgetary surveillance of member states experiencing or threatened with serious difficulties with respect to their financial stability will ensure that surveillance of those member states under a financial assistance programme or, of course, facing threats of financial instability, is robust, follows clear procedures and is embedded in eu law. the proposed regulation on common provisions for monitoring and assessing draft budgetary plans and ensuring the correction of excessive deficits of the member states in the euro area will require these countries to present their draft budgetary plans, which will enable the commission to assess whether the budgetary plans are in accordance with budgetary objectives. the regulation also proposes closer monitoring and reporting requirements for euro area countries in an excessive deficit procedure. i should add that i remain confident that we will reach an agreement which is acceptable to the council and parliament in the end. the two proposals will make an important contribution to further strengthening of the economic governance framework within the euro area and we are ready to start a trialogue as soon as parliament is ready. however, i would like to recall, and i should of course do that very briefly, the context in which we are discussing these two proposals. we are all only too well aware of the persistent financial and economic crisis which continues to impact across europe, and also beyond european borders, despite the reins applied and measures taken after 2010. but having said that, i still think it is important to realise that much has been achieved. we have strengthened economic governance and policy coordination within the framework of the european semester. the 'six-pack' – and i consider this a major achievement by the council and parliament, with very close cooperation on very difficult files – has strengthened the rules of the stability and growth pact and created a new macroeconomic imbalances procedure. we have set up rescue mechanisms such as the efsm, the efs and the esm, and used these facilities to support vulnerable member states. we have strengthened financial supervision through a european systemic risk board and strengthened the role of the european supervisory authorities. as an absolutely crucial part of the eu crisis management strategy, the council and parliament are taking major steps on stronger financial regulations. we have agreed, or are in the process of agreeing, on a number of financial regulation files, including new capital requirements of banks, rules on markets for financial derivatives, rules on short selling and credit default swaps, hedge funds and credit rating agencies. further proposals which will contribute to better regulation of the financial markets are in the pipeline, such as the proposal for an eu framework for bank recovery and resolutions. finally, and hand in hand with our efforts on stronger economic governance and financial regulation, we are working on promoting stronger growth and job creation, with this being done through reforms, initiatives at both national level and european level. however, it is clear that further work is required. the current economic problems have revealed the need for an ever closer policy coordination and decisive action from the council and parliament. mr president, it is against this background that honourable members will be discussing today the 'two-pack' proposals which, although only a part of the overall picture, will nevertheless play a key role in the overall strengthening of the coordination of economic policies. much is at stake, and i therefore have strong hopes that, following your debate today and your vote tomorrow, we can make rapid progress towards reaching an overall agreement. i can assure you of the danish presidency’s commitment to this dossier. i also know that, as we are almost at the end of our presidency term, the incoming cyprus presidency is equally determined to adopt these proposals as quickly as possible. i look forward very much to your discussion and thank you for your attention.
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, let me first of all thank the rapporteurs, mr gauzès and ms ferreira, for their very important work and the presidency and minister margrethe vestager for their excellent and very effective cooperation on these important files. i also want to thank the committee on economic and monetary affairs for a very substantive and, may i say, constructive economic dialogue yesterday evening in which we discussed the european semester as well as our strategy for sustainable growth and job creation, and also discussed issues like the recent decision of the euro area to provide a credit line for the restructuring of the spanish banking sector. we will continue this discussion tomorrow, both in the morning and afternoon, so today we will focus on the two-pack, which is a very important discussion and decision indeed. let me say a few words about the context of this debate. at the summit later this month – as president barroso will explain in more detail tomorrow – the heads of state or government will discuss the next phase of deepening the economic union. they will discuss possibilities such as a growth compact, a financial union and a fiscal union. so we are, yet again, witnessing a potentially defining moment for europe’s deeper integration. and once again, this commission and this parliament have a special and joint responsibility – a unique responsibility – to prove that the community method can be and will be at the centre of the next chapter in the making of europe. i have little doubt that the treaty on stability, coordination and governance will be ratified by at least 12 euro area member states and will thus enter into force. the legislation which is the subject of our debate today has a profound significance in relation to that treaty. that is why it is so important. the first draft regulation, which is the subject of mr gauzès’s report, aims at providing more robust and coherent ways of dealing with vulnerable member states and member states under a programme, including enhanced economic and budgetary surveillance. i fully agree with mr gauzès that prevention is always much better than correction and this regulation was needed in the past two and a half years, even before that. the second draft regulation, which is dealt with in ms ferriera’s report, aims at establishing a common timeline and common rules to allow for real active prior or ex-ante monitoring and assessment of the national budgets of euro area member states. this regulation was needed during the past 12 years because, once again, prevention is always better than correction, as we have learned the hard way. these proposals, submitted to you on 23 november by the commission, decisively informed and shaped the subsequent discussions that led to the new fiscal compact treaty. moreover, the authors of the new treaty explicitly required that certain provisions of the treaty should be achieved through the eu’s normal secondary legislation. they welcomed these specific legislative proposals and clearly saw them as an essential supplement to the treaty itself. and, of course, the authors of the treaty set out the goal that all provisions of the treaty should be incorporated into the legal framework of the eu by 2018 at the latest. so the adoption of this legislation is a vital first step in ensuring that the articulation between the fiscal compact treaty and the eu treaties is done correctly and will set a positive precedent for the future. this means that, as co-legislator, you have a choice: either to move forward in a timely way or to delay and create a legal grey area. if the treaty enters into force before this two-pack, we will be confronted with very difficult legal challenges. what concerns me most is the political signal that such a scenario would send: no legal acrobatics would enable us to hide the fact that we had failed to live up to our political responsibilities, because member states would be subject to intergovernmental treaty obligations which had not been translated into eu secondary legislation as planned. frankly, this would be very bad for the credibility of both the eu treaty and the community method. we have to acknowledge that, in this respect, the council has moved quickly. it agreed its mandate to negotiate on these texts within weeks of the signature of the treaty in february. as a former member of this house, i understand and deeply respect the principles of democratic scrutiny, which means that this house cannot always be as agile as the council. but i think – and i say this with respect – that after almost seven months for discussion, this should now be the moment to move forward from discussion to negotiation and then to decision. the council’s negotiating mandate will surely provide us with challenges in the negotiations. i have studied mr gauzès’s and ms ferreira’s reports. it is already clear that the mandate that you will give to the economic affairs committee will also provide us with some challenges. however, my team and i are ready to roll up our sleeves, get down to serious work with this house and the council, and get this legislation enacted so that we can move forward. it is in our joint interest not to be institutionally overtaken by yet another intergovernmental agreement. (applause)
the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, as rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on employment, i should like to point out first of all that, despite my opposition to the philosophy of economic governance and austerity aimed at drastically reducing the deficits and debts of the member states, my group and i have not followed the procedure of directly rejecting these texts. we took the opportunity and thought that the parliamentary input would improve the text. we have been able, for example, in this committee which is sensitive to solidarity, to introduce the clause on respect for collective agreements, to reject the macroeconomic conditionality for access to the structural funds, to give a greater role to parliament, to give greater importance to the role of national budgets in achieving the objectives of the 2020 strategy for growth and employment. these points were to some extent taken up by the committee on economic and monetary affairs, but in general, and particularly for those matters which were not within the committee's competence, and despite all the efforts of our rapporteur, elisa ferreira, whom i commend, i must tell you that i am not satisfied. this package strengthens economic surveillance, which my group had already opposed when it voted for the six-pack. this procedure is rigid and applies gross and uniform revenue to the member states, irrespective of the structure of their debt and its origin. after the meetings this weekend to release aid to spain, we cannot say that we need the same recipes for everyone. the ferreira report makes good progress towards new approaches to governance, but even with the amendment proposed by our rapporteur, i am still concerned that the expenditure which is useful for growth and employment will be sacrificed in an attempt to satisfy the markets. in the current context in the council - where growth through recovery and investment is finally being discussed again, alongside budgetary austerity - it will not help to calm down the markets and ensure growth to make the processes out of step with a new political situation a legal matter.
it is true that, despite the dozens of measures taken in recent years, emu is still lagging behind. monetary integration is complete, but the economic infrastructure, including the banking system and fiscal union, is still too fragmented. this week, however, we can take another step in the right direction with the adoption of the two-pack, the proposals of which have just been explained by the rapporteurs gauzès and ferreira. we have worked very hard with the ppe, mr president, to ensure that two reports are actually on the table here. i hope that this time all the groups will take their responsibility. in the ferrera report we have ensured that the essence of the commission proposal is preserved, that the link between financial stability and growth and jobs is made - a one-to-one story - that national parliaments are better respected and that the independence of the budgetary institutions of the member states is guaranteed. we have also incorporated a number of key issues from the budgetary treaty into this piece of community legislation, in particular those on the binding numerical budgetary rules and an automatic correction mechanism in national law and also the concept of economic partnership programmes for countries in an excessive deficit procedure. what we have not accepted and will not accept is an attempt to weaken the growth and stability pact. we cannot support this because we do not believe that we can solve the debt problem by creating more debt, and we do not want to be complicit in choices that burden the next generation and undermine the urgently needed restoration of confidence in the short term. we need that confidence, for example, for further budgetary integration, which we in the ppe are also open to. open to a thorough debate on forms of joint debt issuance such as eurobonds or a redemption fund. but we are dealing with such a large amount of money and such a deep reintegration that we have to make sure that the right conditions and the right timing are in place. we cannot therefore simply, as it were, smuggle a whole new chapter on this subject into the articles of this regulation. we do support the review clause, which provides the necessary flexibility and which we have amended in the committee on economic and monetary affairs precisely for this purpose. finally, as regards economic growth, we too want to raise the necessary funds. but to create in this legislative text, in a hurry, a fund the size of the entire current annual budget of the european union, without even saying where the funds come from, is unbelievable. the ppe group will remain consistent and will vote tomorrow in the same way as it did earlier in the committee on economic affairs.
on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, the aim of the ‘two-pack’ is to transpose the fiscal treaty into secondary law, but instead of reinforcing the logic of the treaty the ‘two-pack’ reports water it down. first there is the gauzès report. after the negotiating process, i am sorry to find only a watered-down version of the ‘golden rule’ that puts the essence of the fiscal treaty on a slippery slope. i am happy that another suggestion by mr gauzès was retained. he proposes that a member state at risk of default or suspension of payments should be placed under legal protection. unfortunately he did not go one step further by adding that if a member state is unwilling to surrender its sovereignty in this way it should be given the option of leaving the eurozone. i was criticised as being irresponsible for tabling an amendment in this sense, but i believe it is the other way round: it is irresponsible not to consider the possibility of a country leaving the eurozone. mr president, you can choose to ignore reality but reality will not ignore you. following the greek elections this weekend the facts will come to haunt us all. the report by ms ferreira goes far beyond the aim of the ‘two-pack’. she confuses eu legislation with the manifesto of her socialist party. the rapporteur ties budgetary surveillance to a range of other issues such as eurobonds, a european redemption fund, a european debt authority, a financial transaction tax and a common consolidated corporate tax base. this is a christmas tree out of season. during negotiations mr gauzès ceded too much to the left, more than many members of the ppe regarded desirable. in voting, the left mainly abstained regardless. ms ferreira’s report only achieved a small majority. in fact more members voted against or abstained than voted in favour. parliament has produced a wish-list about which it is itself divided. it will not stand in council and rightly so. the ecr will abstain on gauzès and vote against ferreira.
"blue card" question. what is it? president. - i have a question for mr madlener. he also spoke about keeping to agreements, including the 3% agreement. now his own party in the dutch coalition had the chance to stick to these agreements, but his party leader decided to walk away and leave prime minister rutte with the trouble and the problems. now i wonder: does he not set a bad example by accusing others of running away and not keeping their appointments? shouldn't his party leader be the first to do so?
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, these two texts are necessary. they provide a legal basis for work which the commission is already carrying out when it is part of the troika in the countries which are, as we say, 'under programme' or when it interacts with the member states in the context of their budgetary procedure. i think it is better to operate within a legal framework than outside it, and in that sense these are two necessary texts. secondly, i must stress that the rapporteurs have really worked in a spirit of unity and i hope that this will be demonstrated by a massive vote tomorrow. i should now like to reply, perhaps in particular, to marianne thyssen. yes, it is true that we need sustainable public finances. we cannot build a future on deficits and debt. it's very clear. this must be unambiguous. that's what we said in the six-pack, that's what we say in the two-pack, but it can't be the alpha and omega of politics. in other words, we also need, in addition to, not in place of, of course, a form of debt mutualisation that allows us to have interest rates that make debt sustainable. we also need, and this is even more important, fiscal convergence to ensure that we not only have sustainable expenditure but that we have revenue that is capable of financing that expenditure. finally, we must invest, invest and invest again to enable our economy to be redeployed. that is what we wanted to introduce in the two-pack. of course, paris was not built in a day, but nevertheless, we cannot continue to say that discipline is for now and everything else will follow later. we must now put in place the rules of budgetary discipline, and we have been doing so for a year now. we must start today to put in place a debt pooling. we must start today to implement fiscal convergence. yesterday was the day we should have started investing in our future. so we cannot say yes to discipline all the time and everything else will follow. we are not trying to sneak this in! these measures have been needed for two years and we have not yet started to implement them. so i'd like to get started. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament. i would like to remind you that today the net contributor countries - and in this case my country is one of them - "borrow" money, not "give" it. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. their interest rates are negative. the advantage for germany today is estimated by the bremer landesbank to be between 45 and 65 billion euros. these are immediate benefits, whereas the cost to germany would only become a cost if some of the loans we make were not repaid. so stop saying that we are bleeding ourselves to death for all these people from southern europe. at the moment, it is an operation which is profitable for the net contributor countries.
in writing. despite the significant progress achieved by the socialists as a result of the parliamentary work in the employment and economic affairs committees (a greater emphasis on the achievement of the objectives of the eu 2020 strategy, a roadmap for the implementation of eurobonds, or the necessary respect for social dialogue and collective agreements), this text remains contrary to our wish for a europe of solidarity. we want to send a strong signal and we will vote no if the right wing does not humanize its approach.
in writing. the affirmative vote was a foregone conclusion. the commission's proposal for new means upstream is fully in line with the joint declaration on payment appropriations adopted in november 2011. it was mentioned that the council and the european parliament would decide on any draft amending budget as soon as possible in order to avoid any shortfall in payment appropriations.
in writing. the essence of europe must be solidarity. it was appropriate to accept the request from italy for assistance from the fund following the floods that affected the regions of liguria and tuscany in october 2011. the disaster caused severe damage to homes, businesses and farms, and disrupted major transport networks and basic public infrastructure. the total direct damage caused by the floods is estimated at more than 700 million euros. the floods have been classified as a natural disaster and therefore fall within the scope of the solidarity fund.
in writing. in recent years, latin america has enjoyed one of the highest growth rates. however, it is necessary to maintain development aid funds in this region. one in three people still live below the poverty line. some latin american countries are among the most unequal in economic and social terms, with the highest levels of violence and high crime rates. to combat poverty, cooperation must be refocused on the areas where it is most useful. and in view of the economic and social heterogeneity of the countries in the region, differentiated cooperation must be maintained. development aid can only be reduced gradually so that these countries strengthen their rule of law and further reduce the risks of extreme poverty. latin america shares certain values with the european union. the two regions have common interests and so it is together that we must meet the current challenges facing our societies, which are more interdependent than ever.
in writing. the commission has also been active in the development of the new partnership with latin america, with a view to promoting the development of the region. aid to middle-income countries is being gradually reduced and the criteria for aid are becoming more objective. the degree of inequality will also be taken into account. the gini coefficient will also be taken into account. the gdp is indeed an inadequate measure. the ep rightly calls for greater social cohesion, redistribution of resources through sound fiscal policies and the autonomy of parliaments, regional authorities and civil society to design development strategies. it is also good that the civil society is being asked to play a strong role in the fight against inequality. there is also the importance of monitoring the use of financial resources. the commission has already made a number of proposals. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of biofuels, and to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of biofuels. finally, our suggestions to address impunity and to support the work of the inter-american system of human rights politically and financially, so that violence against women can be addressed, were adopted.
in writing. my support for this text is obvious. indeed, it seems to me that we have a real role to play in respecting international commitments on human rights, food security and environmental sustainability. the european union is working to develop relations and build partnerships with third countries, supporting the sustainable economic, social and environmental development of developing countries with the key aim of eradicating poverty. the european union's policies play a fundamental role in the achievement of the millennium development goals.
in writing. − following on from the european year of volunteering (2011), this own-initiative report is another step towards promoting volunteering at european level. the activities of voluntary organisations provide essential support for the social, intergenerational and environmental needs of today's society. it is therefore an active support for society, which it is essential to promote. voluntary organisations, especially those wishing to operate across borders, face major challenges today, including administrative ones. i therefore welcome the fact that this owninitiative report calls for simplification of administrative procedures and tax relief for organisations involved in voluntary activities. the creation of a centralised eu portal for volunteering with a dedicated section on cross-border activities and a european statute for these associations will also facilitate and promote cross-border work by voluntary organisations.
in writing. encouraging and recognising volunteering as essential for the training of our young people is the message of the scurria report, which was voted on this afternoon by the european parliament. unlike paid traineeships and work experience, voluntary work often remains virtually unknown given the heavy legal constraints faced by professionals in the sector. volunteering is a unique opportunity for our young people, who are studying or starting their professional careers, and it is not well supervised, often stigmatised or neglected. parliament calls in particular for the development of skills linked to volunteering, for its better integration into the study programme via the european credit system 'ects' and for its better public funding. to recognize volunteering as a real passport to future employment.
in writing. we live in a time of crisis and even though we should be more united than ever, individualism prevails. we have just spent a whole year promoting volunteering because there is no greater act than that. it remains essential to continue to draw a clear and unambiguous distinction between the value of voluntary work and for-profit activities. it is also necessary to emphasise the role of volunteering in promoting, in the union and elsewhere, the principles of european identity and the common values of solidarity and social cohesion. i therefore wholeheartedly support this resolution.
in writing. today, the dependence on information infrastructures has increased exponentially. the internet and digital systems are boosting our productivity, encouraging innovation, stimulating growth and quality jobs. but alongside these innovative technologies, cyberattacks are becoming more frequent and increasing the vulnerability of our computer networks. these attacks are particularly serious when they affect institutional infrastructures or those of our companies. the fight against this scourge, which crosses our borders, can only be achieved through a strengthening and better coordination of national measures. developing an effective european cybersecurity strategy is essential to ensure secure information sharing within the european union. however, the eu is not alone in being subject to these attacks, and it is therefore important to develop a globally coordinated strategy to combat future threats.
in writing. i voted in favour of my colleague, mr kalfin's text, because it is a step in the right direction. the internet has become one of the cornerstones of the world. but like any stone, some facets could become harmful and a source of danger for businesses as well as individuals. of course, cyber-attacks should be prevented. no one wants to have intruders visiting their home or business. the european texts must also be updated as quickly as technology, and this can only be achieved with a high degree of cooperation between the various member states.
in writing. the european union already imports more than 60% of its gas and more than 80% of its oil. and as the edith herczog report points out, this dependence of the union on energy imports is likely to increase over the next ten years. faced with this reality, europe needs to develop a strategy of cooperation across eu borders to secure its energy supply. if the union as a whole, which is a major energy buyer, defends a common position on this matter, it will be able to assert its interests in its negotiations with the neighbouring countries. to strengthen their geopolitical independence and energy security, member states must promote the existence of a genuine internal energy market, capable of withstanding external pressures. energy security also requires diversification of suppliers. to diversify its energy sources, the eu must ensure the continuity of the infrastructure construction defined in its energy strategy 2020 . it will thus gradually benefit from more strategic energy cooperation with third countries, notably through the nabucco project and the africa-eu energy partnership.

on behalf of the s&d group. madam president, i join both my colleague salafranca and mrs ashton in saying how powerless and horrified we feel by the images coming from syria. however, i do not entirely agree with ignacio salafranca's idea of a military presence today. or at least we should agree on what we would call a military presence, since there are now observers whose mandate could possibly be extended. but i think the temptation of military intervention in this region of the world -- with lebanon next door, half of which supports bashar al-assad, iran next door, iraq next door, and the destabilization and terrorism that would inevitably creep into the region -- is so great that we really have a dilemma. at the moment, apart from the extremely significant political pressure, apart from a humanitarian pressure that is making some progress today, i believe that the impact of military intervention would be much more disastrous in my opinion. that's why i'd like to ask you a few questions, mrs. ashton. part of the solution, part of the problem is with the russians. there was the eu/russia summit: what was the russian response? for the greens/efa group mr president, it is obvious, mrs ashton, that on 28 november the congolese government was not elected. in relation to what you have just said, i make three concrete proposals for our long-term commitment. the first concerns the notorious case of the assassination of mr chebeya, the human rights defender. i think an appeal is absolutely necessary in this case. i think that we must clarify the possible responsibilities of the police and that the release of thierry michel's film on this issue is a guarantee that can help to make this clarification possible. secondly, mrs ashton, freedom of the press. many intimidations and arrests of journalists have taken place since november 28. this has to be noted and said, it's very important. the last thing you mentioned is insecurity in the east. what's happening is very disturbing. the violence continues, the mutiny, which you mentioned, involving mr ntanganda is extremely worrying and has made the situation worse. it is also believed that rwandan nationals with heavy weapons are operating in the area. in this context, i go a little further than you: i think that not only must things be said, but the rwandan authorities must also be questioned. yes or no, is there any passive or active involvement of rwanda in the presence of nationals with heavy weapons when there is an embargo on these weapons in congo? i think that we must act here too, not only to wish for peace in this region but, above all, to check whether the rwandan authorities are involved. we cannot remain silent about this. these are three things i propose you to implement fairly quickly, independently - i wish it as you do - of our long-term commitment to the congolese people who have been waiting for these elections for so long and who are now so disappointed and in such great difficulty.
mr president, the chebeya case has shocked the international community as much as the human rights defenders in the democratic republic of congo. it has become, in a way, the barometer of the democratic health of the country. so where are we after the high-profile trial in 2011? as mrs vergiat said, murderers have names, faces, but some still get away with it. general numbi was never prosecuted. the body of fidel bazana, the driver of chebeya, was never found. the widow shebaia's appeal was rejected, but after a year i have just been informed today that a date seems to have been set for the appeal to be heard. the film by director thierry michel, dedicated to the chebeya case, was supposed to be released in july in kinshasa, but a letter asking for the film to be banned was sent by the ministry of justice to the president of the republic, the security service and the attorney general. the courage of the international humanist federation, the carter centre in kinshasa, the wallonia-brussels centre and the many parishes and churches that have decided to defy the bans and hold private screenings is to be commended. i shall be attending myself at the beginning of july. the congolese elections - as has been said - were marred by irregularities. it is now up to the congolese authorities to prove to us that despite these irregularities, the project of a truly democratic republic of congo still stands. the chebeya case shows us the way forward.

almost 500 basis points also for italy. we have to ask what is happening. are they mad or are we mad? are we seeing things properly? well, i think we all know the answer in this house - maybe not the ecr and maybe not ukip, but i think everyone else can see that unless we find a structural and a global solution to the crisis we will simply continue to fight fires as we are doing now. because that's what we're doing, fire-fighting, and fighting one fire after the other. greece first, then ireland, then portugal, then spain, and tomorrow, you may be sure, italy. not setting up a systemic answer, a structural solution to the crisis and not building a real firewall that can resist. what do we need? we know we need. three things: i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and whether he is aware that the council has already made a number of proposals to the council, and whether it is not aware that the council has already made a number of proposals to the council. the second thing we know also: a fiscal union including a redemption fund - the one we will vote on today in the two-pack - so there is no need for an initiative from the council or the commission about that. they simply need to respond to the legislative proposal of the parliament in the coming days and weeks. finally we know what we really need, which is a political union with a real economic government. so my plea to you, mr barroso, is to say that speeches here, speeches in the council and recommendations from the commission are all very well, but what we need now are legislative proposals from your side put directly on the table of the council and parliament on a banking union, on a fiscal union and on a political union. there is no need to wait for an agreement between france and germany and italy and spain. you have the right of initiative; you can make things happen and move forward. put your proposals on the table on a redemption fund, and put your proposals on the table for a real banking union. do it now, in the next few weeks, because i am not sure that on 28 and 29 june our leaders in the council will agree on a banking union and can agree on a redemption fund. finally, on the multiannual financial framework (mff) and own resources, i regret one thing, which is that neither the council nor the commission have said a word, a single phrase, on something that is the most crucial element of this multiannual financial framework which is, finally, to return to the initial idea of the founding fathers and to make a direct link between the citizens and the european union. there is no real democracy in europe if you only have a parliament with no say in the resources of its institutions. in every parliament, in every country, in every democratic country in the world, the parliament has a direct say over the resources of the country, over the resources of the state. so that is what we need most now in these discussions and in this debate, and i can tell you that there will be no consent from our group to the multiannual financial framework if there are no own resources in this multiannual financial framework. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is also good that the negotiating box is now being used by the european council, where growth, but also the european semester and therefore budgetary consolidation at the level of the member states, are being discussed. the proposal on the table allows, thanks to own resources, to reduce the contributions of the member states and thus to make room in their own budget and with the own resources of the european union for a growth budget, which is essential for the european budget. the time has come to establish the basic principles that will ensure both growth and budgetary orthodoxy. the budget can make a vital contribution to this. i hope that the european council will also be the occasion to send a structural signal to the euro and the eurozone. we do indeed need a banking union, we need a political union and we must take structural measures if we are to be credible.

in writing. mr president, we are not going to solve the crisis in europe by greece leaving the euro zone. such a scenario will only lead to socioeconomic disaster and a severe loss of prosperity for all europeans. the european union is not a single currency, but a single currency. in short, it's not an option. the only way out of this crisis is the way forward. only by deepening european cooperation can we emerge stronger from this crisis. the european council in madrid in december last year called for the creation of a european union-wide debt management system. or to put it another way: economic growth and sound finances are two sides of the same coin. 'we have three months left,' says christine lagarde of the imf, 'to save the euro.' i assume she knows what she's talking about. three months is more than enough time to do what needs to be done. it is a matter of political courage. the european parliament is setting out some clear signs here today. the heads of state or government should take up their responsibilities and carry them out at the summit on 28 june 2012.
in writing. the european budget is of paramount importance as a powerful investment instrument, a vehicle for cohesion and solidarity between states, a budget that is always in balance, a source of savings for national budgets through the mutualisation of expenditure. a budget financed largely from own resources is needed. the ftt and the vat reform must increase the european budget, reduce the contributions of the states and give europe budgetary sovereignty. the guidelines set out by the former sure committee must be followed, including the increase of at least 5% and the creation of transition regions for the structural funds. the council's 'negotiating box' cannot diminish the european parliament's negotiating capacity. it must be able to play its role as a co-legislator as provided for in the treaties. i shall pay particular attention to the inclusion in this document of the regions in transition (to ensure cohesion throughout the european territory), the egf (to help redundant workers to find a job) and the edp. i hope that the belgian government will defend these theses in the council, because it is a question of defining the budgetary context of the union for the next seven years and therefore a key moment for the european project.
in writing. the european parliament gave its backing to the draft regulation proposed in spring 2011 by the commission to update the eu's generalised system of preferences (gsp) to reflect changes in the structure of world trade. the next gsp, which will come into force on 1 january 2014, will no longer grant tariff preferences to high and middle income countries per capita on the world bank's list. exports to the eu from countries whose per capita income has exceeded usd 4 000 for the last four years will no longer benefit from reduced or zero duties, such as oil-producing and oil-exporting countries (saudi arabia, kuwait, qatar, etc.). the update reduces the number of countries with preferential access to eu markets from 176 to 75. i welcome the vote on this report, which i think is fair: the next gsp will exclude high-income countries in order to devote more attention to the poorest countries. these are the countries we must help first!
in writing. − this report and the whole theme make it clear that a coordinated financial and economic policy is of paramount importance to the eu. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the council's proposals are implemented. we need to make it clear to eu citizens that it is important for all of us to respect the clear rules of budgetary discipline, but also to take growth-friendly initiatives.
in writing. how to vote yes to a text that does not even try to solve the banking hemorrhage and the financing of the debt. europe will never recover without it. the text does indeed contain some progress, such as the part on eurobonds, and this is mainly due to the fight we european socialists have waged. however, i have seen during the debates that the philosophy of blind rigor, of 'everything in finance' and of an almost dehumanized economy is still firmly entrenched in the minds of many of my colleagues from other political quarters. how much time and how many wasted lives will it take before some people take responsibility and agree to effectively combat speculation, to introduce a tax on financial transactions and a rating agency that is not controlled by multinationals, and to protect pension funds?
in writing. - the ppe is satisfied that the two-pack has been approved. we hope to start negotiations with the council as soon as possible in order to complete the dossier before the summer. my group is also pleased that essential elements of the budgetary treaty have been incorporated into the two-pack and that the growth and stability pact remains in full force. budgetary vigilance remains necessary. to restore confidence, but also to avoid putting off today's bills until tomorrow. parliament is not only voting today on strengthening budgetary coordination, but it is also sending a signal that it is time to make progress on budgetary integration. a thorough debate on the feasibility, timing and terms of debt-collective instruments - eurobonds, temporary redemption funds or variants thereof - is in order. we are open to this debate if it is conducted in a serious manner and if the proposals do not create more problems than they solve. the keyword for the months and years ahead remains confidence, the bridge between savings and growth. to insist on a european strategy of confidence, committed to sound public finances, a sound banking system, economic growth and jobs and a socially just society: that's the best way out of the crisis.
in writing. today, the european parliament said it was not ready to approve the next regulation on the multiannual financial framework (2014-2020) without a political agreement on the reform of the own resources system that would end existing rebates and other correction mechanisms, and lead to greater transparency, fairness and sustainability . this is, in essence, the warning we gave to the council when we adopted a resolution on the eu's multiannual financial framework and own resources. and i fully support this message! i also reiterated my call for a robust eu budget for the period 2014-2020. the union's budget is an investment budget with a strong leverage effect, with 94% of its appropriations devoted to stimulating economic growth and employment and to strengthening the union's role as a global player. the union budget plays and must continue to play a strategic role alongside the fiscal consolidation measures currently imposed on national budgets.

in writing. i am delighted that the resolution on the un arms trade treaty was adopted by a very large majority. although not binding, it is a strong signal from the eu to its member states to adopt a strong common position at the international conference to be held at un headquarters in new york from 2 to 27 july. after four years of negotiations on this treaty, it is time to get it done! we call for the new international treaty to be based on standards that are as clear, as strict and as high as possible; strengthen accountability and its enforcement be open and transparent , with enforcement monitoring and reporting mechanisms; it must also propose a strict assessment of the country to which a sale is ultimately destined, i.e. an assessment that makes the conclusion of a contract subject to several criteria (economic, social, democratic and humanitarian standards).
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, the road towards a true economic and fiscal union to complement our monetary union is, of course, full of hurdles. the new legislation in force on economic governance, reinforced today with the voting of the ‘two-pack’ in parliament, has given new tools to the commission to ensure that member states choose the right part of a growth-friendly fiscal consolidation, but also stick to their country-specific recommendations. as a liberal, i truly believe that reforms of our economy and social system, such as labour market reforms and pension reforms, should be at the heart of regaining competitiveness in the eurozone. but unfortunately in the past months we have seen that some member states have overly contradicted the country-specific recommendations. let us take the example of france. it has stated that it will undo the pension reforms and lower the retirement age. this is completely against the spirit of the recommendations given to france to ensure that all workers stay in employment longer. also in my own country, belgium, wage-setting hampers regaining competitiveness, but socialist members of governments have openly said that they will not address this problem. this is a perfect example of how countries choose not to show solidarity with other countries within the eurozone. as long as member states keep looking to other member states to enlarge the competitiveness of the union and are not able or willing to implement or reform in their own country, the fundamental economic crisis in europe will not end. we must ensure that member states finally take ownership of the european semester and implement the structural reforms needed. we must ensure that, instead of blaming the european union for everything that goes wrong, member states are made responsible and more accountable for their policies. the commission will have to ensure that member states put into practice what they preach, but how will it make sure that member states within the council show the necessary solidarity with each other and commit to the fiscal consolidation and structural reforms as requested through the european semester or enable the european union to be the frontrunner in attracting private investment to regain competitiveness and growth? true solidarity implies responsibility from all member states. member states should stop hiding behind europe, but instead use the full potential that europe has to get out of this crisis.

madam president, i would first like to pay tribute to all the young girls, all the mutilated women, who have dared to bear witness to their suffering. it's important that we know what genital mutilation really is. we always think of clitoral excision, but there's also infibulation. let me tell you, the labia majora are also removed, the vulva is sutured with sewing thread or acacia thorns until the healing occurs covering the vagina and urethra. to allow the evacuation of urine, menstrual bleeding, and vaginal secretions, a small reed rod is inserted during suture. it'll leave a tiny hole for the scar to heal. and according to one witness, the worst of it is the bleeding and the urination. to urinate, it takes an hour, sometimes two. later, when these girls have their period, they will have to insert a small stick into the vagina to open a passageway that will allow the blood to drain. the girls squat and contract their bladder to urinate. they roll on the floor and it's very painful. i met gynecologists in brussels who discovered women giving birth while they were in the infibrillator and the husband did not want to allow the infibrillator to be removed. this is our home. this ownership of men over their wives, and more specifically over their sex, is not muslim. it is a thousand-year-old custom that also corresponds, in our christian tradition, to the chastity belt with which the crusaders girded their wives before going to deliver jerusalem from the hands of the infidels. but it is still, and still, centuries later, the same struggle for women's freedom. and even today, the same religious obscurantism in cruel patriarchal systems perpetuates before our eyes these forms of torture, which must be removed from all taboos, and i thank the commission for addressing this issue.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, too many girls and women are still being subjected to female genital mutilation today. the number of girls who have been mutilated is currently estimated at 140 million worldwide, and about 3 million girls are still at risk of being mutilated every year. such mutilations, usually performed on children and adolescents between the ages of four and fourteen, are also performed on infants under one year of age in some african countries. it is high time that the fundamental rights of women, as well as those of children, are respected in all countries of the world and not only in europe, where we are particularly concerned and where - as michael cashman said, before me - about 500 000 women have been subjected to this mutilation. it is not only the physical health but also the mental health of all those whose dignity is being attacked. no reason given by mutilationists can justify such barbarism against women. it deprives them of their basic right to control their bodies. finally, europe must therefore do everything in its power to help all countries to combat this form of abuse and, in particular, to support the efforts of the associations which fight against these practices, as my colleagues nicole kiil-nielsen and jiménez-becerril barrio have already mentioned.

i'm the reporter. - mr president, commissioner, i would also like to welcome the danish presidency, but perhaps it is the day after the defeat, i do not know, we will do without it. first of all, it is important, and i would like to thank in particular mrs de lange, mr sârbu, mr girling and mr liotard, mr carl schlyter, and mr rossi, who during this long work were the rapporteurs for their respective political groups and who have proved that we are obviously stronger together than when parliament is divided on certain issues. this is of course in the best interests of the citizens, the families and the vulnerable groups who are the first to be affected by the future food regulations. the text we are debating now and voting on in three hours is essential for newborns, young children under three and those with long-term illnesses who have specific nutritional needs. to put it simply, this refers to food intended for particular audiences, which cover about 2% of the vast food market. i shall not go into the genesis of this legislation at great length, except to say that the commission has done well, after 35 years of applying the legislation on dietetic food, which was later extended, of course, to other types of products, to undertake the complete renewal of this particular legal arsenal, and for good reason. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's foodstuffs are not used in the same way as the foodstuffs of other member states. the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection, by its unanimous vote on my draft report on 29 february, sent a clear signal, which i will summarize as follows: the approach of the commission to simplify this legislation and abolish the concept of dietetic food, which i have just mentioned, is the right one. we supported her. it is also crucial to maintain a tailor-made legislative framework for three types of food: food for infants and young children - liquid and solid - and food - as i said - for the sick. so many subgroups of captive populations, so to speak, who cannot exercise this free choice, hence the crucial importance for us of maintaining strict rules on labelling and composition. thirdly, we wanted to extend this scope to other vulnerable groups of the population, who are equally fragile, without, of course, wanting to make any gradation here. in this case, people who are overweight and who are on a drastic diet of less than 1,200 calories a day and people who are gluten intolerant, those who suffer from what is called celiac disease. about 2% of the european population suffer from this disease and they support us very strongly in our desire to maintain a specific legal framework for these so-called gluten-free products. i remind you, this legislation is designed to protect the most fragile, the most vulnerable among us, it's essential. we also wanted to - and i cannot go into all of them here, but it is important - think about our most dynamic companies in europe, those smes which are at the heart of innovation and job creation in our european territory. whether they are italian or german and active in gluten-free products, belgian random , active in food supplements, the least that can be said is that smes and they have come very close to us, of course, throughout the work were concerned about the commission's plan to overturn the current legislation in the 2006 claims regulation. i can easily understand them and i will use one argument, but one that is very strong: the cost they have to face to produce a sound scientific dossier to present to efsa, between eur 500 000 and eur 1 million on average. how do you expect to achieve healthy and fair competition in these circumstances? the priority given to access to the internal market for smes must therefore be transversal in all our legislation. of course, we're bringing it up here. i would like to conclude by giving a short message to the fictitious rapporteurs. you will understand that as rapporteur i have to stick to the overall compromise which was finalised in committee. i do not wish to reopen new fronts or to reopen fronts that have been cut in committee. although i may personally sympathize with some of the amendments tabled in plenary, particularly perhaps the 84th on the question of pesticides, i shall stick to our agreement, to our compromises. it is covered by our amendment no 63 and above all a word is a word and i will stick to it. i thank you and would be glad to hear from the following speakers.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i have welcomed the report by mrs ries, and therefore the proposal for a regulation amending and supplementing the rules on food for infants and children and food for special medical purposes, because this text is intended to help europe achieve a clearer and more coherent legal framework. first of all, i would like to emphasize the obligation to provide complete, objective information on food products. a very sensitive case is that of advertising for infant formulae for infants aged 0 to 6 months and for babies aged 6 months to 2 years. the facts show that advertising practices tend to disturb parents by using marketing strategies rather than providing them with scientific and factual information that enables consumers to make a clear distinction in order to avoid any risk of confusion. this lack of clarity is a danger to the health of our children, a danger which must be avoided through stricter controls. finally, i think that the report could have provided for more effective evaluation mechanisms. this assessment should not be made a priori by considering only the consumers to whom the products are intended but on the basis of a careful and comprehensive assessment of all the components of the product in question.

as the commissioner has just mentioned - the cornerstone of the overall, horizontal legislation on which we all worked together a few months ago, and which we have focused on here, on the even more ambitious, more precise and more specific needs of the populations concerned. but we did it together precisely to ensure that the texts were consistent with the transversal legislation. just a word on something that was not mentioned, perhaps because the danish presidency is not here, namely the lack of agreement at first reading. the failure of this attempt is also partly the fault of the presidency, but also perhaps because the fast track procedure is showing its limits. it is a path that has been used far too often since the beginning of this mandate, since 2009, and where parliament and parliament's ambitions have not always met, in my opinion. i therefore continue to believe that our decision to go to the vote and indeed to go back for a second reading - which i hope will be swift - is still the right one. i conclude with the two major differences with the commission, which i can see from listening to the commissioner. the gluten-free issue. there are many of us here - and the majority - who think that the reference in the legislation to 'food information to consumer' will not be sufficient. this goes beyond labelling and labelling issues. in our view, article 36 of the regulation on consumer information on foodstuffs is not sufficient in terms of quality, safety and composition. but we'll get back to that. as regards the question of delegated acts - you mentioned it at the very beginning, commissioner - we as parliament continue to want to be effectively involved in any decision, for example, through our right to scrutiny, concerning the amendment of the famous union list. we are therefore holding firm on the question of delegated acts, but these are open works. i hope we can return there very soon with our cypriot friends.
on behalf of the s&d group. mr president, commissioner, you mentioned three concerns. the first is the accelerated liberalization of the sector. who are you talking to? the accelerated and unbridled liberalization of the market is creating deregulation in all markets, which is causing hyper-volatility in prices, which is the greatest insecurity for the agricultural world. the increase in flexibility of 1% per year did not help because, in an unfavourable market environment - i remember what happened in the autumn of 2009 - it caused the most serious crisis the sector has experienced in the last 25 years. the second is the territorial balance. we need to insure it, and we don't have that right now. you're talking about restructuring measures. restructuring means the disappearance of production in less productive areas. but it is the opposite. we must put our words into action and maintain production even where the regions are less favourable. thirdly, the current market situation. it's turning around, and it's not necessarily because of seasonal peaks, it's clearly increased volatility in the milk market. i wanted to ask you a question, commissioner. we're talking about soft landing for the current system. should we also be interested in a soft take off, and should the commission ensure that the new system which we have tried to put in place and which will soon succeed the previous one is also safe for all european milk producers?

in writing. i welcome the adoption of this report by a very large majority, which is ambitious in providing for stricter labelling of baby milk and food for special medical purposes. the text adopted will constitute parliament's position on this matter in its forthcoming negotiations with the council of ministers. the changes were proposed because of problems faced by both consumers and the authorities. for example, parliament believes that the labelling of all milk-based products for babies up to 12 months of age should not include any representation of infants and no image which could 'idealize' the use of the product. the commission should also review the current complex legal situation on milk for children aged 12 to 36 months and propose new rules if necessary. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'common carrier' to cover all transport services. it was also recommended that the commission prepare a study to address the lack of specific rules on lactose intolerance.
in writing. i welcomed the proposal for a regulation however, i have reservations about this report. first of all, i would like to emphasize the obligation to provide objective and complete information on food products. a very sensitive case is that of advertising for infant formulae for infants from 0 to 6 months and for babies from 6 months to 2 years. the facts show that advertising practices tend to disturb parents by using marketing strategies rather than providing scientific and factual information that enables consumers to make a clear distinction in order to avoid any risk of confusion. there's a danger to our children's health. finally, i think that the ries report should provide for more effective evaluation mechanisms. this assessment should not be made a priori, considering only the consumers for whom the product is intended, but on the basis of a careful and comprehensive assessment of all the components of the product concerned. the regulation which parliament is to adopt should take greater account of consumer protection and health and of marketing strategies put in place by the food industry.
in writing. for better understanding and participation of the european citizen, the principle of multilingualism within the european institutions must remain a reality, despite the budgetary expenditure that this entails. this resolution will ensure that every citizen of the union has the right to address the institutions in the official eu language of his or her choice. it is important that public consultations be conducted without any linguistic discrimination.
