in writing. it was important that the single market act include a programme that takes into account social and consumer protection, which is reflected in the inclusion of a social clause in all internal market legislation, legislation on services of general economic interest, a legislative programme to strengthen workers' rights. it is a workable solution, the result of a tough negotiation. it is only a step and there is still a long way to go, but it is encouraging.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of this non-legislative resolution, which largely focuses on the measures promoted by the european commission to promote economic growth and job creation. in line with the employment package presented by the commission in mid-april, parliament wanted to encourage investment in the green economy, health and new technologies sectors, while welcoming proposals on the introduction of decent minimum wages to combat poverty and social dumping and the creation of a european youth guarantee. i think that the measures proposed by the european commission to stimulate employment are in the right direction, but i would have liked to see more substantial measures adopted, such as the amendments advocating solidarity in taxation systems in order to preserve the financial basis of social protection schemes, or those encouraging coordination of collective bargaining at european level or rebalancing of taxation!
in writing. − many are now convinced that in times of crisis the difficult balance between austerity and growth promotion must be found. the way to get there is very different. the single market offers a unique opportunity, of course, if member states see growth 'together and not just at home'. research, sustainable development, services sector here are some of the niches to find to create jobs. but this will only work if the necessary resources are available. against this background, the multiannual perspective is of particular importance for the eu budget, as it can free up the necessary resources, for example in cohesion policy, mobility and research, to support 'growth that creates jobs'.
in writing. with the european union experiencing record unemployment and the economic outlook for the coming months not looking promising, a rebalancing between the current austerity measures and investment for jobs must be found. it is imperative to boost the labour market through structural reform. economic and employment policies must focus on creating conditions favourable to job creation and encouraging demand for labour. the employment package contains key areas (such as youth employment and unemployment, education, training, skills development, retraining of workers, etc.) which require concrete measures to create the conditions for a competitive european economy, based on real performance and not on endless large public borrowing. the priorities are therefore to facilitate business creation and to identify and overcome obstacles for potential entrepreneurs.
in writing. without job creation, there will never be recovery. but the crisis must not be used as an excuse for precarious employment. so while i agree with a good deal of the text, and parliament's rejection of amendments nos 8, 9, 10 and 11 (which were rejected overwhelmingly by the right and the conservatives) thus casting dangerous doubt on its will to maintain a system of social protection and labour protection, i cannot, as i had originally intended, take the liberty of endorsing this text.
in writing. i am very proud, as a member of the committee on women's rights and gender equality, of the joint resolution adopted by a large majority to end fgm. this resolution is intended to send a strong signal to this effect, especially to the united nations, but also to the member states and the european commission. the ep wants the perpetrators to be punished. every year 3 million girls worldwide are at risk of fgm, including 180 000 in europe. these are intolerable figures for a traditional practice which, i insist, has nothing to do with religion. we therefore call on the forthcoming united nations general assembly to adopt a resolution calling for an end to genital mutilation worldwide, by harmonizing worldwide measures and by drawing up recommendations and guidelines. the united nations, like civil society, should also allocate financial resources to this cause and carry out targeted programmes. as for the member states, i believe that they should all ratify and apply the relevant international instruments.
in writing. my full support for this resolution, particularly as a member of the committee on women's rights and gender equality. there is nothing in islam that requires these practices. these are ancient practices, cultural rather than religious. in addition to the sanctions, which are essential for zero tolerance of these barbaric practices, proactive education and training strategies must be developed at local level, including for mothers who perpetuate the abuse they have experienced in their daughters.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of this resolution as it calls for stronger controls on medical devices, particularly breast implants, to avoid a repeat of the pip scandal. these proposals should feed into the debate on the review of medical device legislation due to take place in the autumn. the pip scandal has highlighted a malfunction at european and national level, including a lack of cooperation between the authorities concerned and a lack of traceability of the raw materials used for medical devices. we therefore called on the european commission to provide for a system of pre-market authorization for certain products, including breast implants, and asked for the introduction of an implant recipient passport, including the product code, its specific characteristics and potential harmful effects, as well as an inventory of breast implants in each member state. we also wanted to see the creation of a system of collective redress to help consumers obtain compensation. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover all the activities of the european union's institutions.
in writing. the government's deliberate or inadvertent inaction in this matter is questionable. in such a situation, victims have a right to expect real accountability and real solutions. the actions of the monitoring committee set up by the health authorities are far from without reproach. i also note that the issue of overcharging surgeons for the removal of defective prostheses has not been really addressed, which is also unacceptable. do i need to remind you that 8,000 women have had their prostheses removed, but that there are 22,000 women who still wear them... it is unworthy of pip to use this illegal homemade gel to fill its prostheses as a replacement for a certified medical gel, and it is equally unworthy to boast that the so-called 95% of prostheses are safe... finally, the health impact of pips is not limited to isolated cases of inflammation. some pathologies developed by wearers of pip prostheses (other than cancers) cannot but be linked to the products contained in these prostheses.
author. - mr president, 17 million people are affected by famine and drought in the sahel, 8 million of whom are in a situation of near desperation. to date, the commission has released €123 million in humanitarian aid to tackle the food crisis in 2012 and €164 million in development aid. the famine is compounded by the growth of criminal and terrorist gangs, which derive their funds from drug trafficking from guinea-bissau to somalia, with cocaine from latin america and heroin from afghanistan. the military intervention in libya has precipitated instability in the region by displacing refugees in all neighboring countries and allowing libyan militias and weapons to destabilize the whole. mali was the first to pay for the libyan operation, but nigeria, niger, burkina faso, senegal and mauritania were not spared. what more can europe do than it is doing? at the last meeting of the acp and the european union in horsens we heard absolutely heartbreaking speeches from our friends in mali, niger and so on. we would say, "but what more do you want us to do?" they were crying out for a pesd mission which is planned, which must come, which is prepared but which should come much sooner to stabilise the region militarily. i am not in favour of military interventions, but the esdp is absolutely essential, otherwise these countries will consider themselves the harkis of europe, the ones who were abandoned during the libyan intervention.
author. - mr president, only half of iran's population is persian. the other half are different ethnicities. the united nations committee against racial discrimination has already pointed out, in august 2010, the phenomena of exclusion of these minorities and they are numerous. i will quote them all: there are arabs, azeri, turks, baluchis, kurds and turkmen, mainly. our resolution deals mainly with a small group of ahwazi arabs. so this is a very small part of the larger problem of ethnic minorities in iran. the group is called "april 15 youth". it's from 2005. he has a demonstration every year in april. in 2011, these protests reportedly left 15 dead and injured. it was reported on facebook and twitter. this year there has been another death and injury and those mentioned in the resolution are 6 ahwazi mainly, who are in prison. i said, "who are in prison and should be tried". information is very difficult to collect on these cases because it's circulating on social media, but still, i think in point 3, the fact that you name them and say they have to have a fair trial is something very important.

"blue card" is the answer. i thank my colleague for the question. first of all, we must distinguish between freight and passenger transport. the liberalization of freight transport has not been successful, precisely because the rules are not sufficiently clear. that's what we're trying to figure out right now. but at the same time, of course, we have much more important issues to deal with. i am thinking of the technical interoperability which now prevents us from being able to organise cross-border freight transport with any real ease. i would just like to say this about passenger transport. that's not the issue at all. we as a group have also contributed to this. that is a debate we are going to have next year and you can be sure that my group will see to it that the rights of workers are safeguarded and that the quality of service, the public service in particular, is maintained.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your work. thank you to all those who have helped us to achieve this result. i am always surprised when people talk about the liberalization of the railways. we've been fighting for it for ten years, but we don't have it, nowhere. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. this is the result. what we want is a railway that is more efficient, a railway that opens up because we know that the railway in europe has a very different future than it has at the moment. we've been trying to do that for ten years. what are the countries doing? they make up their own rules every year. a train from brussels to germany has to change the voltage four times. the trains have to follow local, regional rules, up to 3,000 units. and then you wonder why the road is so efficient. we want an efficient railway not only to preserve jobs, but to create jobs. we want to. and they should tell the citizens and the unions that too. we've been focusing on the important thing, which is a strong regulator. the powerful regulator is supposed to eliminate the distortion that still exists today. why should we discuss large structures when we don't even have an authority that can say: this company should have proper access to the market which is not the case in many countries today. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. and you have to respect the people who say that in my region it's different, even technically different at the moment. could you not give a signal that the fourth package could tackle this problem technologically and find a solution there? that would reassure many today, and tomorrow you might say that the single european railway market is coming, perhaps a little faster than some people think. this would be a good sign, especially for cooperation between parliament and the commission, because we must convince the council together. that will be the most important task.
mr president, since parliament and the council have agreed fairly quickly to postpone any changes to the vertical separation between infrastructure managers and railway undertakings to the forthcoming fourth railway package and have also considerably mitigated the commission's proposals on the separation of stations and maintenance workshops, i could have expressed my satisfaction with this matter to you today. unfortunately, i have recently discovered that the commission took advantage of this revision, in breach of the relevant rules, to amend article 7 (1) and (2) of the treaty, which deal with the so-called essential functions, without explanation. if passed, this amendment to existing legislation will make the alternative to the full separation model that the commission has long favoured less coherent and less efficient. what's the alternative? it is designed to allow member states, while isolating essential functions, to maintain a certain degree of integration in order to achieve better operational performance for passengers and for goods. at a time when britain is beginning to look back on its choices, at a time when it is becoming clear that complete separation is not the way forward, i do not see why we should rush into this one-size-fits-all model today. i will therefore not be able to support the proposal for a directive on the revision of the first railway package, although i am in favour of strong regulation and transparency of financial flows.
i am also very pleased that some positive things have changed in this rearrangement and that a deal has actually been struck. i am particularly pleased with the increased transparency in financial flows for holding companies and also the evolution towards a more european-oriented regulator by better coordinating national regulators is an important step forward. but the fact remains that the council, the various member states, continue to protect their own national monopolies in the first place. as long as we cannot really speak of a single market, as long as these monopolies are protected, there will never be better service for companies, there will never be more reliability for the customers who use rail transport in europe and we will never be able to realise the economic added value of this railway package. so this fourth railway package announced by the commission will have to include these elements, will have to provide a more open transport fleet, will also have to liberalise passenger transport and will also have to achieve the complete separation between infrastructure and operations in rail transport. i hope the commission can show that ambition too.
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, tachographs play an important dual role for road transport: a social and a safety role. they make sure that the rules on driving time and rest periods are properly enforced. this is vital since the rules address the fatigue of drivers, which is one of the main causes of accidents involving heavy-duty vehicles. tacographs also help ensure that the rules cannot be abused, resulting in distortion of competition between road transport operators. driving-time rules are now observed better since digital tachographs were introduced in 2006. operators also have less paperwork than they did with the analogue tachograph, but reports indicate that levels of fraud and the administrative costs of using tachographs are still high. last year the commission proposed ways to tackle these two problems. this would be done by introducing a new ‘smart tachograph’ using satellite navigation and radio communication functions, accompanied by better rules on inspections, workshops, training of enforcement officers and sanctions. i am very glad that the report from the committee on transport and tourism prepared by ms ţicău supports and reinforces the commission proposal. the commission welcomes more than half of the 120 amendments adopted by the transport committee, as they clarify or strengthen its original proposal. they include, for instance, the amendments to strengthen the sanctions regime, to improve training of enforcers and to clarify the rules on the tachonet it system and on the liability of employers. the commission can also accept in principle another quarter of the amendments. the most important of these are on the merger of driver cards with the driving licence, asking for a more detailed analysis before the implementation of such a merger; and, secondly, on moving definitions from the current annex into the main body of the legislation. i would, however, caution against applying full codecision to non-essential elements which may need regular updates, and requiring member states to equip all their control authorities with the devices needed to read the information transmitted by the new radio communication function of the smart tachograph. this is good but costly, and i would recommend a step-by-step approach. there remain 13 amendments that are of concern to the commission. the most important are the amendments numbered 116 to 120, which would result in changes to the existing social rules and legislation on driving times – regulation 561/2006. these amendments would create a derogation for craftsmen driving within a radius of 150 km of their base. this is a substantial increase from the 100 km set down in the commission’s proposal and it would negatively affect road safety. in practice it would encourage many drivers to drive six hours or more on a daily basis without any control on their driving time.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's transport policy is not disrupted by the introduction of new technologies. these social rules are very much needed to ensure that road safety is guaranteed, but also to create a level playing field and fair competition between companies. to achieve that, more control is needed. technology is helping us do that. it is evolving very rapidly and the new digital tachograph which we are now going to describe in legislation will enable remote control which will of course be much more controllable than today. today the truck has to be pulled over before a check can be made. that alone would be an incredible advance. but there are of course other aspects which the rapporteur has mentioned in her report and which we must support. first of all, we must open the debate on the scope of the directive. we want to move towards a situation where all professional drivers are checked and the proposal to go from 3.5 tonnes to 2.8 tonnes is a step in the right direction. on the other hand, we must be more flexible with regard to the exceptions. i agree that it is not the plumber or the electrician who should be checked. that must be clearly defined and the limit of 100 km seems to me to be very reasonable. i'm gonna leave it at that. thank you and good luck to the rapporteur.
i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. i would also like to thank the rapporteur and the shadow rapporteurs for their work. it's a technically difficult dossier. i would also have liked to have had a little more time for debate and discussion. the tachograph simply measures the driving and resting times of the truck driver. so it was a first important priority for me to say who it should apply to. and i think that leaving it up to the member states to decide which craft is excluded and under what conditions was not the best solution. i'm from a border area. with this system, the roofing contractor will have to drive with a tachograph in one state, not one or two kilometres further, and a few kilometres further on. the one who drives 100 miles to do his job and has a ticket writer can't come home at night. and that is why i believe that european legislation must also take account of people's everyday lives. the scheme is intended to apply to professional drivers. all non-professional drivers are to be excluded. the second is the deadlines. we currently have two models of tachograph and are now proposing a third. i therefore suggested that we first set a deadline for the application of one model and then that the commission may adjust the deadlines for a second model. the past has often shown that when we wait for technology, we sometimes set too tight deadlines. it's supposed to be a little easier to solve. the last point is the international area. i know it's about social conditions, working conditions. but what do the citizens expect from our legislation? not that we're saying it's not time to change something. if, as in my region, people spend nights and weekends in their lorries for weeks on end and then perhaps go home, and if these people are employed because they are paid about a third of the wage that is normally paid in the region, then this is not the european transport area that we wanted! we wanted better quality, we wanted better pay and better conditions. i think it is time to change that, even though it was not the aim of this directive to deal with these aspects. (the speaker agrees to answer a question under the 'blue card' procedure under rule 149 (8) of the rules of procedure)
answer to a question on the blue card procedure. the amendments we have proposed, madam president, relate firstly to the fact that we simply want to take the craft workers out of the scope and thus include them in article 3 and secondly to the 2.8 tonnes: we are well aware that certain operators have set up their fleets in such a way that they prefer to use those who do not need a driver's recorder in their trucks for their professional drivers. so when it comes to safety for professional drivers, i have no problem with the 2.8 tonnes. but if it is a question of avoiding harassment of the craftsman, then you can also support my amendment, which simply takes it out altogether. i would prefer not to limit the mileage at all, but that would come at a later stage. we have done something for safety, for flexibility, but also for the european transport area as a whole.

on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, let us first of all welcome a number of decisions that have been taken by the european council. first of all, i think we should welcome the growth package that has finally been decided and the necessary provision for tightening the budget in the union. there is also the decision on a single banking supervisor. i should say: finally a decision. you will remember that two years ago the member states and the council refused to go in that direction, as parliament requested at that time. the commission advised caution and said that we could do it later. well, it is later now and i think it is a good decision. the only thing we should ask is that, if we create this new agency, we do not split it across three member states. it has to be a real single supervisor with powers to close down banks in any member state, and with no exception. mr van rompuy, i will not discuss the third decision: more flexibility for the efsf and the esm. apparently a number of member states today have another opinion – mainly finland. my question to you is what has been really decided now? is finland’s opinion and statement the right one? is it necessary to have unanimity or not? i think this is a key question. why is it a key question? because already today, if you look at the markets, you can see the effect of this attitude. the spanish spreads had gone up again to 491 by mid-morning – that is almost 500 basic points – so we are not out of this crisis. the main thing that we did in this european council was to successfully borrow time again. how much i do not know. is it days, is it weeks, is it months? but we must use this time in a good way. why? not like previous occasions, not like in december 2011 for example, when the european central bank performed two operations and injected eur 1 trillion into the banking system. some of us thought that the crisis was behind us, that the crisis was over. well i think that we wasted time at the beginning of this year. apart from the fiscal compact no structural solution was found to the crisis at that time. so my message today is that we should not waste time again. is it really necessary to wait for an interim report in october and then for a final report by the end of the year? this is not an interim crisis. we need fundamental action now. i think it is better not to wait until october and then wait until the end of the year to take action. we need to use the positive spillover from friday’s decisions to launch directly the economic and political union we desperately need so we can show the outside world – our friends in the g8 and the g20 and the markets – that we have learned our lesson. so action is needed and, mr barroso, action means concrete legislation. my plea to you is very clear. we now have a window of opportunity only of a few weeks, not more than that – and if finland continues in this way, it will even be destroyed within a few hours. we must come forward, before september, with a concrete legislative package. i agree with mr daul that we have until september to do that and to put a legislative package on the table with three elements: firstly, a real banking union – not only supervision but also a deposit guarantee scheme and a joint resolution fund; secondly, a real integrated budgetary framework, including eurobills and a redemption fund – this crisis is not over if there is not at least the beginning of a redemption fund and a partial mutualisation of the debt; and, finally, democratic transparency and accountability. so, mr barroso, use your right of initiative. do not wait until the end of the year. put this legislative package on the table for the council and for parliament. in writing. this new directive, the result of two years of difficult negotiations with the member states, aims to create a 'global and integrated' european railway area, whether in freight or in international passenger transport. the aim is to increase the share of rail in freight transport and to bring down prices for passengers. the new rules require that transmission operators and network operators must keep separate accounts to ensure that any transfers of public funds between the two entities, even if they are within a holding company, are transparent and thus avoid putting any private operators at a disadvantage. all operators, public or private, will have to benefit from the same tariffs and access conditions for a given network, whether it be railways and service facilities (maintenance workshops, stations). finally, the allocation of railway sleepers to operators will include incentives to upgrade bonus/malus equipment to reduce noise and to equip trains with the european control system for maximum safety. the text does not concern internal links within the member states.
in writing. i voted in favour of the legislative resolution to revise the tachograph legislation in depth, in order to prevent fraud, improve the working conditions of professional drivers and, consequently, ensure greater safety on the roads. this opens up the voice to electronic rather than manual use of the device, heralding the advent of the so-called "intelligent" tachograph by 2020. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competition' in the context of the directive on the protection of consumers. satellite technology will allow remote controls to be carried out and only suspicious trucks to be targeted. the smart tachograph is expected to save road transport companies the equivalent of 515 million in administrative costs per year.

in writing. − road safety is one of the cornerstones of european transport policy. the in-vehicle ecall system can make an important contribution to reducing the number of road fatalities. this system automatically sends an alert to the rescue services as soon as the vehicle is involved in a serious accident. i am in favour of the ecall system, because it not only saves lives, but also reduces the severity of injuries caused by accidents by providing immediate assistance. especially when eu citizens travel to another country, they often do not know exactly where they are and do not always speak the language. this automatic signal from ecall is therefore particularly useful, as the information on the exact location of the vehicle is also received by the emergency services. ecall should require an eu-wide interoperable application. if each country provides its own system and can only communicate with its own vehicles, ecall cannot be fully exploited.

become a problem in our society if they are not accessible. receiving wages, paying pensions, paying energy bills, purchasing products or services all prove very difficult if one cannot have a basic bank account. access to this payment service is therefore crucial, especially for vulnerable and highly mobile consumers and people who are increasingly moving between different countries throughout the european union today. all too often, for example, erasmus students abroad, frontier workers and pensioners living part-time in another country are excluded from such essential and basic paid services. i am therefore glad that we are increasing the pressure on the member states with the european parliament. we also advocate that the consumer who does not obtain a bank account in a member state will have access to a basic banking service in the member state where he resides or exercises certain functions. i think it is also very important that if member states do not act, we should call for a binding european legislative proposal before january 2013. the european parliament is in fact following in part what the belgian government did some time ago, which has already provided a to be transparent about how people can access it. the commission has announced that it will examine various systems, but of course the basic banking service must also be broad enough to be fully in writing. russia is, as i have already said, one of the most important partners of the european union in many respects. the numerous political and economic agreements signed with russia prove that the european union regards it as a key partner on the international scene. russia is the eu's third most important trading partner. we have just adopted three resolutions, all on trade agreements with russia, which are favourable to european union companies. the agreement is largely due to russia's soon-to-be-accession to the world trade organization, which will facilitate greater access to the russian market. by becoming a member of the wto, the russian federation will reduce its customs duties from an average of 9.5% at present to around 6% by 2015.
in writing. my vote on mr susta's text is a 'yes but'. the agreement should provide more favourable conditions for european union companies. it is part of a set of three trade agreements which do not preclude further commitments by the union and which should even improve the conditions of producers who depend on raw materials from russia. the trade in services agreement opens up new opportunities for eu shipping agencies looking to establish themselves in russia. it also grants preferential access to staff from european service companies who are going to work in russia with a view to setting up a business there. it provides for a minimum quota of 16 000 work permits per year for this purpose. but it is important that russia fulfils its commitments and ensures that workers' rights are respected: the credibility of the european union is at stake.
in writing. my vote on mr zalewski's text is a 'yes but'. the agreement should provide more favourable conditions for european union companies. it is part of a set of three trade agreements which do not preclude further commitments by the union and which should even improve the conditions of producers who depend on raw materials from russia. the agreement on automotive components protects european automotive parts manufacturers affected by russian measures which will remain in force even after russia's accession to the wto. these measures provide incentives for foreign car manufacturers to relocate to russia and could disadvantage russian imports of foreign automotive components. under the agreement, if european exports of automotive parts fell below the 3% threshold per annum, russia would reduce its import duties on these components by a proportionate amount. but it is important that russia fulfils its commitments and ensures that the rights of workers are respected. the credibility of the union is at stake.
in writing. my vote on the text of ines vaidere is a "yes but". the agreement should provide more favourable conditions for european union companies. it is part of a set of three trade agreements which do not preclude further commitments by the union and which should even improve the conditions of producers who depend on raw materials from russia. in its wto accession negotiations, russia agreed to binding export duties on 80% of the raw materials it exports. under the agreement, russia will consult and negotiate with the european union for at least two months before increasing its export duties on a number of products. the agreement provides the most favourable temporary solution for imports of raw materials which are in great need of the union's manufacturers. however, i am bitterly sorry that the commitments made by russia in this agreement are not strong enough. it is therefore necessary to replace it in the near future by a binding treaty on trade in raw materials.

in writing. with a view to the trilogue with the council and the commission on the 2013 eu budget, we have, by this vote, given our negotiating team a mandate. normally, the council should have agreed on a common position, at least at technical level, before the trialogue on 9 july. but let me be clear, we are really outraged by the commission's proposal to freeze commitment appropriations in real terms in 2013 in order to solve the problem of the spiral of commitments still to be settled. the additional €360 million foreseen for the iter experimental reactor in the 2013 budget, without using an increase in the mff ceilings, is largely financed at the expense of european research programmes under the 7th framework programme. the commission justifies the cuts by underperformance of these programmes.
in writing. the treatment of animals also varies from farm to farm and from one member state to another. we have already been alerted to this problem by the report on laying hens. since some member states do not apply the current eu legislation on animal protection correctly, there is an urgent need not only for greater vigilance to ensure better application of the current legislation but also for general legislation (framework law) guaranteeing animal welfare. we must fill the gaps, punish the perpetrators... beyond the protection of animals, human health is at stake: by preventing the spread of diseases in animals and improving antimicrobial resistance, the health of consumers is protected. finally, on the economic side, this resolution will help to ensure fair competition between producers and greater transparency.
in writing. i voted in favour of the paulsen report on a european strategy for animal welfare 2012-2015. in a modern, peaceful society, no one can be indifferent to the plight of animals. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of animals and the environment. however, i regret that our parliament is succumbing to the trend of labelling prior stunning (paragraph 49 of the resolution). at present, only two member states, sweden and austria, require stunning before slaughtering an animal, whereas the other member states have opted for dialogue with muslim and jewish religious authorities. defending freedoms is also defending freedom of worship and ancestral traditions. if it is really about reducing animal suffering, then let well-meaning people care about the plight of animals during their lifetime. and if health safety is at stake, then it is the controls in the slaughterhouses that urgently need to be strengthened and not the labels on prepackaged meat.
in writing. there is insufficient pressure on member states to apply eu animal welfare rules. a common strategy for animal welfare was therefore needed in the 27 member states. only one third of the member states are doing well. the majority of farmers adopt attitudes which vary from one farm to another. unfortunately, the system still depends on the use of antibiotics. a definition of what animal welfare really is seems equally important, as free trade is becoming increasingly important in agriculture and we europeans are at the forefront of the animal welfare debate, alongside the united states, canada and australia.
in writing. the aim of this resolution is commendable, it advocates rules on animal welfare which should be implemented in a more satisfactory way. the existing gaps must be filled and the perpetrators punished. these rules also protect human health by preventing the spread of animal-related diseases and antimicrobial resistance.
in writing. the measures taken at the european council are in the right direction. i welcome the significant progress on banking union and the aid given to italy and spain. however, i am afraid that the measures for growth are not yet sufficient. i also welcome the transfer of supervision of euro-zone banks to the ecb. this step towards a banking union was indispensable. i regret, however, that no progress has been made on the question of debt pooling. no progress on the eurobonds. i am convinced that part of the solution lies in the creation of eurobonds. the european union, unlike the states, is not in debt. investors would therefore have preferred to see community debt replacing national public debts. the creation of these long-term securities, which angela merkel rejects, as well as the "euro notes" (short-term securities), would have helped the member states to generate growth.
in writing. the commission needs to adopt legislation without delay to tackle the problem of financial exclusion in order to enable every european citizen to participate in socioeconomic life. this exclusion still affects 10% of europeans. these include the homeless, people with very low incomes, students, people with no credit history and finally, expatriate workers. we wanted to promote the idea of a genuine legal right with this resolution, because equal opportunities and non-discrimination are fundamental principles in our democracies. we also wanted to bring the citizen closer to the single market by allowing him to enjoy its full benefits. from now on, everyone will have access to a basic payment account. it can no longer be refused on the grounds of low income, professional status or debt. this basic account should enable every citizen to carry out all essential payment transactions such as receiving his salary on this account, paying bills or withdrawing cash. this resolution is a major step towards non-discrimination, equal opportunities and greater social inclusion.
in writing. i voted in favour of this own-initiative report by parliament. we therefore ask the commission to present a proposal for a directive by january 2013 on the basis of a detailed assessment of the situation in the member states. we believe that the future directive should allow all consumers legally resident in the union to have access to a bank account to carry out essential payment transactions, such as receiving a salary or benefits, paying bills or taxes and buying goods and services, both remotely and physically. we also want the accounts to be free of charge or opened at a reasonable cost. each member state should set a ceiling for the annual amount of fees for the opening and use of an account, and the additional fees should be transparent. finally, we suggest that member states should be required to lay down principles for the imposition of penalties in the event of non-compliance with the framework governing basic payment accounts.
for the greens/efa group mr president, it is true that others have said it before me the state of the european union's public finances is such that we obviously must not deprive ourselves of any tool to promote a number of indispensable investments at european level, in the field of transport, energy or broadband. and project bonds are really an objective that we want to support because they are interesting in that respect. it is a pilot phase, and i think that we must set out clearly from the outset the criteria by which we will assess this pilot phase. i would like to draw everyone's attention to two things. the first is the choice of projects. we have indeed tried - and i thank the rapporteur - to introduce more stringent sustainability criteria in the selection of projects, because all infrastructure construction is not necessarily beneficial for the european union and may also create an ecological debt which we will have to pay off one day. so let us be very careful about the quality of the projects we support. secondly, the question of risks. there is an objective financial risk. it is true that we try to minimize it for the public authorities, but it exists. as such, i believe that we must be extremely careful, in the evaluation phase, to assess the means of limiting the financial risk to public finances as much as possible, while guaranteeing a return for the private investor who has invested in this business. i really think that, as much as it is good, useful and necessary to launch this project, it is equally important to set out in advance the criteria against which the pilot phase will be evaluated, before moving on to the generalization of the project. i think it is an important instrument, but one that deserves our full attention, especially in this early phase.
i should like to begin by thanking the rapporteur and all those who have been involved in this dossier. we are discussing something that has been in the news a lot and that was announced long in advance, the famous project bonds. i think we should support them, because they are a good attempt to channel more private resources into the economy into real investment projects which, in turn, contribute directly and indirectly to economic growth. i am also a little afraid that if we are to achieve our ambitions to, for example, have a transeuropean transport network, we will need a mix of funding sources, and this will be one of them. at the same time, we must also remain modest. we have now set aside eur 230 million to support perhaps two or three projects. that's good, all the little bits help. but - to give one example - if we want to achieve the transeuropean transport network by 2030, we will have to find 500 billion euros to make it happen. these innovative financial instruments must not - and this is important - be an excuse for not making real money available for investment. we must therefore ensure that, in addition to these matters, there are also sufficient appropriations, for example for the cef, so that the various levels of government can also take their responsibilities. but let us go for it anyway.
i think we all know that our european transport network is far from complete and i think it is important to continue investing in those transport networks in the future period through the transeuropean networks and also the connecting europe facility. the project bonds as proposed here are an element of that financing and an important element. we all know that it is becoming more and more difficult for member states to finance large infrastructure projects. we also all know that there is a lot of dormant capital on the sidelines today which makes it very difficult to mobilize capital to invest in the real economy. the project bonds can help to channel that private capital into good infrastructure projects with european added value. the 230 million now being made available is of course only a start, but it will make it possible to finance a number of smaller projects and at the same time to seek answers to a number of technical questions which still exist today with many investors, including credit rating agencies, in order to pave the way and to be able to make the investments in full in a next policy period, also through project bonds. my group therefore fully supports this proposal.
i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the union, but that we should be concerned about the future of the union. but now i'm looking at what we can still do with the modest means we have at our disposal. all this is "high politics", we have discussed it, you are discussing it in the highest circles, but what more can we do? three weeks ago, i met mr. al labouani, a syrian historical dissident who had been imprisoned for five years. when he left the parliament building at that time, he had returned to damascus and was immediately arrested. it wasn't the first time. in my office five years ago, we were discussing conditions in bashar al-assad's prisons and we were talking about suicide attempts. he said, "that's awful". when asked if he was afraid, he replied: "no, i will not commit suicide!" what touched me deeply when he came back and said to me, "you see, mrs. de keyser, i did not commit suicide and i am living the best part of my life today. i never thought it would be like this! whatever the massacres today, i never imagined in my life that i would see this day in syria". it means that beyond the massacres, beyond the horrors that are happening - i'll talk about it - there is hope in the civilian population today in syria that we cannot let down. this message went straight to my heart and i thought, "what can we do?" i have before me the reports of human rights watch, amnesty international, i have everything that is beginning to be listed on crimes against humanity, on torture today in syria and i do not even dare, mr president, to read one of these testimonies to you because it is beyond comprehension. what i would like to say to you is that we need to prepare for the post-bashar al-assad. we have to argue today, take the evidence. i would also like to ask you and the european union, because...
there is no end to the atrocities in syria and the international community seems to be watching helplessly. i do not know whether we can consider the meeting in geneva last weekend a success. the only real solution seems to be to seek some kind of transitional government with representatives of the current regime and the opposition, because the alternative is, of course, a bloody civil war. but the lack of commitment in the declarations on this is worrying. we need to find a way to bring the regime and the opposition to the table. i also agree 100% with mr brok when he says that it is of course an essential condition for success, for the opposition to succeed, that it speaks with one voice, can describe one vision of the future of syria and can also be reassuring to those who still doubt their support for the regime. so i would ask that we keep up the pressure to get real talk.
the past few months have been exciting times in egypt, with a lot of drama, a lot of tension as well. with presidential elections running in parallel with a debate on a new constitution and thus on the power of that president, with a parliament sent home. now, the conclusion is that an opponent of the military regime has won the elections, but at the same time the military is in control of many areas. i personally think that, despite the difficulties, we should see the positive in these developments. the european union now has the task of contributing to the success of this democratic transition. a particularly important challenge will be to get the country back on its feet economically. tourism is practically collapsing. foreign investment is not coming in and the cash register is empty. i would therefore like to ask what initiative the high representative wishes to take to contribute quickly to a re-start of the economy in egypt, which is also a prerequisite for a successful democratic transition.
in writing. this text is a cornerstone of a europe where justice is respected because it is given the means to do so. we cannot stand by while new cases are being opened and the number of cases left unaddressed is constantly increasing. at the end of 2010, there were 1 300 pending cases, compared to 527 cases handled during the year. in order to lighten the workload of the president of the court and the presidents of the chambers of five judges, other amendments were proposed. the constructive proposals made during the first half of the year were not lacking: the creation of a post of vice-president, the possibility for the presidents of chambers with five judges not to take part in cases sent to the main chamber and the increase of the number of judges to 12. it has been argued that most national and international courts, such as the european court of human rights, have vice-presidents, as far as i know.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text, and the outcome must also be positive. the civil service tribunal (cts) has only seven judges. if one of them were to be absent for a long period (for example, sick leave), the functioning of the fpt would be seriously affected. to remedy this problem, it was important to allow the appointment of temporary judges for specialised courts.
in writing. − especially in 'crisis times' it will be seen that these investments are to be considered as 'double' investments. firstly, they will secure or finance jobs in the process of implementation. but these investments also increase the attractiveness and 'accessibility' of europe's regions, which may in effect mean further investment.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution which provides for a european allocation of eur 230 million in guarantees to support the private issuance of bonds to finance infrastructure projects ('project bonds') related to transport, energy and information technologies. our only chance of reviving the european machine is to attract private investors to mobilizing projects in the transport, energy and information technology sectors. by carrying out projects for the future, by making coherent investments, we are giving a positive image of europe. for some time now, europe has been forced to adopt defensive positions which are difficult for the european citizen to accept. it is therefore high time to do everything possible to prevent the disillusionment.
in writing. i opposed this resolution, which is a particularly unbalanced and burdensome text on the situation in the middle east. i also wonder about the almost monomaniacal obsession of this parliament to denounce the policy of the state of israel. i would like him to denounce with the same fervour the atrocities committed in all corners of the world, particularly in syria and mali: not a word during this session, despite the thousands of deaths caused by the damascus butcher and the destruction, looting and bloody clashes in mali.
in writing. we wanted to send a historic message about the west bank and east jerusalem by voting in favour of the resolution. we are firmly condemning the israeli government's construction and expansion of illegal settlements, which are major obstacles to peace efforts. this vote also reflects a change in the european parliament's position. this is the first time that a message of such force has come from our institution. i am very happy and proud to have taken part in this historic vote. the resolution calls on both sides to resume negotiations for a two-state solution without delay and also seeks to ensure that the eu's control mechanism - the 'technical arrangements' - does not allow israeli settlement products to be imported into the european market under preferential arrangements.
i was also a member of the temporary committee on the cia in this parliament at the time. in view of this experience, we can only be pleased with this new report and i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, mrs flautre, on behalf of our group, on the fact that this follow-up report has finally been produced. at the time of the fava report, it was often very difficult for colleagues to know the right approach because of the lack of information on the secret cia detention programme. the report's strength, in my view, was to be found in the formulation of a number of recommendations, such as the establishment of a comprehensive definition of terrorism or the establishment of adequate democratic control of intelligence services. those recommendations were intended to ensure that illegal practices such as enforced disappearances and arbitrary detentions would not be repeated in the future. but what do we determine, five years after the date? that those recommendations have remained dead letter and that no european country has taken account of them. the european union and its member states are still deaf as an east indian to the many calls for transparency about their involvement in the cia programme, despite all the evidence gathered by the european parliament itself, but also by the council of europe, the united nations, the media and organisations such as amnesty international. i hope that after this report parliament will continue to press until there is finally clarity and all the recommendations are implemented. this is absolutely necessary, because the system of arbitrary detention is even more entrenched, particularly in the united states. and guantanamo is still not closed, despite all the promises. the fight against terrorism is necessary, of course, but the balance between the protection of society and the infringement of individual freedoms must be right. respect for human rights is an absolute prerequisite for ensuring the effectiveness of any, and therefore also of european, counter-terrorism policy and, above all, for maintaining the citizens' confidence in the democratic order of the eu. because by uncritically cooperating in that cia programme, the eu has grossly violated that trust. the unconditional use of diplomatic guarantees, the systematic invoking of state secrets and the way in which the intelligence services have assumed the role of judges are contrary to the values on which europe is built, namely the rule of law, human rights and respect for human dignity. my group has therefore been eager to see a strong text condemning these practices. it is only in this way that europe can maintain its moral authority in the world.
mr president, in its second report on this subject, the commission gives a positive assessment of the measures taken in all the member states to guarantee voluntary and unpaid donations of human tissues and cells. it does, however, point out very strongly the dramatic shortage facing a majority of the states. we therefore had to propose improvements and work on these two important and priority areas. i regret that, despite the efforts of the rapporteur, whom i thank here again - but she knows this - a majority of my colleagues do not believe that these intangible principles of free and anonymous treatment must be combined with humanism and humanity, quite simply, and that they must not undermine efforts to overcome this shortage and to meet the desperate expectations of so many patients in the european union, because this is the reality experienced on a daily basis by practitioners and patients, tens of thousands of whom are waiting for a cornea, bone marrow or skin transplant. the liberal and democratic group, which i represent here, which respects - i conclude, mr president - the freedom of research and is concerned about the expectations of these patients, will abstain tomorrow when voting on this resolution.
