madam president, ladies and gentlemen, we shall probably never know the number of deaths attributable to mediator, which is marketed as a treatment for diabetes and is often prescribed, in fact, as an appetite suppressant. in any case, this failure of a system has caused too many victims, at least in france, where it was necessary - as has already been said - to wait until 2009 to withdraw this drug from the market, six years after the same decision was taken in italy and spain. this scandal has obviously served as a catalyst for the revisions we are discussing tonight, revisions that are imperative in light of this widespread but risky practice of marketing over-the-counter medicines. the shortcomings of the legislation we passed in this very house just two years ago are clearly pointed out. the legislative framework is strengthened where necessary. yes, it is essential to require manufacturers to specify the reasons for withdrawing a medicinal product from the market or not renewing its authorization. yes, we must systematically raise the alarm, triggering the emergency procedure when doctors point out that taking the medicine causes side effects in their patients. now we have to be realistic if this health surveillance system is to work effectively. it is up to the national authorities and the european agency in london to monitor the commercial players in the sector more closely and to be more independent of them.
the joint discussion is closed. the vote will take place on tuesday, 11 september, at 12 noon. the commission shall be assisted by the committee of the regions.
marine fuel contains on average 2700 times more sulphur than diesel for trucks. ships therefore contribute up to 30% of sulphur dioxide emissions in the north sea countries. efforts we are making on land are increasingly threatened to be undone by rising shipping emissions. people with asthma and other lung diseases are particularly affected by high sulphur concentrations in the air, which account for some 50 000 premature deaths in the eu. estimated costs for this switchover (between €2.6 billion and €11 billion) are far outweighed by the healthcare cost savings of around €30 billion. north-west europe wins twice with this. the channel between britain and the north sea coast is one of the busiest shipping lanes in the world and because of our location we experience a lot of the negative impact of shipping emissions there. in addition, millions of europeans live in the immediate vicinity of the major ports. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's ports are not affected by the crisis. the need to use the more expensive low-sulphur fuels is thus less of a competitive disadvantage. at the same time, further efforts are needed to increase shore-side electricity in ports and to convert lng (liquid natural gas) to ships, which have a significantly lower environmental impact.
in writing. i welcome the vote on tuesday in favour of a european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) intervention for danish workers. almost €6.4 million will be spent on support and training programmes for the 550 danish workers made redundant at the odense steel shipyard and four of its suppliers and downstream producers. this sector had been hit hard by asian competition. the co-financing of local authorities, an obligation under the operation of the egf, will amount to eur 3.4 million.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. it is only right that we should make a financial contribution to the direct redundancies at odense steel shipyard. these redundancies represent 2% of the local workforce. the closure of the shipyard, which is added to the indirect job losses, is another economic, but also and above all social, tragedy.
in writing. the european parliament has given its approval to the european globalisation adjustment fund (egf) to help spanish workers. following the opinion of the european commission (june 2012) and that of the parliament's committee on budgets (july 2012), we approved these aids by a very large majority, totalling more than eur 7.7 million. at the end of 2011, spain applied for egf support for 320 redundancies in small construction companies in the region of aragon. the reason: the sector was particularly affected by the financial crisis and the subsequent recession. the region of aragon will contribute €700,000 to support programmes for new jobs. i'm glad to hear it.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. unemployment has risen dramatically in aragon. at the end of 2011, the number of unemployed registered with public employment agencies was approaching 100 000, of which 15% had been made redundant in the construction sector. in addition, this same region of aragon had already been hit hard by previous collective redundancies. it is also europe's role to support, wherever it can, the victims, who are still too many, of the crisis.
in writing. − with this new directive, we are taking a big step forward in achieving the europe 2020 climate targets. this is not only good for further reducing co2 emissions; the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the new energy efficiency legislation and will continue to monitor the implementation of the new energy efficiency legislation. better information on energy bills and initiatives by energy suppliers to save 1.5 per cent of energy each year will encourage us all to be more conscious about energy use. the member states will have to set the right example. 3% of central government buildings will need to be renovated annually to make them more energy efficient. this will certainly also give impetus to employment. the long-term strategies for the renovation of residential and commercial buildings to be drawn up by the member states will also contribute to this. this energy efficiency directive could have been more ambitious, i admit. it's a compromise. the job is definitely not done. but what is certain is that we are on the right track to a better environment, more jobs and less energy dependence.
in writing. on tuesday, we voted by a very large majority for a directive which should enable europe to improve its energy efficiency by around 15% by the end of the decade. the stakes were enormous! the text, which will have to be transposed into national law by the beginning of 2014, requires member states to renovate 3% of public buildings every year in order to achieve energy efficiency (note, however, that under pressure from several countries and given the current state of public funds in europe, municipal and regional administrative buildings which represent 12% of offices in europe will not be subject to this obligation). in contrast, european energy suppliers will have to take measures to reduce their customers' consumption by 1.5% each year between 2014 and 2020. the directive also requires member states to adopt national energy efficiency plans which will be submitted to the european commission. if these plans are not ambitious enough, the european executive will then be able to impose binding measures on them as early as 2014.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. we have fought on three main ideas in particular. all measures to reach 20% must be binding, unless all member states set themselves binding national targets equivalent to 20% and this will allow for more flexibility in the measures. 2. the all member states should develop a 2050 roadmap for buildings, addressing, inter alia, social housing and reducing energy poverty. 3. the all publicly owned buildings must undergo a 2.5% annual major renovation (representing energy savings of at least 70%).
in writing. i voted to waive immunity. the polish prosecutor had requested it following an alleged violation of the polish code of criminal procedure and the road traffic act in connection with a road traffic accident in 2011 in poland in which jarosław leszek wałęsa was involved and seriously injured. he himself, wishing to clarify the situation, had explained that he wanted to see his immunity lifted.
in writing. we voted for the waiver of the immunity of mrs birgit collin-langen. the main prosecutor requested it in connection with the proceedings concerning an alleged offence. mrs collin-langen did not object to this, wishing to settle the matter as quickly as possible.
in writing. on the occasion of the painful 11th anniversary of the attacks on the twin towers in new york, the european parliament reviewed the question of the cia's transport and illegal detention of prisoners in certain european countries (particularly lithuania, poland and romania) and called on these member states to shed light on the cia's secret detention programmes on european soil, implemented in the wake of the events of 11 september 2001, and on the human rights violations committed in this context. in other words, it is to investigate the possible existence of secret prisons or facilities on their territory where people have been detained under the cia's secret rendition program. it is clear that there are still areas of shadow. the resolution thus adopted calls on the commission to create, by the end of 2012, a framework to shed light on these actions.
in writing. we voted in favour of this text. we have once again condemned the us-led cia programme of interstate transfers and secret detention. indeed, many human rights violations have been reported, including cases of illegal and arbitrary detention, torture and other ill-treatment, violations of the principle of non-refoulement and enforced disappearances.
in writing. we voted on tuesday in favour of a non-legislative resolution on solidarity between member states in the field of asylum. the resolution deals with future arrangements for the resettlement of refugees in the eu and with the strengthening of the eu's asylum policy. in particular, we called on the european commission to include in its next legislative proposal introducing a permanent intra-eu resettlement system a european distribution key for the resettlement of persons enjoying international protection. the system would take the best account of the interests of the beneficiaries, the prospects for integration and objective indicators such as gdp, population and area of the member states. this european distribution key could help eu countries whose national asylum systems are under disproportionate pressure or are in a state of emergency. we also asked the commission to study the feasibility of developing a european system for distributing asylum seekers, as well as more financial solidarity in this sensitive area and more resources for the european asylum support office in malta.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. solidarity is an essential element, see the essential element of the csr. it is a fundamental principle of union law that member states should share both the benefits and the burdens in a fair and equitable way. without solidarity, europe has no meaning. but solidarity must go hand in hand with responsibility, and member states must ensure that their asylum systems are able to meet the standards set by international and european law. supporting the implementation of asylum procedures in a spirit of solidarity and shared responsibility should be seen as a means of helping member states to fulfil their obligation to provide international protection to those in need, as well as assistance to third countries hosting the largest number of refugees, with the aim of strengthening the common area of protection in general.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. the standardisation regulation adopted enriches the current legislative framework and provides a valuable tool for businesses to achieve harmonisation of the internal market. the definition of clear rules, which have the same value in all the countries of the union, will enable our market to share common production criteria. the benefits of simplification will increase two factors necessary for the development of our free market area: the competitiveness of enterprises and the protection of consumers. the combination of these two elements is essential for the harmonization of the european production system from which social services for citizens must be excluded. on the one hand, common standards improve the market, on the other hand, standards applicable to consumer goods must not be allowed to apply to services for citizens. it is for the states to lay down the criteria for the organisation, management and financing of social security and health services; the standardization process cannot be the means to meet the basic needs of citizens. this limitation is a victory for the s&d group; together we have safeguarded the right and access of citizens to services.
in writing. i voted against this text. as food safety is a major concern of european citizens, member states should be required to modernise their identification and traceability measures in accordance with a defined timetable. giving consumers more information about what they are consuming will increase consumer confidence. however, amendment no 46 incorporated by the epp, alde and ecr which practically abolishes the 'optional labelling of cattle' is totally unacceptable and does not allow me to vote in favour of this text.
in writing. we have approved by a large majority the amendments to the european pharmacovigilance legislation. the amendments are intended to address the shortcomings of the new regulation and the new directive (which entered into force in july 2012), following the infamous 'mediator' scandal... the amendments to the european texts which are of paramount importance for public health will strengthen the european system for detecting and assessing potential problems with medicines in all eu member states. the new rules, which were the result of a co-decision procedure with the council of ministers, will enter into force in 2013.
in writing. we have approved by a large majority the amendments to the european pharmacovigilance legislation. the amendments are intended to address the shortcomings of the new regulation and the new directive (which entered into force in july 2012), following the infamous 'mediator' scandal... the amendments to the european texts which are of paramount importance for public health will strengthen the european system for detecting and assessing potential problems with medicines in all eu member states. the new rules, which were the result of a co-decision procedure with the council of ministers, will enter into force in 2013.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. three points in particular caught my attention. the introduction of an automatic emergency procedure, the creation of a mechanism to trigger this procedure and, finally, the clarification of the transparency obligations of companies (which will, for example, avoid taking citizens for less than what they are as laboratorio servier did by not renewing its authorization for the mediator in spain for commercial reasons).
in writing. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. we have indeed approved by a large majority (606 votes in favour, 55 against and 13 abstentions) the provisional agreement reached with the council on the proposal to reduce particulate sulphur emissions from ships (directive 93/12/eec). according to this text, the maximum sulphur content of marine diesel used in the designated sulphur control areas (csecs) (baltic sea, north sea and english channel) will be lowered from 1,5% to 1% by 31 december 2014 and to 0,1% as from 1 january 2015. the maximum content of 0.5% will be mandatory in all eu seas by 2020 and will also apply to all passenger ships operating outside the cezs, etc. state aid may be granted to avoid the negative impact on the competitiveness of the sector of the investment costs inherent in these new requirements. i'm glad you did!
in writing. - there are no european limit values for maritime transport yet, unlike other modes of transport such as rail, road and air. i therefore welcome the fact that a regulation is now to be created. however, it would have been desirable to have a uniform regulation for the whole of europe rather than setting more stringent sulphur limits for ships operating in seca (sulphur emission control areas). these only concern the north and baltic seas and the english channel. if different limits are set internationally, this could lead to a shift in traffic from water to road. sulphur-free fuel is becoming expensive and this would make road freight transport more attractive. i therefore welcome the provision of state financial support for the shipping industry to counteract a possible shift from sea to land transport. this scheme is an implementation of the international marpol convention. it is important that international standards are set for modes of transport which have a strong international dimension, such as maritime transport. however, it is not understandable that member states agree to a regulation at international level first and then later try to block that regulation at european level.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. the implementation of the limit values for the sulphur content of marine fuels as agreed by the international maritime organization is important. i'll give you a number: 50,000. the commission has estimated that 50,000 premature deaths in europe are caused by air pollutants emitted from ships. the text was therefore eagerly awaited.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. then we'll have the debate on plantation rights. a high-level group set up by the commissioner for agriculture is working to find a compromise. the legislators are considering changes to the common organisation of the market (cmo), which for years has allowed government intervention to support and stabilise viticulture. the question then becomes whether the lifting of restrictions could weaken a sector already struggling with cheap imports, overproduction and falling demand. the share of land devoted to viticulture in europe fell by 12% between 2001 and 2011, according to figures from the international organisation of vine and wine.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. the socialists as a whole accepted the commission's proposal to establish common rules for the operation of imi. improving cross-border exchanges of information between different authorities in the member states is essential in our efforts to complete the single market. imi has proved to be a reliable and effective tool in the two areas in which it is already operational, namely the vocational qualifications directive and the services directive.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. the directive should be amended to further restrict the possibilities of unintentional non-taxation, double taxation, tax fraud or evasion within the member states, which cause more damage to the proper functioning of the internal market than those mentioned in the recently adopted resolution on the call for concrete ways to combat tax fraud and evasion.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environment from harmful substances. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover all the activities of the european union. the commission should 'fully participate' in the european summits in october and december, which are to draw up a roadmap for the consolidation of economic and monetary union, meps said in a resolution adopted with a show of hands. the aim is to create a financial and fiscal framework and to guarantee greater democratic legitimacy. to achieve this, we ask the commission to publish, as an immediate measure, a legislative proposal under the ordinary legislative procedure, which fully involves parliament and ensures transparency. this first proposal should make it possible to set up a real banking union with more powers and responsibilities for the ecb.
in writing. on tuesday, we adopted a non-binding resolution on tissue and cell donation in plenary. the text of the resolution calls on the member states to prohibit financial incentives for tissue and cell donation and to define clearly the conditions under which financial compensation may be granted. the european commission is invited to propose a text to guarantee the principle of unpaid donations. the resolution also stresses the importance of bilateral agreements, which help countries to fill the shortage of tissues and cells and to find a compatible donor when there is no one at national level, and the benefit for eu countries of working more closely with eurocet, which manages the european tissue and cell register. the majority of meps were also concerned about the emergence of the black market in sperm and eggs, which is why we also called on the member states to take coordinated action to combat the associated ethical and public health risks!
in writing. the alde group abstained on the yannakoudakis resolution on human tissue and cell donation, which deals in particular with the aspects of free of charge and anonymity. while the european commission stressed in the debate the balance between respect for its fundamental principles and the availability of cell and tissue products, the european parliament added obstacles to an already complicated situation: 14 member states report regular shortages, especially for bone marrow transplants and gamete donations. there is no reason, as the resolution states, for there to be european requirements for private tissue and cell banks alone. the same applies to the desire of the conservative forces in the european parliament to amend the existing legislation on innovative therapeutic medicines: the pro-life movements are trying to curb a whole range of research, from tissue engineering (skin, heart valves) to somatic cell therapy (used mainly in cancer). as rapporteur for the liberals, i regret that the plenary has rejected a humanistic and open approach to medical research. the only one that would meet the expectations of european patients.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. donations must be voluntary and made without profit in a transparent environment. they are exclusively for tissues and cells that fall into one of the following three categories: tissues of different origins, reproductive cells and non-reproductive cells. except for skin tissue, blood vessels, and amniotic membrane, donations usually come from a deceased donor.
in writing. i voted in favour of this own-initiative report. as the member responsible for gender equality, i share many of the questions raised in this text. for example, does the commission's communication to the eu institutions and committees, entitled 'rio +20: towards a green economy and better governance', not include a gender dimension? i also wonder about the lack of figures on the presence of women in the green economy sector in some european countries, including a delta with previous years, as well as the lack of a clear common definition of what the green economy is for the european commission?
in writing. i have of course voted in favour of this own-initiative report. as a member of parliament responsible for equality between men and women and, moreover, as a socialist, this is a matter which is close to my heart. in the european union in 2010, employment in the services sector represented 69.4% of total employment, while this share was 25.4% in industry and 5.2% in agriculture. the majority of the female labour force is employed in the service sector: 83.1% compared with 58.1% of the male labour force. within the union, women earn 17.1% less than men, although the pay gap between men and women varies between 3.2% and 30.9% in the member states. these are just a few of the statistics that highlight the need for a change in thinking.
in writing. in the resolution we voted today, we drew attention to the social and economic consequences of school drop-outs. indeed, beyond the current crisis, the economic cost of rising unemployment and the difficulties of young people entering the labour market will clearly have negative effects in the long term! this is why we called on member states to develop and finance specific lifelong learning programmes, in line with labour market requirements, and strategies to reduce school drop-out, increase graduation rates and increase student mobility. member countries must target investment of at least 2% of gdp in higher education and 3% for research if they are to be able to compete with the new global economic powers. in order to ensure equal access to higher education, we also asked that member states give greater consideration to the idea of small, easily accessible scholarships awarded at the pre-university stage.
in writing. − education and training are a cornerstone of the europe 2020 goals. the existing disparities in education and training systems need to be addressed and member states need to step up the development of effective lifelong learning strategies. in addition, these strategies should be budgeted, both at national level and within the medium-term financial framework. education has a significant impact and affects all age groups: language skills and multilingualism should be particularly encouraged at a young age. they not only increase access to the labour market later on, but also educational opportunities and contribute to cultural exchange. a comprehensive language provision in schools is an example of achieving this objective. particular attention should be paid to young people, as unemployment in the eu has risen to over 20% and in some member states is as high as 50%. i therefore support the call to reduce school dropout rates, to increase the number of training and continuing training opportunities and to make it easier for young people to enter the workforce. access to learning needs to be extended, for example by promoting exchange programmes between pupils, students and teachers. but also the possibility of learning in old age should be provided, in the spirit of solidarity between generations, in order to promote lifelong learning. (parliament adopted the legislative resolution)
in writing. i voted in favour of this own-initiative report. it draws attention to gaps which need to be taken into account, such as early childhood development, the decisive importance of language learning, consideration for the elderly, etc.
in writing. on tuesday, in plenary, we called for better access to audiovisual works available online (films, music or streaming), particularly outside the national territory and language limitations. while the cultural and creative sector represents a huge economic potential (around 2,5% of the union's gdp), the main challenge for the online distribution of audiovisual works remains the accessibility for users and the attractiveness of lawful audiovisual content. illegal supply is rampant and hinders investment in the sector. the text adopted therefore stresses the importance of a more diversified legal offer and supports more flexible operating slots. we have therefore proposed the systematic use of the isan standard for the identification of audiovisual works and a reduced rate of vat for their online distribution in all member states.
in writing. i support this report, which has the laudable intention of proposing the creation of opportunities and means for the dissemination of european films. it is important that the european union should not be separated from the channels of education and culture. by preserving this, we will be doing a service to future generations of young europeans.
the question is "blue cardboard". madam president, i do not wish to ask a question, but rather to give my testimony. i just want to say that mrs ashton and her services have worked - i have witnessed this - to free the two swedish journalists. i myself went to ethiopia several weeks ago at the request of mrs ashton and with her information to get the intervention to free these two journalists. so i went, with the full agreement and support of mrs ashton, to see prime minister meles. i met with the prisoners. the swedish foreign minister also went to great lengths. i want to testify here to mrs asthon's action to free these prisoners. i think if we hadn't set this dynamic in motion, in complete secrecy, the prisoners might not have been released today. i wanted to say this because i know your passion for ethiopia, and i wanted that truth to be restored. so don't tell mrs ashton that she didn't do anything. she did a lot and we supported her.
madam president, madam high representative, ladies and gentlemen, the debate as it has unfolded so far shows clearly, madam high representative, the impossibility of your task. on the one hand, you are asked to set priorities and focus on priority actions, and on the other hand, you are asked about everything that is happening around the world, asking you to do whatever you can to improve the situation. however, your task is also exciting, and we support you because together - but above all you and your team, who play the lion's share - we are laying the foundations for a completely new common policy: the common security and defence policy of the european union. i am one of those who would, of course, like the whole world to have seen this already, and i think it is gradually coming to light. that said, i have had a rather unfortunate experience, as have my colleagues who were also present last week in charlotte, north carolina, at the democratic convention: not once was the european union mentioned either during the entire convention or during the international leaders forum programme. mr brok writes in his report that the united states is our main strategic partner and that is certainly true , but still not once has it been mentioned about us. i conclude that we have a tremendous amount of work to do.
the day before yesterday, the south african farmer johan fourie and his wife cecile were murdered in appalling circumstances. the police said it was one of the most gruesome crime scenes they've ever seen. the european media are not paying attention to this, but johan and cecile fourie are not just any one case. since the anc came to power, more than 3 000 farmers have been killed in south africa. there's a pattern to these killings. they're usually accompanied by hours of torture. usually, there's not much that gets stolen. it has nothing to do with poverty. these people are just being killed for who they are. it is a political problem, because there are important forces in and around the anc that are working in the hands of these murder parties. the government and the police are doing very little. the land reform that is under way threatens to aggravate the situation even further. so it takes more than a letter to get things done. there must be sanctions and there must be measures.

on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, i would like to thank president barroso very much for his message, because he has recognised – and this was a change from the debates last month and last year – that we are still in the middle of a deep crisis and we have to recognise that when we discuss the state of the union today. but what we are seeing today is more of a deep political crisis. everybody is talking about public finances, about interest rates, about economics. in my opinion it is not about greece, it is not about the public finances, it is not even about economics as being the fallout of the crisis. the real nature of this crisis is a political one and that is the political incapacity today to make the jump forward to a federal union and, more precisely, the incapacity of the national elites of europe to make this jump to a more federal union in europe. that is the real crisis we are going through today. i am saying that, mr barroso, because you have made a whole speech, but at the end of the speech you come up with a concept and that concept we cannot accept: a federation of nation states. no, no federation of nation states, that is more of the same, we have that already – that is the european council, which is a federation of nation states where the heads of government and the heads of state were trying to solve and are incapable of solving this crisis. we do not want more of the same – we do not need a nationalistic or a national future for europe. we need a post-national future for europe. that is what we need. what we need for europe is not a federation of nation states: it is a federal union of european citizens. it is about citizens and it is not about nation states in the future.
on behalf of the alde group. this is the first good news of the day! perhaps mr barroso will say: yes, i am actually going to transform the federation of nation states into a federal union. so we'll have unanimity in the house today! mr president, even after the decision of the constitutional court i am always a little bit critical when we talk about the constitutional court, as if there were no constitutional courts in other eu countries for the moment, but maybe in the future we can give them as much attention as we have given to karlsruhe there are still three remarks to be made. first, it is not the european central bank that will solve the crisis. the european central bank can help; the european bank has done the unavoidable in that for the third time already after trichet with smp, after the ltro draghi has bought us time again. but this is a stopgap solution; this is not a structural solution to this crisis and it is a mistake to think in this house that, if the ecb comes in, then our problems are over. no, our problems are only over if we have the courage to create the federal union that is so desperately needed. do not think that this new measure of draghi can work for more than five or six months. the crisis will come back if we do not assume responsibility that is my second remark today, dear colleagues. we all know what the solution is. we all know - besides a number of individuals on the other side of the plenary - that we need an economic union, we need a political union, we need a fiscal union. we all know that is the solution. i find it unthinkable that we are still waiting to come forward with proposals and what are we doing, in fact? waiting for the outcome of the german elections or something like that, because that is the political reality for the moment. i stress that i think it is very good, mr barroso, that today you have introduced a proposal for a single financial supervisor, which is one of the three building blocks for a banking union. but what i do not understand is why the commission is not also taking the initiative on the other building blocks that are needed to solve this crisis. we desperately need a resolution mechanism for the banks. the establishment of an economic government and a treasury in europe: we need it desperately, if we want to solve the crisis. the mutualisation of debt by the establishment of redemption funds: even the chief of the german sparkasse last week said and he is the former minister of finance in bavaria, a good member of the csu that it is absolutely necessary that we have a redemption fund if we want to solve this crisis. furthermore, he, the chief of the whole sparkasse in germany, has asked the european commission to take the initiative in that regard. so my question to you, mr barroso, is why these two standards? if the european council in june was asking you to come forward with a proposal for a banking union within two months and that is a good thing why is mr barnier not putting forward a proposal for a banking union? if this parliament has already asked the commission four times, in the two-pack and in the resolutions, to come forward with a redemption fund, which is a real structural solution to this crisis, why do we receive nothing at all? why is the european council more important than the european parliament when you come forward with your own initiatives? why? why? (applause) i believe that we are in a balanced union. you have the council and you have parliament. if parliament requests that you come forward with a package of legislative proposals to end this crisis, it is your obligation and duty to do that. we are still waiting. we are still waiting for, i don't know what, perhaps the green light from berlin or from paris before you take action in that regard. that brings me finally to my third point, the proposal that we have on the table today, which is the creation, the establishment of a single financial supervisory authority by giving more powers to the european central bank. i have to tell you, mr barroso, i think it is a good idea that we have that, because we need a single supervisor. but i have some question marks on the model that we are choosing today because, let's be honest, what we are doing is mainly copying the banque de france. in most of our countries - in britain it is the case, in belgium, in the netherlands and in germany too - you have a split between a monetary authority on the one hand and financial supervision of banks on the other. i cannot understand why we cannot merge the three institutions that we have today into what we call a financial supervisory agency or authority, so that we do not mix a financial supervision task with a monetary authority. i see a number of possible conflicts today: euro area banks versus non-euro area banks; banks versus other financial institutions. i see a problem between the ecb and the eba. the eba is creating the roadmap and the ecb is applying the roadmap. i also see a problem on democratic accountability, because democratic accountability is not compatible with the independence of the bank. so my proposal to you, mr barroso, is to look further into that question and also to change the legal basis. my proposal and suggestion to you and mr barnier is not only to use article 127 as a legal basis, but also article 114, so that we have full co-decision with parliament on such an important matter in the future. so i conclude, mr president, but i have a little more time as you interrupted me in the beginning of my speech with good news, so there is no criticism from my side. i think it is absolutely necessary now, mr barroso, that not only do we hear a good speech from you but that you put forward a whole package of legislative proposals in the way parliament wants. because do not forget that your legitimacy comes from this house, from this parliament, and it is also this house that can take it back. on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, firstly, while to start with mr barroso was initially mr farage’s punching ball, is mr farage now becoming mr barroso’s punching ball? i would see that as a positive development in this plenary. i shall now come back to the main discussion we had an hour ago. i think – and my group thinks – that the commission should make more use of its right of initiative on a number of topics and on a number of issues. i would advise the commission not to wait for the green light – or red light – from paris, berlin or other capitals. the red light is always there but not the green light. it needs to do what is necessary and put its legislative proposals on the table. the effect on the market would be so positive that it would be far more difficult for these countries to react negatively. my third point concerns banking union. mr barroso has said that he does not want a delay. i do not want a delay either. but that does not mean that, if he does not agree with a proposal that has the support of the council – and mainly of germany in this instance – that we cannot discuss it. just because germany considers that the ecb should do a given job we do not have to agree with it. i am sorry, but that is not codecision, is it? codecision means that for a short period – and you can be sure that our coordinators will respect that and will examine the proposal – you can suggest other, better possibilities, rather than creating double standards. finally, in germany they need to be serious. they cannot at the same time give tasks other than monetary functions to the ecb and criticise mr draghi for the interventions he is currently making in the markets. it is one or the other: either it is independent – in which case we have no financial supervision – or it is not, in which case they can continue to criticise mr draghi, as they have been doing in recent weeks.
in writing. i voted in favour of this directive to ensure that victims of violent robbery, assault, rape, harassment, terrorist attack or trafficking in human beings have basic rights throughout the union. each year, nearly one in six europeans is a victim of a serious crime. yet, the treatment and protection of victims varied greatly from one country to another. it was time to address this real legal and human gap. in concrete terms, whether a person is the victim of a road accident in the south of france, of a violent robbery in the streets of milan, london or berlin, or of an assault (violent robbery, burglary, rape, harassment, hate crime, terrorist attack or human trafficking), every victim will have the same threshold of protection. we also included a guarantee to provide assistance to the victim when informed of the offender's release; the painful news of the belgian judicial system in september underlines even more that support for victims is also paramount at this time...
in writing. i voted in favour of this agreement. it is only the logical follow-up to the first mutual recognition agreement concluded with new zealand. the aim is to simplify the operation of the mutual recognition agreement, which entered into force on 1 january 1999. the text, for example, removes technical barriers to trade and specifies the objectives of each party in the areas of health, safety and the environment.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. it is important that the union should have an appropriate legal instrument to respond to the lack of cooperation of states which persist in violating the quota rules, for example, and thus endanger our future. highly dissuasive sanctions should also be provided for against offenders.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. as the member responsible for consumer protection, i thought it important to emphasize the aspect of labelling. consumers have the right to better and more intelligible information on the fishery products they buy in order to make informed choices. however, the frozen products concerned should be exempted from this provision as they already bear the indication of the date of freezing (or the date of first freezing) and the indication 'preferably consumed before ...' to avoid the use of dates which would be confusing for the consumer. labels should also contain information on easily identifiable fishing areas to which the consumer could refer, as opposed to the labelling areas currently used. fish species should also be identified by means of locally familiar names on all labels. consumers should be able to know with certainty which fishery products have been frozen and thawed, in particular in the case of so-called 'fresh' fishery products.
in writing. − while this report shows progress in the field of foreign and security policy, it also gives a clear and unmistakable picture of our lack of capacity to speak with one voice. the structural weakness of europe is of course reinforced by the desire of some member states not to surrender their sovereignty. the fact that this has not strengthened any country but has weakened europe is something we must recognise in many areas of policy, including the strengthening of the democratic voice of citizens in many countries and the demand for social or environmental standards. this inability has the additional consequence that we impose rules on ourselves in important policy areas, but we expose ourselves to unfair competition from countries and continents which do not respect these rules.

in writing. i voted in favour of this own-initiative report. indeed, it is worth recalling that the previous cfp did not achieve some of its main objectives: many stocks remain overexploited, the economic situation of some segments of the european fleet remains fragile despite subsidies, jobs in the fisheries sector are being lost and are not attractive, especially for young people entering the sector, and the situation of many coastal communities dependent on fishing and aquaculture remains precarious. the primary objective is to ensure the supply of fish stocks and the development of coastal communities, by promoting employment and improving the working conditions of fishermen. i would just like to point out that if we put the sustainability of resources on the back burner and if we do not allow the stocks that have been overfished to be replenished, for example, we will soon be past the point of no return.
the commission is really putting its own spin on the proposals you have announced today, commissioner, and that is a triple positive. that is a good thing because it is now clear that, after the european council, the commission has now reached the same conclusions as parliament in 2010, namely that we need a banking union. secondly, it is a positive thing because the creation of a european supervisory mechanism offers us the prospect of breaking the vicious circle between the banking problems and the problems of national public debt, and therefore the taxpayers. it is positive for a third reason: concrete policy processes are now under way. it is only by taking concrete and decisive decisions and by taking serious and concrete steps forward that we will be able to regain the confidence of the people and the markets. we shall now have to discuss this in detail. it is a proposal which demands a great deal of attention, because it is a very radical proposal. we shall certainly have the opportunity to return to this later. but i would like to say this: we have been concerned in parliament at one time about the democratic dimension of the problem and about - as you yourself have mentioned - the legal basis and the implications of this for the role that parliament can play in this important matter. i am glad that we are not going to go down the road of choosing another legal basis: that would have cost us at least another six months, a lot of time, time we can't afford. so i am pleased that you have announced - as mr barroso said this morning - that we should consider these two proposals together. we hope that this will be the case and that there will be agreement with the council on this. that is the best guarantee of quality supervision, the quality supervision needed to ensure that banks can once again play the role they are supposed to play, namely to serve the real economy and to create growth and jobs. i hope to cooperate constructively with everyone.

the rapporteur. - mr president, friends of culture, good taste and venture capital, good evening. it is a good feeling to be among friends in this intimate place, the strasbourg chamber, on this summer evening. thank you for being here, a large audience came to applaud the performance of the two rapporteurs! the risk capital dossier is important because god knows if in europe today we need investment. our economy has been too much absorbed over the last thirty years, both by the public and private sectors, mainly by consumption and speculation to the detriment of investment, which creates value. in this perspective, venture capital plays an important role, especially in an innovation dynamic. we have to admit that in europe we have a dynamic of innovation that is probably excessively driven by regulation, so innovation from the top down, where it is targets set, for example, by the european commission or by member states, that drive innovation, as opposed to the american model, which is more of a bottom up innovation model, where it is people who risk both their money and their reputation in innovations that do not all succeed, far from it. but here's a model that we would do well to take inspiration from, not just to adopt it, but to have a better balance between top-down innovation, driven by regulation, bottom-up innovation, driven by investment, and innovation, i would say, autonomous of the market and the actors on the ground. encouraging venture capital is not easy, because it is obviously a matter of culture. if we in europe have a culture that is a little allergic to risk, it is clear that we will not change it overnight. however, anything that can be done to remove regulatory barriers or, on the contrary, to put in place mechanisms that encourage venture capital is a good thing to do. in this context, the commission's proposal to create a european passport for venture capital funds, which is in some ways a guarantee of quality and also a guarantee of access to the entire internal market, is a good idea. from this point of view, the case should have been simple and it was. the negotiations were quite quick. in the end, we didn't have a lot of disagreements. one of the questions was whether or not a depository system was necessary in the spirit of consumer protection. we, the rapporteur and the fictitious rapporteur, have decided that perhaps venture capital is one of the areas where we want to encourage risk, with investors who know a little about the risks they take, and that therefore consumer protection concerns, from the moment we turn to professionals, can take a back seat to efficiency considerations. we therefore left the depositary system aside and quite quickly finalized an agreement on 28 june last - an agreement which was welcomed by the three partners, parliament, the council and the commission. i am grateful to the commission for helping us to reach an agreement fairly quickly. then, what was my surprise to discover, five days or seven days later, i don't know - sophie, you will remind me - almost through public rumour, that the council was denying its agreement. and i would like to say that, from a procedural point of view, this is unacceptable. we may realize that we've gone too far in a negotiation, that we've made too many concessions and all that, but an agreement is an agreement. when a deal is made, and it's announced and you're happy about it, well, you honor that deal. that is what we are going to do tomorrow when we vote on this text and i therefore ask parliament not to play the stupidest game with the council, that is to say to go back on its initial positions and pretend that we are going to start the negotiations again. no, we are committed to the process we have been through and to the agreement we have reached, and it is on these terms, and on these terms only, that we shall be voting tomorrow. i would therefore urge you to support us in this and i hope to hear a lot of encouragement from the many in the audience.
on behalf of the s&d group, i would like to begin by thanking the rapporteurs, mrs auconie and mr lamberts, for the good cooperation we have had over the past few months. we, as the s&d group, can support what is on the table as a good compromise. we have heard this morning in mr barroso's state of the union that we must do everything possible to promote economic growth. we are of course in full agreement with that. investing in innovative companies through venture capital and also in social economy projects through those social investment funds can make a difference. the issue of such passports is a modest but effective contribution to that end. it will create a framework which can give investors confidence and help - as you yourself have said, commissioner - to make more use of the savings in the real economy. this leads to investment in the innovative start-ups and in the social economy. the text before us is a step forward on several points. there will be a passport for the funds and better protection for investors. it is a balanced package which came after difficult negotiations with the council. we were therefore very surprised and disappointed that the agreement reached, which was even officially communicated by the previous danish presidency, could not be concluded in time by the cypriot presidency. this allowed a number of countries to organise and blocked the political agreement. this situation means that although we will be voting on the agreement reached tomorrow, we will not be able to hold a final vote. i would say to the commissioner that we appreciate the commission coming forward with proposals to bring about a compromise, but frankly i think the ball is in the council's court. we have accepted the deal we have made in all honesty and we are prepared to approve it. i think the council must also be consistent. the point of no return is more topical than ever and goes far beyond these two proposals. the question is: we are not talking about a tax haven, but about a tax haven. is the fight against tax evasion at european level merely a verbal battle, or are we really taking a strong stance? we as a parliament must keep up with that.
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, i should first of all address the council, but in such matters it is never there, so it is quite difficult to negotiate in this way, of course. i will therefore address you to say that we must not distort reality altogether. we have an agreement. it is not that there is a small obstacle to an agreement; we have an agreement. simply, one of the partners is not living up to the word he gave publicly. there is therefore a real problem of trust between the negotiating partners. i'm sorry, these are things that are not done. they may have realized afterwards that they had gone too far, that they had made too many concessions, but that was their business. it was their job to prepare and conduct their negotiations properly. if they are not capable of doing so, woe to them! that's how it is in business! if you get scammed for bad negotiation, that's the price you pay! the council has too often disregarded this house to be able to pass the torch by saying to it: 'we understand that you have made a bit of a mess, so we will discuss it again'. no, you can't! i am in favour of a first reading agreement, well it is very simple: if we want a first reading agreement, we keep our word, they keep theirs, and that's it, end of story! if they want to do anything else, it's going to be expensive, it's going to be hard and it could take a long time! the aifm clause is a paper tiger! today, according to the oecd, there are no longer any jurisdictions on their blacklist. you are in liechtenstein? you are clean! you're in the cayman islands? you are clean! you're clean all over the world! there is no more tax haven. we don't have to fight tax havens anymore. it is to avoid this situation that we want to include in an article - and not just in recitals - some rather serious standards on tax havens. stop selling us wind just to please us, saying "look, we work well against tax havens". this kind of cinema is over! (applause) i would like to thank
the report before us is indeed a nail-biter, and it says in the very first paragraphs, and i quote: 'we stress that the european institutions must respect the principle of subsidiarity in the legislative process'. this is my most basic comment on the subject. what we need, therefore, is not primarily the new european treaty announced by mr barroso yesterday. no, what we really need is a thorough review of where and how european policy is really useful and really necessary and where it is not useful or necessary at all. we must also re-examine how many european institutions, how many european administrations and european rules are really useful and necessary and how many are not useful or necessary. i believe that this is not only a very big saving for the taxpayers, but also a real respect for the principle of subsidiarity, much more respect than the 'courier am symptom' to which we seem doomed. unfortunately, in reality we are very far from the union of nation states that president barroso preached yesterday. one more thing. there is a bit of a celebration going on about the so-called yellow card procedure. let's face it, she's barely been used once. it is little more than a towel to bleed on, a voice for national parliaments which is hardly a voice in reality.
mr president, we are in the same situation, sophie auconie and i, regarding this report. i just want draw your attention to the fact that this is a quite exceptional case where, after successfully concluding the trialogues, with an agreement openly announced by the commission, parliament and the council presidency in the person of the prime minister of denmark, the council has decided to renege on the agreement. what we are going to vote on today is the text as agreed with the council. we are urging the council to stand by their word and to adopt that text, with the wording we agreed at the end of june. as we still want to give some leeway for discussion with them – but just discussion – we will not take the final vote. at least that is what i will propose to you; but we intend to stay firm on this matter. i might add that the reason why the council has reneged on this agreement is in order to prevent us fighting all too effectively against tax havens – which is really a disgrace.

rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on transport and tourism. − mr president, i am speaking on behalf of the transport committee, which provided an opinion for the budget committee. the committee on transport and tourism has underlined the importance of investments in transport. investing in transport is important, especially in compliance with the europe 2020 strategy for growth and jobs. it creates economic growth and improves territorial cohesion, but it also helps in achieving safety targets. the transport committee carefully assessed the relevant budget lines and set its political priorities very clearly. on the one hand we have investment supporting economic growth and job creation, which are essential for the committee. it therefore voted in favour of a higher budget than that proposed by the commission for the ten-t programme. unfortunately this was rejected by the budget committee, which supported the commission’s draft proposal. on the other hand, the transport committee also identified budgetary lines where it was possible to make savings. it therefore supported the council’s cuts on two budgetary lines concerning administrative expenditures. in terms of the rest of the transport budget, the committee supports the commission’s draft proposal, and it clearly feels that investment in transport infrastructure is necessary for the correct functioning and achievement of the single market within europe.
in writing. it seems to me essential, in the context of the current discussions on the eu budget and its 2014-2020 budgetary framework, to take into account the efforts made by all europeans to overcome the economic crisis we are experiencing. it is important to give meps, the only directly elected members of the eu, the right to decide for themselves the arrangements for their place of work. it seems to me even more logical to grant this right when a decision on a single seat would, according to the latest estimates, reduce the cost of running the ep by eur 200 million annually. beyond this saving, the cessation of the monthly shuttle service between brussels and strasbourg would reduce the ecological footprint of our assembly by 20 000 tonnes of co2 per year. i therefore welcome the vote today, in which a large majority of members call on the member states to reconsider the question of the seat and places of work of parliament at the next revision of the treaty by amending protocol 6. the vote today once again gives a clear signal of the willingness of meps to participate fully in the budgetary and environmental efforts of its member states.
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, let me first welcome the decision of the european council on the banking union. nevertheless, i have to tell you that i thought it had already been decided in june – but better a european council that decides the same thing twice than nothing at all. i am also glad to see that the council finally agrees with the position of the european parliament and that of the european commission. we said from the beginning that this system has to be applicable to all banks, and it was an absurd idea to supervise only some banks. my second remark is also what our colleague mr swoboda said on the timetable. i have to tell you that i am less positive, because this timetable that they agreed on is firmly linked to – what? to the electoral calendar of germany. i find it irresponsible that we first of all have to wait nine months before we can start to implement something that we will approve here through legislation by the end of the year. nine months to wait for what? for the electoral calendar and the outcome of the elections in germany. this is the intergovernmental method; if every time you have to wait for the electoral calendar of france, then italy and now germany, you will do nothing. my second point concerns the paper. i think there are many good ideas in the paper, and there are also ideas with which i have some difficulties: the idea of individual contracts, for example. i do not think that our european union is what i would call a private law concern, based on contracts between the member states and the european institutions. no: we want common policies and binding legislation. my second point on the paper is on mutualisation of debt. may i ask you, mr barroso – you know, i will do it every time – every month i will come back to you and ask you: where is your proposal on the redemption fund and on the start of a eurobond market? because you know that it is the only way that interest rates can go down in the european union. take, for example, slovenia. slovenia is a country with less than 60 % public debt; at the same time it has a fiscal deficit of more or less 3 %, and it has an interest rate of 6 %, because it is a small market and nobody is interested today, with this crisis, in buying slovenian bonds. the eurobond market is the only way to solve it. i can tell you i have talked about the necessity to approve the two-pack, while parliament’s position is clear: if there is no proposal on the redemption fund on the table, we will not approve the two-pack. it is the same for mr schäuble’s proposal. mr schäuble can say, ‘i want the super-commissioner for economic and monetary affairs’. we will not go in that direction – even if i am in favour of that proposal – if there is no proposal on neutralisation of the debt as a first step towards the redemption fund or a first step towards a eurobond market. we are firm on this within parliament because we know that it is the only way to end this crisis, besides all the other measures which need to be taken. finally, something completely different, mr president: syria. it is time for the european council to do something, for the nobel peace prize winner to do something. we all know what is needed in syria. we need, as fast as possible, a no-fly-zone so that assad can no longer do what he is doing now: using his air force to massacre his people. aleppo, dear colleagues, is becoming more and more like dresden. daily bombardments, shelling by the syrian air force and a whole city in ruins. i find it a scandal that neither the european council nor the other european institutions are taking their responsibility regarding that scandal together with the international community.

blue-card answer. – mr president, the only point i wanted to make is that while i do not wish to interfere in the internal affairs of spain, i do not approve of a remark made by a vice-president of this parliament suggesting that the army should be sent to a province. the matter should be left to the voters and not to the army. we know the history of the guardia civil, and the señor knows about it better than i do! so let him please talk about it, and let us talk about democracy and let the catalonians decide themselves.
in recent years a great many measures have been taken to deal with the multiple crises. standard and non-standard measures. measures within the treaty and measures outside the treaty. but confidence has not yet been restored, and it is precisely that confidence which is needed to restore a financial and economic climate conducive to economic growth and employment. complex structural problems naturally require structural solutions, in the member states as well as in the union. and that is why we are anxiously awaiting the final road map towards a close economic and monetary union, which you, mr van rompuy, are working out together with the presidents of the other institutions, and which will remove the weaving of the economic and monetary union. it must be an ambitious plan, one that is carried out without delay, because the crisis does not wait for politics. that is why we in the european parliament are not sitting still. we voted on my report in the committee on economic and monetary affairs in brussels last week, on the building blocks of the road map. as rapporteur, i would of course recommend that you read it. we are speaking about the four building blocks, but we are adding a fifth: a social pact to remember what it's really all about. as regards the banking union, mr president, we are making substantial amendments to the proposals, because there are a number of points where the commission's proposal can really be improved, but we do not need to delay. we can make important progress in europe this year if everyone takes responsibility. we in parliament will certainly not delay.
in writing. the reasons today lacked inspiration, which immediately dashed the hope that the vision report could contribute to a strong response to the crisis. we want the union's leaders to give real answers to the questions of the thousands of ford genk workers who are threatened with unemployment and to the concerns of the 'lost generation' of 14 million young people in the eu who are neither in school, nor in work, nor in training. these young people, who must have the future of the union in their hands, have nothing to do, are powerless and have lost all faith in politics. the union is breeding a generation of mistrust, which is feeding populist and nationalist tendencies that are encouraging europeans to retreat to the island of their member state. we must really opt for a social pact in europe. what we see today is that the union is continuing to blunder along the economic margins, to cut the edges of the malfunctions of the unregulated market, without really looking to the future, giving hope to young europeans and developing a real vision.
in writing. in this time of crisis, it is essential that the eu assert itself as a responsible actor, capable of reviving growth and employment. the cuts proposed by the council for the 2013 budget were unacceptable in this respect, as they reduced the scope for eu action in terms of stimulating growth, entrepreneurship and research, which are necessary to improve the daily lives of european citizens. i am therefore glad that we have voted a budget proposal which is in line with the commission's proposal. i would also like to point out that while the ep voted against budget cuts for the headings intended to boost employment and growth, it is not opposed to austerity measures where possible. the proposal which was voted on contains a reduction in real terms of the ep budget.
in writing. parliament voted for a 2012 budget focusing on growth, jobs and innovation in the union and support for development and democracy in neighbouring countries on wednesday 26 october. first of all, i'd like to thank derek vaughan for his work. we voted in favour of this budget. we have tried to restore the draft budget proposed by the commission after the cuts made by the council in july. parliament's position implies an increase in payments of 5.2% compared with this year's budget. the resulting budget is €133.1 billion. the resolution on the budget was adopted by 431 votes to 120, with 124 abstentions.
in writing. − in times of economic and financial crisis and when member states are closely scrutinizing their budgets, the multiannual financial framework, within which the eu sets out the broad lines of expenditure from 2014 to 2020, is of the utmost importance. the eu must strike a balance between budgetary discipline, planning certainty and flexibility to respond to unforeseen situations. recent developments in particular have highlighted the fact that unforeseen events can have global implications requiring an immediate response. a more flexible design through appropriate adjustments is envisaged within the new financial framework 2014-2020. it is essential that, apart from the budgetary architecture, crossborder programmes, whether in social, education, employment or cultural policy, continue to be financed at european level and that this aspect is therefore taken into account in the adjustment of the structural funds in the context of this report. the european parliament will look particularly hard at the council if it takes up the pen and starts to cut appropriations. the cef, which is intended to provide a major boost to the european transport area, is another challenge in the debate with the council. the commission has already taken steps to improve the mobility of goods and people, to improve the connectivity of remote regions and to make the whole transport system environmentally friendly. many governments have set themselves these objectives, but now they must also show the political will to achieve them.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. i would like to highlight the admirable work of my colleague mr canfin who has done a job of a quality rarely achieved for an exercise of this magnitude. the multiannual financial framework is a spending plan which reflects the union's priorities in financial terms. this text is an excellent basis for the annual budgetary periods. it defines expenditure over a given period and the maximum amounts available for each major category of expenditure. it thus provides a financial as well as a policy framework to concentrate resources and investment where they are needed.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. the reform of the own resources system, including proposals for a financial transaction tax, was foreseen. a new union vat was needed to feed into own resources, to bring the share of member states' gni-based contributions to the union budget to 40% by 2020, which would contribute to member states' consolidation efforts. it is also worth noting that it is planned to simplify the method of calculating vat and to improve the transparency of the vat own resource.
the committee on foreign affairs has tabled a number of amendments. - madam president, we see three major problems with the acaa project today. the first is institutional. it is shocking to discover that once the european parliament has given its consent to the pharmaceutical sector, it will have no say in any other sectors that israel decides to open up. we call this buying a cat in a bag or, if you prefer, taking the european parliament for a pigeon. we will always fight for the prerogatives of parliament to be respected. that is why, in order to clarify this point, my group has asked for this report to be referred back to committee in order to obtain guarantees on this matter. the second problem is legal and concerns the jurisdiction of the israeli authorities over the products of the settlements, which are illegal under international law. we have presented an interpretative statement covering this aspect. you have just mentioned this, mr de gucht, but this declaration is a wishful thinking if it is not endorsed by the three institutions and, in particular, by the council, and to date we have no guarantee on this point. the third problem is political and it is not the least. this agreement, which was part of israel's upgrade proposal, frozen in 2008, although it can be legally concluded under the current framework, is a de facto upgrade. and nothing has changed on the ground since 2008, if not for the worse. the blockade of gaza continues, the settlement has expanded dramatically in violation of international law and peace talks have stalled. the lieberman plan announced last august to the european foreign minister and to mrs ashton, which envisages palestinian cantons around israeli cities, destroys any two-state solution and benjamin netanyahu has just tried, a few days ago, to partially implement the levy report which would legalize the settlements which the israelis themselves have so far declared illegal. that's not our policy. that is not the position of international law. and is it to this position of the netanyahu government that we want to give the acaa as a gift? this is unacceptable, at least for the socialists and the democrats, unacceptable in the light of our values, unacceptable in the light of the lisbon treaty and the new neighbourhood policy which provides for more for more. and it is on this basis that, if the report is not referred back to committee today, our group of socialists and democrats will in any case vote against the acaa.
mr president, commissioner, this technical agreement has been in the hands of parliament for two and a half years. today, despite the concrete responses given a few moments ago by commissioner de gucht and all the legal services, the obstruction continues. mr moreira, who is certainly never short of imagination, is therefore now about to ask for yet another postponement. we must remember, however, that voting for the acaa means facilitating the free movement of medicines between the european union and israel, especially generic medicines which, thanks to the agreement, will cure european citizens more quickly, while allowing our national budgets to make savings which they can hardly do without today. our job here is to evaluate, judge and vote for or against, in soul and conscience. that is what a handful of colleagues in this parliament have prevented us from doing for two and a half years now. it's been long enough. shimon peres used to say: "at one point, let's agree to disagree, obviously this is the time".
