in writing. it was important to grant this discharge to efsa. almost 500 million consumers expect efsa to work effectively. i am not renouncing my wishes for independent work to be the rule, for full account to be taken of auditing and for a real challenge to restore the necessary trust between this body and the consumer!
in writing. i voted for this text. in view of the many serious problems recently posed by dangerous medicines circulating freely on the market with authorisations from the european medicines agency, there is an urgent need for much clearer, more transparent procedures, with sanctions for infringement, on data relating to clinical trials, the selection and training of the agency's scientific staff including the declarations of interest of its experts and of the members of the committees which have actively participated in the work of the agency.
in writing. the ep voted to make 2013 the european year of citizens. in a general context of economic, political and social crisis, it seemed to me important to dedicate a place to european citizenship. the aim of this event is to inform the general public, especially young people, on how to put their rights into practice. being a european citizen gives you many rights that europeans are not always aware of, such as the right to move and reside freely in another member state, the right to vote and stand as a candidate in european and local elections, and the right to petition the ep. another important aspect for me is the need to emphasize cooperation with local authorities, working in conjunction with national parliaments, the europe direct centres and the involvement of civil society.
in writing. citizens also complain about shortcomings in eu regulations, problems with the eu's administrative efficiency, a lack of transparency and excessive bureaucracy. the gap between the eu institutions and citizens persists, as does the low level of citizen involvement in the functioning of the eu. abstention rates in the european elections are high, especially among young people. there is a real lack of visibility of union citizenship and the concrete benefits it brings to union citizens. among the main problems faced by eu citizens when living, studying or working in another member state are lack of information and lack of awareness of their rights, as well as language barriers and non-recognition of diplomas and qualifications, especially in times of financial, political and social crisis within the eu. the proposed european year will therefore aim to raise awareness of the rights attached to union citizenship, in order to help citizens to exercise fully their right to move and reside freely within the territory of the member states.
in writing. it was important to me that we take a stand against human rights violations. the death of sergei magnitsky is just one more injustice and manipulation among many perpetrated by the russian government and the judiciary. more recently, the pussy riot conviction has been the subject of much media attention as it represented an intensification of repression in russia, where the government has cracked down on protests, allegedly rigged elections, intimidated the media, banned gay rights marches for the next 100 years, jailed and purged opposition figures like chess champion garry kasparov. the application of these sanctions finally confronts the kremlin's high dignitaries with concrete consequences that make it possible to fight against their systematic attacks on democracy and transparency. we must send a strong political message to putin and the russian regime against corruption and impunity.
in writing. i welcome the resolution adopted on tuesday to relaunch the single european sky. the single sky is supposed to reduce travel times, and therefore co2 emissions and airfares, while increasing safety and job creation. it is important to use all the means at our disposal to manage european air traffic effectively and to avoid consumers wasting more time and money. the key is 328 000 jobs, which is very significant, especially in times of crisis. the european parliament has adopted a resolution on the future of the single european sky, which aims to achieve a safe, efficient and sustainable single european sky, which is favourable to both our travellers and our businesses.
in writing. − as part of the objective of a single european transport area, the creation of a single european airspace is essential. as early as 1999, it was decided to merge the existing fragmentation of airspace from 27 different airspaces. a number of functional airspace blocks (fabs) should be created, which will shorten routes, reduce delays, save fuel, thus protect the environment and also reduce air fares. in concrete terms, this means that the introduction of fab and sesar technology would result in a reduction of flight times by approximately 10%, as well as a 50% reduction in cancellations and delays and a co2 saving of 50 million tonnes. these effects directly demonstrate the need for the creation of a single european airspace. the deployment of fab and sesar technology was to be completed by 4 december 2012, but the result is more than sobering. the member states do not want to be reminded of the commitment they made ten years ago to create a single european airspace. the objective will not be reached by the end of this year due to a lack of willingness on the part of the member states. how can the eu be a respected negotiating partner at international level in a transport sector as international as aviation if its 27 member states cannot speak with one voice? (parliament adopted the legislative resolution)
in writing. the single european sky has many advantages. the new provisions will help to intensify the expectations for 2004: better congestion control; enhanced and truly unique security; a reduction in flight times by 10%, a reduction in delays and cancellations by 50% and finally a reduction in ticket prices; a reduction in co2 emissions; the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area. in this respect, according to a study by the european commission which i found particularly interesting, the rapid and full implementation of the new generation european air traffic management system (sesar) would reduce co2 emissions by 50 million tonnes and create 328 000 jobs, which i believe is a key element. following the changes in recent years, the adaptations to eu legislation will ultimately improve aviation performance and finally lead to the creation of a unified airspace, a truly single sky. this vote also gives me the opportunity to recall the voluntary political approach which the member states must adopt in this area.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text and would like to stress in particular the importance of member states' compliance with the completion of the single european sky in order to achieve significant safety and environmental savings by creating a more sustainable aviation sector and a more efficient air traffic management system at european level. i also stress the importance of taking regional airports into account because of their role in reducing congestion on the network and increasing capacity.
in writing. i welcome the resolution we adopted where we expressly ask the european commission to harmonise passenger rights in europe, across all modes of transport. while there are currently six directives, the aim is to create a common basic framework of rights that apply to all modes equitably and take into account all the characteristics of the different modes of transport. this should create more transparency as the current situation remains unclear for thousands of european travellers. in the event of an emergency and unforeseen circumstance, the traveller should no longer be in need and should be able to rely on a simple document to remind him of his rights. it must have a clear definition of what is meant by the term 'exceptional circumstances' and it must also have a clarification of the rules relating to carrier bankruptcy. we must work on better protection for travellers who must be able to benefit from a safe return. all prepaid trips should also be reimbursed. the transparency of prices on the internet should also be ensured, as the advertised price is too often not the same as the final price.
in writing. − in a single european transport area, uniform and easily understandable eu passenger rights are essential. at european level, individual rights have already been established for all modes of transport. the commission has already adopted the measures in force for air and rail transport, and they will be introduced for shipping and bus transport in december 2012 and march 2013 respectively. the report is welcome because it draws attention to the problems still existing in the field of passenger rights. the commission has already taken a number of measures to improve the situation in the member states. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the new rules and to monitor the implementation of the new rules. information campaigns are a good way to do this. enforcement can be strengthened by clear rules for the establishment of national enforcement authorities, ensuring harmonised enforcement of passenger rights in all member states. the commission is already planning to review air passenger rights at the end of this year. the commission should take the european parliament's proposals to heart, so that not only are there clear passenger rights, which do not require the interpretation of a lawyer, but these rights can also be enforced in a uniform manner throughout europe.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text which once again stresses the many problems and risks faced by passengers in all modes of transport. in particular, and as a matter of urgency, the commission is invited to improve transparency in the computerised reservation systems governed by regulation 1008/2008/ec which leave the consumer unarmed in the face of a series of abuses, such as abusive clauses in the overall calculation of ticket prices which automatically include unwanted surcharges. in addition, it is necessary to insist once again that the commission finally table a legislative proposal to revise directive 90/314/ec on package holidays, which is no longer appropriate at all and therefore deprives travellers of a legal framework in both normal and exceptional situations. finally, the current levels of consumer protection in the event of airline bankruptcy or insolvency are insufficient and require a legislative proposal providing for measures to protect stranded passengers in the event of cessation of operations, compulsory insurance for airlines or the creation of a guarantee fund.
in writing. i voted against the macovei report, which meant voting for the 2010 discharge of the european environment agency. i regret that this report and its motives have jeopardized the granting of this important landfill, which is consistent with europe's desire to make the environment a priority.

in writing. the issue of export controls on dual-use goods is a key one. this control should limit the risks of proliferation and military use without harming legitimate trade. it was therefore necessary to streamline the procedures applied under the european system of export controls by introducing delegated acts with a view to regularly updating annex i and, consequently, amending annex ii. regular updating of annex i and simplification of procedures are necessary in view of the rapid pace of technological progress. any delay in this could lead to serious safety problems and could even harm the competitiveness of european exporters if a third country were to remove export controls faster than the european union.
in writing. the adoption of the resolution on the harmonisation of the minimum level of training for seafarers brings european legislation into line with international standards (stcw convention of the international maritime organisation). the text, while improving the competitiveness of the european fleet, also maintains the stricter european standards for rest periods. by ensuring that all seafarers in europe have the same minimum level of training in the future, i am pleased that the directive is promoting progress in health and safety (e.g. better management of acts of piracy).
in writing. i voted in favour of mr simpson's text. the purpose of this report is to incorporate into union law the amendments to the international convention on standards of training for seafarers which were adopted in the framework of the work of the international maritime organisation. it should be noted that the social partners were well integrated into the conciliation process and were able to make constructive comments.
in writing. i have followed the opinion and the arguments of the rapporteur, mr moreira, and voted against this agreement. i have no complaints against the israeli citizen but against the decisions of his government. the acaa is not just a technical agreement, it is the strengthening of eu-israel relations. giving our consent to the acaa is to admit and encourage the illegal settlement of the palestinian territories. in addition, some passages are still rather obscure, which is why, rather than voting for or against the agreement, i was one of those who wanted a referral back to the commission to answer a number of questions. this agreement is ultimately a defeat for all parties. to want to do things quickly is harmful, and what is more, it is detrimental to certain values that i believe are central!
in writing. i am particularly pleased that the report on the future of the union's development policy takes the cultural dimension into account. culture is a vector of openness and a motor of democracies acting against all the nationalist tendencies, racist temptations and exclusions that the current economic and financial, climate and food crises fuel. that is why i think it is necessary to encourage the free movement of artists. the culture of a country is also a part of the universal space. this diversity is essential to break the impoverishing standardization of globalization. culture, as a system of thought, norms, values and interactions between societies, will, as abdou diouf says, be a determining factor in the management of globalisation. finally, culture creates wealth and jobs. its contribution to gdp can be as high as 3%. it is therefore important that poverty reduction strategies at national and international level take account of this.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. the programme for change is innovative in that it focuses on budget support, the combination of grants and loans, and the promotion of the private sector, among other things; i believe that the use of these mechanisms must first of all help to lift the citizens of developing countries out of poverty and dependence on aid, and to spread and apply the principles of good administrative and fiscal governance.
in writing. on 23 october 2012, the ep adopted a resolution to support smes. as the driving force of the european economy, they represent one of our best opportunities for developing job creation, which is welcome in this period of crisis. i am therefore pleased that parliament has voted this resolution, which highlights some of the measures which are indispensable to the development of the european economic fabric: reduction of administrative burdens, easier access to credit where it is less than eur 25 000, the establishment of an erasmus programme for young entrepreneurs... in the context of the negotiations on the new mff, the ep also decided to advocate an increase in the budget for smes.
in writing. − according to the commission, in 2010, the 23 million smes in europe accounted for two thirds of private sector jobs and 59% of total value added in the non-financial sector of the business economy. in addition, about 80% of the jobs created in the last five years were in smes. this shows that smes are at the heart of the eu's economic and employment policies and thus make a significant contribution to the europe 2020 strategy. it is therefore essential that the role of smes be strengthened. this initiative by the european parliament is an important step forward. it calls, inter alia, for the creation of a multilingual online portal with information on the laws and administrative systems of third countries and the mapping of local, national and european support programmes, which will particularly benefit smes operating across borders. initiatives that help reduce the administrative burden on smes are also supported. these are good approaches to strengthen european smes, to increase their competitiveness and thus to promote the european single market as a whole.
in writing. i welcome the signal sent by the ep, which gave the go-ahead for the negotiation of a transatlantic free trade agreement between the eu and the usa, which should embody an 'ambitious and reciprocal opening' between two partners representing together almost half of the world's gdp. while ensuring that the eu does not sacrifice its values and interests (particularly in agriculture, the environment, public health and workers' rights), the prospect of such an agreement is likely to strengthen the eu's stability and economic development.
in writing. i fully support this resolution, as it is a very concise state of play of the trade and economic relations between the eu and the us. it stresses the importance of this bilateral relationship, since it is the most important economic relationship in the world, and at the same time it draws up a very detailed plan of all the aspects to be improved. the potential of this transatlantic relationship is far from being optimised. i therefore support it and am willing to support the removal of non-tariff barriers and regulatory incompatibilities, which would, among other things, lead to a significant increase in gdp in the eu and the us, and would obviously have a decisive impact on growth and employment. in these times of crisis, close cooperation between the eu and the us is needed.
member of the commission. − mr president, in repeated debates in plenary and also in committee, i have made it clear that i am a staunch supporter of a free trade agreement with japan, but also that such support is conditional. we have the scoping exercise and we also have agreement on a number of non-tariff barriers that have to be removed. these cannot be dissociated. it is very clear to me that japan has to deliver on those ntbs, and within a very clear timetable. we have agreed on the roadmap, setting out how to do this. i must say that i am not very pleased at the moment, in fact, because there were a couple of obligations japan should have fulfilled by the end of september, and we are now at the end of october. we should stick to our timetable. i will say this very clearly to our japanese counterparts; i did so as recently as a week ago, and i will say it again. i will also say it to the council, at the council meeting of ministers responsible for trade that will take place on 29 november. we have a clear understanding with the japanese on the scoping exercise and on what needs to be fulfilled and resolved, including for example the zoning problem that has been mentioned by a couple of members. we should take them at their word, and their words should be followed by deeds. i think the review clause that a number of members of parliament mentioned in their speeches – especially the rapporteur, mr kazak – is an essential element. i do not want it to be automatic, however; i do not believe that automaticity is a good negotiating tool. if it should be demonstrated that japan is not fulfilling its commitments, you should summon me before your parliament and ask me why i am not putting an end to the negotiations. i do not believe, however, that it is realistic to expect automaticity to work in negotiations. on sustainability, mr bütikofer, that issue will be part of the negotiations and it will be part of the agreements, as by the way it is in the whole new generation of agreements that we have been negotiating. i also believe that, in the case of japan, there are a number of very important topics in that area that we should address. mr caspary mentioned – although i see that in the meantime he has disappeared – that the only aim of the japanese is to export more cars to europe. i do not know about that. it is very interesting to look at their export figures to europe, because they are gradually going down, not up. they have gone from eur 900 000 down to about eur 600 000 over a couple of years. it is also not true that japan is just exporting smaller cars to europe. japan is in fact exporting cars in the mid- and upper range, so we should take care that when we discuss these matters we do so on the basis of the facts. i have one last remark to make on industrial policy, which mr zalba bidegain mentioned. if i understand his reasoning, it is as follows. if we want industrial production to increase from 16 % to 20 %, then we should take care with the japanese. i believe this is a very defensive strategy or tactic – whatever you want to call it – for a number of reasons. first of all, the industrial production share of gdp is not going to go up from 16 % to 20 %. that simply will not happen. to people who make this claim, my answer is to ask what is going down in that case, to make up the full 100 %. what is going down? services? i cannot see that. could it be agriculture? i do not think we would like that. of course, in your country at the moment the industrial sector’s percentage share of gdp is going up, because you have seen a tremendous dip in real estate, which appears in the statistics as services, whilst the industrial sector as such has not changed. what can go up is the output of the industrial sector, and that is in fact happening. if you look locally at europe, you can see that is what is happening, but not, i believe, in terms of industrial production as a share of gdp. secondly, i believe the kind of economy that we have can only grow if it is in interaction with the other big players on the world market. we have become very interdependent, and that goes for the united states, japan, china and also europe. we have to take that interdependence into account and make sure that our position in that global arena is as strong as possible.
the european automotive industry, which employs 12 million people, has been hit hard by the financial and economic crisis and is facing increasing competition from third countries. today we are faced with the possible start of negotiations on a free trade agreement between the eu and japan. we must be very careful not to put the automobile industry under more pressure. let us look at ford genk in belgium, where there is great social unrest today because of the possible restructuring/closure of the factory. we must ensure that the guarantee we are putting in place today, namely the removal by japan of non-tariff barriers which have an impact on the automobile industry, will be respected in the future. only then can our companies compete fairly with japanese companies. within the year we must assess the removal of non-tariff barriers and on that basis take a decision on a future free trade agreement with japan.
in writing. i remember that some time ago we were a handful of meps who denounced several whole sections of the acta treaty. the text readily transgressed individual freedoms or the processing of personal data, among other things. we have to be very careful not to let the commissioner's words get in the way of our proposals. we were a handful, but, through hard work, we were the majority in rejecting this liberty-killing text. then came ceta, negotiating a trade agreement between europe and canada. i was not surprised to find that the treaty contained whole paragraphs of acta. commissioner de gucht told me not to worry. it is clear that the text has been modified since then, but it still contains some difficult passages. so you will understand that in the new treaty between europe and japan, which we are discussing today, i am again concerned that we will find one or the other trojan horse with new attempts at legislative whitewashing. the citizens of europe would be grateful if you could allay their fears.

the single market act is a strong initiative in this respect and we should all welcome it. we must jointly boost the single market and improve its functioning. you have, commissioner, put forward a good proposal for a directive on the modernisation of public procurement and we are trying, as you said, to improve it in this parliament. we are certainly moving in a constructive spirit towards greater efficiency in public spending, which, as you have very often pointed out, mr commissioner, still represents 19% of the gross domestic product of the european union. we are trying to improve this efficiency in terms of sustainable growth, employment, innovation, social inclusion and compliance but also environmental protection. it is fundamental because it is also from the single market and certain derivatives, because perhaps we have forgotten too much about social harmonization, that the frustrations and doubts of citizens arise because of a competition which has set states against each other, citizens against each other and, in particular, workers against each other, rather than uniting them in a more prosperous europe for all. i would like to give two examples of this directive which are of particular concern to us. it is the fight against uncontrolled subcontracting where the too large cascade generates a loss of public money over each stage. we are simply trying to regulate it, to make it more transparent, because subcontracting is desirable, because it obviously generates better participation by smes in public procurement, which is what we all want, because today there may be a deficit in this respect. we are also concerned about compliance with the rules. the 1994 international labour organization convention is difficult to enforce, since only ten states have ratified it. compliance with existing national rules is equally important. therefore, commissioner, can you join me in the fight for better compliance with the rules which will improve this single market, which will make it more harmonious, at the service of all european citizens?
in writing. the amendment to the 2012 budget tabled by the european commission sends a strong signal to member states to honour their commitments. in april this year, eu education ministers reaffirmed the target of having 20% of european students study abroad for a period by 2020. of course, this must be met with the necessary resources. it is therefore vital for the erasmus programme that the 2012 deficit of eur 90 million be addressed as soon as possible. this is how we prevent students from receiving a lower or no erasmus grant. if there is no agreement soon, it will be the students with the lowest grades who will have to give up their foreign experience. the european union must make a decisive effort to promote education and employment for young people as a way out of the crisis. a foreign experience adds so much to the profile of recent graduates. year after year more students go abroad. now that the erasmus programme is in full swing, it is a bad idea to back off.
in writing. i voted in favour of this procedure. as a reminder, a stabilisation and association agreement (saa) between the european communities and their member states, of the one part, and the republic of serbia, of the other part, was already signed in 2008. the amendments tabled in this text by my colleague mr winkler are intended to bring the text into line with the changes made by the omnibus i regulation on trade to the five regulations governing the application of both the stabilisation and association agreements and the interim agreements with the western balkan countries.
in writing. it must be admitted that the cfp (common fisheries policy) has been a failure on the whole for the last 30 years. however, it must also be admitted that in the midst of this chaos, the rule of the 12-nautical-mile zone is one of the few reasons for satisfaction. i therefore followed the rapporteur's advice, in favour of extending the 12 nautical mile zone both in time and space. however, the debate will need to take place within the framework of the wider debate on cfp reform. the current 12-nautical-mile zone is working well, but will expire on 31 december 2012 if the current proposal is not in force by that date. it provides a degree of protection for vulnerable coastal communities; the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the fishermen concerned are protected.
in writing. i voted in favour of mr moreira's report. the agreement ensures the reciprocal preservation of geographical indications (pdo and pgi) protected by the respective parties. moreover, it properly protects the interests of the union. the agreement under consideration paves the way for the strengthening of trade relations between the european union and moldova and as such deserves to be supported.
in writing. consular protection must be effective, efficient and well coordinated, its primary objective being that citizens can fully enjoy it. i do not think that the proposal gives the european union delegations sufficient responsibility for local consular protection on a day-to-day basis. i think that the union delegations should be given a greater role, including consular responsibilities. the proposal for a directive could therefore have been an opportunity to give the delegations a greater role and to give them the means to play their role to the full. this would be a way of integrating a more european diplomacy, improving the union's visibility and strengthening european citizenship. we must also have a guarantee that fundamental rights will be respected. for example, are we sure that the embassies or consulates of all the member states guarantee the same rights to same-sex couples and their family members? this guarantee is given by the union delegations which are bound by the charter of fundamental rights. i think, however, that a good compromise has been reached on a matter of great importance to the citizens of the union.
in writing. i voted in favour of the bauer report. in order to facilitate access by union citizens to their rights, including the right to protection in third countries, a new legislative instrument is needed. the stockholm programme, like the european parliament, advocates common concepts of consular protection. given the growing number of crisis areas, a common approach is needed more than ever.
in writing. - this report is far from exhaustive, especially as regards the expectations of people in border areas. in many cases, they see europe as the solution to the problem - whether it is a crossborder student, worker or employer - rather than the problem as it is today. the single market will never convince the citizens if we do not dare to pursue a coherent economic, fiscal and social policy. and here most countries still pursue a short-sighted 'national' or even regional policy. we are constantly weakening the internal market with 87 different rules where one rule is appropriate, with enormous differences - see tax havens like luxembourg - which completely upset the economic and thus social structures of the surrounding regions. as long as europe does not respond to the most basic questions of its citizens, it will be pouring water on the mills of the eurosceptics, and therefore will be totally untrustworthy.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text and i would like to stress in particular the urgency with which the commission must periodically launch european information campaigns with the member states and regional and local authorities in the national, regional and local media to strengthen the dialogue with citizens on the benefits of the single market. on the other hand, i strongly support the call on the commission to develop, together with the member states, a genuine competitive digital single market which serves european consumers and can confidently deliver the socioeconomic benefits that benefit all european consumers. finally, i would like to stress the urgency for the commission and the member states to ensure that passenger rights legislation is properly implemented, but also that european citizens are aware of these rights and the means at their disposal to enforce them.
in writing. the conclusion of a free trade agreement with japan can be beneficial for the eu, its businesses and its workers. i am therefore pleased that the ep has given its consent to the opening of negotiations. however, for these to be successful, it is essential that the negotiators ensure that concessions are made on the japanese side, particularly in the area of the removal of non-tariff barriers or public procurement (particularly for postal services and transport). the ep therefore calls for the negotiations to be automatically suspended if japan does not make the necessary efforts.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution. but i do have a few reservations which i hope will be taken into account when the trade agreement is voted on. first of all, i would like to avoid the unpleasant surprise of reading the ceta agreement (eu-canada trade agreement) in which we find many paragraphs from the acta treaty that parliament has rejected. i would therefore like to see it treated in trade agreements as a trade only. secondly, the japanese system does not always allow all our products to be exported. i therefore ask that the conditions for opening up be perfectly reciprocal. i will never be one of those people who advocate backward protectionism. i would nevertheless like to see a debate on the impact of the korean and japanese trade agreements on the european automobile industry.
in writing. − thanks to my green colleague ska keller, first rapporteur on policy coherence for development, we managed to secure a regular reporting on eu policy and its impact on development cooperation. this report is the first monitoring report. at least, that was the idea. it does not expose the weaknesses in the implementation of policy coherence, nor does it provide an overview of the eu's achievements in this area. it did become a global report with a clear vision of what policy coherence should be in relation to development cooperation. and that's good. that is why i voted in favour of the report. it covers five priority policy areas (trade, agriculture and fisheries, climate change and energy, security and migration) and regularly addresses the wound (for example: 'regrets the publication by the commission of two separate reports on trade in general and trade and development, which has proved counterproductive from the point of view of coherence'); the greens have also strengthened the report: the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject. we also brought in elements on fair trade and conflict minerals.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. policy coherence for development must be based on the recognition of the right of a country or region to define democratically its own policies, priorities and strategies to protect the livelihoods of its people. the breakdown into eight action areas chosen for the period 2011-2014 is a good choice. i would point out, amongst others, the proposed new corporate social responsibility policy, the importance of binding csr obligations and the importance of encouraging employers to apply social standards which are more ambitious than the legal provisions in force, including the possibility of designing and obtaining a label, such as a social label. i would also ask the commission to encourage the member states to monitor strictly the implementation of these obligations. the cfp is not a mere technical issue, it is first and foremost a political responsibility which parliament, as a democratically elected institution and co-legislator, has the essential duty of translating into concrete action.
on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, in principle i support the european semester, to keep national budgets in control and prevent budget derailing as we have seen over the past years. a monetary union without fiscal discipline will explode. country-specific recommendations make sense but add little new. the imf and the oecd have been making the same recommendations for years. it is like a severely overweight person visiting the doctor. his doctor will tell him ‘you have to lose weight’, but obviously the patient knows this already. the remedy looks very simple – exercise up, calories down, but you have to do it. member states are also recommended to do things they already know and when this is not the case they simply ignore it and live in denial. the number one recommendation for france is that people should work longer, but the first thing mr hollande did was to cut the retirement age. likewise, it remains to be seen if france will take orders from brussels on budgetary policy. french public opinion is mainly in denial about the oversized welfare state. when public sector unions are confronted with facts they start striking and the political leadership of france does little to raise the much-needed awareness. with regard to the economic dialogue, i would like to remind parliament of the exchange of views we had in the committee on economic and monetary affairs (econ) with the finance minister of belgium, mr vanackere. he painted a very rosy picture of the belgian economy. he depicted the belgian economy as a rose garden. a few weeks later it turned out that he had used overly optimistic figures in his presentation. instead of being a top performer, belgium is heading towards recession. its economy is shrinking at a much higher pace than the eurozone average. serious budget cuts are needed. the economic dialogue with belgium was no more than a good news show intended for domestic consumption. the value of such exchange is rather limited. those who live in denial are punished by the facts later on, as was the case with mr vanackere, so the proposed form of the european semester is an exercise in irrelevance. forecasts are far too rosy year after year. the commission wants to spread optimism but predicted growth is often followed by even more contraction. a european semester only works when we stop fooling ourselves, face reality as it is, and act accordingly. blue-card answer. – mr president, i will be very brief. i was referring to the report by the commission. the imf is much more realistic about what is going on, but the commission wants to spread optimism, hoping that all the troubles will go away by themselves. as members know, that is not the case. the commission is doing this because it is afraid that public opinion will get demoralised, and so the process becomes increasingly messy. that is the wrong basis for doing things. it is better to stick to the facts.

in writing. in 2006, i had the opportunity to lead a mission to cambodia to assess the democratic developments in that country. one of the things we saw during this mission was that the functioning of the judiciary was a weak link in the system. the cambodian people were also waiting for the khmer rouge tribunal to be set up. this court, although in place since 2006, does not seem to be functioning efficiently. the court is plagued by financial problems, which has slowed down the pace of hearings, as it cannot finance the staff needed to run the court. moreover, numerous accusations of political interference by the phnom penh regime have been denounced by many international organizations, as for this court. apart from cases of restrictions on freedom of expression, i am particularly appalled, as a member of parliament's committee on agriculture and rural development, by the many cases of displacement of people and dispossession of land for economic purposes. many land rights activists have also been imprisoned in cambodian prisons. it is high time that cambodia showed greater respect for human rights.
in writing. i voted for the text, of course. the role of a mediator is important and even indispensable. the mediator is the link between the citizen and the administrations. it allows them to reconsider their position and has the power to compel them to do so if necessary. the ombudsman registered 2 510 complaints in 2011, compared with 2 667 in 2010. it is also interesting to note that more than 60% of complaints received in 2011 were filed via the internet. in the european spirit, we must meet the citizen on his daily journey and facilitate access to information or to protest. the considerable reduction in the number of requests for information received in recent years (1 000 in 2010, 1 850 in 2009, 4 300 in 2008 and 4 100 in 2007) is a testament to the success of the ombudsman's interactive guide available on his website. it allows interested parties to obtain information without having to make a request. the ombudsman opened 382 inquiries based on complaints and launched 14 additional inquiries on his own initiative, which is proof that the ombudsman can and should also be proactive.
in writing. the emir regulation (european market infrastructure regulation) will introduce mandatory centralised clearing of a significant part of derivatives in the course of 2013. the clearing houses will increasingly concentrate the risks previously borne by banks operating on a contract basis. only clearing houses with banking status will then be subject to ecb supervision. again, the principle of identical supervision for identical activity is to be applied and thus all clearing houses will eventually be included in the scope of the single supervisory mechanism. the proposal to extend the scope of supervision to other sectors is neither realistic nor appropriate in the short term. however, in order to be able to do so in the long term, the ambition to do so must be affirmed now.
in writing. i supported this resolution because it not only encourages russia to continue on the path of open and fair trade but also recalls the eu's concerns about russia's measures to restrict trade. russia has recently adopted measures favouring domestic producers in public procurement, establishing significant seasonal duties on the import of a certain category of sugar and on vehicle recycling. i would remind you that these measures are contrary to international trade and should be abolished. this vote is also a call by parliament for the fight against corruption and for the rule of law. finally, russia's accession to the wto is important for the future of eu-russia relations, particularly in the area of raw materials and energy. i am convinced that this accession will undoubtedly facilitate trade flows between the eu and russia, while stimulating economic growth and job creation.

(applause) i would like to thank
the rapporteur. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, this debate on shadow banking comes at a very good time. just yesterday, the financial stability board issued its conclusions and recommendations on how to deal with shadow banking. in many respects, these are the same as those which will be put to the vote tomorrow. the shadow banking phenomenon actually encompasses a whole range of very different forms of credit intermediation outside the traditional banking sector and is therefore not subject to the same rules of the game. as the name suggests, it concerns complex, often opaque financial activities over which no one, including no institution or supervisor, has sufficient control. this does not mean that shadow banking could not contribute to the financing of the real economy and therefore cannot be described as positive. but first, there are clearly links between the traditional banking sector and shadow banking in various ways. secondly, we know that there could be potential systemic risks - which also played a role in the financial crisis - and also a shift to shadow banking to escape the supervision and regulation of the traditional banking sector. and thirdly, it is a very large and growing phenomenon, estimated to cover 25 to 30% of the financial sector. for all these reasons, it is necessary to create the necessary instruments to enable the authorities to obtain a global picture of the phenomenon in order to be able to assess the risks and take action if necessary. ideally, we should be able to use the instruments that oxygenate the real economy and can contribute to economic growth without encouraging excessive risk-taking and curbing more speculative or dangerous forms of activity. that is the subject of my report and i thank all my colleagues and in particular the shadow rapporteurs for the excellent cooperation which has led to a number of conclusions which have been widely accepted. i will not go into all of them in detail, but the most urgent one seems to me to be the need for monitoring, the mapping of financial flows. supervisors should have at least a general overview of the size of repurchase transactions and all forms of debt or debt-collection arrangements entered into by institutions. hence the idea of reporting information to a trade repository. there should also be a kind of central eu database of repo transactions. common standards for reporting transactions relating to capital, interest, collateral, valuation adjustments, maturities, consideration, etc. should be developed. there is also a need for a more complete overview of risk transfers by financial institutions in the form of a central register. in fact, we should be able to find answers to simple questions such as 'who should do what to whom and when and at what price'? in addition, the traditional banking sector should be protected by the introduction of a limit on exposure to shadow banking and shadow banking activities initiated by banks should be included in the balance sheet and consolidated. securitisation should be subject to stricter rules to encourage quality products and it should be examined whether there should be no cap on the number of times a financial product can be securitised. finally, a series of recommendations are made with regard to certain specific funds, such as money market funds and index funds, in order to increase transparency and mitigate potential systemic risks. the ball is now in the court of the g20 and the commission. it is clear that we can only formulate a good answer by taking a global, global approach. the institutions and regions concerned must now reorganise themselves over the coming months so that light can finally be shed on that part of the financial world which has been under-reported until now. and that is an absolute must if we are to avoid another financial crisis in the future. we are waiting for the commission's proposals, which will be communicated. we are anxiously awaiting this and then a number of very concrete steps will be taken, which we will of course examine closely as a parliament.
- i'm the reporter. president. - mr president-in-office, ladies and gentlemen, when we speak today about the completion of economic and monetary union, we are not having a technical debate but a political debate. what we are doing today is making fundamental choices for the europe we want to leave to our children and grandchildren tomorrow. and yes, in the short term the interests of our member states may differ, but in the long term they are clearly complementary. as representatives of the european people, we have a moral duty to think and act in the long term, to give europeans a bright future. we must also do so in a way that involves our citizens, because ultimately it is they, and they alone, who give us political legitimacy. that, ladies and gentlemen, is the aim of the debate we are having here today. the key words are clear to me: hope and trust. confidence must return because entrepreneurs and families must dare to invest and banks must dare to lend again, because the economic engine must be re-boosted to create growth and jobs. we also need to give people hope. there is a prospect of recovery. we will emerge from this crisis stronger, but only if we act with courage, decisiveness and action. with this report we are sending an unequivocal message to the heads of state and government. the four building blocks of the van rompuy roadmap must be ambitious. we are thus indicating that european banking supervision must be brought into being, that the two proposals for regulations must be treated as one legislative package, on which we can take part in the decision-making process. that the quality of supervision should be high within a european mechanism accountable to parliaments. that the member states participating, whether by compulsory or opt-in, should have equal rights in the supervisory board of such a mechanism and that we as parliament will complete our work in time so that we can hopefully officially launch this mechanism on 1 january. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the reforms and will continue to monitor the implementation of the reforms. we can then look at whether and in what sense it is necessary to work towards more european systems. the budgetary union is of course important, mr president, because sound public finances are essential and absolutely necessary. the first thing to do with budgetary union is to stick to and implement what we have decided in this house alone: the six-pack. if it is up to us, this week the trialogue in connection with the two-pack should be completed. we also need to work on the revenue side. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the rapid introduction of the ftt and the ccctb, whether or not through closer cooperation, is essential. parliament can be in favour of a gradual transition to a debt relief fund if the strict conditions are met, because we certainly cannot risk an adventure in this area. and, of course, we must complete everything through a stronger economic union to really aim at sustainable economic growth with more employment and greater social cohesion. we need structural reform now to reap the benefits in the future. we must have a better business climate, we must have a sense of investment, we must complete the internal market, we must have a midterm review of the 2020 strategy and we must of course also call for increased attention, and we are doing so, to a number of basic social principles which we have incorporated into a social pact. and that fourth building block: the commission has already taken a number of measures to improve the quality of the information provided by the member states. it is of course essential, because without a democratic foundation no policy can stand the test of time. president. - ladies and gentlemen, what we have to do today cannot be delayed. we will make clear choices today and then it will be up to the heads of state or government.
for the greens/efa group mr president, i think we all now understand why we are talking about the 'four presidents' and not the 'five presidents minus one'. they did not want to involve parliament because we are well aware of the difference between a paper by van rompuy, which becomes more and more vague with each edition, and the one that marianne thyssen is putting forward on behalf of us all. the difference in ambition is glaring. it is obvious that either the european parliament would have found itself very uncomfortable among a group of presidents who were ultimately very unambitious, or it would have had difficulty in making its voice heard because it was clearly in the minority with this level of ambition. marianne thyssen has made it clear: europe needs a federal seal. none of us can do it alone, not even germany! i hope that the economic turnaround that is now hitting, including germany, will help our german friends to realize that we are all in this together. marianne thyssen shows ambition in all aspects of what this federal europe should become. on the budgetary side, marianne thyssen dares, on behalf of us all, to challenge a number of taboos and, in particular, to make it clear that we must work not only on budgetary expenditure but also on revenue, and that we cannot ensure sufficient budgetary revenue without moving from a competitive to a cooperative register. the same applies to the question of debt pooling, which has obviously become a word that herman van rompuy is no longer able to write. he probably forgot how to spell it or what it means. in any case, parliament dares to write that in its report. it is an absolute necessity. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. how else can we understand the idea of making the objectives of the eu 2020 strategy, which is nothing more than the investment strategy of the european union, more binding? i am very pleased that mrs corien wortmann-kool herself has stressed the need for action in this direction. we cannot, in my view, have an economic policy without having an extremely robust investment component. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption. this is another statement that in other circles is completely taboo. let's see what this week's council has to say. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a welcome one. where a taboo is really broken is on the question of social union. it is true that this is a difficult subject: we all have a different history in terms of social security and social protection systems, but this report affirms the need for a form of social convergence but not downwards. i think this is again an ambitious move by the european parliament. finally, the democratic union. it was on this subject that we were waiting for the 'five presidents minus one' who have already delivered us, before the summer, two reports in which the democratic chapter is becoming increasingly diluted. it is not enough to say in parliament's report that parliament would do well to exchange views with national parliaments from time to time. this text proposes to give this parliament, which is the parliament of the european union and the parliament of the euro area, real powers of decision-making and of forming the broad lines of economic and social policy on our continent. i would just like to conclude by saying that we are running out of time. today, one in four europeans is at risk of poverty and social exclusion. one in four of our fellow citizens! the hungarian speaker reminded us of this. you can't tear apart the fabric of society with impunity. when you add to that this week's reminder from the world bank that we are exploding the physical limits of the planet and that climate change itself carries risks to human existence, i think we need to realize that time is running out and we need to act now. we know that if we do not do this, we risk seeing this continent sink into nothing but violence.
member of the commission. - mr president, i should like to begin by thanking each and every one of you for the quality of this debate, the frankness of your words. at the beginning, mrs berès, who is here, and also mr klute and many other members of parliament, mentioned something very important: the need for balance in everything we do. you mentioned this new social pillar, this social dimension that is missing or would be missing in everything we do. i feel concerned and i think the commission should be concerned by this question. my belief, ladies and gentlemen, is that we have finally brought a comprehensive response to all these crises. that is why, after the summit on 29 june, there was a rather favourable response, a certain calm in the markets, because after two or three years of urgently responding, with our backs to the wall, all the responses have been put together, including those on growth. i think that in order for this to work, in order to win back what mrs thyssen called the battle of trust and hope, we must at all costs include in this overall response, or rather ensure that this overall response does include this pillar or this human, social and civic dimension. in any case, ladies and gentlemen, it was with this conviction that i returned to the commission three years ago. it is with this conviction that i work with my colleagues. and precisely, beyond words, if you look closely at what i have proposed to you in the single market act, with your support and that of your committee on the internal market, you will clearly find this balance with several major levers devoted to social cohesion, services, public services and social entrepreneurship. mr cercas said earlier that he had a blind faith in the markets. i have no blind faith in the markets. i don't believe in self-regulating the markets. we have been either naive in the past or sometimes too confident in this self-regulation, in this deregulation, in the last 15 or 20 years. we can learn from this. the 28 texts i have proposed to you, on the lessons of the crisis - as the g20 has asked us to do - are proof that we are restoring order, governance, transparency and simply morality where they have disappeared. i also want to say more specifically that, in the context of economic and monetary union, we must strengthen coordination in employment policy and social policy as a key factor in the success of economic and monetary union. you will find this concern in the union 2020 objectives, with several priorities on employment, education, social inclusion, in the european semester which gives them priority importance. i also believe that in the effort to maintain budgetary discipline and stability, which is logically required of many member states, particularly in the euro area, we must be very selective in order to preserve solidarity expenditure or expenditure that preserves or prepares for the future, particularly in the area of education and research. i would like to say to mr gualtieri that i agree that we must improve ex ante coordination of budgetary policy, which is one of the keys to economic coordination. mrs wortmann-kool, mr strejček and, more recently, mr zalba bidegain have all referred to banking union. i want to confirm that in building this banking union we are starting with the first stone, namely supervision, and i remind you that there are other elements of this banking union on your table, in terms of deposit guarantees or the resolution of banking crises. we are also - i say to mrs wortmann-kool - very careful about the calibration of the measures we are taking to consolidate the banks. it's one of the keys to stability. this good calibration is also one of the keys to banks returning to their role of financing the economy, the territories and, in particular, small and medium-sized enterprises. in this spirit, mr president, i shall be engaging in a debate with you in the coming weeks on the financing of longterm investment in the european union through a green paper. i would like to reply to mr hoang ngoc who, a moment ago, criticized the commission for being, in a way, the cause of the more supervision'. this is precisely what the commission does in all its proposals. i think we should be fair to each other, even if you're right to be demanding. i'll be answering several of you by telegraph. to mrs swinburne: yes, we must build a clear and solid legal framework for the banking union. you talk about the big banks, but there are not only big banks. i want to remind you that many of the banks that have been or are currently in trouble - northern rock, bankia, dexia - are not among the big or very big banks. there is therefore a need for effective supervision, in general and in the euro area in particular, of banks that present risks. to mr klinz: i do not think that parliament is being left out of the banking union, even though the idea for the proposal came from the he is seeking the presidency of the council. - the mr president, i will try to be brief. i have listened carefully to all the speeches, comments and questions put by the honourable members of the european parliament. i would like to refer to mr simon's intervention on the deposit guarantee scheme. we recognise the importance and role of the system as one of the key elements of the single rulebook that will underpin the banking union. the discussions on this proposal are inextricably linked to the proposal on the resolution and recovery of banks. the member states therefore consider that the deposit guarantee scheme cannot be promoted unless the financing details are clarified in this proposal. i will certainly, however, convey your labelling to the relevant minister. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the establishment of a european centre for the development of vocational training, the establishment of a european centre for vocational training and the establishment of a european centre for vocational training. the october european council agreed to explore ways of ensuring a proper debate in the european semester, both in the european parliament and in national parliaments. i would add that the member states which are parties to the treaty on stability, coordination and governance in economic and monetary union intend to improve cooperation between national parliaments and the european parliament. the european council in brussels in december last year, in the context of the european council in brussels, expressed the hope that the european union would be able to take action in the field of social policy, and that the european council in brussels in december would be able to take action in the field of social policy. austerity policies take this into account as far as possible; the commission has also taken the view that the community's financial resources should be used to support the development of the common agricultural policy. my country is no exception. it is clearly very important that we continue to have a strong dialogue on this subject between ourselves, that is between the two institutions that we represent. the deepening of economic and monetary union will require a more active involvement of all the institutions, including this parliament. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i think we all agree that an ambitious european plan must be adopted at the european council in december and that, as mrs thyssen has said, the new measures to be included in this plan must mark the beginning of a renewed relationship of trust. we believe that this meeting is very useful and that it demonstrates in practice that the european parliament has a very substantial role to play in the whole process, both in the basis and in the mid-term evaluation of mrs thyssen's report. the cooperation between the european parliament and the council is already very close and mr van rompuy has on many occasions invited mr schulz to present the european parliament's positions. the council wishes to involve the european parliament actively and makes every effort to strengthen its voice. i can assure you that we are listening to your concerns and taking them into account in our work, while looking forward to further strengthening our cooperation. thank you for this interesting discussion. i shall convey to the council and to the president of the european council the issues raised.
- i'm the reporter. president. - i have three points to make. i would like to thank the presidency of the council and commissioner barnier for the positive words on the report, but i would also like to express the hope that we can end the journey towards genuinely strengthening economic and monetary union in the same positive cooperation. because in the end, actions speak louder than words. secondly, this report is the product of intense collaboration between many colleagues. firstly, the rapporteur, mr gualtieri, the rapporteur for opinions of the various committees and all the econ shadow rapporteurs. thank you very much for that, colleagues. thirdly, mr president, i should like to say something about constructive cooperation. constructive cooperation is not only pleasant and not only good because it is pleasant, but it is good above all because it is a testimony to the fact that we want the determined in this house, and that the political skills are also present to write a vision that we share, that we want to share across national borders and across party and group boundaries. that, ladies and gentlemen, is what the citizens expect of us. we are their representatives. we must fight for our rights to make their voices heard, but above all we must find solutions to their problems. people do not want an endless game of ping-pong between institutions or parties, they want us to be able to overcome differences of opinion by means of an exchange of views, and above all they want all these exchanges of views to lead to actual decisions. and that brings me back to my starting point: actions speak louder than words and we will all be judged by them.
the commission’s latest survey on economic recovery in industry shows that since 2008 almost 10% of jobs in the manufacturing industry have been lost. last year industrial production was 3% lower than the year before. however, there is some good news as the value of exports went up last year. but at the same time we know that our industrial base is changing. this requires companies to change their business models, and workers to change their skills and become more flexible and it also requires governments to make their regulations and policies much more modern and to display a clear vision. my question is therefore twofold. how can the commission help in a very tangible way to create a context in which markets will rejuvenate and modernise existing industries and, at the same time, allow the process of creative destruction to go ahead, do its work and create new niche markets? i think that additional tools have to be found to make additional finance for r&d available and also to bring this r&d into the market and bridge the value of that for new products. also regarding workers’ skills, i think the commission should really push forward to ensure that member states implement new policies to ensure that workers acquire the new skills needed for new industries.
mr president, i would just like to ask one question. we all know that our industry is going through a process of transformation. it is therefore really important not only to stimulate r&d, but also to bring those products to the market. this is an area where europe is clearly lagging behind compared to other continents. so maybe i could ask an additional question on that; how will the commission help, not only to promote r&d, but also to bring those products and services to the market?
