the rules state that when there is no debate on the subject, there is no explanation of vote. i am therefore sorry, but i cannot give you the floor, under the rules. i'm sorry about that.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. the proper functioning of the internal market requires clear rules on jurisdiction and the improvement and acceleration of the recognition and enforcement of judgments in civil and commercial matters. the speed of procedures and legal certainty are essential requirements in this respect at a time when the increase in trade between individuals and economic operators from different member states is leading to an increase in litigation.
in writing. on tuesday 20 november, a large majority of meps voted in favour of the report presented by jan mulder to restrict private access to chemicals. if the text is adopted by the council, it will become much more difficult to buy large quantities of chemicals, which can be used to make homemade bombs. while this is clearly not a sufficient guarantee to prevent the construction of homemade bombs with complete certainty, it is nevertheless an important step in the right direction.
in writing. handmade explosives are among the weapons of terrorists. chemicals that are generally considered harmless and readily available to the general public can be used in the manufacture of dangerous explosive devices. it is therefore important to limit private users' access to high-risk chemicals through a system of licensing and registration of transactions. the commission is proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. the illegal manufacture of improvised explosives should therefore be made more difficult by setting concentration limits for substances which may be used as precursors to explosives. the proposal as such will not stop all terrorists or guarantee total protection, but it will significantly limit access to these products.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report so that substances or mixtures which could be used in a circumventing way for the illegal manufacture of explosives are regulated in a harmonised and non-differentiated way between member states. in particular, i stress the urgency of formulating rules which ensure at national and community level the safety of workers and the protection of the environment, areas not covered by this regulation.
in writing. this regulation is particularly urgent: it should enter into force at the latest on the date of accession of croatia, otherwise recruitment, especially of linguists, will be compromised, and this will affect the ability of the institutions to work in croatian, which will then have become an official language of the union. croatian officials and other servants may be recruited only from the effective date of accession of croatia. the croatian staff recruited before that date will have contractual status, at least as things stand.
in writing. i am pleased that the european parliament has given its consent to the ratification by the european union of the protocol for the protection of the mediterranean sea against pollution (...), or the 'offshore protocol'. as the envi committee has already said, it is essential to do everything possible to avoid oil and gas accidents in the mediterranean such as the deepwater horizon accident and to ensure 'greater safety in oil and gas activities'. while this protocol alone will not solve the problem of the sustainable management of mediterranean resources, it is already a first step in this direction, which will have to be followed by others.
in writing. the importance of preserving the environment, alongside economic development, is quite clear, especially when it comes to a semi-enclosed sea like the mediterranean. i am a firm believer in the principle that freedom of enterprise must be guaranteed so that it can develop anywhere and at any time. but freedom of enterprise must also be combined with security measures. only in this way could the natural environment be preserved and businesses based not on short-term profit but on long-term development be able to operate.
in writing. i am very sensitive to issues relating to nature conservation. i therefore voted in favour of this text. a policy of sustainable governance of offshore oil and gas activities is, i believe, indispensable! the need for increased safety of offshore oil and gas activities is dramatically illustrated by recent accidents, especially the oil spill caused by the deepwater horizon oil rig in the gulf of mexico in 2010: it is likely that offshore activities will develop in the mediterranean following the discovery of new fossil fuel reserves and such an accident could have immediate and wide-ranging effects in a semi-enclosed sea such as the mediterranean. it would also have dramatic consequences for its fragile economy and ecosystems and would undermine the efforts of the mediterranean coastal member states of the union to achieve and maintain the good ecological status of their marine waters required by the marine strategy framework directive.
in writing. the current fisheries partnership agreement with greenland appears to be consistent with the objectives of the common fisheries policy. i also think it is important that this agreement is in line with the principle of sustainable fishing. it is mutually beneficial and represents an interesting milestone for cooperation between the union and greenland, as well as for nordic cooperation in the field of fisheries resources.
in writing. the common market must not only be an economic and social area but also a political one: it is important that the european union ensures that european citizens can easily stand for election to the european parliament in a state other than their state of origin. i therefore welcome the introduction of simplified procedures in this respect, after several years of deadlock at council level. it is now essential to ensure that these measures can be implemented in practice in 2014.
in writing. − this report deals with a question raised during the last european parliament elections in 2009, namely whether a candidate can stand for election in a member state of which he is not a national. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the european parliament is not a matter of where you come from, but of how much you are willing to do for a particular region. today, many eu citizens choose to leave their country of origin and establish a centre of life in another country. if someone decides to do so, i do not see any reason why he should not be able to take part in political activities there. i therefore welcome the improvement, albeit slight, through administrative simplification envisaged in this report. this concerns the administrative authorities, which must check whether a candidate has lost his right to vote in his own member state. this simplification is to take effect for the next elections in 2014.
in writing. i support the draft council directive amending directive 93/109/ec of 6 december 1993 as regards certain procedures for the exercise of the right to vote and to stand as a candidate in elections to the european parliament for citizens of the union residing in a member state of which they are not nationals. the amendment reduces part of the burden on national authorities to check whether someone has been prevented from standing as a candidate in their own state. this amendment will facilitate the situation in terms of administrative convenience for the potential candidate and for the states concerned.
in writing. it is important to relax the obligations imposed on union citizens residing in a member state of the european union of which they are not nationals in order to stand for election to the european parliament. it is necessary to allow candidates to stand in more than one constituency in the same election to the european parliament, regardless of residence qualifications, and to improve the regulation of general voting rights in the states of residence.
in writing. − companies offering consumer credit must provide consumers with correct and clear information about their offers. however, the implementation of the eu consumer credit directive by member states by mid-2010 is still lacking. it was found that 70% of the websites where financial institutions offer consumer credit agreements either lack important information or misrepresent the cost. this is especially true for crossborder loans, where easy-to-understand information on foreign currency loans and the cost of ancillary services is essential in the choice of loan. i very much welcome the discovery of the existing shortcomings in the implementation of the consumer credit directive. this is another example of the eu creating a single directive from 27 different national rules to benefit consumers, but the member states are not implementing it correctly, thus missing the purpose of the directive, which is to protect consumers.
in writing. in times of economic crisis, consumption is the most appropriate way to boost the economy and productivity. given that household consumption forms the basis of general consumption, it is all the more important to ensure consumer protection and continuity of consumption. this is only possible if there are common measures in the member states. given the cross-border nature of borrowing, the availability of information on the possibilities of consumption, but also on the risks, is important. to this end, i find measures (1) requiring financial institutions to provide consumers with personalised, complete and easily understandable information on the risks associated with foreign currency loans, well before they are contractually bound, to be entirely appropriate. (2) to encourage financial institutions to pay particular attention to the granting of consumer credit repayable over five years and not to grant any consumer credit guaranteed by the consumer's home when the consumer does not have a sufficient salary.
in writing. as belgium, one of the six founding member states of the european union, has just concluded its 2013 budget without an ambitious stimulus plan in terms of growth and consumer purchasing power, the european parliament today adopted a motion for a resolution on consumer credit agreements. this vote is important in terms of consumer protection. the european union countries were supposed to implement this directive by 11 june 2010. however, some member states, including belgium, have been late in transposing all or some of the provisions of the directive. the delay was mainly due to the short transposition period of two years and the size of the legislation concerned. the directive has two objectives: (1) to ensure a high level of consumer protection and (2) to strengthen the internal market for crossborder consumer credit. parliament called on the commission to examine the transposition of the directive and to make a clear assessment of its impact on citizens in the context of the financial crisis we are currently experiencing.
in writing. − while there is still work to be done, the 2008 consumer credit directive contains all the elements necessary to better protect consumers from banks and other creditors. that is the conclusion of the interim evaluation report which is before us and which i voted for. the directive provides for (pre-) contractual information for consumers, harmonisation of the 14-day cooling-off period, protection in the event of early repayment of debts and the obligation to check consumers' creditworthiness in advance. the mid-term evaluation highlights problems with the transposition of the directive. there are specific problems with the information obligation: not all lenders inform consumers correctly, or give too much detailed information that is no longer understandable. the right to change your mind is not always respected, there is still no uniformity in the assessment of the creditworthiness of customers and there is a growing problem with sms loans, especially for vulnerable groups and young people. the ability to pay back early works pretty well. i agree with the rapporteur that all the elements are in the directive, but that there is a need for better monitoring of the transposition and implementation of the law.
in writing. as the member of parliament responsible for consumer protection, i believe that the consumer credit directive pursues two objectives which are of paramount importance to the consumer: a high level of consumer protection and the strengthening of the crossborder internal market for consumer credit. the commission should now look at the transposition of the directive and insist on its correct application in the member states. finally, a thorough evaluation of the legal and practical implications of the directive should be carried out before considering, if necessary, amendments to the directive on the basis of this evaluation.
in writing. the economic and social crisis europe is currently experiencing cannot be addressed by setting stricter budgetary criteria alone. while this is necessary, we must also give hope to all citizens by ensuring that they have jobs and a decent life. i am therefore pleased that parliament has voted in favour of a renewed approach to social investment in europe, one which considers social investment not as sterile costs or expenditure but as investment expenditure, the dividends of which will benefit the whole community.
in writing. we are going through a serious economic and financial crisis. it is likely to have long-term and severe effects on many citizens. the european economy, but also the employment rate, public saving and social investment in europe are directly affected by the slowdown. most recent and current responses to the crisis are based on short-term objectives to bring public finances back to a sustainable level, which is essential for the union's economy. in this context, however, we must not overlook the negative effects that these austerity measures could have on employment and competitiveness in europe and we must combine them with measures that are favourable to growth and employment, such as well-targeted social investment.
in writing. i am in favour of promoting local markets and short-term supply chains in areas which may require a european-wide approach. individual projects must be financed under rural development policy, which can be adapted to local situations. on the other hand, promotional actions should draw attention to a 'taste of europe' that is both authentic, varied and of high taste quality for the consumer. this approach applies to internal as well as external promotion. the union must strive to export a sustainable model of agriculture and the products derived from it. consumers often buy "concepts" when choosing their food, as the example of fair trade products illustrates.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. the 'right of succession' directive was designed with a dual objective: to ensure that authors of graphic and plastic works of art have an economic share in the success of their creations, on the one hand, and to harmonise the application of the right of succession in the european union, on the other. the report calls on the commission to work closely with stakeholders to strengthen the position of the european art market and to address certain difficulties, such as the 'cascade effect'.
in writing. i voted in favour of these amendments. in the wake of the economic and financial crisis, parliament was keen to join its efforts with those of the member states and undertook to take a series of measures to achieve savings. the three amendments are, in short, about the irrelevant, because underused, translations of the cre.
in writing. the importance of the motorcycle as a means of transport continues to grow from year to year. it is therefore necessary to lay down transparent, simple, harmonised and consistent rules on the safety, type-approval and environmental protection of such vehicles which are beneficial for both undertakings and users. drivers of these vehicles are a vulnerable group, with one of the highest rates of injury and death among all road users. it is therefore important to introduce essential and effective safety measures in order to provide drivers with vehicles equipped with the safest technologies at a reasonable price. i also share the view that strict limits on emissions of pollutants should be introduced.
in writing. - i welcome this report because it replaces the 24 directives currently in force on the construction of agricultural and forestry vehicles, such as tractors, trailers and the equipment pulled by them, by only one. this reduction in regulation ensures efficient and forward-looking simplification in this sector, as it has also been ensured that the current level of protection is not lowered. this level of protection covers safety and environmental aspects which are now being harmonised across the eu, thus also preventing fragmentation of the internal market due to different national product standards.
in writing. i would like to highlight the excellent work of my colleague mr panzeri on this issue. i agree with his conclusions. it has worked on the commission's proposal and ensured that the result is to further promote the competitiveness of the sector by simplifying the current legislation, while taking into account our opinion in the committee on the internal market and consumer protection. agricultural and forestry tractors may be excluded from the scope of the machinery directive provided that the new regulation and its delegated acts transfer the provisions laid down in that directive, which currently apply to tractors, in order to avoid any legal loophole and to ensure that the current level of safety is maintained.
in writing. the diversity of payment instruments is constantly expanding both at european and global level. while new modes of using internet and mobile payments appear to be emerging rapidly and are of interest to consumers and businesses alike, even the card payment market is still far from exploiting its full potential and efficiency. the markets for card, internet and mobile payments are still fragmented in the european union. there are also significant differences between member states in the use of card payments. the process of creating the single euro payments area (eupe) facilitates bank transfers in 32 european countries, and by 2014 all participating countries will have to have implemented the common eupe standards. in my view, since the eupe will bridge the gap between national and crossborder payments, the aim of integrating the card payments market should make crossborder payments as convenient as payments at national level. it would be good to have more crossborder actors in this field.
in writing. at a time when belgium has just recapitalised dexia to the tune of eur 2.9 billion, shadow banking, a parallel banking system covering entities not subject to banking regulation (securitisation funds, hedge funds, commercial banks, money funds, etc.) remains problematic. although the g20 adopted a series of regulatory measures aimed at making the financial system safer and more sustainable following the financial crisis of 2007, this shadow finance, connected to the conventional banking system, is still poorly or not very well regulated. valued at €67 trillion in 2011 (compared with €26 trillion in 2002), half (46%) of this is supplied by europe, a trend that is continuing to increase. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competition' in the context of the directive on the protection of the environment.
in writing. in order to have a complete picture of the shadow banking system, more and more reliable data and analytical capabilities are urgently needed. the ecb should play an important role in this respect. the initiative to create a data bank for pension transactions can be decisive in achieving transparency. in addition, in order to shed light on risk transfers in the financial sector and to be able to determine who has bought what, from whom and with what support the transferred risks are associated, the commission should urgently examine whether the creation of a public body is feasible. in addition, this data analysis exercise should identify those aspects of the shadow banking system that have a positive impact on the real economy and those that present systemic risks or raise concerns in regulatory arbitrage, such as excessive maturity or liquidity transformation, leverage and regulatory arbitrage. once these systemic risks have been identified, it seems to me essential to be able to deal with them by improving their control and regulation. my colleague el khadraoui proposes in this text a number of very interesting ideas.
in writing. internet is becoming more and more important in most households. the child discovers the canvas at a very early age, before even entering school and learning to read and write. these young internet users are therefore exposed to multiple risks, such as child pornography and sexual exploitation, which must be curbed at european level. i think that, in the same way that it is impossible to put a "gendarme" behind every "deviant and perverted" user, it is equally difficult to put a parent behind every child. the majority of computer skills are acquired well before the age of 18, and the changing family lifestyle means that teenagers, sometimes left to themselves or left alone at home after school, are now receiving sexual proposals more often than in the past. this is why parents, teachers, civil society, internet service providers, businesses and public authorities need to work together to protect children from online abuse and crime. because the whole problem of the growing vulnerability of young people on the internet and social networks must be taken into account, it seemed to me more than legitimate and even necessary to support this report.
in writing. the protection of children, especially on the internet and social networks, is a major policy issue that needs to be extended at the level of the european union. that is why i welcome the adoption this afternoon by the european parliament of a resolution on the protection of children in the digital world. the internet revolution is becoming more and more important in most families, often leaving parents baffled by the technological "precocity" of their children. most computer skills are acquired well before the age of 18. young people on the internet are exposed at an increasingly early age to the dangers of digital technology: violent games and videos, but also and especially the risks of coming into contact with deviant adults and even members of child pornography networks. so, of course, it is not a question of putting a "gendarme" behind each user, nor of imposing on parents a systematic control of their child "surfing the web", but of having a global approach. parents, teachers, internet service providers, businesses, public authorities and law enforcement agencies should also join forces to best protect children, who are particularly vulnerable and exposed to the most serious abuses, on the internet and social networks.
in writing. i am in full support of this text which deals with a theme which should not be underestimated. as a reminder, a few figures that i think are very striking: young europeans spend up to 40 hours a week watching television, they spend 88 minutes a day on the internet, usually from the age of nine, but some are exposed to the internet much earlier, before they even enter school and learn to read and write. member states need to commit to protecting children in the digital world. the european citizens should also be given the opportunity to better understand the cultural and economic dimension of all types of media related to digital technology and to contribute to the achievement of the lisbon objectives by fostering the emergence of a knowledge-based economy and boosting competitiveness.
in writing. - europe is in crisis. there are more and more unemployed, more and more elderly, more and more people in need of care, more and more poverty, and more than ever there is a need for a greener economy that takes care of the planet and its people. to meet these challenges, the european parliament proposes to support the necessary investments and experimental projects in social entrepreneurship. the key measures proposed by the commission can provide a useful basis for this, for example a map of social entrepreneurship in europe, the creation of a certification database or extensive information platforms on mutual learning. the development of a kind of quality label for social enterprises could also be very helpful for their further development. social enterprises have a public utility, a social utility, and social and environmental effects. they have enhanced profit-margin or employee participation models, as well as principles of openness and transparency. the report rightly advocates orderly competition (no competition in price or quality between densely populated areas and rural areas), social banking and social franchise models. social enterprises deserve our support, they are key players in the social economy and social innovation.
in writing. i supported the becker report to accelerate the establishment of a multilingual platform to enable team learning and the exchange of best practice which should facilitate the sharing of information on access to finance and training opportunities and serve as a network for cross-border cooperation. in addition, the proposed measures, such as a map of social entrepreneurship in europe, a certification database or large-scale information platforms for mutual learning, could prove particularly useful.
in writing. in these times of economic and social crisis, it is vital that europe starts to create hope again. i therefore welcome the vote on the report by my colleague, mrs marianne thyssen, on economic and monetary union. as the report states, it is essential to add a social dimension to the stricter monitoring of budgetary policies, which cannot be an end in itself. i also welcome the emphasis on the need to strengthen democratic legitimacy (i.e. of the european parliament) in return for greater economic and budgetary integration.
in writing. i am in favour of greater parliamentary control over the four strands of emu: banking union, budgetary union, economic union and political union. as guy verhofstadt has written: 'the euro can only survive if a federal union is established with a european government setting economic, fiscal and fiscal policy.' the report reinforces the democratic character of the institutions by emphasising the participation of citizens and national parliaments; by requiring all new authorities to report regularly; by establishing an open procedure for the appointment of the president of the governing council of the ecb. the authority would then be obliged to explain its decisions to the national parliaments.
in writing. - i voted in favour of the thyssen report because it sends a strong message to the european council: europe must be more democratic and more social. to restore confidence in the euro, stronger european institutions are needed: i have been promoted from european finance minister to european banking supervisor. we must also strengthen democratic control. a future european finance minister is accountable to the european parliament on an individual basis. the president of the european banking supervision mechanism, which is due to come into force as early as 2013, needs to be approved and monitored by the ep. the european semester, the procedure for assessing the financial and economic policies of the eu countries, must be democratized. the ep must have control over the guidelines adopted by the commission. national parliaments must discuss in advance the reform plans submitted by their governments to the commission. the reforms, cuts and investments that governments commit to under the european semester should be more rigorously assessed for their impact on employment and social protection. so, in addition to a banking union, a budgetary union, an economic union and a democratic union, there is a fifth pillar: the social union. the eu must not be blind to the social consequences of the crisis: poverty and unemployment are increasing.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution but i want to be very clear: there can be no complete economic and monetary union without democracy and a social pact. if this is not the case, the result will be increased competition between member states and social dumping. and as for the federal transition, i can assure you, it cannot be successful without solidarity, without social progress and without democratic progress!
in writing. − as the economic and monetary links between the european union become closer, coordination of the economic policy-making of the member states is important. it is equally important that the institutions which ensure the functioning of monetary union are transparent and subject to democratic control. at the same time, i cannot agree with several sections of the action plan. first, the gradual creation of a debt-relief fund, which would accumulate public debt exceeding 60% of the gdp of individual euro-area member states, would reduce the individual responsibility of the countries. it is already apparent that some member states are unable to enact the necessary legislative measures to reduce their external debt. the creation of a common fund does not repay its debt. secondly, i do not support the long-term plan for a common issuance of debt securities in the euro area. eurobonds will allow the weaker countries to continue to borrow and spend while raising the cost of borrowing for the rest of the countries that adhere to eu fiscal conditions. in view of the above arguments, the ep report to the commission on 'moving towards a genuine economic and monetary union' was not voted on.
in writing. - i am still amazed that the single answer to the eurocrisis in this parliament is always the same: more of the same, more europe, more decisionmaking power in the european institutions. those who, on the basis of deceit and lies, allowed totally unsuitable countries into the eurozone have learned nothing from their mistakes. the question remains whether it is logical or even practicable for countries with very different labour markets and very different political preferences or social and fiscal traditions to be brought together. we have experience of this in belgium, and then it is only two peoples. that is why i did not approve this report. the solution today is not more europe but, on the contrary, less europe.
that concludes the explanations of vote.
mr president, i should like to begin by thanking our two rapporteurs, as is the custom, but we tend to forget this a little. we have forgotten to do so because our debate today is so controversial and polemical. this shale gas exploitation has done a great deal of good for the us economy, which has gone from being an importer of natural gas to a potential exporter in seven years. a providential manna but which, as we have heard, creates controversy. i shall not repeat all that has been said, but i will perhaps mention the risk of divesting from renewable energy research and thus freezing the fight against global warming. the union must therefore adopt a realistic, open but cautious approach. no blank checks to the oil industry, no insults to the future and innovation. the commission must draw inspiration from the american experience in order not to repeat the mistakes of the past, namely the environmental cost of fracking, which is far too high, and the waste of water, which has of course already been mentioned. we must also take account of our european reality, which is that of a densely populated territory with very different legislation on basements. all this to say that - i conclude, mr president, we are an hour late, so allow me another ten seconds - shale gas can only be a possible complement to the european energy mix, as commissioner oettinger has just mentioned.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament. the essential question before us is not who is influenced by which lobbyist, but is about 'what is the future of shale gas in the global context of the energy debate'. that is why we have two commissioners in front of us who must guide us and also help us to find a solution. first of all: we must decide very quickly within the european union what the long-term strategy is in the field of energy. let us be clear about this - at least as regards the options of my group - and i think of several groups in this parliament. if we look at the long term, we have to make sure that by 2050, we have a fully renewable energy system. and yes, gas will be an important part of that. the question is: what kind of gas and under what conditions? and then the other commissioner, mr potočnik, comes into view, because he has to ensure - despite all the emotions in this house - that if we ever go all the way to exploiting shale gas - i have my questions about that, i am also sceptical about that - but if we do, it will be within a strict regulatory framework, which takes into account the impact on our environment and the impact on our climate. let us wait for that to happen and let the emotions settle down a little and make sure that the european framework is tight and good, as we have done in the past. i think that the future is energy and environmental policy when it comes to this subject.
president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, i am not going to repeat what has already been said by many colleagues. we have already noted some very sensible things, proposals and comments. for example, mr seeber says that we are not going to have the illusion that we, as the european parliament, are going to stop the extraction and exploitation of shale gas. some of my colleagues would like that, but we will not succeed. that is why, ladies and gentlemen, we must of course do everything we can to ensure that this extraction is properly regulated. we know the great potential of shale gas, especially in the united states. the effects of this have already been clearly demonstrated. so let us opt for the framework, for the proper application of eu law. let us ensure that no chemical is used for shale gas anywhere in europe that is not covered by reach legislation. mr president, of course shale gas is useful in reducing our dependence on foreign energy suppliers from outside the eu. and commissioner oettinger has rightly pointed out that this is an important element. but of course it should not relieve us of our obligation to continue to work on renewable energy, and especially, commissioner, as you have said, on energy efficiency. so even the availability of shale gas in the future should not put us to sleep and prevent us from further efforts to increase energy efficiency. so in summary, mr president, we must go for a balanced, for a coordinated, but also for a cautious approach with clear rules of the game to be agreed at european level, and all member states must apply an environmental impact assessment to every eu project.
member of the commission. − mr president, in the last few months a number of restructuring processes have been announced in many industrial sectors in various member states. 274 cases of restructuring were registered in the third quarter of 2012, according to the latest data from the european restructuring monitor. these cases amount to a net job loss of more than 74 000, thus showing a reversed situation compared to the pre-crisis one, which saw the net creation of 6 600 jobs in the third quarter of 2007 for the same number of restructuring cases. these figures reflect the current difficult macro-economic situation linked to the sovereign debt crisis but probably also a structural change in the european economy and labour market. this requires clear policy action. more than ever, good anticipation of change and responsible management of restructuring is crucial. if not managed properly, restructuring operations can have negative consequences for the economy of the surrounding area, incur high social costs across regions and negatively affect workers and their families. the situation is particularly worrying in the automotive sector, where major restructuring operations have been announced in recent months. there is a significant risk that more such announcements will be made in the coming months. the latest announcements concern psa and ford. psa has announced its intention to close its plant in aulnay-sous-bois and to restructure the plant in rennes. ford has announced its intention to close its plant in genk in belgium as well as the plants in southampton and dagenham in the united kingdom. the total amount, including indirect job losses in the supply chain, could be as much as 22 000 job losses. moreover, announcements were also made by general motors, volvo and, in the supply chain, by faurecia. while the difficulties of the automotive industry are linked to the situation on european markets, it has to be noted that the industry also suffers from long-standing structural overcapacities. the commission is ready to help member states and all stakeholders to mobilise instruments and policies that can help the sector to restructure and build a sustainable future. most urgently, the commission wants to explore all possibilities to support workers affected by restructuring or likely to be affected in the future. the commission has already spelled out in detail its strategy for the automotive sector in the cars 2020 action plan, adopted on 8 november 2012. in this strategic document, the commission announces how it intends to conduct its policies supporting the sector in many different policy fields: research, development and innovation, trade policy, a better functioning internal market, investment in human capital and the anticipation of change and restructuring. the objective is to boost the competitiveness of the industry. some actions will thus produce effects in the longer term; others, however, can be implemented already. the competitiveness council of 10 december 2012 will focus discussions on these, with a view also to minimising the social and economic consequences of announced restructuring. in the field of anticipation of change, the commission will support the creation of a european automotive skills council, which will bring together existing national organisations conducting research on skills and employment in the automotive sector. the commission will also support the creation of a social dialogue committee that should bring together manufacturers and suppliers representing workers and employers at the joint request of all interested parties. with regard to overcapacity and restructuring, it has to be stressed that this is mainly the responsibility of the industry, but the commission also has a role to play. first of all, the commission will monitor restructuring activities to ensure their strict compliance with eu legislation, in particular concerning state aid and internal market rules. the commission also expects each individual company to comply with the rules established in the directives on information and consultation and on collective redundancies, as well as with any existing company agreements. secondly, the commission will make all possible efforts to minimise the negative social effect of restructuring, which is the core of the commission’s message today. for that purpose, the commission will encourage the use of the european social fund for retraining and re-skilling of workers in the new financial perspective of the structural funds. the commission will also encourage the member states to use the european globalisation adjustment fund, the egf. it is important to note that the automotive sector is already the sector with the highest number of egf cases – namely 16 – and that almost 21 000 automotive workers have been allocated egf assistance worth a total of eur 113 million. in this context, it would be more important then ever to keep a reinforced egf in the multiannual financial framework (mff) after 2014. this is far from being guaranteed and i count on your support in this regard. in order to ensure policy consistency, the commission will consider re-launching the inter-service restructuring task force, which will bring together the member states, regional authorities, industry and the social partners to study and follow up the main cases of automotive plant closures or significant downsizing. the task force would streamline the use of the relevant eu funds by providing technical assistance, reducing waiting time, advising on the most effective use of resources, monitoring and reporting. in addition, for each region affected by a plant closure or a significant downsizing, the commission will advise, facilitate and liaise with a partnership of regional stakeholders, led by local authorities who would pool the resources available and prepare a complete package of measures such as measures for workers made redundant, support for reconversion of suppliers, community support, measures to develop technology, and so on and so forth. in order to benefit from existing experiences, the commission also wants to identify good practices and promote an anticipative approach in restructuring, in consultation with representatives of the automotive-intensive regions, employment authorities and the sector’s stakeholders. more generally, the commission will encourage member states to make use of labour flexibility schemes and esf co-financing to support suppliers who may need additional time to find new clients following the closure or downsizing of an automotive plant.
president. - mr president, you have spoken about overcapacity and restructuring. we are not blind to this, of course, but today we are taking it a little too lightly that we cannot do anything about it at european level, or at your level as the european commission. you talk about monitoring and supervising the application of european legislation, but today multinationals like opel, general motors or ford simply play off the various national subsidy systems or see where they can produce the cheapest at the lowest social cost, and then simply move their production to that place. that is the ultimate disregard for the inadequacy of the european internal market when companies start to relocate on the basis of the size of the national market, and that cannot of course be the intention. it also illustrates, commissioner, in a particularly painful way, the appalling lack of minimum social conventions within europe. i refer to article 26 of the european treaty. it expressly provides for the possibility for the european commission to make proposals to ensure the balanced functioning of the internal market and we could use that article perfectly to guide such restructuring. two other points, commissioner, firstly, the new cars 2020 action plan to which you referred expressly provides for measures to assist suppliers affected by the closure of an automobile plant. it is very important that subcontractors can use bridging measures to find new customers. are you prepared to make that work in the short term? finally, commissioner, the european investment bank - to say just one more thing - must stop granting low-interest loans to large companies, large car companies, which then use these subsidies to start producing outside the european union and to relocate. of course, that cannot be the intention. it's naive, it's unacceptable and it has to stop. and can we expect you to take action on that front?
on behalf of the green group, mr president, commissioner, you mentioned cars 2020. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. yes, we need to invest in advanced technologies and clean vehicles. yes, we need better market conditions and financial incentives for the environment. yes, we need better market access. yes, there is a need for training of older and younger workers and for the european social fund to be used, but all this is of course not an answer to the concrete social dramas facing the workers at ford genk, the workers in southampton, the workers in rennes. the reality is that the self-styled giant member states are pitting themselves against each other under the slogan 'one man's death, another man's bread'. the reality is that it involves lay employees being fired or forced to surrender their wages, while ceos are lavishly rewarded with millions in bets and millions in bonuses. the reality is that when a company closure is announced, stock prices go up. the reality is that the car giants are far too low on innovation, on people and on the environment. commissioner, you have mentioned a whole toolbox of possible measures, but you have actually been very vague. can you say very specifically that you will support very concrete initiatives in all the affected regions to bring about a change of direction? can you promise that you and your services will do everything possible to ensure that the tens of thousands of workers at ford, psa and volvo who are likely to be out of work soon will have a new perspective thanks to the political commitment of the european parliament? that is my very specific question.
question for the commissioner: the commission is not i have been here in parliament for a number of years and have witnessed these debates. but i have also been faced with this for all these years and i would like to ask my colleague: how is it that seven or eight years ago such firms received eu, local or national subsidies and six years later they closed their doors? the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. can you imagine that we here in parliament would come together to set even stricter rules, and in these cases even call back public money to pay precisely those whom we are trying to help today?
"blue card" question. what is it? if ford is indeed making such a loss, 1.6 billion, how can you explain, mr president, that the ceo of ford, mr mulally, is being awarded an annual wager of almost 30 million dollars, 30 million dollars a year! and last year, he even got a bonus of $36.3 million! a company that's making such losses shouldn't be paying out that kind of bet to those ceos. do you agree with that?
mr president, the market industry is under very heavy pressure in europe. it is therefore reasonable to expect that the member states and the european union will do everything possible to help the automobile industry, for example, to cope with competition from outside the european union, competition which does not have to follow the same rules. but nothing could be further from the truth. the european investment bank granted a low-cost loan of eur 100 million to ford to set up a plant in kocaeli, turkey. some sources even speak of a loan of eur 190 million to ford in turkey. in other words, the european union is actively financing and supporting the relocation of european companies, such as ford genk. turkey also receives billions from the commission in so-called pre-accession aid. so it was perfectly understandable that some of that money would also be used to move industrial activities out of europe and into countries like turkey. how in the world did it get this far? how do you explain that to the ten thousand people in limburg who are losing their jobs and the thousands of others in, say, southampton and dagenham? are there any other cases where the investment bank or the commission finances factories in low-wage or relative low-wage countries, creating unfair competition with companies in europe? can the commission give more details on the question of how the globalisation fund can be used to help redirect the workers in genk, for example, to help them on the labour market? it is time that concrete answers were found.
mr president, it must be some two years since we were here debating. one of the reasons was the closure of opel in antwerp. i was angry and incredibly outraged. firstly, because so many people are losing their jobs and this has a great impact on their families, but also because the commission did not intervene at the time. now, a good two years later, nothing has really changed. the commission still does not intervene and hides behind a number of powers which it may or may not have. commissioner, the commission can do many things. let me say three things that i really expect the commission to do now. first of all: the commission is perfectly capable of intervening in restructuring, at least by talking to the multinationals and not by setting up the various firms and fortresses against each other. secondly: i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter. thirdly, and this is what really gets the ford employees in genk mad: how on earth is it possible that we are using european investment bank funds to modernise a company outside the european union in turkey, so that further relocation is possible. it is time, commissioner, that you and your colleagues took an active part in restructuring and in shaping the future of industry.
the announcement of the closure of ford genk is a tragedy for my province of limburg, because no less than 10,000 people will lose their jobs directly or indirectly. in the municipality where i will be mayor from january, more than 700 families have been affected. the flemish government and the limburg provincial government are now joining forces to work out a plan for the future. that's fine, but the question is: where is europe? what is europe doing for the region? i think europe cannot stand by and watch. in a stroke of the pen, a multinational company is indeed moving an entire plant from one european region to another, when it has (a few months ago) made a promise, a serious promise, on paper, for the future of that plant! apparently, none of that is worth much anymore. you have mentioned a number of concrete measures, but i have a question for you about the cohesion fund. limburg as a region needs special help, given the exceptional circumstances. and there are precedents: for example, bavaria received an additional eur 75 million under the current cohesion programme. can limburg expect something similar and will the commission give a signal in this direction in the near future?
mr president, commissioner, since 2008, eighteen applications to mobilise the globalisation adjustment fund have been for restructuring in the automotive sector, amounting to over one billion, with more than 20 000 workers involved. the automotive sector has therefore been a significant part of these mobilizations for several years. it was therefore high time that the commission proposed to take action for this sector in difficulty. is the crisis the only cause of the automobile's difficulties? have not some companies made bad management choices? couldn't we have anticipated this by reorienting earlier and developing a comparative advantage in the market? anticipating restructuring is, in my view - and i agree with alejandro - a necessity. why not make the globalisation adjustment fund a european tool for anticipating restructuring? the united states has a fund with wide powers and does not hesitate to use it to reorient sectors. i have tried to introduce this notion of anticipation in the context of the revision of the regulation, but without success so far. i think that this new orientation of the globalisation adjustment fund could appeal to the member states and i wonder how the commission could support such an initiative to revise the fund.
for the greens/efa group mr president, let me first of all express my thoughts on the workers of florange and liège, some of whom are waiting for a potential buyer and others are faced with a social plan which they consider to be largely inadequate. steel is, however, a very important basic industry in the economy. we must re-industrialize europe, commissioner. it is up to you, the european commission - and why not on 6 december - to launch a 'steel' initiative in connection with the framework programme for research and innovation. a coordinated initiative at european union level which will make the member states cooperate with each other instead of leaving the right of life or death to the large steel groups over subsidiaries, including those that have invested in the tool or in human capital. large groups that profit from non-harmonisation of taxes or, worse still, that play with the advantages they receive. the regulation, commissioner, will not be made by itself. we have to organize it. it is intolerable that co2 emission allowances have been diverted to subsidiaries outside europe or stored in reserve. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament and the council should be given the opportunity to present their views on the proposal. the great champion in all categories of this reservation is obviously arcelormittal, the same company which, in seraing, had made the reopening of a blast furnace conditional on an allocation of emission rights. apart from the steel question, there is of course also the question of the economic redeployment of europe, of an ambitious europe with ambitious policies. what is certain is that this will not be possible without steel, and i would like this steel to be european.
mr president, commissioner, the steel industry is still a sector with a future, let us not hesitate to say so. our workers in this sector have developed a real know-how. steel is the raw material of manufacturing industries which we all want to revive, such as the automobile industry. this is a sector which provides jobs, which contributes significantly to the structuring of the economic fabric and in which major investments have been made, often with the participation of the public sector, in research and development of new steel products with high added value. but let's take a shining example of the deviations of large groups. for several years, the arcelormittal group has been implementing a strategy of gradual destruction of production capital in the moselle-luxembourg-liège steel basin. what are the bases of this strategy? the group closes production sites which are still profitable but encourages competition between them to redirect their investments and rejects the idea of selling the tools, which are still efficient, in order to avoid their being taken over by a potential competitor. we have to act. the commission is sometimes quick to defend the rules of fair and healthy competition. is it not an abusive competitive practice to prevent a recovery? could the commission not also look into such cases?
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, six years ago tony blair, who was then the president of the council, when presenting the multiannual financial framework for 2006–2012 said in this house ‘never again must we repeat the negotiations on this framework in the same way’. but here we are again still talking about the same budget, a budget of more or less 1% of europe’s gdp, and we are still dealing with reluctant britain. the only difference now is that mr blair has been replaced by mr cameron and that the labour party is led now by mr edward miliband who a few weeks ago – for purely populist reasons – made a coalition with the europhobes of the conservative party to make a deal on the eu budget even more difficult, if not impossible. so there we are for the moment. i will not talk about the details of this budget, but let me be very blunt: the whole debate on a little bit more or a little bit less than 1% of the european gdp is completely ridiculous. first of all, the budgets of member states like germany and france are eight to 10 times bigger than the european budget. in recent years they have increased faster than the european budget. the european budget is eur 140 billion. do you know what the total budgets of all the member states of the european union are? eur 6 300 billion, eur 6.3 trillion, that is 50 times bigger than the european budget of eur 140 billion. the daily contribution of citizens to the european budget is 67 cents a day; that is less than a cup of coffee or, for mr callanan, less than a cup of tea. secondly, in the united states, the federal budget is 24% of gdp. switzerland is a confederation and has a budget of 12% of gdp, and we cannot agree on 1%. the european budget is smaller than the budget of smaller member states like belgium or austria. i have the latest figures here: between 2000 and 2010 national budgets in the eu increased by 62%, that is more or less twice as much as the european budget. if we want to make a real federal european union, which we will need to do for tomorrow, the least we can say is that 1% will not be enough in the future. it is a good starting point, but it cannot be the endgame for the future if we want to really tackle the problems. if we want to overcome the euro crisis or if we want to have a general union, we will need more. finally, cuts in the european budget are seen in many of our member states, and certainly in britain, as the way to solve their budgetary problems. but the opposite is true. a strong european budget is the only way to overcome the budgetary problems of the member states: pooling money at european level instead of wasting money at the national level. we have to be very firm on this, and politically this parliament has to be ready to block a deal if it does not correspond to what was proposed by the commission.
mr president, in any democracy in the world, whether annual or multiannual, the budget is the most comprehensive, the most significant political exercise, which expresses a vision or a project. let me ask you about the council's proposal. the european parliament has a duty to ensure that the budgetary authority is able to take account of the needs of the citizens of the union. so, is offering them to do even less really going to relieve national budgets? will doing less really make national budgets much better off? of course not! it is anything but safe, and it will be even less so when we have moved on to own resources. in this case, the council will have to explain to the european citizens which projects can be supported when cuts are envisaged in all areas. it will be necessary to explain that perhaps strategy 2020 is an exercise in style, that research and innovation are a luxury, that economic redeployment in our regions will be achieved with fewer resources for cohesion. good luck explaining that! good luck also in explaining to the europeans who still believe in the european project that we are going to finance solidarity with the east, which is what we must do. good luck explaining how we're going to fund solidarity with southern europe, which is what we have to do. good luck to the net contributors too! do you think the northern countries will do much better with this reduction? of course not! they have a vested interest in seeing the economies of their neighbours improve. the british and european employers seem to have understood this at the last minute. we can ask a lot of parliament and we have done a lot of work in the committee on budgets to really try to come up with a good proposal, one that is solid. we are willing to be flexible, to revise certain things, but we cannot be asked to go below the commission's proposal. parliament will say no to that! i think the council must also know that there is a twenty-eighth partner here who is not prepared to accept a proposal that is below that of the commission.
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, thank you very much for the publicity. mr farage has talked the whole morning about salaries and the biggest waste of money. well, colleagues, what i think is the biggest waste of money in the european union today is the salary we are all paying to mr farage – that is the biggest waste of money! mr farage, let us be honest about it. you are a member of the committee on fisheries, for example, and you are never there, never! in 2011, no attendance. in 2012, no attendance. it is fantastic what you are doing. you come here saying that the salaries that are paid are a scandal, and you pay yourself a salary without doing any work in your own committee: that is the reality of the european union today. (applause) you can laugh. i hope that maybe this can be sent out on the bbc this evening, and on all the other private television channels in britain, showing how you are in fact cheating your own citizens here, all the time, three years in a row already. i think it has to stop. we know that mr alain lamassoure is right. this union, if it wants to become a real federal union, needs own resources; it needs in fact its own income, and that is the big battle now. we shall do it together and, if necessary, without you, mr farage. (applause)
the eu has been under heavy attack lately. it is barely managing the crisis and the divisions between member states are greater than ever, especially when it comes to the new multiannual budget. however, an ambitious agreement is of the utmost importance for the future of the union because the budget is the only solidarity mechanism in the union. and that solidarity mechanism is under pressure. net payers are no longer willing to pay for countries that are in more difficulty, which is putting the whole european construction on the back foot. the financial crisis is undermining that whole solidarity mechanism. it is ironic that it is precisely those countries which feel that 'coupled' austerity is needed that want to prevent member states from being truly solidarity-oriented. we can cut together, but we can't build together. it is therefore essential that the union really has its own resources. otherwise, solidarity within the eu will only be under pressure. and only in this way can we create a european budget with sufficient resources to invest in sustainable growth and jobs. without these investments, we face a lost decade and lost generations that threaten to turn their backs on the eu.
in writing. following the resignation of commissioner dalli, it was essential to appoint a new commissioner quickly who could take on many urgent issues, such as the tobacco products directive, the gmo issue and cosmetic testing. i therefore welcome the vote of parliament in favour of the appointment of tonio borg. the commissioner showed a great deal of knowledge of the matters in his portfolio when he was heard by several parliamentary committees. while i am aware of his various positions, i wish commissioner borg well in his work.
in writing. i'm sorry about this casting! mr. borg, by the words he has spoken, throughout his career, is a man of exclusion far more than of gathering, trampling on gender equality and women's rights, stigmatizing homosexuals or rejecting those who do not share his faith. to be a european commissioner is to be able to represent all europeans. to put forward the candidacy of a man whose positions, throughout his career, were regressive for women's rights and even human dignity, is a mistake and an aberration, even though the crisis already excludes many citizens. we are for a europe of all europeans and not just a few, so we must vote against this appointment.
in writing. in may 2012, the region of emilia-romagna (italy) was hit by a major earthquake that caused severe human, economic, cultural and industrial damage. i therefore felt it was essential that the european union should show solidarity with this devastated region. i am therefore pleased that parliament has shown the necessary budgetary flexibility to provide €670 million in aid to the victims through the solidarity fund.
in writing. as this is a natural disaster with major damage, it is important to act so that this region can restore its infrastructure and rebuild its situation. coping with such an unpredictable situation is certainly not easy for the regions concerned and the states directly concerned. the solidarity fund was created and its purpose is to help in such cases. i also believe that it is extremely important to provide financial assistance from the solidarity fund to victims of natural disasters quickly, and i commend the italian authorities for the speed with which they submitted their application for financial assistance from the solidarity fund and for the rapid submission of the commission's proposal for assistance from the solidarity fund.
in writing. italy requested assistance from the fund following a series of earthquakes that occurred in its territory in may 2012. on 20 may 2012, a powerful earthquake measuring 5.9 on the richter scale struck much of northern italy, causing significant damage in many towns and villages, especially around the epicenter, mainly in the provinces of modena and ferrara, in the emilia-romagna region. on may 29, a second earthquake of 5.8 on the richter scale, with an epicenter slightly farther west, occurred. both earthquakes were followed by many large aftershocks. the earthquakes killed 27 people, injured 350 others, and forced more than 45,000 people to evacuate. it is up to europe to come to the aid of this region after such events.
in writing. it is important to adopt an implementing regulation implementing the provisions of the agreements on the modification of concessions with regard to processed poultry meat, which the european union signed with brazil and thailand in june 2012. in accordance with article xxviii of the general agreement on tariffs and trade (gatt) 1994, mutually satisfactory countervailing measures were found to offset the increase in the consolidated duties. it is also important to take measures to develop international trade and to regulate and standardize it so that all the union's trading partners can benefit from reciprocity. moreover, as the autonomous duties laid down in regulation (eec) no 2658/87 on the tariff and statistical nomenclature and on the common customs tariff are higher than the current consolidated tariff within the wto, the rates of the 'pre-cycle' autonomous duties of uruguay should be increased to the usual customs duty rates.

in writing. effective monitoring of the application of eu law is crucial. citizens of the union must be assured that they can enjoy their fundamental rights within the eu. the rule of law, which is a prerequisite for the legitimacy of any form of democratic governance, forms the basis of european states. the role of citizens as complainants is essential when it comes to ensuring eu law on the ground. i believe that this report is of the utmost importance, since the citizen is once again the centre of the union's concerns.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report and insist that the commission should abandon non-binding legal provisions in infringement proceedings and instead propose a regulation defining the different aspects of the infringement procedure and the upstream procedure, in order to strengthen citizens' rights and ensure transparency. i also think it is essential that the commission makes use of all the means at its disposal to ensure that member states transpose union legislation in a timely and appropriate manner, particularly in the field of the environment, one of the areas most prone to infringements.
in writing. - the debate on energy, energy dependence, traditional energy and renewable energy is often a source of great contradiction. in fact, the transition from conventional energy, from nuclear to renewable energy, will not be possible without transitional solutions. the gas is one of these transitional solutions, according to almost all experts. interestingly, the green group in the eu parliament seems to be particularly concerned, while their party colleagues, for example in government in germany, are clearly in favour of gas as a 'transitional energy'. energy production must be discussed objectively if we are to convince citizens to take part in shaping the energy transition. the citizens are called upon not only to understand the democratic process but also to understand that private households, and in particular heating, are among the largest consumers of non-renewable energy.
in writing. creating jobs and ending our dependence on traditional fuels is attractive, but at what cost? certainly not one that endangers the future of our children. i see three problems that, until they find a solution, will cause me to refuse to exploit. the first concerns water consumption, which is very high, measured in millions of m3. the second problem concerns the risk of pollution of groundwater near the wells by the chemicals used. the last problem is earthquakes caused by hydraulic fracturing. they can occur when the generated vibrations encounter natural faults. the disturbance can then release existing tensions. there seems to be little serious alternative to hydraulic fracturing today. tests have been carried out in the united states with compressed air instead of water and in canada with liquefied petroleum gas (lpg). electric arc fracturing, which has been patented, is only at the experimental stage. i am therefore not against exploitation, but against the methods of exploitation currently proposed.
i would like to begin by congratulating the rapporteur, mrs koppa, on her excellent report, which we have indeed discussed on several occasions and which my group was pleased to adopt in committee. i would then like to take the liberty of pointing out that i am the first speaker to come from one of the founding member states of the european union. all the others come from countries which have joined the union at various later stages, which shows how the gradual enlargement of the union is a fundamental aspect of its existence and also one of the most successful policies developed over the years. we will approve this report because we agree, as i said, that the enlargement of the european union can indeed be considered a success in general. we also agree with the new approach proposed and elaborated by commissioner füle, which includes opening chapters 23 and 24 very quickly in negotiations and keeping them open for a very long time during those negotiations. this should avoid painful surprises at the end of the road and, above all, give the applicant countries time to implement difficult reforms in the field of justice and fundamental freedoms in a timely and thorough manner. this report contains a paragraph 38, which has just been mentioned. not all members of my group, myself included, will approve it, not because we are so much against the notion of associate membership, but because we regret that it has found its way into a report on enlargement. we feel it doesn't belong there. thank you for your attention.
i am sorry, mr president, but this report on enlargement policy is a shocking example of global alienation. at no point is the question asked whether the european union has no other cat to beat at the moment than further enlargement with new member states? i am thinking of the euro crisis, the problems with schengen, the problems with the multiannual budget, the democratic deficit, etc. perhaps i have read over it, but nowhere in this report is attention paid to the absorption capacity of the european union to take in certain candidate countries. the report also contains a number of flagrant contradictions. for example, paragraph 28 welcomes the so-called positive agenda for turkey, while the next paragraph stresses that the candidate countries must make improvements in the area of democracy and human rights. and as far as turkey is concerned, this is a rather euphemistic term. candidate countries either meet the standards for accession or do not. turkey was not even ready to start accession negotiations. and meanwhile, the problems in that country have only gotten worse; but think of all those professors and journalists who are in prison for expressing an opinion. this parliament should finally speak out and call for the negotiations with turkey to be stopped. this is quite different from the propaganda advocated in paragraph 35 of the report to shape public opinion, which - it may be said - is generally more sensible and realistic on the enlargement issue than all the eu institutions put together.

in writing. several countries are actively preparing for cyberwarfare by setting up structures dedicated to defensive cyberwarfare, but also to espionage and cyber penetration. many public and private organizations are daily targets of attacks. large-scale cyberattacks have been carried out in recent years around the world. modern, highly computerised societies are vulnerable to this. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat this problem. on the technical side, never before has a set of technologies penetrated so rapidly and so deeply into human activity. the rise of a safer cyberspace requires increased involvement of states in network regulation and crisis management. it is therefore essential that europe emerges as the platform for discussion and regulation in this field. in fact, too often in recent times we have had in our hands, in the european parliament, texts with an unfortunate tendency to try to extinguish many individual freedoms, under various and varied pretexts. europe must guarantee to all its citizens that at no time is the boundary between security and privacy preserved and controlled to the maximum!
in writing. mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i encourage you all to close the loopholes in the current legislation on shark finning by ensuring that all sharks caught by eu vessels worldwide are landed with their fins naturally attached, without - and i insist - any exceptions being tolerated. i therefore urge you to reject any attempt to introduce amendments to preserve the derogation through which the practice of 'finishing' could go unnoticed. the adoption of this proposal would not only provide increased protection for eu sharks, but also allow the eu to contribute to the global effort to eradicate the cruel and unnecessary practice of shark finning by advocating for policies for shark fins naturally attached to the body, without exception, to regional fisheries organisations such as iccat and iotc. my view is shared by a majority of citizens of the european union. we represent them. don't you forget that!

in writing. let's say it straight away: a few days before the doha conference, the commitments made by the international community to limit global warming to a maximum of 2°c are far from being achieved. the united nations and the european union are sounding the alarm: action must be taken quickly and decisively! i have today supported, along with 484 other members of the european parliament, a resolution which recalls that this objective is essential to safeguard the survival of the planet's inhabitants and ecosystems. the resolution stresses that to be credible, industrialised countries must not only honour their commitments if they want to help the poorest countries, but also raise the bar if they want to reduce their carbon footprint by 2020. the hopes of tomorrow will be built on the addition of ambitious measures in the field of clean technologies, energy efficiency in buildings and transport, and the promotion of green jobs. the european parliament also stressed the need to create strategic alliances with key industrialised countries in order to renew the tokyo protocol, which is due to expire very soon, with a view to concluding a new international treaty with quantified targets for 2020.
in writing. i am in favour of this resolution. it's just amazing to see the risks that too many people are willing to take on future generations. if no urgent action is taken, the average global temperature will rise by 3 to 5 degrees celsius over the course of the century. the international community's 2°c target is the threshold beyond which, according to scientists, the climate system is likely to be overwhelmed with effects that would greatly accelerate further warming. even if the most ambitious promises made by individual states are kept, there will be 8 gt too much, two gt more than the estimates in the previous unep report in 2011. the new assessment is partly due to the fact that countries have provided clarifications on their pledges and how they should be interpreted in practice. how many warnings, verbal and concrete, will we need before we act? the 2°c target remained achievable, provided that rapid action was taken.

in writing. the last text of the european security strategy was in 2008. the situation is clearly not the same: upheavals in the middle east and north africa (including revolutions in libya, tunisia, egypt and syria), the emergence of new actors with regional - or even global - ambitions on the international scene and the reorientation of the priorities of the us defence policy towards the asia-pacific area. there are also local crises in the eu's neighbourhood with major regional consequences, the vagaries of transition processes in the arab countries and their scale, the development of the pakistan-afghanistan area in view of the future withdrawal of nato military forces, and the increase in terrorist threats in the sahel, the horn of africa and nigeria regions of africa. the european union is intended to be a comprehensive political actor on the international stage, in order to promote international peace and security, protect its interests in the world and guarantee the security of its own citizens. we have an important role to play as a provider of security services to the member states and their citizens.
