madam president, i am very pleased that the european patent is finally here. the compromise that has been reached – the foundations of which were laid during the belgian presidency in 2010 – is one which, i believe, will allow europe to keep pace with progress in business and technology and maintain its competitive position in a global market. i would also like to say that it has just been announced that the advocate-general advises the court to reject all the claims brought by spain and italy, and so the last legal stand against the european patent now appears to be over. the unitary patent – as, of course, we all know – will bring lower administrative, legal and translation costs for applicants. harmonised litigation and legal decisions will provide more legal certainty and enforceability, which is particularly welcome for small and medium-sized enterprises. adequate patent protection is only the first step towards more innovation and new products on the market. patents which have been lying on the shelf continue to do so. more efforts are also needed, both on the public and the private side, to bring new innovations to the market; this can only enhance our competitiveness.
madam president, commissioner, this is a historic week for european business. after 30 years of delay, europe is finally overcoming the fruitless polemics to create this single european patent, with the objective of protecting inventions and also the imperative of reducing costs. it cost €30,000 to apply for a european patent in ten countries and to protect the rights for the first ten years, twice as much as in the united states or japan. so i do not know where my colleague mr wojciechowski got the figures he quoted. the first single european patents, which could be filed as early as 2014, will cost inventors €5,000, will spare them the real struggle they have had to face up to now and will guarantee them, above all, greater legal certainty. but more will be needed to fully restore the morale of our inventors and researchers. to put it simply, the european patent is necessary for innovation but it is certainly not sufficient, and if european companies need this better industrial protection, they also need capital, easy access to finance and a favourable tax environment to compete in the global competition we are currently experiencing.
in writing. kyrgyzstan is an important partner for the union in central asia. since the 2009 global crisis, which severely affected the country, the union has supported every stage of the transition in recent years, including through the holding of democratic elections. the popular uprising of april 2010 brought an end to the regime of president bakiev, who was accused of corruption and undermining democracy. although some efforts still need to be made in the area of the rule of law and human rights, the current regime of president atambayev is making important steps forward, as was recently confirmed by the high representative catherine ashton during her visit to the country last november. in this context, it is of the utmost importance that the union, aware of the efforts of the democratic authorities of kyrgyzstan, should provide financial support to the country. moreover, macro-financial assistance seems to be the appropriate instrument in this perspective since it combines loans and grants and is complementary to the assistance already provided by the imf (to which the union also contributes).
in writing. voted in favour of the amendment proposed by mr moreira on amendment no 1 to the proposal for a decision concerning recital 18 and concerning macrofinancial assistance to the kyrgyz republic. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the programme to include the following areas: in order to ensure uniform conditions of implementation and for reasons of efficiency, the commission should be empowered to negotiate those conditions with the kyrgyz authorities under the supervision of the member states' committee provided for in regulation (eu) no 182/2011.
in writing. in voting in favour of this report, i particularly emphasise the need for the commission to include the results of the negotiations between the governments of the united states and the european union, by updating the references to the eu quality labelling and certification systems introduced by directive 2009/125/ec of 21 october 2009. secondly, i would like to stress that the objectives of the improvements to be made in energy efficiency must be to achieve a high level of consumer and environmental protection.
in writing. i am satisfied with the work that has been done towards the completion of this digital single market. this is a key element in making the european union the most competitive and dynamic knowledge-based economy in the world. it is smart, sustainable and inclusive growth. the development of e-commerce and online services has now become a priority for citizens and businesses alike. services can now move freely within a market of 500 million consumers. this completion of the digital single market is of course synonymous with job creation. it is therefore a key driver of competitiveness within the union. the achievement of this objective is vital in order to achieve the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy for the internal market. this is a further step towards a knowledge-based economy, a process which is particularly close to my heart.
in writing. the digital revolution has changed the way businesses operate, and the impact of the internet economy has been considerable in recent years. the figures also speak for themselves: in some g8 countries, the internet has generated 20% of economic growth and 25% of net job creation over the past five years. for every job destroyed in the world 'offline', the online sector generates 2.6 new jobs. companies that exploit the full potential of the internet create more than twice as many jobs as those that do not. if the internet economy is considered as a sector, internet-related consumption and spending is currently higher than in the agriculture or energy sectors. and the internet economy will continue to grow. the four actions deserve to be welcomed and supported: 1. support for small and medium-sized enterprises; 2. overcoming the remaining barriers in the digital single market; 3. building trust in the digital single market; 4. laying the foundations for a more competitive europe.
in writing. increased participation of european smes in global markets requires better access to capital and protection against unfair competition. the european parliament adopted a resolution that targets the funding gap, unfair competition from third countries and lack of information as the main reasons why only 13% of european smes export outside the eu. we suggest a new instrument which would provide additional aid to smes through export credit agencies. this instrument could include soft or fixed rate loans, short-term working capital and refinancing facilities, and professional insurance packages, including export credit insurance to cover foreign exchange risks. parliament also called on the commission to help smes in international markets by improving market access and combating non-tariff barriers, dumping and infringement of intellectual property rights. after the adoption of the single european patent, it is a new tool adopted in favour of our smes.
in writing. small and medium-sized enterprises are the real pillars of our economy. however, 99% of all enterprises in the union are small and medium-sized enterprises (smes), of which 92% are micro enterprises with between one and nine employees, 6.7% are small enterprises with between 10 and 49 employees and 1.1% are medium-sized enterprises with between 50 and 249 employees. there are 23 million of these companies. they also account for 75% of private sector employment. over the last five years, they have contributed to the creation of some 80% of all new jobs. moreover, smes are indeed the driving force behind economic recovery in these times of crisis. this is why it is important to enable them to obtain information on foreign markets and business opportunities for investment abroad, while facilitating their access to credit and capital, which will enable them to internationalise.
in writing. it is clear that in our current society digital freedom takes on a special meaning in the implementation of the union's foreign policy. it is the primary means of communication. to this end, it is a dimension which the union must integrate perfectly into all its policies. it facilitates access to information, freedom of expression, freedom of the press and economic, political, cultural and social development. what particularly impresses me is that the development of the internet in recent years, with its uncensored access, has had a positive influence on human rights and fundamental freedoms. the use of this tool by governments has enabled them to conduct direct diplomacy. the areas in which icts are particularly effective are diverse. they contribute to transparency and good governance, literacy, education, health care, effective monitoring of elections (the last elections in ukraine...), and disaster relief (there are many examples in this area too!). the integration of digital freedom into the union's foreign policy is a key element in this.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. only by creating synergies between our trade, security and foreign policy policies, in line with our values and interests, can the union exert its full weight and assume a global role. in view of the rapidly changing technologies, it is essential to promote structured collaboration between policy makers, business and civil society. this balance in time is likely to best serve the cause of the global open internet, in the interest of all.
in writing. i voted in favour of the text by mr moreira on the council's position at first reading with a view to adopting the regulation of the european parliament and of the council establishing transitional provisions for bilateral investment agreements between member states and third countries. the fact that this regulation, and in particular recitals 17, 18 and 19 thereof, provides for the use of the procedures referred to in regulation (eu) no 182/2011 does not constitute a precedent which would allow the union, in the context of future regulations, to empower member states, pursuant to article 2 (1) tfeu, to legislate and adopt legally binding acts in areas falling within the union's exclusive competence. furthermore, the use of the consultation procedure, as opposed to the examination procedure, in this regulation should not be considered as a precedent for future regulations establishing the framework of the common commercial policy.
in writing. since the armed conflict in august 2008, the union has committed itself to further strengthening its relations with georgia. committed since 1999 through a partnership agreement (pa) and since 2006 through the european neighbourhood policy (enp action plan), the union is providing key financial assistance to the country, complementing the assistance provided by the united nations, the world bank and the imf. the country has been hit hard by the international financial crisis since the third half of 2008. as štefan füle, european commissioner for enlargement and european neighbourhood policy, recently said following his meeting with georgian opposition leader david baradze, the focus must be on building strong and depersonalized institutions to consolidate the country's democratic process. in view of these economic and political considerations, it is essential that the union provides macro-financial assistance to georgia in order to strengthen its efforts towards democracy and the rule of law.
in writing. i voted in favour of the proposal by mr moreira to allow the union to make available to georgia macro-financial assistance (hereinafter referred to as 'the union's macro-financial assistance') of up to eur 46 million to support the stabilisation of its economy and to cover its balance of payments needs as defined in the current imf programme. of this maximum amount, a maximum of eur 23 million shall be paid in the form of grants and a maximum of eur 23 million in the form of loans. the disbursement of the union's macro-financial assistance shall be subject to the approval of the 2013 union budget by the budgetary authority. it is, however, important that the commission periodically verifies that georgia's economic policies are in line with the objectives of the union's macro-financial assistance and that the agreed economic policy conditions are being satisfactorily fulfilled. to this end, the commission shall carry out this task in close coordination with the imf and the world bank and, where appropriate, with the economic and financial committee.
in writing. the issue of maritime safety has two equally important aspects. the aim is to ensure the safety and therefore the continuity of maritime trade, which is an essential means of international trade, and to protect the environment. recognising the scale of the environmental damage caused by the sinking of the oil tanker erika, the european maritime safety agency (emsa) was set up to address this reality. the agency contributes to maritime safety and security and to the prevention of pollution. it also has to prevent and deal with environmental disasters such as oil spills. it will use the cleanseanet information system to monitor the impact of pollution from offshore drilling platforms and to monitor greenhouse gas emissions from ships. the agency will also be responsible for helping to combat piracy. in view of all the above and the needs in this area, the signing of this act is necessary and fully justified.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report so that the maritime safety agency can have greater capacity to predict and combat environmental disasters using the clearseanet information system, which monitors the impact of pollution caused by offshore drilling rigs. i also urge the agency to play a greater role in improving the training of seafarers.
in writing. i am pleased that an idea that was already put forward 55 years ago, at the time of the creation of the european economic community, has now been put into practice! i see two main advantages. firstly, our companies and universities will finally be able to protect the results of their research and innovation programmes at a lower cost. administrative procedures will also be greatly facilitated, as will legal proceedings in the event of a dispute. secondly, as a corollary to the first advantage, we are strengthening the position of our companies on the world stage by improving their competitiveness vis-à-vis american or asian groups. this is a considerable improvement over the current fragmented and costly system. in general, for budgetary reasons, our smes are currently protecting their achievements in only a limited number of countries. once this new system is in place, they will have the opportunity to protect them in a much larger territory.
in writing. the creation of such unitary patent protection will obviously be beneficial in this area. the creation of a truly unitary patent system will undoubtedly simplify the current system and reduce its costs (thus moving to a cost of approximately eur 680 per patent). the europe 2020 strategy will be strengthened, as this measure will, among other things, allow for more favourable conditions for investment in research and development. innovation and entrepreneurship will thus be further encouraged. the creation of a unitary patent will have a whole range of positive impacts in the member states of the european union. the free movement of goods in the internal market will be reinforced and effective once again. moreover, as the committee discussion has pointed out, i believe that cooperation between the european patent office and the national intellectual property services really needs to be strengthened. the only regret is that the agreement was reached by 25 member states and not 27.
in writing. the ep approved the unitary patent, making it easier and cheaper to apply for patents in europe. i voted against because the legal uncertainty that has now been created defies all imagination. in their haste to pass this bad piece of legislation, the entire judiciary is getting a fat clutch. the european patent office (epo), a non-eu institution, is not only entrusted with the supervision of european patents. there is great concern about the far-reaching consequences of this law. the epo has been criticised in the past for its ease of granting patents. patents on living organisms, for example. it prevents farmers from growing their own seeds, which makes them dependent and, moreover, is harmful to biodiversity. but there are also examples of software packages. despite clear (and restrictive) rules on this, the epo still allows patents. this is not a trusting relationship. a genuine unitary patent, embedded in the legal and political structures of the union, would be a good thing. unfortunately, this law goes the other way and places the control of, for example, european innovation policy in the hands of lawyers rather than democratically elected bodies.
in writing. i voted in favour of the creation of unitary patent protection because it is a real breakthrough which european texts really need. i want to stress one point in particular. such unitary patent protection should be established by giving european patents unitary effect after their grant, pursuant to this regulation, in respect of all the participating member states. the main feature of european patents with unitary effect should be their unitary character, that is to say, they should provide uniform protection and produce the same effects in all participating member states. consequently, a european patent with unitary effect should be limited, transferred, revoked or extinguished only for all the participating member states at the same time. a european patent with unitary effect must be capable of being the subject of a licence agreement for all or part of the territories of the participating member states. in order to ensure effective uniformity of unitary patent protection, only european patents granted for all participating member states with the same set of claims should be granted unitary effect.
in writing. i welcome the vote on the single european patent in plenary today! this vote represents a crucial step in the reform of the european patent system. after decades of political and legal discussions (the idea of such a patent was already being floated in 1957 at the time of the creation of the eec!), the european patent is finally on the rails! the single european patent represents a considerable improvement over the current fragmented and costly system. for belgian companies, and in particular for smes, the european patent will bring great simplification and considerable savings in the patent application procedure.
in writing. a well-functioning patent system that meets the real needs of users is essential for the economic growth and competitiveness of the european union. the use of intellectual property rights, in addition to being one of the main drivers of innovation, contributes significantly to the economic development of the whole field. the objective of achieving unitary patent protection within the union has been pursued by the member states for almost half a century. in view of the impossibility of reaching an agreement on a community patent, the so-called monaco convention was drawn up in 1973 and subsequently all the countries now members of the european union have acceded to it. it now seems clear to me that the creation of unitary patent protection must go hand in hand with the establishment of a unified judicial system.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report. after decades of failed attempts, a genuine single market in patents, which guarantees legal certainty and international competitiveness, is now within reach. parliament must support such a project! the establishment of a well-designed patent system in europe can be a major factor in growth through innovation. european companies, especially smes, need such a tool to cope with the current economic crisis and to remain competitive on a global scale. the patent system in europe consists of national patents issued by the industrial property offices of the member states or a european patent issued by the european patent office. once granted, the european patent breaks up into a bundle of national patents governed by different national laws. this system, which is very expensive, leads to costly litigation involving several courts, with the risks that this entails, and often results in conflicting decisions. the proposals on unitary patent protection and the related language regime, currently under consideration in the parliament and the council, aim to put an end to this fragmentation.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text because it aims to ensure that the safeguard clause and the stabilisation mechanism for bananas work effectively and become truly applicable, and has provided for enforcement measures to avoid serious damage to the sectors which could be most affected in the short term by the implementation of part iv of the agreement. moreover, the liberalization process, which was very important to me, is taking place in full compliance with the fundamental international conventions on labour law and the environment. the implementation of the regulation under consideration is carried out in the most transparent manner. another factor which motivated my vote was that the text contains specific provisions on monitoring and reporting, including an analysis of the impact on employment, working conditions, organic production and fair trade flows, and an assessment of the impact of the scheme on the outermost regions, particularly in the banana sector, and on small production structures and cooperatives.
in writing. i welcome the adoption of the association agreement with central america, which was voted on by the european parliament on tuesday and will enter into force in 2013. this is the first agreement with another regional bloc, which greatly simplifies the procedures since with one agreement we are reaching all the member states of these two regional blocs. the controversial debates which preceded the vote were not necessary, since the agreement, in addition to enshrining political dialogue, cooperation and free trade between the two signatory parties, contains clauses on human rights and on social and environmental standards which are dear to us. these two agreements open up great prospects for both regions, particularly in the industrial, automotive and machine tool sectors.
in writing. the current temporary trade regime based on a unilateral system of generalised preferences is evolving towards a reciprocal and negotiated structure in favour of the progressive liberalisation of trade in goods and services and public procurement, and the promotion of investment, leading to a predictable framework of legal security and certainty that fosters mutual trust, which is essential for the development of trade and investment. however, as a socialist mep, i strongly urge that this opening up should not be a matter of liberalisation to the full, without any social aspect! social cohesion is a priority objective of regional cooperation policy, and reducing poverty rates, inequalities, social exclusion and all forms of discrimination are, in my view, key elements of this.
in writing. i am very mixed on the issues concerning colombia. on the one hand, i must admit that the present government seems to be making fruitful efforts, the catastrophic situation 10 years ago is still improving, the socialists have introduced very interesting clauses in the text on respect for human rights and on the establishment of a roadmap to be respected by the government. however, these clauses are not suspensive, people continue to die for their ideas and the government still has, it seems to me, to make efforts not only in the short term but also in the medium and long term. i do not therefore reject the text in order to welcome the efforts made, but i cannot accept it because, although colombia seems to be moving in the right direction, the path traveled is not yet significant.
in writing. the european parliament has called for the eu to be given a role in the negotiations on the free trade agreement with peru and colombia. a non-binding agreement on human rights in exchange for this far-reaching trade agreement is - the day after the nobel peace prize was awarded - an extra painful mistake. colombia, which is the most dangerous country in the world, is violating human rights in many ways. many workers' social rights are under pressure, trade union leaders or human rights defenders are threatened with death or murdered. not a word about this. in peru, it is estimated that there are no less than 250 pending environmental conflicts, mainly related to mining. this agreement will not contribute to sustainable development in the two south american countries, it will not eradicate poverty. it will not mitigate the catastrophic effects on many indigenous populations. it will in particular raise the prices of commodities. finally, the agreement provides for the deregulation of the financial sector. it's incomprehensible in times of global crisis. money laundering will only get easier. it is clear that this agreement does not draw the map of the south! needless to say, i voted against this report.
in writing. i have been very involved for several years in issues concerning colombia. on the one hand, i must admit that the present government seems to be making fruitful efforts, the situation, which was catastrophic 10 years ago, is improving, the socialists have introduced very interesting clauses in the text on respect for human rights and on the establishment of road maps to be respected by the government. however, these clauses are unfortunately not suspensive, people continue to die for their ideas and the government still has to make efforts, it seems to me, not only in the short term but also in the medium and long term. i do not therefore reject the text by my colleague mario david, in which he welcomes the efforts made in colombia, but i cannot accept it because, although colombia seems to be moving in the right direction, the progress made is not yet significant.
in writing. 2012 was the european year for active ageing and solidarity between generations. the right to health is a fundamental right. this right can be considered as one of the foundations of the right to live in dignity. in fact, is not the first article of the charter of fundamental rights of the european union (2000) devoted to the inviolability of human dignity? in addition, population ageing is one of europe's main challenges: 87 million people in the union are over 65 years old (17.4% of the population), and this will be more than 150 million in 2060 (about 30% of the population). given that older people are much more active than in previous decades and that the evolution of life expectancy continues to increase markedly in our societies, it is important to prevent all kinds of threats that could undermine this progress. it is in this context that measures such as prevention and early diagnosis of certain diseases which mainly affect women are justified and necessary.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report. the population of the european union can only age gradually because of low birth rates and longer life expectancy. european policies have increasingly integrated this dimension, as demonstrated by the european year of active ageing and solidarity between generations in 2012. the aim is to promote healthy ageing for all by providing access to quality health care and preventive measures that help older people to remain independent for as long as possible. i hope that the european commission will join parliament in the importance of publishing a new report on the state of women's health, paying particular attention to the over-65 age group and the indices of active ageing.
in writing. i welcome the adoption by a very large majority of this new resolution to take action against antibiotic resistance. this was important given the fact that the number of resistant bacteria in europe is growing exponentially. in view of this, we must ensure that the use of antimicrobials for both humans and animals is reduced. but we also need to close the gap between the rise in resistance on the one hand, and the development of new antimicrobials on the other, by stimulating research and innovation. if we don't take action now, growing antibiotic resistance could threaten our ability to treat patients. i also welcome the fact that the resolution stresses the need for better training of doctors, pharmacists, veterinarians and farmers and calls for more information to the general public on the harm caused by inappropriate use of antibiotics.
in writing. the development of antimicrobial resistance is not new. the impacts of this development are many. if antimicrobials are used irrationally and inappropriately (for example, if patients do not take the full course of treatment or if the antimicrobials are of poor quality), the risk of ineffectiveness is high. the dangers can be many: antimicrobial resistance is killing people, undermining the fight against infectious diseases, sometimes raising health care costs, and jeopardizing some of the advances in modern medicine. it is therefore important to regulate and limit the use of antimicrobials, and thus to ensure their responsible and prudent use. there are several leads that i think we can follow. policies at member state level need to be more firm. prevention must also be more effective (campaigns, communication and training). investment in research is also important for the development of alternative treatments. i would like to conclude by saying: "let us act today so that we can still treat tomorrow".
in writing. seven months after the plenary vote on a resolution sounding the alarm about the resurgence of bacterial resistance to antibiotics, the european parliament is still in the process of adopting the rosbach report on the microbial challenge. this is a major health issue and much remains to be done, particularly in terms of preventing and reducing the use of antibiotics in veterinary medicine. this is without affecting the health of the animals. clearly, the health system needs to evolve and it is essential that veterinary health professionals increase the awareness of farmers about new farming practices and the importance of hygiene conditions. however, contrary to what was voted this afternoon, i think that the decoupling of the right to prescribe and the right to distribute veterinary medicines is a false good solution. that is why i voted against the second part of paragraph 16 of the resolution. the point here is not to decide in favour of a professional organisation, veterinarians against pharmacists, but to face reality: veterinary prescribers, especially in rural areas, play a vital role as sentinels in the event of emerging zoonoses (brucellosis, foot-and-mouth disease, bse, etc.).
in writing. i voted in favour of this report, in particular because the revision of directive 2001/82/ec will allow effective measures to be taken to limit antimicrobial resistance by strengthening the provisions on veterinary medicines. it should be remembered that the widespread use of antimicrobial drugs, especially in livestock, leads to resistance to these drugs and therefore reduces their effectiveness in humans as well. to that end, the commission should review the conditions for prescribing and marketing antimicrobials in order to avoid over-prescribing, over-use or misuse of antimicrobials.
mr president, commissioner, in politics sometimes we are led to swallow slugs and sometimes we are forced to do so for reasons of realism. but i have the feeling that the council is trying to sell us a bill of goods which we are likely to digest for a long time and which does not bode well for the next steps. it is true that, despite the commitments signed by the presidents, the logic of delayed payment is still being followed, and the effects of this will be felt in the coming months. we are persisting in iter, whose urgency and relevance in these times of crisis will have to be demonstrated to me. the growth pact, which was the council's own wish, is being buried in a certain way, and so i think that if the council proposes this, it is not so surprising when we know how it works, but if parliament accepts it, even if i say with the tip of my fingers, when we have to vote tomorrow, i think it is a little worrying, especially since jacques delors reminded us in an interview yesterday that 'we would need a budget of the order of 20% of european gdp'. i think that the position we are taking, regardless of how we assess this compromise, is not one which will strengthen us for the next steps but rather one which will leave us fragile and weak for the future debates, which are far more important than the debate on the 2013 budget.
mr president, without the adoption of the sixth amending budget, the bills relating to students, erasmus, social inclusion, the european social fund and aid for workers made redundant, with the mobilisation of the globalisation adjustment fund, would remain unpaid. we can therefore only adopt this budgetary adjustment, even if it is less than the real needs, and the council knows this. i therefore regret that once again the member states, through the logic of the package, are imposing their minimalist views on us, and that we are falling victim to this blackmail of 'it's all or nothing', this game of dupes without the appropriate means of payment. we must force the member states to honour their commitments and, tomorrow, break the tie of dependence between the european budget and national budgets by means of new and sufficient own resources and an exemption from the member states' contributions to the european budget. because now more than ever we need ambitious and credible european budgets.
on behalf of the s&d group. mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would first like to thank the rapporteurs and the shadow rapporteurs for their hard work. it hasn't been easy, and we're still a long way from the finish line. the airport package comprises three very different proposals, to which we as a group would like to adopt a very different attitude each time. in the noise issue, mrs leichtfried has struck a very good balance between the need to achieve a minimum of harmonisation in the way noise is dealt with and the procedures to be followed, on the one hand, and the principle of subsidiarity and the need to take account of the outcome of local negotiations, which are usually very delicate, on the other. as regards slots, new mechanisms have been adopted to optimise the use of slots: the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the rules on the free movement of goods are respected. but we must dare to go further, and we wanted, as a group, to be more flexible for longer series, for example with regard to the 80-20 rule, in order to optimise the case even further. but it's a step in the right direction. as regards the further liberalization of groundhandling services, i want to make it very clear that we as socialists and democrats cannot support either the commission's proposal or the rapporteur's proposals. in both cases, the emphasis is far too much on increasing the number of dealers, when we know that there are airports with few dealers and very good performance, and there are airports with many dealers and perhaps poor performance. we want to see a shift in focus towards quality of service and better social rules of the game to make unions binding, to establish high-quality training for employees, and to make the transfer of employees automatic. we shall therefore vote tomorrow to reject the commission's proposal. but i would like to add, for the sake of clarity, that we remain prepared to negotiate if there is an opening on the basis of our proposals and our conditions. we think the current rules of the game are not good either. so something has to happen in the right direction and that's what we want to work on. but what is on the table today is not enough and that is why we will vote against it.
i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. the airport package has indeed led to a lot of discussion, and as the epp coordinator, the work has not been easy. you have heard it, and that is why i would like to introduce some nuance into this subject - particularly in the area of ground transport services - in german today. but first, the noise. i think that the work - and i would like to thank the rapporteurs in general - is good in this respect, because it essentially takes account of the respect of many agreements reached with local residents under regional or national legislation and environmental legislation, and i do not think that this leads to different situations in europe. i come from a region where this has cost several million euros and where we have been able to link businesses, residents and service providers in a very good contract. i think that is the goal we want to achieve and can achieve. as far as the newsstands are concerned, i would like to see improvements in this area too, because the most important thing for me is to prevent the misuse of newsstands and to really show the unused niches and to improve transparency. i would like to say the following about ground transport services: if you look at what we passed in 1996, there were no minimum standards in terms of quality, there were no standards at all in terms of training, there were no standards for continuing training. so if we are to take this opportunity - and you must tell the 3000 people outside - then this dossier - and the epp has decided by a majority - must be referred back to committee for further study and improvement. this is the chance. it is not a question of drastically expanding the market if we envisage a liberalization from two to three suppliers, which affects a minimum number of airports in europe. i believe that citizens should be given fair information and not information that is presented in exaggerated form. i would also have liked to have had this debate on the part of certain colleagues when we discussed the postal service directive. because the market was getting smaller, and there was little concern about the mailmen and their social conditions. here the market is growing, and here people are worried. it's too local for me. so i hope that through a good debate in the committee we can come to a sensible solution and, in particular, to better working conditions for all the people. and better working conditions, that's my conclusion... mr president, it is not illegal to want to improve the accessibility and quality of airports, but the impact of aircraft noise and night flights on health is, in my view, no longer to be demonstrated. it is not simply a matter of convenience, as some try to make it seem, but a matter of health, and in some cases, life or premature death. secondly, mrs meissner, to take a balanced approach by ignoring the recommendations of the who, excuse me, but that is not a balanced approach. on the other hand, we are not asking for all operators to be required to have the same noise controls. we are asking for an identical rule which prevents the commission from unraveling what local authorities have long and sometimes difficultly agreed at local level. finally, as regards the liberalization proposal, i do not see what difference it makes to go from two to three operators. i do not see how liberalisation will help us in any way to make services more competitive. you'll have to prove it to me! in the transport sector, i do not see how this improves the quality of service. i therefore really hope that this plenary will follow the committee's advice and reject this proposal rather than refer it back to committee.
in writing. it is important that the commission in its proposal establishes the causal link between the quality of the final product - the service - and the quality of human capital - the workers and their working conditions. but it is precisely on this point that i would have liked to see the commission take a more ambitious and consistent approach. the safety of the service, the expertise, the continuous training of the employees and the guarantee of a fair remuneration are essential for us. we must not allow the competition to be settled purely on the basis of the wages and working conditions of the workers. the quality of service is guaranteed to deteriorate. the increase in the minimum number of bidders is not a miracle solution either. at many european airports where the market is already fully liberalised, the big global players are swallowing up the small ones. in the event of such takeovers, workers must not be the victims of market forces and their job security must be guaranteed. we must give the european parliament's committee on transport a second chance to formulate a socially balanced position.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, there will be no budgetary union without fiscal union, without abandoning the dogma of fiscal competitiveness, which in reality undermines the sustainability of public finances. and for whose benefit? the richest 20% of our society, or even 1% of the population. to do that, we must break the lock on the rule of unanimity in tax matters. it is unacceptable that a member state alone can block any initiative in tax matters. in this sense, enhanced cooperation on the financial transaction tax is a first opportunity to break this deadlock. but there must be others, because, beyond the financial transaction tax, it is high time to tackle these multinationals which, by making billions of profits in europe, thanks to european consumers, are content to pay at best a few million in taxes. we will therefore need stronger cooperation on corporate taxation and, why not, a joint initiative to put an end to the tax tourism of bernard arnault, gérard depardieu and others of the same kind.
in writing. we must vote for enhanced cooperation on the financial transaction tax! the european parliament's support for the ftt has had a significant impact on the commission and the council in the past. parliament must continue to support the ttf. firstly, to ensure consistency with previous positions, but also to send a strong signal to european civil society and to those states that do not yet support the project, to join the coalition. enhanced cooperation shall comply with union law and respect the rights of non-participating states. it can also have a positive effect on the internal market. the fears of mass relocation are also unfounded. the scope of the ftt must be as broad as possible: not only shares and bonds but also all derivatives. finally, the ftt with 11 countries could raise 37 billion euros in revenue. these vast sums could be usefully spent to meet social needs in europe, but also to help poor households around the world who are suffering the consequences of a crisis for which they are not responsible.
in writing. animals travelling from one member state to another are sometimes transported for 40 hours without food, water or rest. slaughterhouses which cannot find enough animals to slaughter in the area where they are located are willing to pay the price to bring in animals from far away, so that they can operate at full capacity. it is therefore only for economic reasons that animals are transported over long distances. there is no valid reason for such inhumanity. animals should be slaughtered close to their breeding grounds and their carcases should be transported. in addition, veterinary checks must be increased, the loading densities of animals must be significantly reduced, horses should travel in separate compartments ... there is still much to be done, and we cannot do it by backing down. that is why i am asking for a strong gesture from our parliament: that the total duration of transport of animals be limited to a maximum of 8 hours, whether they are intended for slaughter or for fattening.
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, i have to say that i am very sceptical about the summit tomorrow and friday, as indeed is mr draghi. he is quoted in today’s press as saying that he fears vague and slow decisions and that he is disappointed by the lack of ambition for the summit – and, let us be honest, it is true. last june the european council decided that, before the end of the year (and i am quoting now), ‘we should have a time-bound roadmap for the achievement of a genuine economic and monetary union to bring back confidence in the markets’. we all applauded here in parliament – and what draft conclusions do we have for tomorrow’s summit? almost nothing. they are very vague; there is no clear roadmap; there is no real backing of the van rompuy paper in the conclusions; now this is the third time that we will be deciding on the single supervisory mechanism. it is becoming ridiculous. what is also ridiculous, in my opinion, is what i hear from some european leaders such as mr hollande, stating in oslo that the crisis is over now. what crisis is over? the crisis is not over – not economically, not socially (just look at all the damage in the economic field) and not financially. look for example how over the last few days the spreads have gone up again – for spain they were above 400 today, yesterday, three days ago. for portugal they have gone up again, for ireland as well – not to mention italy, but there are other reasons, naturally, for that phenomenon. my question to the president of the council is: is that the type of conclusions? maybe you had better have no summit tomorrow and the day after tomorrow than this type of summit, because in the end it will create more problems in the market than it resolves, because you are clearly not delivering what you promised six months ago. my second request is to the commission, which has done a lot of work on the blueprint, but every time it says: ok, it is in the treaties and the treaty does not allow this and the treaty does not allow that and we have to come back after 2014. maybe it is a good idea to have fast european elections, because the real reason why we cannot do a number of things – for example the redemption fund, a fiscal capacity for the euro zone, treasury bills – is, as we all know, not the treaty, mr barroso: the real reason is political, because there are elections in germany and before september we cannot decide on all these topics that are so important. that is the real reason. my proposal is to be very honest on this and not to say every time: ok, it is not possible because of the treaty here, it is not possible because of the treaty there. there is now a new decision of the european court of justice, the pringle judgment. the pringle judgment makes possible treasury bills, the redemption fund and a fiscal capacity for the euro zone, and if you say ‘no’, look to the council. in the last trialogue even the council legal service contradicted you on this issue and said that it was possible under the conditions of the pringle judgment. et enfin, monsieur le président, ma dernière remarque porte sur l'italie. ce qui se passe est tout de même insensé: un homme politique dit qu'il va revenir aux affaires et le gouvernement tombe, et l'italie est en crise, et c'est toute l'eurozone qui est de nouveau en crise. je sais que joseph daul, hier, a pris ses distances. je voudrais simplement lui demander une chose supplémentaire: mets-le à la porte du parti populaire européen, qu'on en finisse avec cette histoire, que l'italie puisse retrouver la stabilité le plus vite possible, et aussi l'eurozone, parce que, en fait, quel jeu est-on en train de jouer? qui sera là demain au sommet du ppe? il sera là? peut-être que vous pouvez nous le dire: il sera à côté de mme merkel et à côté des autres? blue-card answer. – naturally, before i attacked mr farage on his presence in the committee on fisheries – i am not stupid – i looked at my own presence in the committee on constitutional affairs. from the files i see that i was present 23 times in this period and mr farage twice – i was there ten times more, i think, so maybe it would be a good thing if he received 10 times less salary than me!
madam president, i was amazed seeing the three presidents receiving the nobel peace prize. it was pretentious, pompous, if not pontifical – the trinity of self-complacency. why not hand the nobel peace prize to three ordinary european citizens right away? but no, it went to three shiny, well-paid gentlemen presiding over european institutions and being served at their will. ordinary citizens only came in today, after the show. gentlemen, what did you achieve to deserve it? the entire euro zone is heading for recession. greece, spain, italy and portugal are already there. france and germany will follow suit. economic forecasts are always too rosy. according to you, the greek economy will grow steadily in 2015. this is too rosy and you know it, but you lack the courage to tell ordinary people. you are creating a european transfer economy by which some countries have to support many others. the many are stuck in recession and demand more, while the few will slip into recession too. the result is growing resentment – the old demon of europe. gentlemen, you are sowing the seeds of future conflicts. take president schulz, who mysteriously disappeared. he proposed that british meps should not be allowed to discuss euro matters in parliamentary committee. he intends to exclude them. he is starting the process of unravelling the eu and this note is a smoking gun. it is all in here. now, mr schulz, i cannot imagine that you want to become the milošević of the european parliament. resentment is the poison of europe, and the impact of his letter is spreading it, so i urge him not to open this can of worms. ( the speaker agreed to take a blue-card question under rule 149(8))
blue-card answer. – madam, of course i honour the achievements that we have made. i have always been a supporter of european integration. the only criticism i had is that it should have been given to ordinary citizens right away, and not to the presidents of the three institutions. furthermore, i am just wondering about the timing. we are in the deepest of crises at the moment in europe; we are facing a monetary, financial and economic crisis. we have an unemployment rate in spain of 25%, and for young people it is 50%, so there is nothing to be proud of. what is the future showing us? we are slipping into recession and there is no way to come out of it any time soon. that worries me, and in time resentment will rise. i am against resentment, but it is the old demon of europe: we have to contain it and prevent it. mr farage is spreading it, i am not spreading it. but we have to be careful, because resentment is the thing that will finish europe.
president, commission president, president of the council, ladies and gentlemen, a great many measures have already been taken, but the flaws in the fabric of economic and monetary union which have painfully emerged from the crisis have not yet been completely eliminated. trust has been restored, but it is still too fragile and very weak. there is still a lot to be done, of course, but our work is far from finished, and so we need to know where we are going, in the short term, in the medium term and in the long term. we delivered a broad parliamentary report on 20 november and the commission last week launched its blueprint in which we agree and which provides excellent guidance. i hope that the heads of state or government will reach conclusions that maintain this level of ambition. the ecb has also been creative. it has announced decisions which give us some breathing space, but these measures are like paracetamol; they don't cure the disease, they just make the fever go down temporarily. the real cure is something which must be politically manufactured, and so let us use the breathing space to carry out concrete structural reforms, reforms which we can also reap the rewards of in a few years' time. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the european banking supervisory system is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. i fear that the sense of urgency is fading away, and i hope that the conclusions of the european summit will contradict me in this.
on behalf of the alde group. – mr president, if mr šefčovič wants an agreement on the two-pack then i want agreement on the two-pack, but he has to take a step towards parliament. if you do not do so, commissioner, you will not have a majority, and you will have a problem. you have known for six months what the problem is, because six months ago we adopted — by a majority of more than 500 votes — a resolution on this subject which stated that you have to take a step towards the mutualisation of debt through a redemption fund, as the german sachverständigenrat (council of economic experts) has proposed. why do you have to do this? because otherwise it will be impossible for countries like spain to recover. they still have these high interest rates; you have to do something to get the interest rates down, and this is the only way to achieve that. you say there are legal problems: there are no legal problems. since the pringle judgment the situation has been very clear. the pringle judgment makes it possible to establish such a fund under certain conditions, which are specified in that judgment, in full compliance with article 125 of the lisbon treaty. why the objection to intergovernmental action? you accept intergovernmental things every day. the efsf is intergovernmental, and you accept it; the esm is intergovernmental, and you accept it; the fiscal compact is intergovernmental, and you accept it. it is only when parliament asks you for something that you have a problem.
the european council will be considering the report by mr van rompuy. i can only say that this report has gone completely wrong since its outline was presented in june. i was only moderately positive in june. the fundamental balance that must be found to build economic and monetary union in a sustainable way was still there. the balance is that on the one hand there is a strengthened central control over the budgetary policies of the member states and the economic direction they take. but it also sets out a framework in which normal solidarity mechanisms are anchored within the eurozone. the version of the report to be debated on thursday makes a prominent case for further budgetary discipline and punishment. the proposals are concrete and the timing is tight. the institutionalisation of greater solidarity is already very vague and the tracks (no concrete proposals) are being put on the back burner. and on the further democratization of the eu, perhaps the most essential component for building a sustainable monetary union, we can only spew out vague ideas.

in writing. i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution rejecting the commission proposal on the regulation on european statistics on crime prevention. the proposed instrument must be based on an appropriate and consistent methodology and an adequate analysis of the costs and benefits in relation to the expected results. the proposal does not answer any questions about the method used and i therefore insist that the commission should propose a clear and fully coherent instrument, which is not the case with the commission proposal.
in writing. - the rapporteur, mr kirkhope, is not wrong to argue that it is unclear whether the €12 million for statistical data on crime in the european union is a useful expenditure. i agree with that, especially since experience shows that the really important data - such as the ethnic origin of the perpetrators - are still being withheld because it is not politically correct. i am surprised that parliament has suddenly become thrifty. tomorrow we are voting on a multiannual framework for the 'european union agency for fundamental rights', a much more expensive spectacle which is mainly concerned with the fight against 'racism and xenophobia' - as if not enough efforts are already being made at every possible level of government. can we therefore expect them to be abolished, or is that too much to ask?
in writing. − ground handling services at european airports have already been liberalised since 1996, with the exception of four main ground handling services, namely baggage handling, apron handling, refuelling and cargo and mail handling. in such cases, the number of providers may be limited to two. the commission's proposal is to increase the number of providers from two to three. most european airports are not faced with this expansion, but there are some airports which want to protect their market and not be exposed to competition because of their existing monopoly. the argument of social dumping, security and loss of quality is used as a pretext for protecting the market. however, these arguments do not stand up to scrutiny, because this report sets out for the first time training and continuing training schemes, a minimum of social regulations and quality standards for the ground handling service providers. the commission has already made a number of proposals to improve the quality of the information provided by the member states. moreover, an increase in the number of service providers does not lead to social dumping, provided that certain standards are set, as envisaged in this case. in addition to improving quality and safety, this further liberalisation of ground transport services may even lead to an increase in wages. it is even absurd to reject such an important report, which has a clear added value for the industry and is another step towards the single market, because of the resistance of some european airports. (parliament adopted the legislative resolution)
in writing. at the heart of the proposal put forward by the european commission was the objective of increasing the minimum level of groundhandling service providers from two to three for airports handling at least 5 million passengers per year. i voted against the commission's text and against the referral back. my main criticism was the social impact of the liberalisation of ground services on workers. we also wondered about the effect of the proposal on quality of service, about the possibility of including more providers at the larger airports and about safety issues. the s&d group has expressed its firm opposition to any further liberalisation of this market as long as social safeguards are lacking. further liberalisation of the market for groundhandling services at the expense of workers, in the form of lower wages and increased job insecurity, is not the solution required to increase the competitiveness of european airports and airlines. the share of stopover services in the ticket price is around eur 2.
in writing. - in addition to their considerable commercial value, the time slots at airports are still very opaque in their allocation. i therefore consider it essential to renew the existing 1993 regulation. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the rules are applied in a uniform manner, and that the rules are applied in a uniform manner. however, there are also aspects which i do not think need to be changed, and one of them is the existing 80/20 rule. this provides that if an air carrier uses less than 80% of a sequence of slots, it loses those unused slots. these then enter a time-space pool. although the commission had envisaged 85/15, this change would ultimately result in a 90/10 system and therefore result in airlines having to operate the flights at all times, including empty planes, in order not to lose their slots. this would be contrary to the very purpose of this proposal, which is to increase efficiency.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report which aims to strengthen the mechanisms for allocating and using slots and to promote fair competition by stimulating competitiveness between operators. i would particularly like to stress that the commission's proposal must be a starting point for improving airport efficiency. the impact study clearly shows that the current system of allocation of slots in the union is not optimal for airports with limited capacity and that it is therefore imperative to use existing airport capacity as efficiently as possible.
in writing. - noise pollution is an important issue in transport, but in aviation in particular it is a matter of local and regional responsibility. i therefore welcome the fact that the commission has put forward a balanced approach to noise in this proposal, covering several aspects such as land use planning and noise abatement, but i welcome the fact that these are guidelines. the present report has established that the commission has no supervisory powers, so that it may review the noise-related operating restrictions imposed by airports from the point of view of the procedure, but it cannot intervene in this matter. this is important to maintain local and regional responsibility.
in writing. i voted in favour of this text. the proposal takes into account the comfort of citizens by updating existing measures to enable local authorities to improve the noise environment around eu airports in the international framework of the icao's balanced approach to noise management. the balanced approach must be applied consistently. this balanced approach covers all noise mitigation measures. first, the reduction of aircraft noise at source is mentioned, and then planning and land management measures are added. in addition, operational noise reduction procedures (use of runways, roads and specific procedures) will be provided. finally, there is mention of exploitation restrictions where appropriate. all this requires an assessment of the noise abatement operating procedures to be carried out and provides for adequate consultation.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report. good, regulated and reliable statistics are a must for economic policy. they are the basis of all politics. equally important, they generate confidence among citizens and in the markets. as the current economic crisis has shown, inaccurate or falsified statistics are completely undermining the confidence of international financial institutions and markets, which is exacerbating the crisis. this dossier aims to establish a rigorous governance structure to ensure the integrity and quality of statistics in the european union. the proposals in my dossier for the five-year european statistical framework are based on the recommendations adopted in my report on the quality management of european statistics, including: stronger governance frameworks, including for independent agencies; public and standardised audit systems; user-friendly statistical information; support to member states to comply with the programme. these are the key themes of this report.
in writing. − eleven member states, including belgium, but also france and germany, are today ready to take the lead and introduce a financial transaction tax. this will require financial institutions to make a significant contribution in times of financial and economic turbulence in the future. this is a two-in-one shot. once implemented at a rapid pace, it is estimated that it will generate over eur 32 billion of additional revenue per year (eur 57 billion when implemented in the 27 member states). in addition, this tax provides for more stable markets by curbing financial speculation. the agreement of parliament to go ahead with a number of member states is therefore an important signal. i am convinced that this will be a strong incentive for other member states, but also for countries outside the eu, to reach a global agreement on the introduction of a financial transaction tax.
in writing. the european parliament today gave its green light to the enhanced cooperation decided by 11 eu countries to introduce a financial transaction tax. this is the first enhanced cooperation in the field of taxation, where all decisions require unanimity. france, germany, belgium, portugal, slovenia, austria, greece, italy, spain, slovakia and estonia are among the first signatories to this enhanced cooperation. the netherlands, which has been openly reluctant, has recently expressed interest and could join the club. as a reminder, all transactions between financial institutions (banks, stock exchanges, investment companies, insurance companies, hedge funds) should be taxed. the tax rate would be 0.1% for equity and bond trades and 0.01% for derivative contracts. the tax would apply where at least one financial institution participating in the transaction is established in the union, even if the transaction takes place outside the union. the tax could bring in between 10 and 30 billion euros. so this is a good first!
in writing. we must vote for enhanced cooperation on the financial transaction tax! the european parliament's support for the ftt has had a significant impact on the commission and the council in the past. parliament must continue to support the ttf, both for consistency with previous positions and to send a strong signal to european civil society and to those states that do not yet support the project to join the coalition. enhanced cooperation shall be in accordance with european union law and respect the rights of non-participating states. it can also have a positive effect on the internal market. the fears of mass relocation are also unfounded. the scope of the ftt should be as broad as possible: not only shares and bonds but also all derivatives. finally, the ftt with 11 countries could raise €37 billion in revenue. these considerable sums could be usefully invested to meet social needs in europe, but also to help poor households suffering from the consequences of a crisis for which they are not responsible.
in writing. i am one of the meps who in 2011 signed the written declaration to limit the transport time for animals for slaughter to eight hours. it was therefore important that parliament should reiterate this request this afternoon when voting on the wojciechowski report. and that by a very large majority: 479 votes in favour, 140 against. not only does better protection of animals during transport meet a growing demand from citizens, but it also demonstrates the european union's commitment to an ambitious and coherent animal welfare policy. some findings already need to be made: the lack of uniform compliance with european legislation on the transport of animals in the member states; the significant increase in the number of animals transported for the period 2005-2009: 8% for cattle, 70% for pigs and 3% for sheep. the recognition that animal welfare during transport also depends on the adaptation of vehicles and the correct handling of animals. therefore, animal welfare must be based, like many other policies, on a holistic approach based on scientific foundations and on the belief that animals are sentient beings.
in writing. i voted in favour of this report. during the period 2005-2009 covered by the regulation, there was a significant increase in the number of animals transported: 8% for cattle, 70% for pigs, 3% for sheep, with only the transport of horses falling by 17%, so that the objective set out in point 5 of the preamble to regulation (ec) no 1/2005, which provides that 'for reasons of animal welfare, the transport of animals for long periods of time, including animals for slaughter, should be limited as far as possible', is not met. the objective of the regulation, namely to restrict the transport of animals, has therefore not been achieved and the union's policy in this area must be reviewed. i have in fact consulted the commission in recent weeks to express my concerns about the transport of animals and the lack of homogeneity of national legislation. i was also very attentive in this report to paragraph 14 because there is no question of allowing transport for too long for any reason.

yesterday, the committee to protect journalists announced that a record 232 journalists are in prison worldwide. turkey, not a candidate country for membership of the european union, leads the dubious list with 49 journalists in prison, followed by iran and china. i can predict with near certainty how the european union will respond. we take note of the facts, we regret the facts and then we move on to the agenda. in the meantime, the negotiations with turkey are still proceeding as if nothing had happened. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. another issue, which is never discussed here, is the situation in south africa, where violence is becoming more extreme and more dramatic, and where farmers and other white citizens are being specifically targeted. it is still waiting for the first serious initiative from the european union in this area, and that too is a disgrace.
firstly, a simple observation which is important. what we call here so easily universal human rights are not really universal human rights, but things, achievements, which are first and even almost exclusively achievements of what i would call western civilization. these are accomplishments that, in fact, are hardly ingrained in other cultures. this is important because this science can prevent us from making wrong or foolish interpretations of world events. for example, the report by mr tavares rightly points out that the arab spring, which is so much celebrated by this parliament, has in fact in many cases led to a deterioration in respect for human rights, for example the rights of non-muslims in muslim countries. anyone who wasn't blind could have predicted that back then. these reports lack clear language! communism, post-communism and the rise of intolerant islamism are the main ideologies currently leading to systematic violations of human rights.

president. - the next item is the report (doc. i think that the fundamental rights office is one of those european institutions which are completely redundant and can therefore best be abolished immediately; a nice saving of a little over 25 million euros a year. the relevance of this successor to the notorious european observatory on racism and xenophobia, which was set up at the time as a kind of anti-democratic reaction to the success of identity and euro-critical parties in europe, is actually a mystery to me. such institutions already exist - and are largely subsidized - in all our member states. even for people who find this work useful - and i'm not one of them - it's got to be getting a little too much of a good thing. what i think is that in these institutions it is usually not about fighting real racism or real xenophobia, but that this is just a ploy to sweep under the rug the very real immigration problems and to circumvent free speech about them.

in writing. in voting on this report, i would like to stress with the rapporteur that, while the quality and relevance of certain written declarations can indeed be disappointing, it is important that the institutions to which a written declaration is addressed inform parliament of the action they intend to take within three months of receiving the said declaration.
in writing. i abstained this afternoon from voting in the european parliament's annual vote on human rights and democracy in the world. i regret that the text has been distorted by the addition of a totally unbalanced paragraph on the western sahara conflict. this historic conflict, a legacy of decolonization, has been going on for more than 40 years. it is a conflict between two legitimate aspirations and cannot be resolved by partial, radical or false positions. our parliament, and this is its role, condemns oppression, all oppression. president schulz recalled this yesterday when he awarded the sakharov prize to two iranian oppositionists. demands must be expressed by peaceful means and in compliance with the rule of law, which implies respect for the rights of the defence, including those of the majority of the population. the case of western sahara is particularly explicit in this respect. parliament is not impressed by this addition, which reflects a truncated view of history.
in writing. i will highlight five main points on which i have made a commitment and whose presence in the text confirms my positive vote: 1) the need for the union to strengthen its human rights policy and to speak with one voice on the subject; (2) the promotion of basic labour standards through free trade agreements; (3) stricter controls on exports, transfers, brokering and transit of dual-use goods; (4) the call on member states to put an end to discrimination and intolerance on any ground: sex, race, age, sexual orientation, religion or belief; 5) the priority protection of the rights of women and children through european policies.
in writing. the report welcomes the strategic framework and action plan to improve the effectiveness and coherence of the union's human rights policy. it stresses the role of parliament in implementing this revised policy framework. we fought over several amendments: 1. lessons from the arab spring: the eu needs to engage with a wide range of stakeholders (in addition to official authorities), such as civil society organisations, trade unions, political parties and other relevant actors. 2. the improved coordination and cooperation between the union and its member states to define clear priorities and common objectives in the field of human rights. 3. the to be a credible actor in the field of human rights, the union must be consistent in its internal and external policies by applying the same standards within and beyond its territorial borders. 4. the it is necessary to develop precise indicators and specific benchmarks in the context of the strategic framework, to serve as a basis for monitoring and measuring human rights progress in third countries.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report by mr swoboda, which gives an update on the negotiations with russia on a new agreement to replace the current one dating from 1997. the rapporteur, mr swoboda, stressed the union's desire to benefit from a strategic relationship with russia, but said that this could only be possible if russia moved closer to european values of democracy, respect for human rights and the rule of law. at the same time, the report takes the pragmatic view that cooperation with russia is needed on a number of pressing global issues, including the un security council syria issue, iran's nuclear programme and global efforts to counter proliferation and terrorism. the report is highly critical of russia's disturbing internal developments, which are manifested in laws and judicial practices that increasingly stifle freedom of expression and legitimate political activities. despite these negative developments in russia today, the best options for relations with that country remain dialogue and engagement, not a renewal of cold war rhetoric.
in writing. i strongly support the motion for a resolution on the israeli government's decision to expand settlements in the west bank, and particularly emphasise the call on the israeli government to resume the transfer of tax revenues to the palestinian authority, in accordance with the signed agreements and with the essential aim of not aggravating the humanitarian situation. i also stress the importance of the agreement of 21 november 2012 negotiated by egypt, which puts an end to the conflict between the gaza strip and southern israel. finally, i would stress the call for palestinian reconciliation, the only way to reunite the palestinians living in the gaza strip, the west bank and east jerusalem, who are part of the same palestinian people living in the occupied palestinian territory.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution calling on the ukrainian authorities to find a reasonable solution to the tymoshenko case, in collaboration with the european parliament's envoys aleksander kwaśniewski and pat cox. it also welcomed the decision of the verkhovna rada to set up a parliamentary inquiry committee to examine the problems that had occurred during the elections and the violations of the electoral law. the resolution also urges the president, the government and the parliament of ukraine to continue, intensify and strengthen the process of modernisation and reform in order to bring the country closer to the standards common to the countries of the european union. it also notes that close cooperation with the european union in the framework of the association agreement would be the best way to achieve many of these reforms. the resolution also makes a strong appeal to the high representative/vice-president ashton and commissioner füle to increase the union's commitment to ukraine with a view to achieving political association and economic integration. the resolution also stresses the need for the european union to continue to strive to exploit the full potential of eu-ukraine relations for the benefit of the citizens of ukraine and the union.
in writing. i voted for this resolution. i believe in the importance of free and fair elections which establish the legitimacy of democratic institutions and ensure their proper functioning. i call on all political forces to conduct next year's election campaign and the 2013 parliamentary elections in a free and fair manner. i am firmly convinced that the elections will be an important test of the maturity of albanian democracy and the ability of all political forces to commit to a common european agenda for the country, while being essential for further progress in the accession process. the consolidation of democracy requires free and fair elections whose results are considered legitimate by all the political parties concerned!
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution because i remain convinced that the union and its member states have a role to play in coordinating policies to ensure that the steel industry, which is sustainable and competitive, is able to adapt to the conditions prevailing in the changing european and non-european markets. in many member states, the european steel industry is suffering from a sharp drop in world demand, in some cases from competitiveness problems and rising costs.
in writing. i voted in favour of this resolution. the steel industry employs some 360 000 people and is therefore a key economic sector in the european union. i believe that parliament has a duty and an obligation to show its solidarity with the workers at the ilva steelworks and their families, who are affected by this totally unacceptable situation. in addition, it is strategically important for the industrial policy of the union to avoid any further relocation of steel sites and steel production outside the european union and to ensure that employment is guaranteed. it is equally important to apply the polluter pays principle; and the ecological balance must be restored or even improved, as required by directive 2004/35 on environmental liability.
